# Schema serde porting deviations

This file records how SDK ports differ from their sources beyond the package rename (Requirement 1.2). Each section covers one ported module. Add a section per module as types are ported.

## `codegen-c2j-to-smithy` (C2J_Converter, tasks 1.20 and 1.21)

**Source.** `codegen-c2j-to-smithy/src/main/java/software/amazon/awssdk/c2j/smithy/C2jToSmithyConverter.java` (946 lines) on the `smithy-java-bridge` branch, taken from the local copy at `fork/smithy-java-bridge-alexwoo` (commit `ee9a74f69db`). That branch's only test, `ConvertAndAssembleTest`, is a `main` method.

**Target.** Package `software.amazon.awssdk.codegen.c2j2smithy`, module `codegen-c2j-to-smithy`. Compile-scope dependencies: `smithy-model`, `smithy-aws-traits`, `smithy-protocol-traits` (for `smithy.protocols#rpcv2Cbor`; already transitive through `smithy-aws-traits`), `smithy-rules-engine`, `smithy-waiters`, `jackson-core`, `jackson-databind`. Test scope adds `smithy-aws-endpoints`: validating a converted model needs the AWS rule-set built-ins (`AWS::Region`, ...) and functions (`aws.partition`, ...). The converter only copies the rule set, so it does not need that module, but the codegen pipeline's assembly stage (task 1.22) does.

### Input type: `C2jInput` instead of codegen's `C2jModels`

The source converter took a Jackson `JsonNode` for `service-2.json`. Its module depended on `codegen` only for `SmithyToServiceModel`, the inverse converter, which is not ported.

Task 1.20 asks the converter to take `C2jModels`. That type lives in `codegen` (`software.amazon.awssdk.codegen.C2jModels`) and holds codegen POJOs (`ServiceModel`, `Paginators`, `Waiters`, `EndpointRuleSetModel`, ...). `codegen` will depend on this module (task 1.22), so this module cannot depend on `codegen` without a cycle.

Decision: a converter-local input type, `C2jInput`, with the same contents as `C2jModels` but as Jackson trees: the service name, `service-2.json` (required), and optional `paginators-1.json`, `waiters-2.json`, `endpoint-rule-set.json` and `endpoint-tests.json`. `C2jInput.fromDirectory(serviceName, dir)` loads them from a `codegen-resources` directory.

- Why not an interface implemented in `codegen`: it would need one method per file returning a format-neutral value, which is a JSON tree anyway, and it would tie the converter's API to codegen's POJO shapes.
- Why not move the C2J POJOs out of `codegen`: they carry codegen-specific Jackson mappings and customization hooks, and the move would touch every C2J consumer in `codegen`.
- The name differs from `C2jModels` so that `CodegenPipeline` (task 1.22) can import both without a fully qualified name.
- Not a 1:1 file read: stage 2 of the Codegen_Pipeline applies C2J customizations to codegen's `ServiceModel` POJO. Task 1.22 must hand the customized model to the converter, for example as `ObjectMapper.valueToTree(serviceModel)`. Whether that tree keeps every C2J field that the converter reads (`jsonvalue`, `eventstream`, `wrapper`, `document`, `union`, `contextParam`, ...) is not verified here; task 1.22 needs a test that converts the raw file and the round-tripped POJO and compares the results. Done in task 1.22: `C2jServiceModelRoundTripTest` (see the `codegen` section).

### API and behavior deviations

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `static Model convert(Path)`, `static Model convert(JsonNode)` | `static ConversionResult convert(C2jInput)`; `ConversionResult(Model, ConversionGapReport)` | Requirement 17.10 (gap report on every run); input type above |
| `java.util.Set.of(...)` for `RESERVED_HEADERS` (never read) | `Collections.unmodifiableSet(new HashSet<>(Arrays.asList(...)))` with Smithy's `HttpHeaderTraitValidator` blocklist, used to unbind reserved headers (task 1.21, below) | Java 9+ API (Requirement 1.4) |
| `IllegalArgumentException` for an unsupported shape type, location or `timestampFormat` | `C2jConversionException(service, c2jName, reason)`, where `c2jName` is the shape, `Shape$member` or operation name | Requirement 17.14 |
| Other runtime failures (for example an invalid Smithy identifier) propagate unchanged | Wrapped in `C2jConversionException` naming the shape or operation being converted, or `metadata` for the service shape | Requirement 17.14 |
| Marker traits emitted as `DynamicTrait` only | Markers only for C2J information with no standard trait (task 1.21, below), plus one `ConversionGapReport` entry per marker property (`C2jMarkerTrait`); unconverted properties without a marker are also recorded | Requirement 17.10 |
| No definitions for `com.amazonaws.c2j#*` | `META-INF/smithy/c2j-markers.smithy` (with a `manifest`), discovered by `ModelAssembler#discoverModels` | A.14 stage 4: the assembled model must validate |
| Shape-level C2J `flattened` emitted as `@xmlFlattened` on the list or map shape | `@xmlFlattened` on every structure or union member that targets the flattened list or map | `@xmlFlattened` is only valid on members; the shape form fails validation (SQS). The SDK treats a member as flattened if it or its target is, so no information is lost for members. A flattened list that is only used as an element of another list loses the flag |
| `@jsonName` and `@xmlName` on list and map members that have a C2J `locationName` | `@xmlName` only | `@jsonName` is only valid on structure and union members; the source form fails validation (Route 53, SNS, Query fixtures). JSON protocols never name list elements or map entries |
| Fully qualified `software.amazon.smithy...` names inline | Imports | SDK style; no behavior change |
| `ConvertAndAssembleTest` `main` | JUnit 5 `ConvertAndAssembleTest` over C2J fixtures and the Prototype_Service_Set C2J models | Requirement 41.1 |

Not ported: `SmithyToServiceModel`, `SmithyIrParityRunner`, `SmithyTypesCodegenRunner`, `SmithyTypesCodegenAllRunner`, `codegen-diff.sh`, `IR-REMOVAL-PLAN.md`. The parity runners depend on smithy-java `codegen-plugin` (JDK 21), and the new pipeline never converts back to C2J.

### Standard traits (task 1.21)

The converter now reads all five C2J files and emits a standard trait for every row of design A.14's "Ported converter adds" table. The rule for markers: a marker carries only C2J information that no standard trait carries, and it is not emitted when that information is empty.

| C2J source | Emitted | Notes and deviations from the A.14 table |
|---|---|---|
| `metadata.protocols` (else `protocol`), `jsonVersion`, `protocolSettings.h2` | One trait per entry, in C2J order: `restJson1`, `awsJson1_0`/`awsJson1_1`, `restXml`, `awsQuery`, `ec2Query`, `smithy.protocols#rpcv2Cbor`. `h2: eventstream` → `http: [http/1.1, h2], eventStreamHttp: [h2]`; `optional` and `required` map similarly | Unknown protocol names get no trait and a `metadata.protocols=<name>` gap. CloudWatch gets `rpcv2Cbor`, `awsJson1_0` and `awsQuery` (Requirement 12.6) |
| `metadata.targetPrefix` | The service shape name | Not in the table. awsJson's `X-Amz-Target` and rpcv2Cbor's request path use the service shape name, so it must be the target prefix (`AmazonSQS`, `GraniteServiceVersion20100801`, `DynamoDB_20120810`), as in the published AWS Smithy models. When the prefix is absent, not a valid identifier, or collides with a shape or operation name ignoring case, the sanitized serviceId is used and `targetPrefix` stays on the metadata marker |
| `metadata.serviceId`, `endpointPrefix`, `signingName`, `uid`, `serviceFullName` | `aws.api#service(sdkId, endpointPrefix, arnNamespace, cloudFormationName, cloudTrailEventSource, docId)` and `@title` | `arnNamespace` = signingName, else endpointPrefix; `cloudFormationName` = sdkId without non-alphanumerics, capitalized; `cloudTrailEventSource` = `<arnNamespace>.amazonaws.com`; `docId` = uid. `serviceAbbreviation` stays on the metadata marker: smithy 1.71's `aws.api#service` has no `abbreviation` member |
| `metadata.auth`, else `signatureVersion`; operation `auth`, else `authtype`; `unsignedPayload` | `aws.auth#sigv4(name)`, `aws.auth#sigv4a(name)` (which also applies `sigv4`, as its selector requires), `smithy.api#httpBearerAuth`; `@auth` on the service only when its C2J default order differs from what Smithy derives from the applied auth traits; `@auth` on an operation only when it differs from the service default; `aws.auth#unsignedPayload` | `sigv4` name = signingName, else endpointPrefix. `none` → `@auth([])`; `smithy.api#noAuth` together with other schemes → `@optionalAuth` plus `@auth(others)`; `v4`, `s3`, `s3v4`, `iam` → sigv4; `v4a` → sigv4a; `v4-unsigned-body` → sigv4 plus `unsignedPayload`; `bearer` → httpBearerAuth. A scheme only an operation uses is also applied to the service. `custom` and other values keep the `authtype` marker |
| `metadata.awsQueryCompatible` | `aws.protocols#awsQueryCompatible` | Only when the service has `awsJson1_0` or `rpcv2Cbor` (the trait's selector); otherwise dropped with a gap |
| `metadata.xmlNamespace` | Service `@xmlNamespace` | Now for every service that has it, not only `query`; derived as before when an awsQuery or ec2Query service lacks it |
| Exception `error.httpStatusCode`, `error.code`, `senderFault`, `fault` | `@error`, `@httpError(code)`, `aws.protocols#awsQueryError(code, httpResponseCode)` | `awsQueryError` is emitted whenever the wire code (C2J code, else the C2J shape name) differs from the Smithy shape name, for every protocol; `httpResponseCode` defaults to 400 or 500 by fault. Fault rule changed from the source: `fault: true` → server; else `senderFault` decides; else a status of 500 or more is server (CloudWatch `InternalServiceFault` has a 500 and no `senderFault`). The `error` marker is removed |
| `http.requestUri` labels | `@http` with each label renamed to the input member bound to it (`{Id}` → `{HostedZoneId}`, `{resource-arn}` → `{ResourceArn}`), greedy `+` and query literals kept (`UriLabels`) | The `locationName` marker stays on the member (design: "`#locationName` kept"). A label bound to no `location: uri` input member, or a URI Smithy cannot parse, fails the conversion with `C2jConversionException` naming the operation (Requirement 17.14); the source's `operation.http.requestUri` gap is gone. The `http` marker is now emitted only for operations with no `@http` (RPC protocols), per the table |
| `httpChecksum` | `aws.protocols#httpChecksum` | Unknown properties of the block are recorded as `operation.httpChecksum.<property>` gaps |
| `requestcompression.encodings` | `@requestCompression(encodings)` | Same unknown-property rule |
| `endpoint.hostPrefix` | `@endpoint(hostPrefix)`, and `@hostLabel` on each input member a label names | Smithy only allows `@hostLabel` on a required member targeting a string. If any label of a prefix does not qualify, the operation keeps the `hostPrefix` marker and gets no `@endpoint` |
| `staticContextParams`, `operationContextParams`, member `contextParam`, `clientContextParams` | `smithy.rules#staticContextParams`, `operationContextParams`, `contextParam`, `clientContextParams` | Values copied unchanged (the C2J and Smithy shapes match) |
| `endpointoperation`, `endpointdiscovery`, member `endpointdiscoveryid` | Service `aws.api#clientEndpointDiscovery(operation, error)`, `aws.api#clientDiscoveredEndpoint(required)`, `aws.api#clientEndpointDiscoveryId` | C2J does not name the invalid-endpoint error. The converter uses an `InvalidEndpoint*` error that every discovered operation declares; when there is none (DynamoDB), the trait has no error, the service gets `@suppress(["ClientEndpointDiscovery.MissingErrorDefinition"])`, and an `endpointdiscovery.error` gap is recorded. `endpointdiscovery` without an `endpointoperation` in the service gets no trait and a gap |
| `eventstream: true` structure, `event: true` | `@streaming` union; event structures get no trait | Event membership is implied by the union. `eventstream` and `event` markers removed. Union members never get `@required` |
| member `queryName` | `aws.protocols#ec2QueryName` | — |
| string `enum` | IDL 2 `enum` shape with `@enumValue` members | Member names are synthesized from values: characters outside `[A-Za-z0-9_]` become `_`, a leading digit gets a `_` prefix, a value with no letters or digits becomes `VALUE_<index>`, and names unique ignoring case get `_2`, `_3`, ... The SDK names constants from values, so only validity matters. Duplicate values are dropped. An enum with an empty value stays a string with the legacy `@enum` trait and a `shape.enum(empty value)` gap |
| `box` | Dropped | IDL 2 members are optional unless `@required` or `@default`, and the converter emits no `@default` |
| `required` | `@required` plus `@clientOptional` on every required structure member | C2J `required` is not a client-side guarantee: the SDK keeps required members nullable. `@clientOptional` says exactly that |
| `pattern` | `@pattern` | An invalid regular expression is dropped with a `shape.pattern(invalid)` gap instead of failing the conversion |
| `jsonvalue` | `@mediaType("application/json")` on the target | `@mediaType` is only valid on string and blob shapes, not members. A target used only by `jsonvalue` references gets the trait; a target also used elsewhere is copied as `<Name>JsonValue` (unique ignoring case), the copy gets the trait and a `shapeName` marker, and only the `jsonvalue` references target it. `jsonvalue` marker removed |
| `location: header` with a reserved name | Unbound, with a `reservedHeader` marker carrying the header name and its gap entry | Per the table. Consequence: in a REST operation that also has an `@httpPayload` member, the unbound member is a body member and Smithy's payload validation fails. No prototype service has a reserved header. Alternative to consider in phase 6 (S3 `Content-Length`): keep `@httpHeader` and add `@suppress(["HttpHeaderTrait"])`, which keeps the wire binding as a standard trait |
| `paginators-1.json` | `@paginated(inputToken, outputToken, items, pageSize)` (`PaginatorConversion`) | Converted when Smithy can express it: one input token that is a non-required top-level input member targeting a string or map, one output token path resolving to a string or map. Otherwise no trait and a gap (multiple tokens, missing tokens, a required input token, an unresolvable member). A result key that is not one path to a list or map (CloudWatch's lists of result keys) or a limit key that is not a top-level integral member (Route 53's string `MaxItems`) only drops `items` or `pageSize`, with a gap. `more_results` and `non_aggregate_keys` are recorded as gaps. Map tokens, non-integer page sizes and required output tokens are valid C2J pagination that Smithy flags as DANGER; those operations get `@suppress` for exactly `PaginatedTrait.WrongShapeType` / `PaginatedTrait.ShouldNotBeRequired` |
| `waiters-2.json` | `smithy.waiters#waitable` on each waiter's operation (`WaiterConversion`) | `delay` → `minDelay` (`maxDelay` = delay only when delay exceeds Smithy's 120 s default); `description` → `documentation`. `path`/`pathAll`/`pathAny` with a string → `stringEquals`/`allStringEquals`/`anyStringEquals`; `path` with a boolean → `booleanEquals`; `error` with a code → `errorType`, with `true`/`false` → `success: false`/`true`; `status` 200 → `success: true` (2xx in Smithy, as the published AWS models map it). Any other acceptor (a status other than 200, a numeric expected value) drops the whole waiter with a gap. `maxAttempts` has no Smithy equivalent and is always a `waiter.maxAttempts` gap |
| operation `input.locationName`, `input.xmlNamespace` (rest-xml request root element) | `@xmlName` (only when it differs from the structure name) and `@xmlNamespace` on the input structure (task 1.23) | The candidate mapping from task 1.21. Applied only when every operation using the structure as input agrees on both values and no other shape targets the structure (where the traits would change that member's serialization); otherwise both stay on the `inputMeta` marker. A structure-level C2J `xmlNamespace` wins. Route 53: 22 operations, all converted |
| `endpoint-rule-set.json`, `endpoint-tests.json` | `smithy.rules#endpointRuleSet`, `smithy.rules#endpointTests` | Copied unchanged. Rule sets that use `AWS::Auth::AccountId`, `AWS::Auth::AccountIdEndpointMode` or `AWS::Auth::CredentialScope` get `@suppress(["RuleSetAwsBuiltIn.<built-in>"])`, the advisory DANGER that the published AWS models also suppress (DynamoDB) |

Other decisions:

- **Case-insensitive shape name collisions** (SNS `String`/`string`, `Endpoint`/`endpoint`). Smithy shape IDs must be unique ignoring case. In each group of C2J names that differ only in case, a structure (a generated class) keeps its name; otherwise the name that sorts first (`String.compareTo`, so uppercase first) keeps it. The others get the `C2jShape` suffix (`stringC2jShape`, `endpointC2jShape`; a number is appended if that also collides), every reference is retargeted, and the renamed shape gets a `com.amazonaws.c2j#shapeName` marker with the C2J name plus its gap entry. Operation and service names are also checked ignoring case now.
- **Duplicate query error codes** (CloudWatch `DashboardNotFoundError` and `ResourceNotFound` both use `ResourceNotFound`). This is in the C2J model; for awsQuery, ec2Query and awsQueryCompatible services whose bound errors share a wire code, the service gets `@suppress(["QueryErrorCode"])` and an `error.code(duplicate)` gap.
- **OD-19 markers**, kept with gap entries: `wrapper`, `synthetic`, `operationName`, `resultWrapper` (now only when it is not `<OperationName>Result`), and new markers for member `deprecatedName` and `alternateBeanPropertyName`, the service `authorizers` block and the operation `authorizer`.
- **Markers removed** (fully covered by standard traits): `clientContextParams`, `httpChecksum`, `requestcompression`, `endpointdiscovery`, `endpointoperation`, `staticContextParams`, `operationContextParams`, `contextParam`, `queryName`, `jsonvalue`, `unsignedPayload`, `eventstream`, `event`, `error`. **Narrowed**: `metadata` (only unconverted properties, for example `globalEndpoint`, `serviceAbbreviation`), `http` (operations without `@http`), `hostPrefix` (unbindable labels), `authtype` (unmapped values), `resultWrapper` (non-standard), `inputMeta` (the input reference documentation, plus a root element name or namespace that cannot go on the input structure; task 1.23). **Unchanged**: `required`, `errors`, `outputDoc`, `locationName`. **New**: `shapeName`, `reservedHeader`, `deprecatedName`, `alternateBeanPropertyName`, `authorizers`, `authorizer`.

### Converted prototype models

`ConvertAndAssembleTest` converts the seven fixtures and all five C2J files of each Prototype_Service_Set service, and re-assembles them with the marker definitions and `smithy-aws-endpoints`. All seven services (SQS, Secrets Manager, Account, Route 53, SNS, CloudWatch, DynamoDB) assemble with zero ERROR or DANGER events, so the Route 53 `HttpLabelTrait` and SNS `ShapeIdConflict` failures pinned in task 1.20 are fixed.

Information still only on markers or gap entries, and how task 1.23's IntermediateModel builder does without it (see the `codegen` section):

- `operation.http` on RPC operations: not needed; the rpcv2Cbor request URI is derived from the protocol and the service shape name.
- `shape.required` order: not needed; the generated code is the same with Smithy member order.
- `operation.errors` order and duplicates: the order is kept by the converted model, and the Codegen_Pipeline restores it after re-assembly. Duplicates are dropped, which changes nothing generated.
- `operation.input` (`inputMeta`): converted to `@xmlName`/`@xmlNamespace` on the input structure (task 1.23; row above). Only the input reference documentation stays on the marker, and the generators do not read it.
- `metadata`: `globalEndpoint` (Route 53): not needed. `serviceAbbreviation` (SQS, Route 53, SNS, CloudWatch, DynamoDB): not available, so Javadoc uses `@title` (documentation-only difference).
- Pagination: Route 53 `ListResourceRecordSets` (multiple tokens) and six string `MaxItems` page sizes; CloudWatch `DescribeAlarmsForMetric` (no tokens) and three paginators with several result keys; DynamoDB `BatchGetItem` (required input token). Waiters: `maxAttempts` (Route 53, CloudWatch, DynamoDB) and CloudWatch `AlarmMuteRuleExists` (status 404 acceptor). The Codegen_Pipeline takes these definitions from the sidecar files (`C2jSidecarSupplement`).
- `shape.synthetic` (CloudWatch, DynamoDB), `shape.name` (SNS), `error.code(duplicate)` (CloudWatch), `endpointdiscovery.error` (DynamoDB): not needed for the prototype services' generated code.

## `codegen` (Codegen_Pipeline, task 1.22)

**Target.** Package `software.amazon.awssdk.codegen.pipeline` in `codegen`: `CodegenPipeline` (`run`, `prepare` for stages 1–5, `generate` for stage 6), `ServiceInputs`, `PreparedService`, `CodegenPipelineException` (`service()`, `stage()`), `CodegenPipelineListener`, `C2jCustomizations`, `C2jServiceModelSerializer` and `SmithyModelDiscovery`, all `@SdkInternalApi`. Not a port; deviations are from design A.14 and the task text.

| Design / task | Implementation | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Stage 1 loads `C2jModels` | Loads the converter's `C2jInput` (JSON trees of all five files) and codegen's `ServiceModel` POJO. The sidecar files go to the converter unchanged | Stage 2 customizations edit only the service model; the converter reads JSON trees (`C2jInput`, task 1.20) |
| Stage 2 hands the customized POJO to the converter "for example as `ObjectMapper.valueToTree`" | `C2jServiceModelSerializer`: databind with mix-ins that use the C2J keys (`enum`, `member`, `key`, `value`), write auth types in file form (`v4`), leave out `false`/`0`/null/empty values, and keep an explicit `min`/`max` of 0 and an empty `awsQueryCompatible` | `valueToTree` writes the POJO accessor names (`enumValues`, `listMember`), `AuthType` constant names and derived getters (`isRetryable`) |
| — | POJO fields added so the round trip loses nothing the converter reads: `ErrorTrait.senderFault`, `ServiceMetadata.globalEndpoint`; `Shape.min`/`max` boxed (`getMin()`/`getMax()` still return `long`) | `C2jServiceModelRoundTripTest`: for all seven prototype services the raw `service-2.json` and the written POJO convert to the same Smithy model (shape by shape, including member order) and the same gap report. Before the fix SQS and CloudWatch lost `awsQueryCompatible`, Route 53 lost `globalEndpoint`, and fault classification lost `senderFault` |
| `disabledCustomizations` per service | New `customization.config` key `disabledCustomizations` (list of names). Names are the keys of the new `DefaultCustomizationProcessor.namedProcessorsFor(config)`: the `customization.config` key for config-driven processors (`shapeModifiers`, `renameShapes`, ...), else the class name without `Processor` (`smithyRpcV2CborProtocol`, ...). `getProcessorFor` now builds its chain from that map, in the same order | A name per customization that can be written in `customization.config` (Requirement 17.11) |
| Skipped customization logs `WARN customization <name> skipped for <service>: <reason>` | Same text, through `CodegenPipelineListener.warning` (default: `software.amazon.awssdk.utils.Logger` at WARN). A customization that throws is undone (the model is restored from a snapshot taken before it ran) and skipped. A `disabledCustomizations` entry that names no customization gets its own warning | Skipping a half-applied customization would leave an inconsistent model |
| — | `ModelInvalidException` from a customization (`lowercaseShapeValidator`) is not skipped; it fails stage 2 | It is a model validation result, not a customization failure. The mojo still writes `validation-report.json` for it |
| Stage 4: `Model.assembler().addModel(converted).discoverModels(classLoader)` | The converted model is serialized and re-assembled with `addDocumentNode`, as in `ConvertAndAssembleTest`. Discovery is `SmithyModelDiscovery.discoverModels`: `discoverModels` without the `smithy-aws-protocol-tests` and `smithy-protocol-tests` JARs. `SmithyBuildGenerator` uses it too | Every trait is validated against its definition. The protocol test JARs (now on the plugin class path) declare dozens of test services in their manifests; discovering them adds those services to every model. Task 1.33 must import the test models explicitly |
| Stage 4 validation errors | Message lists each `ERROR`/`DANGER` event as `<severity> <event id> [<shape id>]: <message>` | Requirement 17.13 |
| Native Smithy model: `smithy-build.json` or `model/*` | `smithy-build.json`: unchanged route through smithy-build; the `AwsSdkJavaCodegenPlugin` now calls `CodegenPipeline.run` with the projection model and its events (`ServiceInputs.smithyModel`), so it starts at stage 4. `model/`: new mojo parameter `smithyModelDirectory` (default `${basedir}/model`); when it holds `.smithy`/`.json` files the module is generated from them even if `codeGenResources` has a C2J model, and `customization.config` is still read from `codeGenResources` | Requirement 17.4. Behavior change: the smithy-build plugin now stops on an `ERROR`/`DANGER` event instead of generating and failing afterwards |
| Generate into a staging directory and move it into place | Staging is `<build dir>/codegen-staging/<service>-<random>`; files are moved one by one into the existing output directories (files not generated in this run are kept, as before). All services of one mojo execution share one staging directory and are moved only when all succeed (`generate(List, shareModelsTargets)`) | `shareModelConfig` (DynamoDB Streams shares DynamoDB's model classes) needs the target's generated models in the same sources tree. A failure in a move itself can leave part of the output in place |
| "A failed stage writes nothing" | Two exceptions: the gap report is written in stage 3, so it exists even when a later stage fails (Requirement 17.10: produced on every converter run), and a requested `validation-report.json` is kept when stage 6 fails | Both are reports, not generated sources |
| Gap report service name | The module's `artifactId` when `service-2.json` is directly in `codeGenResources`, otherwise its directory name (`dynamodb`, `dynamodbstreams`) | One report per C2J model |
| Stage 5 uses `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilder` as is | One fix in `AddSmithyOperations`: `staticContextParams` values and `operationContextParams` paths are now jackson-jr trees (`JrsValue`, `JrsString`) instead of databind nodes; `AddSmithyOperationsTest` updated | The endpoint generators cast them to jackson-jr types, so DynamoDB (which has `operationContextParams`) failed in stage 6 with a `ClassCastException` |
| — | `codegen` depends on `codegen-c2j-to-smithy` and, at runtime scope, `smithy-aws-endpoints`; `codegen-maven-plugin` adds `codegen-c2j-to-smithy`, `smithy-protocol-test-traits` and `smithy-aws-protocol-tests` (`${smithy.version}`, 1.71.0) | Stage 4 needs the rule-set built-ins; the protocol test dependencies are for tasks 1.32 and 1.33. `.brazil.json` has no mapping for `smithy-aws-endpoints`, `smithy-protocol-test-traits` or `smithy-aws-protocol-tests`, and `codegen-c2j-to-smithy` is `skipImport`, although `codegen` is imported |

### Output of the C2J route compared with the legacy C2J IntermediateModelBuilder

Task 1.22 generated the seven prototype services by both routes and found the gaps below in the phase 2 `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilder`. Task 1.23 fixed them; `LegacyRouteParityTest` (in `codegen-maven-plugin`, because generation needs the packaged `codegen` JAR) now generates every prototype module both ways and fails on any difference other than the ones listed in `src/test/resources/legacy-route-parity/<module>.txt`. Its comparison ignores layout and the `serviceAbbreviation`/`serviceFullName` documentation difference. `LegacyRouteParity.main` prints the differences.

| Task 1.22 finding | Status after task 1.23 |
|---|---|
| No paginators or waiters (all services) | Fixed: paginators from `@paginated`, waiters from `smithy.waiters#waitable`, supplemented from the sidecar files where the converter could not express them (below). DynamoDB's tests, including `src/it`, compile and pass |
| CloudWatch rpcv2Cbor request URI `/` (wire-breaking) | Fixed: `/service/<service shape name>/operation/<operation name>` for `smithy.protocols#rpcv2Cbor`, from the protocol and the service shape name, not from the `SmithyRpcV2CborProtocolProcessor` customization |
| SNS awsQuery wire names from `emitPropertyName` and map value `value` (wire-breaking) | Fixed: query reads `@xmlName` for member, list member and map entry names, as rest-xml and ec2 do |
| DynamoDB `AttributeValue.BuilderImpl.setNull` missing | Fixed from `customization.config`, not from the OD-19 marker (below) |
| `@throws` and `throws` order | Fixed: the pipeline restores the operation error order lost in re-assembly (below) |
| CloudWatch `LimitExceededException` Javadoc | Fixed, and with it a wire difference task 1.22 did not list: CloudWatch's `LimitExceededException` and `LimitExceededFault` both map to the class `LimitExceededException`. The C2J route keeps the shape declared last (`LimitExceededFault`, error code `LimitExceededFault`); the Smithy route kept the first one it met (`LimitExceededException`), so the two routes registered different error codes. The Smithy route now keeps the error shape whose ShapeId sorts last, which is the C2J declaration order for name-ordered C2J models |
| — | Also fixed, not found by task 1.22: Route 53 rest-xml requests had no `xmlns` (22 marshallers, wire-breaking), because the request namespace was only on the `inputMeta` marker. The converter now puts it on the input structure |
| Route 53 path labels named after the member (`{HostedZoneId}` instead of `{Id}`) | Remaining, listed in `legacy-route-parity/route53.txt` (17 request classes and their 17 marshallers). The URI template and the member binding agree, so the wire is unchanged. Visible effect: the `sdkFieldNameToField()` key and the `LocationTrait` name of those members are the member name (`HostedZoneId`), not the C2J label (`Id`). Smithy requires the label to equal the member name; the C2J label is only on the `locationName` marker |
| Service names in Javadoc | Remaining (documentation only): the Smithy route has `@title` (`Amazon Simple Queue Service`), not `serviceAbbreviation` (`Amazon SQS`), which smithy 1.71's `aws.api#service` cannot carry |

## `codegen` Smithy-only IntermediateModel (task 1.23)

**Target.** `IntermediateModel`, `ShapeModel`, `OperationModel`, `MemberModel` and `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilder` in `codegen`; new `PaginatorTraitTranslator`, `WaiterTraitTranslator` (package `software.amazon.awssdk.codegen.smithy`), `AlternateBeanPropertyNameProcessor` (package `...codegen.customization.processors`) and `C2jSidecarSupplement` (package `...codegen.pipeline`). Deviations are from design A.15 and the task text.

| Design / task | Implementation | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `IntermediateModel` holds the validated `Model` (`@JsonIgnore`) and `serviceId` | `smithyModel()`, `serviceId()` and `withSmithyModel(Model, ShapeId)` (fails if the ID is not a service shape); `getShape(ShapeId)`. `ShapeModel.shapeId()`, `OperationModel.operationId()`, `MemberModel.memberId()` with `with...` setters. None is serialized, and none is part of `equals`/`hashCode` | Requirements 17.5 and 17.6. Non-bean accessor names keep them out of both Jackson serializers. Equality is left alone because `SharedModelsValidator` compares the shapes of two services (DynamoDB and DynamoDB Streams), whose ShapeIds differ by namespace |
| Remove C2J-typed fields and views (`c2jName`, `c2jShape`, `getShapeByNameAndC2jName`, `ParameterHttpMapping`, `ShapeMarshaller`/`ShapeUnmarshaller`, `Protocol`, ...) | **Not removed in this task.** The Smithy route no longer uses `getShapeByNameAndC2jName` (it links outputs by ShapeId), and the method is documented as a legacy view. Every other field stays | The legacy C2J `IntermediateModelBuilder` and every marshaller, unmarshaller and client generator still read these fields, and the codegen golden tests (`codegen/src/test/resources/.../poet`) still run through the legacy builder. Design A.15 removes them together with those generators, which task 5.1 deletes. On the Smithy route the fields hold Smithy names: `c2jName` is the Smithy shape or member name and `c2jShape` the target shape name, which equal the C2J names because the converter keeps names |
| Builder populates paginators from standard traits | `PaginatorTraitTranslator`: `@paginated` with the service defaults merged (`PaginatedIndex`) → `input_token`, `output_token`, `result_key` (`items`), `limit_key` (`pageSize`); `more_results` is never set. `OperationModel.isPaginated()` is set as the C2J route sets it | Requirement 17.9 |
| Builder populates waiters from standard traits | `WaiterTraitTranslator`: `minDelay` → `delay`; `output` `stringEquals`/`allStringEquals`/`anyStringEquals`/`booleanEquals` → `path`/`pathAll`/`pathAny`/`path` with a boolean; `errorType` → `error`; `success: true` → `status` 200; `success: false` → `error` `true`. An `inputOutput` matcher skips the waiter with a warning. `maxAttempts` is 25 (`DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`) | Smithy waiters bound waiting by time; the generated waiters need an attempt count. 25 is the C2J value of the DynamoDB table waiters, which therefore come from the trait unchanged |
| — (paginators and waiters the converter cannot express) | `C2jSidecarSupplement`, stage 5 of the Codegen_Pipeline, C2J route only: per operation and per waiter name, the trait-derived definition is kept when it equals the `paginators-1.json`/`waiters-2.json` definition, and the sidecar definition is used otherwise. `PreparedService.supplementedPaginators()`/`supplementedWaiters()` list them. For the prototype services: Route 53 7 paginators and `ResourceRecordSetsChanged`; CloudWatch `DescribeAlarms`, `DescribeAlarmsForMetric`, `GetMetricData`, `ListMetrics` and 4 waiters; DynamoDB `BatchGetItem` and 4 waiters. Every other paginator and waiter comes from the traits | Smithy cannot express several tokens, a required input token, several result keys, string page sizes, `more_results`, a status other than 200 or an attempt count. Dropping those would remove public API (`DynamoDbClient.batchGetItemPaginator`, which the Enhanced Client calls, and Route 53 `listResourceRecordSetsPaginator`). The sidecar files are read in stage 1 with the service model, so the builder itself stays marker- and C2J-free. A native Smithy model has no sidecar and gets the trait-derived views only |
| `SmithyIntermediateModelPostprocessor` runs IM-level customizations such as `shapeModifiers.emitPropertyName` | `emitPropertyName` already runs in stage 2 on the C2J model (the member is renamed, and its wire name reaches the Smithy model as `@jsonName`/`@xmlName`). New: `AlternateBeanPropertyNameProcessor` in the postprocessor applies `shapeModifiers.<shape>.modify.<member>.alternateBeanPropertyName` (DynamoDB `setNull`), looking a renamed member up by its `emitPropertyName` name and skipping missing shapes and members | OD-19 keeps `alternateBeanPropertyName` on a marker, and Requirement 17.9 forbids reading markers, so the IM postprocessor does not read the marker either: it reads the customization itself, which is where the information comes from. `existingNameDeprecated` (`deprecatedName`) is not applied on the Smithy route; no prototype service uses it. `ShapeModifiersProcessor`'s IM half (`excludeShape`, `emitEnumName`, `marshallLocationName`, ...) is not run either; no prototype service uses it |
| rpcv2Cbor request URI | `AddSmithyInputShapes`: `POST /service/<service shape name>/operation/<operation name>` for `smithy-rpc-v2-cbor` | rpcv2Cbor specification; the converter emits no `@http` for RPC protocols |
| Member wire names from `xmlName` for XML protocols (Requirement 17.8) | `SmithyWireNames` reads `@xmlName` for `query` too | The C2J route uses the C2J `locationName` for every protocol; the converter writes it as `@xmlName` for XML-based protocols |
| — | Error shapes that get the same exception class name: the one whose ShapeId sorts last is generated (`AddSmithyExceptionShapes`) | Matches the C2J route, which builds exceptions in shape declaration order and keeps the last |
| — | `CodegenPipeline` stage 4 restores the operation and service error order of the converted model after re-assembly | `ModelSerializer` sorts error lists by ShapeId; the order decides the generated `@throws` and `throws` order |
| — | `codegen` declares `smithy-waiters` (it was transitive through `codegen-c2j-to-smithy`) | `maven-dependency-plugin:analyze` (used undeclared dependency). `.brazil.json` has no mapping for it |
| Marker-free builder (Requirement 17.9; Property 46 is task 1.24, skipped) | `MarkerTraitIndependenceTest`: for each prototype service, removing every `com.amazonaws.c2j` trait from the converted model gives the same intermediate model JSON | A smoke check of the requirement on the seven prototype models |

**Codegen golden tests.** The `codegen` poet golden tests build their models with the legacy C2J `IntermediateModelBuilder` and are unchanged; they pass. The Smithy route is covered by the `codegen` unit tests of the builder (`PaginatorAndWaiterTraitTranslatorTest`, `SmithyIntermediateModelPostprocessorTest`, `AddSmithyInputShapesTest`, `C2jSidecarSupplementTest`, `MarkerTraitIndependenceTest`) and by `LegacyRouteParityTest`, which compares the whole generated output of the seven prototype modules with the legacy route.

### Trait_Inclusion_Policy and Trait_Initializer (task 1.25)

**Sources.** smithy-java 1.7.0 `codegen/codegen-core`: `TraitInitializer`, `integrations/core/CoreIntegration` and its initializers (`AnnotationTraitInitializer`, `StringTraitInitializer`, `DefaultTraitInitializer`, `HttpTraitInitializer`, `HttpErrorTraitInitializer`, `XmlNamespaceTraitInitializer`, `EndpointTraitInitializer`, `RetryableTraitInitializer`, `RequestCompressionTraitInitializer`, `PaginatedTraitInitializer`, `StringListTraitInitializer`, `LengthTraitInitializer`, `RangeTraitInitializer`, `HttpApiKeyAuthTraitInitializer`, `GenericTraitInitializer`, `NodeWriter`), and `CodeGenerationContext#collectRuntimeTraits`/`PRELUDE_RUNTIME_TRAITS`.

**Target.** Package `software.amazon.awssdk.codegen.schema` in `codegen`: `TraitInclusionPolicy`, `TraitInclusionConfig`, `TraitInitializer`, `TraitRenderContext`, `SchemaCodegenExtension`, `TraitInitializers` (registry) and `SchemaCodegenException` (`@SdkProtectedApi`); `CoreSchemaCodegenExtension`, `GenericTraitInitializer`, `DefaultTraitInclusion`, `DefaultTraitInclusionSpec`, `SchemaRuntimeType` (`@SdkInternalApi`); package-private `DocumentLiterals`, `DefaultTraitRenderContext`. New `customization.config` key `schemaTraits` (`SchemaTraitsConfig`). Nothing calls the policy or the registry yet; the Schema_Generator (task 1.27) builds both before it writes code.

| Source / design | Implementation | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `TraitInitializer<T> extends BiConsumer<JavaWriter, T>` with `traitClass()`; the first initializer whose class `isInstance` the trait wins | `traitId()`, `traitClass()` and `CodeBlock render(T, TraitRenderContext)`. The registry is keyed by trait ShapeId; a trait with no initializer renders as a `GenericTrait` | Requirement 9.8 registers initializers per trait ShapeId and fails on two for one ID, which a class-hierarchy match cannot express. JavaPoet instead of `JavaWriter` (design A.16) |
| Initializers read smithy-model getters (`HttpTrait.getMethod()`, ...) | Built-ins read `Trait.toNode()`; `traitClass()` is `Trait` | Renders the same code whether the trait is typed or a `DynamicTrait` (for example when its smithy-model class is not on the plugin class path), and follows the runtime factories, which also build from the value |
| `CoreIntegration` is discovered through `ServiceLoader` with other integrations; the order decides which initializer wins | `CoreSchemaCodegenExtension` is always registered directly; `SchemaCodegenExtension`s from `ServiceLoader` (`TraitInitializers.load(ClassLoader)`) or `TraitInitializers.of(...)` are added. An extension that registers a trait the SDK already covers fails, naming the trait and both classes | Requirement 9.8; a built-in cannot be dropped by a missing service file |
| 15 initializers, several shared by trait class (`AnnotationTrait`, `StringTrait`, `StringListTrait`) | 48 initializers, one per typed runtime trait of task 1.7 (`TraitInitializersTest` checks the set against `TraitFactories.factoryFor`): annotation traits render the `INSTANCE` singleton, string traits `new X("v")`, the rest the runtime constructor with `null` for absent optional properties; protocol traits with no `http`/`eventStreamHttp`/`noErrorWrapping` use the no-argument constructor; `@http` with code 200 the two-argument constructor | The runtime trait classes have no builders (task 1.7). Added beyond smithy-java: `error`, `auth`, `httpChecksum`, `awsQueryError`, `sigv4` and the five protocol traits |
| `LengthTraitInitializer`, `RangeTraitInitializer` | Not ported | Constraint traits are excluded (OD-13); a configuration that adds one gets a `GenericTrait` |
| `HttpApiKeyAuthTraitInitializer`, `StringListTraitInitializer` | Not ported; those traits render as `GenericTrait` | No typed runtime class |
| `GenericTraitInitializer` renders `new <TraitService>().createTrait(ShapeId.from(id), <Node>)` | `new GenericTrait(ShapeId.fromParts(ns, name), <Document literal>)`; not a `TraitInitializer`, it is the registry's fallback | No smithy-model at runtime (Requirement 1.6). `fromParts` instead of design A.16's `ShapeId.from`, so class initialization does not parse IDs (design A.1). `TraitRenderContext.shapeId` lets task 1.27 use a namespace constant |
| `NodeWriter` renders `Node` builders | `DocumentLiterals`: null → `null`; boolean, string → `Document.of(...)`; integral → `Document.of(int)` if it fits, else `long`, else `BigInteger`; `BigDecimal` → `Document.of(BigDecimal)`; other numbers → `Document.of(double)`; arrays → `Document.of(List)` (`Collections.emptyList`/`singletonList`/`Arrays.asList`); objects → `Document.of(Map)` in member order (`emptyMap`, `singletonMap`, or for two or more members a `Stream.of(new Object[][] {...}).collect(LinkedHashMap::new, ...)` expression cast to `Map<String, Document>`) | Java 8 and `utils` have no ordered, null-tolerant map factory, and an anonymous `LinkedHashMap` subclass adds a class per trait. Multi-member generic trait values are rare (`deprecated`, `cors`). The Dynamic_Client's node-to-document conversion (task 13.1) must use the same mapping so converted and generated trait values compare equal (Property 42) |
| `collectRuntimeTraits`: `PRELUDE_RUNTIME_TRAITS` (includes `length`, `pattern`, `range`, `uniqueItems`, `smithy.ai#prompts`) plus each protocol and auth definition's `traits` list plus settings' `runtimeTraits`/`runtimeTraitsSelector` (additions only) | `TraitInclusionPolicy.forService(Model, ServiceShape, TraitInclusionConfig)` and `forService(IntermediateModel)`: design A.16's default list; the protocol or auth trait itself as well as its `traits` list (Requirement 9.1(b)); minus constraint traits (Requirement 9.3, OD-13) and hard exclusions (Requirement 9.4); then `schemaTraits` `add`, minus `remove` | Requirements 9.1–9.5. The SDK runtime validates no constraint trait. No selector support: no requirement asks for it |
| — | `forService` fails before any code is written, with one `SchemaCodegenException` listing every problem: an identifier that is not an absolute shape ID or has a member (9.11), adding a hard-excluded trait (9.10), removing an SDK-owned trait (9.6), removing a trait `add` does not list | Requirements 9.6, 9.10, 9.11. A removal of a trait that was never added has no effect and is most likely a typo, so it fails too |
| Requirement 9.3 vs 9.5 | `schemaTraits.add` may add a constraint trait (it renders as a `GenericTrait`); only defaults and protocol and auth lists exclude them | 9.4/9.10 forbid only the hard exclusions; 9.3 governs what the SDK includes by itself |
| Design A.16 error type `CodegenException` | `SchemaCodegenException` (unchecked) | `codegen` has no `CodegenException` |
| `TraitInclusionPolicy.includes`/`effectiveTraits` | Also `sdkTraits()`, `configuredTraits()`, `serviceId()` and `includedTraits(Shape)`: a shape's or member's own included traits, sorted by trait ShapeId (Requirements 9.9, 10.4, 10.9) | Deterministic order for the Schema_Generator |
| smithy-java marks `TraitInitializer` `@SmithyInternalApi` | The SPI types are `@SdkProtectedApi` | They are an extension point for Codegen plugins (Requirement 9.8), not internal-only |

**Shared default trait list (for task 13.1).** `DefaultTraitInclusion` in `codegen` is the single source of the default, excluded-constraint and hard-excluded lists. The Dynamic_Client cannot depend on `codegen` (Requirement 1.7), and `core/schema` is a runtime module that should not hold codegen policy, so the lists reach it as generated source: `DefaultTraitInclusionSpec(ClassName)` renders a class of `public static final List<String>` constants (`DEFAULT_TRAITS`, `EXCLUDED_CONSTRAINT_TRAITS`, `HARD_EXCLUDED_TRAITS`, `HARD_EXCLUDED_NAMESPACES`) that compiles with no dependency beyond the JDK and `annotations`. Task 13.1 either generates it into `core/dynamic-client` at build time (for example `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient.schema.internal.DefaultTraitInclusion`) or checks in the output with a test that regenerates and compares it. The class name must be singular: checkstyle's `PluralEnumNames` rejects classes with public static fields whose names end in `s`.

**Runtime types and tests.** `codegen` names the runtime classes as strings (`SchemaRuntimeType`), so it has no compile dependency on `core/schema`; it gains a test-scope dependency on `schema`. `TraitInitializersTest` renders 80 trait values (every built-in, several shapes of each structured trait, and generic traits with nested lists, nulls, `long` and `BigInteger` numbers and multi-member maps), compiles them with `javac --release 8` against the runtime module, and checks each value equals `TraitFactories.create(id, document)` for the same model value (class and value), that the rendered document equals the documented mapping, and that member order is kept. `TraitInclusionPolicyTest` covers the effective set (restJson1 plus sigv4, a three-protocol service, a custom protocol definition listing `length` and `documentation`), each failure rule and `customization.config` loading. `DefaultTraitInclusionSpecTest` compiles the generated constants class and compares it with `DefaultTraitInclusion`. Property 10 (task 1.26) is skipped.

### Schema_Generator (task 1.27)

**Sources.** smithy-java 1.7.0 `codegen/codegen-core`: `generators/SchemaFieldOrder`, `generators/SchemasGenerator`, `generators/SchemaIndexGenerator`, and `CodegenUtils#getOriginalId`/`#recursiveShape`.

**Target.** Package `software.amazon.awssdk.codegen.poet.schema` in `codegen` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `SchemaCodegen` (entry point and builder), `SchemaFieldOrder`, `SchemasGenerator`, `SchemaIndexGenerator`, `ServiceSchemasGenerator`, and package-private `SchemaTraits` and `BytecodeEstimate`. `SchemaGeneratorTasks` in `codegen.emitters.tasks`, registered in `CommonGeneratorTasks`. Generated per service: `<Svc>Schemas`, `<Svc>Schemas1`, … (package-private, model package), `<Svc>SchemaIndex` (public, model package) with `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaIndex`, and `<Svc>ServiceSchemas` (public, `<client package>.internal`). `<Svc>` is `Metadata.getServiceName()` (`Sqs`, `CloudWatch`, `DynamoDb`).

| Source / design | Implementation | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Order: `TopologicalIndex` ordered shapes, then recursive shapes (design A.17 says the same) | Strongly connected components of the member graph in dependency order (Tarjan; roots in ShapeId order, members in declaration order) | `TopologicalIndex` puts recursive shapes after the non-recursive shapes that reference them. smithy-java makes those references through `X_BUILDER`, which works inside one class but, across partitions, reads a field of a class still being initialized (null). In dependency order every reference is to an earlier field, so a partition references only itself and earlier partitions (Requirement 10.7) |
| Every recursive shape gets `X_BUILDER`; every member that targets a recursive shape uses the builder; the schemas are built in a `static {}` block and resolved at the end | Inside a recursive component, shapes are built in depth-first post-order; only a member whose target is still on the depth-first path (a cycle-closing edge) uses `X_BUILDER`, and only those targets get a builder field. Other members reference the built field. Each field is assigned directly (`static final Schema X = X_BUILDER.putMember(...).build()`), with `.resolve()` only when the schema has a deferred member | Requirement 10.5. One builder per cycle instead of one per recursive shape. The runtime resolves a deferred root right after `build()`, because deferred members read their target builder lazily |
| Partitions of at most 50,000 source characters, with a single-class fast path under 500 shapes | Greedy partitioning by an estimated `<clinit>` bytecode size, budget 48 KB (`SchemaCodegen.DEFAULT_PARTITION_BUDGET`, configurable through `SchemaCodegen.Builder.partitionBudget` for tests); a recursive component is never split | Design A.17. The estimate (`BytecodeEstimate`) counts instructions and overestimates: `SchemaCodegenTest` compiles the output and checks estimate ≥ actual ≤ 3× actual. The seven prototype services fit in one partition each (largest actual `<clinit>`: DynamoDB 25 KB, Route 53 22 KB) |
| `ShapeId.from("ns#Name")` | `ShapeId.fromParts(NS, "Name")` with a per-partition `private static final String NS` for the service namespace, and string literals for other namespaces | Design A.1: no parsing at class initialization |
| Enum and int enum shapes are excluded; their schema is `$SCHEMA` on the enum class | `Schema.createEnum`/`createIntEnum` fields in the partitions, values in declaration order (`Collections.singleton` or `new LinkedHashSet<>(Arrays.asList(...))`). A string with the IDL 1 `@enum` trait also becomes `createEnum` | Design A.17 example. Generated enum classes are unchanged in this task |
| `.builderSupplier(X::builder).shapeClass(X.class)` on structures and unions | Not emitted | Generated model classes do not implement `ShapeBuilder` until task 3.7 ("emits Schema classes only"). Task 3.7 adds `.builderSupplier(X::newShapeBuilder).shapeClass(X.class)` to aggregate fields and the enum class to `createEnum`, through the `ShapeModel` of each closure shape (`IntermediateModel.getShape(ShapeId)`) |
| `SchemaBuilderGenerator` uses `structureBuilder` for a recursive union | `unionBuilder` | smithy-java bug: the schema type would be `STRUCTURE` |
| Field names: upper snake case of the shape name, or of the full ShapeId when taken or equal to the shape name | Same rule; also when the name equals a generated model class name (`IntermediateModel.getShapes()` keys) or `NS`; then a numeric suffix if still taken. Builder names are `<field>_BUILDER`, suffixed the same way | Avoids a field obscuring a model class in the same package |
| `set` shapes | `listBuilder`, without `uniqueItems` | Design A.1 maps `set` to a list; `uniqueItems` is excluded (OD-13) |
| `GeneratedSchemaIndex` with a `HashMap` and `_initEnums()` | `<Svc>SchemaIndex` with a `LinkedHashMap` filled by `_init<partition>()` through a `put(Schema)` helper; `visit` iterates in initialization order; no enum method (enums are partition fields). The class is `@SdkInternalApi` | Deterministic `visit` order; 6 bytes of bytecode per entry |
| — (operation schemas are fields of per-operation classes, the service schema of the service class) | `<Svc>ServiceSchemas`: `SERVICE = Schema.createService(...)` with the service's included traits (protocol, auth, `awsQueryCompatible`, …) and one `Schema.createOperation(...)` per contained operation, in ShapeId order, named like schema fields (`GET_QUEUE_URL`); `SERVICE` is reserved | Design A.17 / A.18: one shared class instead of one class per operation. Not in the schema index (Requirement 10.3 counts closure shapes only) |
| `OriginalShapeIdTrait` (smithy-model synthetic trait) names the schema; a shape whose original ID is `smithy.api#Unit` keeps its own ID and gets `unitType` | Same. Two closure shapes with the same original ID fail with a `SchemaCodegenException` | Requirement 10.6. No runtime `OriginalShapeIdTrait` is added to `core/schema`: the trait only exists at codegen time, and synthetic traits are not in the inclusion policy |
| — | Members that target prelude shapes reference `PreludeSchemas` (`STRING`, `PRIMITIVE_INTEGER`, `UNIT`, …); a prelude target with no constant fails | Requirement 10.1 |
| Directive-driven generation | `SchemaGeneratorTasks` prepares everything in its constructor (trait inclusion policy, `TraitInitializers.load` with the codegen class loader, ordering, rendering of every class), which runs when the composite task tree is created, before any generator task writes a file. Tasks exist only for an intermediate model with a Smithy model and no `shareModelConfig`. The `META-INF/services` file is merged with entries already in the resources directory, sorted | Fail fast (Requirements 9.6, 9.8, 9.10, 9.11). The C2J route has no Smithy model, so its output is unchanged. Services that share another service's model package (DynamoDB Streams) are left for phase 6 |
| — | Synthesized empty request and response classes (a `smithy.api#Unit` input or output) have no Smithy shape, so they get no schema here | Requirement 10.1 excludes prelude shapes. Task 3.7 must give those classes a `SCHEMA` (for example a synthetic structure schema with `UnitTypeTrait`, or `PreludeSchemas.UNIT`) |
| — | `codegen` declares `smithy-utils` (`CaseUtils`), already transitive through `smithy-model` | `maven-dependency-plugin:analyze`. `.brazil.json` has no mapping for it |
| — | `LegacyRouteParity` (codegen-maven-plugin tests) no longer reports pipeline-only files named `*/model/<X>Schemas<N>.java`, `*/model/<X>SchemaIndex.java`, `*/internal/<X>ServiceSchemas.java` or the `SchemaIndex` service file | The legacy route cannot generate them; every file both routes generate is still compared |

**Tests.** `SchemaCodegenTest` (`codegen`) generates a two-namespace model with three recursive components (a structure and its list, a self-referencing structure, two mutually recursive structures), enums, an int enum, a union with a `Unit` member, a map, errors at operation and service level, prelude targets and a shape outside the closure. It checks the closure, the dependency order, that exactly the three cycle-closing edges are deferred, and compiles the output with `javac --release 8` against the runtime `schema` module (`RuntimeCompiler.compileAll`, now public) to check through the loaded `SchemaIndex`: IDs, member order, member traits apart from target traits, enum values, recursive targets resolving to the indexed instances, and `null` for an unknown ID. With a 200-byte budget it checks that components are not split, that no partition references a later one, and that each partition can be initialized first in a fresh class loader with every reachable schema non-null. It also checks the size estimate against the compiled `<clinit>` (`ClassFiles`), byte-identical output for the model files added in reverse order, name collision handling, and original-ID and synthetic-unit handling. `SchemaGeneratorTasksTest` checks the task wiring: files and the merged service file for a Smithy intermediate model, no tasks for a C2J one, and a `schemaTraits` error raised when the task tree is created. `LegacyRouteParityTest` passes for the seven prototype modules; their generated schema classes were also compiled against the runtime module and walked through `SchemaIndex.getCombinedSchemaIndex()` (SQS 114 schemas, Secrets Manager 140, Account 71, Route 53 410, SNS 174, CloudWatch 356, DynamoDB 489), outside the test suite. Properties 4, 11 and 12 (tasks 1.28–1.30) are skipped.

### Prototype service generation (task 1.31)

**Target.** The seven Prototype_Service_Set modules generate their Schemas through the Codegen_Pipeline. Secrets Manager and Account are generated from native Smithy models, `services/<module>/model/model.json`, copied unchanged from `aws-models/<service>/smithy/model.json` (OD-15). The mojo's native route reads them (Requirement 17.4), and their `customization.config` still comes from `codegen-resources`. SQS, Route 53, SNS, CloudWatch and DynamoDB go through the C2J_Converter; their gap reports are in `docs/design/schema-serde/c2j-gaps/`. `docs/design/schema-serde/disabled-customizations.md` lists no disabled customization.

| Design / task | Implementation | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Native models generate as they are | `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilder.withEnumShapes`: before building, IDL 1 `@enum` strings become IDL 2 `enum` shapes (`ModelTransformer.changeStringEnumsToEnumShapes`, names synthesized where the trait has none). If an `@enum` string in the service closure cannot be converted, the build fails and names it | The Account model uses `@enum` strings (seven of them). Without the conversion the builder generated them as plain `String` members: the enum classes, the enum-typed getters and builder overloads, and `RegionOptStatusListCopier`'s enum methods disappeared (Requirement 16.1). Generated constant names come from the values, so the synthesized member names do not reach the API. The C2J_Converter already emits `enum` shapes, so the C2J route is unchanged. The intermediate model keeps the converted model, so the Schema_Generator sees `enum` shapes too |
| — | `codegen` adds `smithy-smoke-test-traits` and `smithy-aws-smoke-test-model` (runtime scope, `${smithy.version}`) | The Secrets Manager model has `smithy.test#smokeTests` with `aws.test#AwsVendorParams`; stage 4 fails on the unresolved traits otherwise. Nothing is generated from them. `.brazil.json` has no mapping for either |
| Each prototype module needs the runtime `schema` module | `software.amazon.awssdk:schema` (compile scope) in the seven service POMs, not in `services/pom.xml` | Generated Schema classes reference `software.amazon.awssdk.schema`. The `fleet-services` profile is not changed; a non-prototype service generated by the branch's codegen would need the same dependency. `schema` depends only on `annotations`, `utils` and `reactive-streams`: no `smithy-model` in any prototype's runtime tree (Requirement 1.6) |
| `LegacyRouteParityTest` covers the native modules | `LegacyRouteParity` generates a module that has `services/<module>/model` from that model, the same way the mojo does, and still compares the output with the legacy route's output for the module's C2J model. For those modules it also ignores comments (a scanner that keeps string and character literals) and the order of `throws` clauses | Documentation comes from the Smithy model's `@documentation` traits: same text, different markup and whitespace. A native model lists operation errors in ShapeId order, not C2J declaration order, so the `throws` and `@throws` order differs. Neither ordering affects source or binary compatibility. Every other generated difference is still reported |
| — | Secrets Manager: no difference left. Account: `legacy-route-parity/account.txt` lists only model-version differences. The native Account model is newer than the module's C2J model: it adds the `SendPhoneNumberVerification` and `VerifyPhoneNumber` operations (request, response and marshaller classes; client methods, as `default` methods on the interfaces), the `PhoneNumberVerificationStatus` enum and `GetContactInformationResponse.verificationStatus` | Additive API. Against the C2J model of the same version (`aws-models/account/2021-02-01`) the generated code is identical, apart from the ignored comments and `throws` order. The module's `service-2.json` is left as it is, because it is the Baseline_Build input |
| — | Schema counts through `SchemaIndex.getCombinedSchemaIndex()` with all seven modules on the class path: SQS 114, Secrets Manager 140, Account 72, Route 53 410, SNS 174, CloudWatch 356, DynamoDB 489 | Account's native model has more shapes (above). Operations with a `smithy.api#Unit` input or output get no schema (task 1.27) |

**Verification.** `codegen` tests, including the poet golden files, and `codegen-maven-plugin` tests (`LegacyRouteParityTest` for all seven modules) pass. `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilderTest` covers the `@enum` conversion and its failure. The seven service modules build and their existing unit tests pass, and Secrets Manager's and Account's generated endpoint tests (not part of the default Surefire run) pass against the trait-derived rule sets. A `ServiceLoader` check outside the test suite loads all seven `SchemaIndex` registrations. `mvn dependency:tree -Dscope=runtime` shows no `software.amazon.smithy` artifact for any of the seven modules.

### Protocol_Test_Generator (task 1.32)

**Sources.** Design D.1. Value conversion follows smithy-java 1.7.0 `protocol-test-harness` (`ProtocolTestDocument`, `HttpClientRequestProtocolTestProvider`, `ProtocolTestExtension`), which runs the cases at test time through `ApiOperation` builders instead of generating code.

**Target.** Package `software.amazon.awssdk.codegen.poet.protocoltest` in `codegen` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `ProtocolTestGenerator` (entry point and builder), `ShapeValueLiteralGenerator`, `ProtocolTestSkipList`, `ProtocolTestCodegenException`, and package-private `ProtocolTestRuntimeTypes` and `UngeneratableCaseException`. `ProtocolTestGeneratorTasks` in `codegen.emitters.tasks`, registered in `CommonGeneratorTasks`, writes into the tests directory. `CustomizationConfig` gains `protocolTests` (boolean, default false) and `protocolTestSkips` (path). Generated per service, in `<client package>.protocoltests`: `<Svc>RequestTests<N>` and `<Svc>ResponseTests<N>`, with the protocol name after `<Svc>` (`ProtoDemoAwsJson10RequestTests1`) when the service has cases for more than one protocol. `codegen` adds `smithy-protocol-test-traits` (compile) and `smithy-protocol-tests-core` (test scope). `smithy-protocol-tests-core` gains `ProtocolTestHarnessFactory`, `ProtocolTestHarnesses` and `ProtocolTestValues`.

| Source / design / task | Implementation | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| smithy-java runs each case dynamically (JUnit `TestTemplate` over the operation's traits) | One generated `@Test` method per case, `@DisplayName("<id>")`, method name `request_`/`response_`/`error_` + the id (non-identifier characters as `_`, suffixed on a clash); `@Tag("protocol:<protocol ShapeId>")` on each class, so classes are grouped by protocol as well as kind and split at 150 methods (`Builder.maxTestsPerClass`) | Requirements 32.1, 32.11 and the D.1 generator; `ProtocolTestSummaryExtension` counts by the class tag |
| smithy-java runs an error-shape case once for every operation that lists the error | Once, on the first operation in ShapeId order that lists the error (any operation for a service-level error), preferring one with an empty input | Requirement 32.1: exactly one test per case |
| Response cases: smithy-java builds the input with `errorCorrection()` (required members filled with zero values) | The input is `<Op>Request.builder().build()` | Generated builders have no error correction; response cases do not assert on the request. An operation whose empty input cannot be serialized (a required `httpLabel`) will need a skip entry in task 1.33 |
| `params` read through a `Document` view at run time | `ShapeValueLiteralGenerator` walks the `Node` against the Smithy shape and emits builder calls. Same value rules: blob = UTF-8 bytes of the string; timestamp = epoch seconds (fraction kept to the nanosecond) or `Instant.parse`; `"NaN"`/`"Infinity"`/`"-Infinity"` for float and double; null member values leave the member unset. Enums go through the string setter (`fooWithStrings` for collections). Lists and maps use `Arrays.<T>asList`, `Collections.<T>emptyList()` and `ProtocolTestValues.<V>mapOf(k, v, …)` with explicit element types, so sparse `null`s and nested empty collections type-check. Documents become `software.amazon.awssdk.core.document.Document` with numbers kept as source strings (`Document.fromNumber("2.5")`). The exception `message` member, which generated exception classes do not have, is set through `AwsServiceException.Builder#message` | Requirement 41.3: expected values never come from the SDK deserializer. Smithy model validation has already checked `params` against the shapes, so a node of the wrong kind, a non-integral or out-of-range integral value, or a member the generated class lacks makes the case ungeneratable instead of being coerced |
| Design D.1: request tests use `RetryMode.NONE` | `overrideConfiguration(o -> o.retryStrategy(AwsRetryStrategy.doNotRetry()))` | The SDK has no `RetryMode.NONE` |
| Design D.1: anonymous or fixed credentials, fixed idempotency token through a test-only `IdempotentUtils` hook | Region `us-east-1`; `StaticCredentialsProvider` with fixed `akid`/`secret` when the service has `aws.auth#sigv4` or `sigv4a`, else `AnonymousCredentialsProvider`; passed as the configuration lambda of `ProtocolTestHarnesses.forClient`. Request classes set `IdempotentUtils.setGenerator(() -> "00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000000")` in `@BeforeAll` and restore the previous generator in `@AfterAll` | `IdempotentUtils.setGenerator` is the existing hook. The generator is JVM-global, so request test classes must not run in parallel with other tests in the same JVM |
| — (no harness exists before task 3.33) | Each class holds `private static final ProtocolTestHarness<C> HARNESS = ProtocolTestHarnesses.<C, B>forClient(protocol, C::builder, config)`. `forClient` returns a lazy harness that looks up exactly one `ProtocolTestHarnessFactory` through `ServiceLoader` on first use; with none registered every test fails with an `IllegalStateException` naming the SPI, and the class still loads. Task 3.33 registers the transport-backed factory | The generated code is final now and does not change when the transport lands. Passing the protocol lets the factory set a protocol override for cases whose protocol is not the client's default |
| — | Streaming payloads follow the generated client signature: a request stream is `RequestBody.fromBytes(<UTF-8 bytes of the param>)` (or `RequestBody.empty()`), a response stream is read with `ResponseTransformer.<Out>toBytes()` and compared as `SdkBytes`. A `params` value for a member the intermediate model marks streaming while the operation has no streaming input or output (an `@httpPayload` stream under awsJson) is ungeneratable | Generated builders omit streaming members (`getNonStreamingMembers`) |
| — | Operations with an event stream input or output are ungeneratable | The protocol tests run on the sync client, which has no event-stream methods. Task 1.33 lists such cases in the skip list |
| `ProtocolTestSkips` (runtime, `smithy-protocol-tests-core`) parses the skip list | `ProtocolTestSkipList` parses the same JSON format with the Smithy `Node` parser and the same rules (non-blank `id` and `reason`, optional `protocol`, no unknown properties, no repeated `(id, protocol)`), and `validateAgainst` fails naming every entry whose id is not a selected case (including `appliesTo: server` cases and cases of unsupported protocols) or whose protocol the case is not defined for | `codegen` cannot depend on the test module at compile scope. Both parsers are covered by their own tests against the same format |
| Design D.1: skip file `test/smithy-protocol-tests/protocol-test-skips.json` | `customization.config` `protocolTestSkips`; a relative path is resolved against the directory of the `customization.config` that sets it (`CustomizationConfig.resolveProtocolTestSkips`, called by `GenerationMojo` and `AwsSdkJavaCodegenPlugin`). No path means an empty list; a missing file fails generation | One place to configure both routes. Task 1.33 points the protocol test services at the module's skip file |
| — | A skipped case is still generated when possible, with `@Disabled("<reason>")`; a skipped case that cannot be generated gets an empty body with a `// Not generated: <cause>` comment. Every ungeneratable case that is not skipped is reported in one `ProtocolTestCodegenException`, as `<id> (<protocol>): <cause>`, before any file is written | Requirements 32.6–32.8 |
| — | `vendorParams`, `authScheme` and `tags` are not used | No prototype or protocol test service needs them in phase 1; the harness picks credentials per service |

**Tests.** `ProtocolTestGeneratorTest` (`codegen`) builds two fixture models through the Codegen_Pipeline's native route. `demo.smithy` is an awsJson1_0 + restJson1 service with a request case using every simple and aggregate type (sparse list, nested and recursive structures, union, document, enum and int enum collections, NaN and infinity, fractional timestamps), a restJson1 case with query, host and resolved-host assertions, `appliesTo: server` and unsupported-protocol cases (not selected), output response cases, operation-level and service-level error cases, and an event-stream case. `streaming.smithy` is a restJson1 service with streaming upload and download. The tests check selection and order, class grouping, tags and display names, splitting with a lowered threshold, `@Disabled` skips, the failures (ungeneratable case, unknown skip id, server-only or wrong-protocol skip, empty reason, missing skip file, malformed skip lists, a stream the client does not expose), and the rendered literals and assertions. `GeneratedProtocolTestsCompileTest` generates both fixture clients with `"protocolTests": true` and a skip file, compiles the client and test sources together with `javac --release 8`, and runs generated tests through the JUnit Platform launcher with a test-only `HttpClientHarnessFactory` (a fake `SdkHttpClient`) against the clients' current marshallers: six response, error and streaming cases pass, the disabled case does not run, and `PutThingAllTypes` fails on its deliberately short expected body after its method, URI and header assertions pass. `ProtocolTestHarnessesTest` (`smithy-protocol-tests-core`) covers the lazy lookup and `mapOf`. Not covered by compilation: `BigInteger` literals, because the current marshallers have no `BigInteger` marshalling type (the fixture leaves it out). The Protocol_Test_Services themselves are generated in task 1.33.

### `<Svc>Operations` and the Error_Registry (task 3.15)

**Sources.** Design A.18 and B.5. smithy-java generates one `ApiOperation` class per operation (`OperationGenerator`) and has no error registry class; nothing is ported line by line.

**Target.** Package `software.amazon.awssdk.codegen.poet.schema` in `codegen`: `OperationsClassSpec` (public, `@SdkInternalApi`) and package-private `ErrorRegistrySpec`, created by `SchemaCodegen` after the partitions and `<Svc>ServiceSchemas`, so `SchemaGeneratorTasks` writes the class for every schema-enabled service with no new task. `SchemaRuntimeType` gains `SERIALIZABLE_STRUCT`, `API_OPERATION`, `API_SERVICE`, `SIMPLE_API_OPERATION` and `ERROR_REGISTRY`; `SchemaCodegen.Builder` gains `errorShapeOwner(Function<ShapeId, ShapeId>)` (default identity). Generated per service: `<Svc>Operations` (public, `@SdkInternalApi`, model package) with `SERVICE`, `ERROR_REGISTRY` and one `ApiOperation` field per contained operation.

| Design / task | Implementation | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Task 3.15: a service-wide `<Svc>ErrorRegistry` | No separate class: the registry is the `ERROR_REGISTRY` field of `<Svc>Operations`, as in design A.18's example and K4 | Requirement 36.4: both clients get operations and error resolution "from the same single generated per-service class". One class fewer per service (D.5) |
| Design A.18: `<Svc>Operations` (package not stated), suppliers `() -> GetQueueUrlRequest.SCHEMA` | The class is in the **model package** and its suppliers read the Root_Schema fields of the partitions: `() -> SqsSchemas.GET_QUEUE_URL_REQUEST`, `() -> CloudWatchSchemas.DASHBOARD_NOT_FOUND_ERROR`; a `smithy.api#Unit` input or output reads `PreludeSchemas.UNIT` | Generated model classes have no `SCHEMA` constant until task 3.7, and the partitions are package-private (task 1.27), so only a model-package class can read them without going through `<Svc>SchemaIndex` (a map lookup that initializes every partition). Same reason `<Svc>SchemaIndex` is in the model package. Each supplier is a non-capturing lambda over a static field, so initializing `<Svc>Operations` initializes `<Svc>ServiceSchemas` (operation and service schemas) but no partition and no model or exception class; `OperationsClassSpecTest` checks that no partition class is even loaded. Task 3.7 can switch the suppliers to `X.SCHEMA` (same instance) and move the class to `.internal` if wanted |
| `ApiOperation<GetQueueUrlRequest, GetQueueUrlResponse>` | `ApiOperation<SerializableStruct, SerializableStruct>`, built with `SimpleApiOperation.<SerializableStruct, SerializableStruct>builder()` | Model classes do not implement `SerializableStruct` until task 3.7, which then emits the model types (one place in `OperationsClassSpec.operationField`). Nothing calls the operations before task 3.32 |
| Requirement 23.1: an entry for every closure shape with the `error` trait | Every closure shape with the `error` trait that **owns its generated exception class**. Several error shapes can get one class name (CloudWatch `LimitExceededException`/`LimitExceededFault` → `LimitExceededException`, `ResourceNotFound`/`ResourceNotFoundException` → `ResourceNotFoundException`); the intermediate model generates the class for one of them (the ShapeId that sorts last, task 1.23), and only that shape is registered. `SchemaCodegen.errorShapeOwners(IntermediateModel)` maps each error shape to the shape of its class: itself if the model has an exception `ShapeModel` with its ShapeId, else the exception whose class name `NamingStrategy.getExceptionName` gives, else null (not registered) | The other shapes have no Generated_Model_Class of their own, so an entry could not map them to one (23.1's second sentence), and the legacy clients do not resolve their codes: CloudWatch's legacy exception switch has no `LimitExceededException` or `ResourceNotFound` case. Registering them would throw a modeled exception where the Baseline_Build throws `CloudWatchException` (Requirement 23.8). Error shapes reachable only through a member are registered too (23.1) |
| Operation `errors` | `errorIds` lists the operation's errors in model order, then the service's errors, each mapped to its class owner, without duplicates; shapes with no owner are dropped. CloudWatch `DeleteAlarms` lists `ResourceNotFound` and gets `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND_EXCEPTION`, matching its `throws ResourceNotFoundException` | `SimpleApiOperation.errorSchemas()` fails for an ID the registry does not contain. Same source as `OperationModel.exceptions` (operation plus service errors) |
| Task 3.14: emit `errorCode` calls in the Baseline_Build registration order, because CloudWatch's `DashboardNotFoundError` and `ResourceNotFound` share the code `ResourceNotFound` | `errorCode` is emitted for each registered shape with an `aws.protocols#awsQueryError` trait, right after its `putLazyType`, in ShapeId order; when two registered shapes have the same code only the first is emitted (what `ErrorRegistry.Builder.errorCode` keeps). For CloudWatch the question does not arise: `ResourceNotFound` has no `awsQueryError` trait (the C2J_Converter emits one only when the code differs from the shape name) and is not registered (it shares `ResourceNotFoundException`'s class), so `ResourceNotFound` resolves only to `DashboardNotFoundError`, as in the legacy route, where `ResourceNotFound`'s class is dropped and `DashboardNotFoundErrorException` is the only class registered with that code | The legacy registration order is the iteration order of the intermediate model's `HashMap` of shapes (`ProtocolSpec.registerModeledExceptions`), which is hash order and differs between models built differently, so it is not reproducible and Requirement 10.9 forbids it. It only matters when two kept classes share a code, which no prototype service has (`PrototypeErrorRegistryTest` asserts it) and which Smithy's `QueryErrorCode` validator reports as `DANGER` (the pipeline stops on it unless suppressed) |
| — | The code index is emitted whenever a registered shape has `awsQueryError`, whatever the protocol (SNS 31 codes, CloudWatch 8; SQS's current model has none: every code equals the shape name) | The protocol decides whether to call `resolveCode` (Requirement 23.3, task 3.19) |
| Design A.18 `effectiveAuthSchemes(SIGV4)` | `ServiceIndex.getEffectiveAuthSchemes(service, operation, NO_AUTH_AWARE)` key order: the operation's `@auth`, else the service's, else every auth trait in ShapeId order; `smithy.api#noAuth` appended for `@optionalAuth` and alone when nothing applies (`@auth([])`). One `private static final ShapeId` per scheme (`SIGV4`, `NO_AUTH`, …) | Design A.15 and C.4 use the same call for `OperationModel.effectiveAuthSchemes` and the Dynamic_Client (Property 40) |
| ShapeIds as local constants (`QUEUE_DOES_NOT_EXIST`) | `ShapeId.fromParts(NS, "Name")` with a `private static final String NS` (allocated last if an operation field takes `NS`), string literals for other namespaces; error IDs are the schema IDs (original IDs) of the registered shapes. Operation fields have the `<Svc>ServiceSchemas` names; a clash with `SERVICE` or `ERROR_REGISTRY` fails with `SchemaCodegenException`; other constants get a numeric suffix when taken | Design A.1 (no parsing at initialization). The public field names stay stable |
| — | Pre-3.7 runtime limits: `ErrorRegistry.getSchema`/`createBuilder` and `ApiOperation.errorSchemas()`/`inputBuilder()` throw `IllegalStateException` (the partition schemas have no shape class or builder supplier yet); `contains`, `resolveCode`, `schema()`, `inputSchema()`, `outputSchema()`, `effectiveAuthSchemes()` and `service()` work | Task 3.7 adds `.builderSupplier(...)`/`.shapeClass(...)` to the Root_Schemas |
| — | `<clinit>` size is not budgeted or split. Largest prototype: `Route53Operations` (71 operations, 68 errors), 7.5 KB of static-initializer bytecode (DynamoDB 5.7 KB, CloudWatch 4.3 KB), about 100 bytes per operation | Far below the 64 KB method limit; a fleet service with several hundred operations (EC2) still fits, but the partition treatment of task 1.27 can be applied in phase 6 if one does not |
| — | `LegacyRouteParity` (codegen-maven-plugin tests) also ignores the pipeline-only `*/model/<X>Operations.java` | The legacy route cannot generate it |

**Tests.** `OperationsClassSpecTest` (`codegen`) generates `operations.smithy`, an awsQuery service with sigv4 and bearer auth, `@optionalAuth`, `@auth([])`, `@auth([sigv4])`, `Unit` input and output, a service error, two errors sharing an `awsQueryError` code (with the `QueryErrorCode` validator suppressed), an error mapped to another's class through `errorShapeOwner`, an error reachable only through a member and one outside the closure.
