### Serde helpers (task 3.1)

All in `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.serde`. Public (`@SdkPublicApi`): `SpecificShapeSerializer`, `SpecificShapeDeserializer`, `InterceptingSerializer`, `ListSerializer`, `ShapeTranscoder`, `ToStringSerializer`. `@SdkInternalApi` in `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.serde.internal`: `ShapeTranscoderSchemaExtensions` and the SDK addition `ToStringPlanExtension`, registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`. `MapSerializer` (task 1.17) and `SerializationException` (task 1.7) were already ported and are unchanged. `TimestampFormatter` and `HttpDateFormat` are not part of this task (codec-commons, task 3.5).

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `writeDataStream`/`writeEventStream` overrides (`InterceptingSerializer`, `ShapeTranscoder`), `readDataStream`/`readEventStream` (`SpecificShapeDeserializer`, `ShapeTranscoder`), and the transcoder's `DATA_STREAM`/`EVENT_STREAM` kinds | Not ported yet | `DataStream` and `EventStream` are design A.13 types that do not exist yet, and the interface methods are not ported (task 1.17). They land with A.13 |
| `SpecificShapeSerializer.throwForInvalidState(String, Schema)` throws its `IllegalStateException` itself, and the private overload used by the typed writes builds its own, so a subclass override only affects `writeNull` | The protected method returns the exception, as its Javadoc says, and every write goes through it. The default type (`IllegalStateException`) and messages are unchanged | A subclass that overrides it (for example an HTTP binding serializer that must throw `SerializationException`, Requirement 15.8) gets the same exception from every unexpected write |
| `SpecificShapeDeserializer.throwForInvalidState` returns `IllegalStateException` | Returns `SerializationException` with the same message (`Unexpected schema type: Schema{id='...', type=...}`, which names the ShapeId) | Requirement 7.7: a read whose type does not match throws `SerializationException` naming the ShapeId |
| `SpecificShapeDeserializer.readBoolean` returns `false` | Throws, like every other unimplemented read | Requirement 7.7: a mismatched read must not return a value |
| `ShapeTranscoder` messages (`Expected STRING, but found INTEGER`, `Shape did not serialize a value`, `Expected a number, but found ...`, `Cannot write a value while forwarding ...`, ...) | Same messages plus the target ShapeId (`Expected STRING for example.target#Envelope$name, but found INTEGER`). Reads that take no schema (`readDocument`, `readNull`) and the "no value" check name the target member or, at the root, the target builder's schema, kept in a new `expectedSchema` cursor field | Requirement 7.7. Error paths only; the hot path is unchanged |
| `ShapeTranscoder` reads `struct.schema()` and `target.schema()` | `sdkSchema()` | OD-1 (task 1.17) |
| `ShapeTranscoderSchemaExtensions` in `core.serde`, with package-private `KEY`, `mapping` and `MemberMapping` | Same name and members in `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.serde.internal` (not `.schema.serde` as the design Porting_Map row says); `KEY`, `mapping`, `MemberMapping`, `matches` and `targetMember` are public. Checkstyle suppression for the plural-name rule | `ServiceLoader` needs a public provider class, and the `architecture-tests` `PackageContainmentTest` requires a public `@SdkInternalApi` type to live in an `internal` package; `ShapeTranscoder` then reaches the members across packages. The name is kept, as for `PreludeSchemas` |
| `ToStringSerializer` format `Name[m=v]`, placeholder `*REDACTED*` (private) | `Name(m=v, m2=v2)` and `*** Sensitive Data Redacted ***`, exposed as `public static final String REDACTED` | Design Porting_Map and Requirements 16.1, 40.2: same format and placeholder as the Baseline_Build generated `toString` (`ToString.builder`) |
| Sensitive structure, list and map: `Name[*REDACTED*]`, `[*REDACTED*]`, `{*REDACTED*}`; a list or map with a sensitive element, key or value redacts each one | The placeholder alone. A list or map whose element, key or value is sensitive, at any list or map nesting depth, is replaced by the placeholder as a whole | Baseline_Build rule (`AddShapes.isSensitiveShapeOrContainer`): such a member is sensitive and `toString` writes the placeholder for the whole value. The rule is computed once per schema by `ToStringPlanExtension` (design A.5 table) |
| A member written with `writeNull` appears as `m=null` | Left out. Null list elements and map values are still written as `null` | Requirement 40.3; `ToString.add` skips null values |
| No special handling of streaming members | A member whose schema has the `streaming` trait (a streaming blob or an event stream) is left out without reading its value | Requirement 40.3. Members are written through a private `ShapeSerializer` rather than an `InterceptingSerializer` subclass, because `InterceptingSerializer.before` must return a serializer and cannot skip a write |
| A root structure with `sensitive` is written as `Name[*REDACTED*]` | Its members are written, each redacted by its own traits. A member targeting a sensitive structure is redacted | Baseline_Build: a sensitive shape is redacted where it is a member's value, not in its own `toString` |
| `writeFloat` never redacts | Redacts like every other scalar | Source bug; Requirement 40.2 |
| Blobs as `Integer.toHexString` of each byte (sign-extended for negative bytes, no zero padding), using `mark`/`reset` | `SdkBytes(bytes=0x<two lowercase hex digits per byte>)`, read with absolute gets | Same as `SdkBytes.toString()`, which the Baseline_Build getter returns. The buffer's position and mark are left untouched |
| Documents as `TYPE.Document[...]` through `Document.serializeContents` | The legacy `software.amazon.awssdk.core.document.Document#toString` format (`{"k": "v","n": 1}`, `[1, 2]`, quoted and escaped strings) | Baseline_Build document members print that way; `serializeContents` is not ported yet (task 1.17) |
| `toString()` returns the trimmed output | Returns the output as written | Nothing is written with surrounding whitespace, and trimming would change string values at the ends of the output |

**Tests.** Sources: smithy-java `core/src/test/java/software/amazon/smithy/java/core/serde/` `ListSerializerTest` (1 method), `ToStringSerializerTest` (7) and `ShapeTranscoderTest` (17). All 25 are ported under the same names in `core/schema/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/schema/serde/`, with `TestHelper` ported as a package-private helper. Adapted: the `ToStringSerializerTest` expected strings use the SDK format; `ShapeTranscoderTest` drops the `DataStream` member and assertions (`convertsDistinctGeneratedShapeGraphsWithoutReadingMembers`, `canBeReusedAfterFailedConversion`) and the event-stream case of `forwardsScalarValues`, and replaces the source's test records with final classes. `HttpDateFormatTest` and `TimestampFormatterTest` are ported with those types (task 3.5); `TypeRegistryTest` with `TypeRegistry`. SDK additions: `ListSerializerTest` (callback order, null arguments), `ToStringSerializerTest` (unset and streaming members, nesting depth, container redaction, value formats, documents, `close`), `ShapeTranscoderTest` (unknown members reported to `unknownMember` in error-correcting mode and not in strict mode, mismatched types naming the ShapeId with nothing built, sources writing no value or two, nested conversions), and `SpecificShapeSerdeTest` (every unimplemented write and read, overridden exceptions, `InterceptingSerializer` routing and `after`).

### Operations, services and registries (task 3.14)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.schema`: `ApiOperation`, `ApiService`, `ApiResource` (`@SdkPublicApi`, ports) and the SDK addition `SimpleApiOperation` (`@SdkProtectedApi`). In `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.serde`: `TypeRegistry` (`@SdkPublicApi`, port), the SDK addition `ErrorRegistry` (`@SdkProtectedApi`), and package-private `DefaultTypeRegistry` and `ComposedTypeRegistry`. In the new package `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.internal`: `OperationMembersExtension` (`@SdkInternalApi`), registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`.

**Module.** Task 3.14's text places `ApiOperation`, `ApiService` and `SimpleApiOperation` in `sdk-core`. Design A.18's component index and the D.14 Porting_Map put them in `core/schema` (package `software.amazon.awssdk.schema`), next to `TypeRegistry`, which `ApiOperation.errorRegistry()` returns; the design is followed. Nothing in `sdk-core` uses these types yet, so `sdk-core` does not gain its `schema` dependency in this task (an unused declared dependency fails the dependency analysis). Task 3.16 adds it with the `ClientProtocol` SPI, whose methods take an `ApiOperation`.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `ApiOperation` defaults `idempotencyTokenMember()`, `inputStreamMember()`, `outputStreamMember()` loop over the members on every call | Read `OperationMembersExtension.of(inputSchema()/outputSchema())`, computed once per schema; same results (first member with the trait) | Design A.18 and D.14; `OperationMembersExtension` is an SDK addition in the design A.5 extension table. Like `ToStringPlanExtension`, it computes the value directly when the provider is not registered |
| `ApiOperation`, `ApiService`, `ApiResource` | Same methods, return types and defaults | Requirement 10.2. Javadoc rewritten |
| One generated `ApiOperation` class per operation | `SimpleApiOperation` with a builder (`schema`, `inputSchema(Supplier<Schema>)`, `outputSchema(Supplier<Schema>)`, `errorIds(ShapeId...)`, `errorRegistry`, `effectiveAuthSchemes(ShapeId...)`, `service`) | Design A.18, D.5. Input and output schemas are suppliers so that `<Svc>Operations` initializes no POJO class (OD-8, Requirement 22.7). `schema`, both suppliers and `service` are required; errors, error registry (`TypeRegistry.empty()`) and auth schemes default to empty. A non-operation schema is rejected |
| — | `SimpleApiOperation` caches the input schema, output schema and error schema list in volatile fields published once with `compareAndSet` (field updaters, no extra objects per operation). The supplier runs outside any lock; a racing thread may run it too but every caller gets the first published value. A supplier that throws or returns null leaves nothing cached (`IllegalStateException` naming the operation for null) | Design A.18 "memoizes each supplied schema in a field written once", with the A.5 publish-once rule. No lock is held while a supplier initializes a generated class, so a class initializer cannot deadlock against an operation |
| — | `errorSchemas()` resolves `errorIds` on the first call: through `ErrorRegistry.getSchema(ShapeId)` (the registered schema supplier) for an `ErrorRegistry`, and through `createBuilder(id).sdkSchema()` for any other `TypeRegistry`. An ID the registry does not contain throws `IllegalStateException` naming the operation and the error | Design A.18 ("errorSchemas() resolves errorIds through the registry's schema suppliers, once"). `TypeRegistry` has no schema lookup, so `ErrorRegistry` gains `getSchema`, an addition to the design's B.5 signature |
| `TypeRegistry.EMPTY` public interface constant | Not public; `empty()` returns a package-private singleton | Design B.5 lists only `empty()`. `TypeRegistry.builder().build()` with no entries also returns that singleton |
| `putType(id, Supplier<Class<T>>, Supplier<Supplier<ShapeBuilder<T>>>)` (lazy) | `putLazyType(ShapeId, Supplier<Schema>)`; class and builder come from `Schema.shapeClass()` and `Schema.shapeBuilder()` | Design B.5 (one lambda instead of two). The supplier runs on the first `getShapeClass`/`createBuilder`, never on `contains` (Requirement 22.7), and the schema is published once with `compareAndSet`, as above. On first resolution the schema must have the registered ShapeId, a shape class implementing `SerializableStruct`, and (for `createBuilder`) a builder supplier; otherwise `IllegalStateException` naming the ShapeId, with nothing cached |
| `putType(id, Class, Supplier)` stores its arguments unchecked; `HashMap` then `Map.copyOf` | Null arguments throw `NullPointerException` naming the parameter; the entries are copied into an unmodifiable `HashMap` | `Map.copyOf` is Java 10 (Requirement 1.4). Later builder changes do not affect a built registry |
| `compose` falls back to `second` when `first` returns null; short-circuits only for an empty `DefaultRegistry` | Falls back when `first.contains(id)` is false; also short-circuits for an empty `ErrorRegistry`. A named package-private `ComposedTypeRegistry` replaces the anonymous class; null arguments throw `NullPointerException` | Requirement 22.5 states the rule in terms of `contains`; the results are the same for registries whose `contains` agrees with their lookups. Requirement 22.9 (empty is an identity element) |
| `createBuilder(ShapeId, Class)`: `type.isAssignableFrom(getShapeClass(id))` | Same, with `null` type rejected and a null registered class reported as the same `SerializationException` | A custom registry returning a builder but no class no longer throws `NullPointerException` |
| `createBuilder(Document)`, `deserialize(Document)`, `deserializeStrict(Document)` | Not ported yet | `Document` has no discriminator or `asShape` yet (task 1.7 port). Task 11.16 adds `deserialize(Document)` with the null-discriminator `DiscriminatorException` (Requirements 22.2–22.4) |
| — | `ErrorRegistry` (new): `putLazyType(ShapeId, Supplier<Schema>)`, `errorCode(String, ShapeId)`, `build()`; implements `TypeRegistry` by delegating to a `DefaultTypeRegistry`; `resolveCode(String)` and `getSchema(ShapeId)` | Design B.5, Requirements 23.1 and 23.3. smithy-java's query protocols scan operation error schemas for `awsQueryError` codes instead |
| — | `resolveCode(rawCode)` ignores text from the first `:`, then looks the custom code up exactly (case-sensitive). It returns null for null, unknown codes, shape names and ShapeIds: relative and absolute ShapeId resolution and the protocol-specific lookup order are `ErrorTypeUtils`'s (task 3.19) | Requirement 23.3. Stripping here keeps `resolveCode` correct for unnormalized wire codes; it is idempotent with `ErrorTypeUtils`'s own stripping |
| — | Two `errorCode` calls with the same code keep the first one added. `build()` throws `IllegalStateException` for a code whose ShapeId is not registered with `putLazyType` | The Baseline_Build query unmarshaller (`AwsXmlErrorUnmarshaller`) uses the first registered exception for a code (`stream().filter(...).findAny()` on a sequential list); CloudWatch has duplicate codes (`ResourceNotFound`, see the C2J_Converter section). Task 3.15 must emit `errorCode` calls in the Baseline_Build registration order |

**Tests.** smithy-java `core/src/test/java/software/amazon/smithy/java/core/serde/TypeRegistryTest` has 6 methods: `deserializesFromRegistryToSpecificClass`, `deserializesFromRegistryWithJustId`, `throwsIfTypeIsIncompatible` and `composesRegistries` are ported under the same names in `core/schema/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/schema/serde/TypeRegistryTest` (Hamcrest replaced by AssertJ; `Person` and `Bird` are small hand-written structures in `RegistryTestShapes`). `deserializesDocumentsFromRegistry` and `registersLazyTypes` are skipped until task 11.16 because they call `deserialize(Document)`; `registersLazyTypes`' lazy-entry behavior is covered by the `putLazyType` tests below. smithy-java has no tests for `ApiOperation`, `ApiService` or `ApiResource`. SDK additions: `TypeRegistryTest` (lazy suppliers not invoked by `contains` and invoked once, concurrent first lookups publish one schema, invalid lazy schemas, replacement, builder isolation, first-wins composition in both orders, empty identity, null arguments), `ErrorRegistryTest` (`resolveCode` for `awsQueryError` codes, `:` suffixes, unknown codes, shape names and IDs, duplicate codes, codes for unregistered shapes, lazy delegation, `getSchema`, composition) and `SimpleApiOperationTest` (accessors, builders from schemas, unmodifiable copied lists, defaults, lazy and once-only suppliers, concurrent first calls, null suppliers, error resolution through `ErrorRegistry` and a plain `TypeRegistry`, missing errors, the default member lookups, builder validation).

## `core/codecs/codec-commons` (task 3.5)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.commons`: `Codec`, `TimestampFormatter` and `ByteBufferOutputStream` (`@SdkPublicApi`, ports), the SDK addition `CodecSettings` (`@SdkPublicApi`), `ByteBufferUtils` (`@SdkProtectedApi`, port) and package-private `HttpDateFormat` (port). In `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.commons.internal`: `TimestampCodec` and `StripedPool` (`@SdkInternalApi`, ports).

**Module.** The design Porting_Map puts `Codec`, `TimestampFormatter` and `HttpDateFormat` in `core/schema` (`.schema.serde`) and `ByteBufferOutputStream` and `ByteBufferUtils` in `core/schema` (`.schema.io`). Task 3.5 puts `Codec`, `ByteBufferOutputStream` and the timestamp and blob helpers in `codec-commons`, and task 3.1 deferred `TimestampFormatter` and `HttpDateFormat` to it; the task is followed. `ByteBufferOutputStream.toContentStreamProvider()` returns the `http-client-spi` `ContentStreamProvider`, and `core/schema` depends only on `annotations`, `utils` and `reactive-streams`. `Codec.serialize` uses `ByteBufferOutputStream`, so `Codec` moves with it. As a result `codec-commons` depends on `schema`, `http-client-spi` and `utils` (the design's module table lists `schema` only), and `sdk-core` (`ClientProtocol.payloadCodec()`, task 3.16) and, when it is ported, `http-binding` (`TimestampFormatter`, `ByteBufferOutputStream`, `ByteBufferUtils`) depend on `codec-commons`. There is no cycle: `codec-commons` depends on neither.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `Codec extends AutoCloseable` | `extends SdkAutoCloseable` (a subtype of `AutoCloseable` whose `close()` throws no checked exception) | SDK checkstyle forbids implementing `AutoCloseable` directly. `ShapeSerializer` (task 1.17) does the same |
| `Codec` convenience methods dereference their arguments | `serialize`, `serializeToString` and the three `deserializeShape` methods throw `NullPointerException("<param> must not be null")` first | Design A.8, Requirement 39.5 |
| `Codec.serializeToString` writes to a `ByteArrayOutputStream` and calls `toString(UTF_8)` | Writes to a `ByteBufferOutputStream` and decodes `new String(array(), 0, size(), UTF_8)` | `ByteArrayOutputStream.toString(Charset)` is Java 10 (Requirement 1.4). Same string; no extra copy |
| — | `CodecSettings` (new): abstract base with `useTimestampFormat()` (default `false`), `defaultTimestampFormat()` (`TimestampFormatTrait.Format`, default `EPOCH_SECONDS`, `UNKNOWN` and null rejected), `defaultNamespace()` (default null) and `forbidUnknownUnionMembers()` (default `false`); a self-typed `CodecSettings.Builder<S, B>`; `equals`/`hashCode` over class and values, and `sharedSettingsToString()` for subclasses | Task 3.5 and Requirement 8.3: the settings every codec shares. smithy-java declares them on each settings or builder type, with the same defaults as `JsonSettings`. The default format is a `Format` rather than a `TimestampFormatter`, so settings stay plain values; codecs call `TimestampFormatter.of(format)`. `jsonName`/`xmlName` and the protocol presets belong to each codec's subclass (tasks 3.6, 7.1, 7.4) |
| `ByteBufferOutputStream.write(byte[], int, int)` uses `Objects.checkFromIndexSize` | Explicit range check with the same `IndexOutOfBoundsException`; null array → `NullPointerException("b must not be null")` | `checkFromIndexSize` is Java 9 |
| `new ByteBufferOutputStream(-1)` throws `NegativeArraySizeException` | `IllegalArgumentException` naming `initialLength` | Clearer error; no other behavior change |
| — | `toContentStreamProvider()` (new): a provider over the bytes written so far, sharing the internal array. `contentAsByteBufferOrNull()` returns `ByteBuffer.wrap(array, 0, size)`, a new writable, array-backed view on each call; `newStream()` returns a new `ByteArrayInputStream` over the same bytes on each call; `name()` is `Bytes` | Task 3.5 and Requirement 13.6 (zero-copy body). The view is deliberately **not** read-only: a read-only heap buffer hides its backing array, and Apache5 (`Apache5HttpRequestFactory.byteArrayEntityOrNull`, task 3.4) then falls back to streaming the body. The `ContentStreamProvider.contentAsByteBufferOrNull()` contract (task 3.4) says callers must not modify the content and allows `wrap`/`duplicate` views |
| `toByteBuffer()` and later writes or `reset()` share one array; after `reset()`, new writes overwrite bytes that a returned buffer still covers | `toByteBuffer()` and `toContentStreamProvider()` capture the bytes written so far. Later appends go past the captured range (or into a grown copy) and are not part of the captured content. `reset()` after either call moves the stream to a new array of the same capacity, so a reused stream never changes content it handed out. `reset()` with nothing shared still reuses the array | Task 3.5: the behavior of writes after the provider is obtained must be defined. Copy-on-reset costs one allocation only when a shared stream is reused. `array()` still exposes the live array without capturing it, as in smithy-java |
| `ByteBufferUtils` (public, no annotation) | `@SdkProtectedApi` | Design D.14 (helper used by codecs and `http-binding`, not customer API) |
| `ByteBufferBackedInputStream.transferTo(OutputStream)` override | Not ported; `InputStream`'s default copy loop is used | `InputStream.transferTo` is Java 9 |
| `TimestampFormatter` pattern `switch` on `Number` and `switch` with `case null` | `instanceof` chain in the same order; explicit null check in `match` | Java 8 (design "Porting conventions") |
| `TimestampSyntaxError` for a null value throws `NullPointerException` while building its message | Message says `but found null` | Source bug: `EPOCH_SECONDS.readFromNumber(null)` meant to throw `TimestampSyntaxError` |
| `EPOCH_SECONDS.writeString` computes `toEpochMilli() / 1000` as a `double` and writes `%.3f` only when the fraction is at least `0.001`; `readFromString` parses a `double` and truncates `* 1000` | `writeString` uses `TimestampCodec.writeEpochSeconds` (the JSON codec's writer): exact seconds and nanoseconds, up to nine fraction digits, trailing zeros dropped (`1422172800.001`, `1398796238.1`, `-0.001`). `readFromString` parses with `TimestampCodec.parseEpochSeconds` and falls back to the `double` parse only for other spellings (exponents, more than nine fraction digits) | Source bug: the `double` test dropped some 1 ms fractions (`1422172800.001` was written as `1422172800`) and the `double` parse read `1.001` as `1`. Sub-millisecond precision was also lost. Only `xml-codec` calls these methods; JSON writes through `TimestampCodec` directly and reads strings strictly, CBOR writes tag 1 doubles |
| `HttpDateFormat` (package-private, no annotation) | Package-private with `@SdkInternalApi`; `format` reads each `LocalDateTime` field next to its use | SDK checkstyle requires an SDK annotation on every class and limits declaration-to-use distance; same output |
| `TimestampCodec` (`@SmithyInternalApi`, `codecs.commons`) | `@SdkInternalApi` in `codecs.commons.internal` | `PackageContainmentTest` requires a public `@SdkInternalApi` type to live in an `internal` package. `json-codec`, `cbor-codec` and `xml-codec` may use it: `InternalApiBoundaryTest` allows internal APIs within one root package (`software.amazon.awssdk.codecs`) |
| `TimestampCodec.writeEpochSeconds` calls `NumberCodec.writeLong` | A private `writeLong` with the same output (`Long.toString` digits) | `NumberCodec` is not ported yet (below). Switch to it when it lands |
| `TimestampCodec` writers repeat the year-writing block; `int` time products are widened to `long` | One private `writeYear`; `hour * 3600L + minute * 60L`; locals declared next to their use | SpotBugs `ICAST_INTEGER_MULTIPLY_CAST_TO_LONG` and checkstyle; same results |
| `StripedPool` (`@SmithyInternalApi`) uses `Thread.threadId()`, `AtomicReferenceArray.getPlain`, `compareAndExchangeAcquire` and `weakCompareAndSetRelease` | `@SdkInternalApi` in `codecs.commons.internal`; `Thread.getId()`, `get` and `compareAndSet` | Java 19 and Java 9 APIs. `get`/`compareAndSet` give stronger (volatile) ordering with the same pool semantics. The slot count is at least two, so a multiplier that yields fewer than two slots no longer creates an empty pool |

**Not ported yet.** `NumberCodec`, `Zmij`, `DecimalToBinary` and the `ByteArrays` shift-and-mask replacement for `VarHandle` (design "Porting conventions") are number formatting and parsing for the text codecs. `NumberCodec` also needs a decision on its `FastDoubleParser` dependency. They land with `json-codec` (task 7.1); the CBOR codec (task 3.6) needs only `StripedPool`. `CompactStringAccess` (`sun.misc.Unsafe`) and the runtime codegen classes (`internal.codegen.*`) are never ported (design "Porting conventions", D.3).

**Tests.** Ported under the same names: smithy-java `codecs/codec-commons` `TimestampCodecTest` (all nested classes, as `internal.TimestampCodecTest`; the `@Nested` classes with argument sources use `@TestInstance(PER_CLASS)`, because Java 8 allows no static methods in inner classes), `core` `TimestampFormatterTest` and `HttpDateFormatTest` (JUnit assertions kept), and `io` `ByteBufferUtilsTest`. Not ported, with their types: `NumberCodecTest`, `ZmijTest`, `ZmijBoundaryTest`, `DecimalToBinaryTest`, `DecimalToBinaryBoundaryTest` (and the `Schubfach` reference), `CompactStringAccessTest`, the runtime codegen tests, and the `fuzz` and `jmh` source sets. smithy-java has no tests for `Codec`, `ByteBufferOutputStream` or `StripedPool`. SDK additions: `ByteBufferOutputStreamTest` (writes, ranges, ASCII, growth from 0 to small capacities, argument errors, `toByteBuffer` without copy, the provider's view being array-backed, writable and over the same array, a new view per call, `newStream` repeatability, empty content, appends and growth after the provider is created, reset after sharing, two providers from one stream), `CodecTest` (the default methods against a minimal UTF-8 string codec: byte-identical `serialize`, flushing on close, the three `deserializeShape` sources with error correction, `createDeserializer(byte[])` without copy, sliced buffers, failures building nothing, null-argument messages, concurrent use of one codec), `CodecSettingsTest`, `StripedPoolTest` (reuse, rejection, drop, probe limit, no item handed to two threads), and extra `TimestampFormatterTest`, `ByteBufferUtilsTest` and `TimestampCodecTest` cases (lookup by schema and trait, every `readFromNumber` type, strict strings, `writeToSerializer`, years outside 0001–9999, `writeLong` extremes).

## `core/codecs/cbor-codec` (task 3.6)

**Sources.** smithy-java 1.7.0 (`/Users/alexwoo/java/smithy-java`, commit `142eecf4d`), module `codecs/cbor-codec`, package `software.amazon.smithy.java.cbor`.

**Target.** In `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.cbor` (`@SdkPublicApi`): `Rpcv2CborCodec`, `CborSettings` (extends `CodecSettings`) and `CborSerdeProvider`. In `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.cbor.internal`: `CborSchemaExtensions` and `DefaultCborSerdeProvider` (public, `@SdkInternalApi`), and package-private `CborSerializer`, `CborDeserializer`, `CborReadUtil`, `CborMemberLookup`, `CborConstants` and `BadCborException`. `CborSchemaExtensions` is registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`. The module depends on `annotations`, `schema` and `codec-commons` (for `Codec`, `CodecSettings` and `StripedPool`); the task 1.2 `maven.javadoc.skip` and `mdep.analyze.skip` are removed.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Implementation classes package-private in `cbor`; `CborSchemaExtensions`, `CborReadUtil`, `CborDocuments` `@SmithyInternalApi` public in `cbor` (the design Porting_Map row keeps them in `a.codecs.cbor`) | Everything except the three public types moves to `cbor.internal`. `CborReadUtil` is package-private there; `DefaultCborSerdeProvider` becomes public so `CborSettings` can create it | `PackageContainmentTest` requires a public `@SdkInternalApi` type to live in an `internal` package, and `ServiceLoader` needs a public `CborSchemaExtensions`; the serializer, deserializer and extension must then share that package |
| `CborSchemaExtensions.CborExtension` record with public accessors | Final class with package-private accessors | Records are Java 16. The arrays it holds are never exposed outside the package |
| `CborSettings` (own class: `defaultNamespace`, `provider`), `defaultSettings()` | `CborSettings extends CodecSettings` with a self-typed `Builder`, `toBuilder()`, value `equals`/`hashCode`/`toString`; preset `RPCV2` (the shared defaults); `defaultSettings()` kept and returns `RPCV2`. `defaultNamespace` and `forbidUnknownUnionMembers` come from the base. `useTimestampFormat` and `defaultTimestampFormat` are inherited but documented as ignored | Task 3.5, design A.8 and Requirement 8.3: rpcv2Cbor always uses tag 1 epoch seconds |
| Default provider chosen in `CborSettings`' static initializer; the system property `smithy-java.cbor-provider` selects a provider by name | Discovered on first `provider()` call that needs it, once, under a lock, and not cached if discovery throws; the registered provider with the highest priority wins, else `DefaultCborSerdeProvider`. A settings object built without `overrideSerdeProvider` stores no provider (`toString` says `provider=default`). No system property | Same pattern as `TraitFactories` and `ClientProtocolFactories`; avoids `ServiceLoader` during class initialization. SDK checkstyle forbids `System.getProperty` |
| `Rpcv2CborCodec.Builder`: `defaultNamespace`, `overrideSerdeProvider`, `settings` | Same, plus `forbidUnknownUnionMembers(boolean)`; `Rpcv2CborCodec.settings()` added; `serialize(null)` throws `NullPointerException("shape must not be null")` | Design A.8 settings; Requirement 39.5 / `Codec` convention |
| `VarHandle` big-endian array views (`CborReadUtil.BE_SHORT/INT/LONG`), `Arrays.equals(byte[], int, int, byte[], int, int)` | Private shift-and-mask readers and writers in `CborReadUtil` and `CborSerializer`; `CborReadUtil.regionEquals` | Java 9 APIs. The design's shared `codec-commons` `ByteArrays` does not exist yet (task 3.5 deferred it to 7.1); switch to it when it lands |
| `CompactStringAccess` Latin-1 fast path and deprecated `String.getBytes(int, int, byte[], int)` | ASCII fast path: write the header for `length()` bytes and copy four `char`s per iteration; on the first non-ASCII character restart with the UTF-8 encoder (unchanged, including U+FFFD for unpaired surrogates). Member names are encoded with `getBytes(UTF_8)` | Design "Porting conventions" (racecar E6); output is byte-identical |
| `CborDeserializer` constants duplicated from `CborConstants`; multi-variable declarations; inner assignments; `switch` expressions in `Token.name` and `readDocument` | One `CborConstants` (unshifted `MAJOR_TYPE_*` and shifted `TYPE_*`); statements rewritten; `Token.name` returns `UNKNOWN(n)` instead of throwing while building a message | SDK checkstyle and Java 8 |

**Behavior fixes and additions** (all on error paths or input the source accepted incorrectly; the encoding of valid values is unchanged):

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Lengths of integer arguments, string payloads and indefinite-length chunks are checked only when the next token is read, or not at all; a chunk length near `Integer.MAX_VALUE` overflows the scan position (smithy-java's own test expects `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`, marked TODO) | Every argument, finite string and chunk is checked against the end of the input when its token is read; overflow-safe (`long`) arithmetic | Requirement 8.7: malformed input throws `SerializationException`, never an unchecked index error |
| `readLong`/`readByte`/... at end of input (`FINISHED`, -1) passes the `token > NEG_INT` check and reads garbage; `readTimestamp` similarly accepts `FINISHED ^ TAG_FLAG`; a tag at end of input yields a `FINISHED` token | Explicit token checks; a tag with no value throws `BadCborException` | Same |
| `readByte`, `readShort`, `readInteger` narrow with a cast | Values out of range throw `SerializationException("Value <v> is out of range for a <type> for <ShapeId>")`. `readLong` keeps the source's saturation of 64-bit values above `Long.MAX_VALUE` | Requirement 7.7 (a value that does not conform to the member's type) |
| Type-mismatch messages `Can't read <TOKEN> as a <type>` | Same, plus ` for <schema ShapeId>` when a schema is given (member schemas name the container and member, `test#Nested$name`) | Requirement 7.7 (message names the ShapeId and member) |
| NaN or infinite tag 1 timestamp reads as the epoch (`Math.round(NaN)` is 0); `readBigDecimal` of a NaN float throws `NumberFormatException`; `readBigDecimal` of a 64-bit integer above `Long.MAX_VALUE` saturates | `SerializationException` for non-finite timestamps and decimals; integers go through `readBigInteger` | Requirement 7.7 |
| Empty bignum byte string reads one byte past the item and throws `NumberFormatException` | Empty bignum is 0 (tag 2) or -1 (tag 3), per RFC 8949 3.4.3 | Spec |
| `writeTimestamp` lets `Instant.toEpochMilli`'s `ArithmeticException` escape | `SerializationException` naming the timestamp | Codec errors are `SerializationException` |
| `close()` throws unless the current token is `FINISHED`, so it threw after every successfully read value | Advances past a completely read top-level value, then requires end of input: trailing data and unread collections still throw | Source bug; `close` can now be used to reject trailing content |
| Union: an unknown member, including `__type`, is reported to `unknownMember`; no `forbidUnknownUnionMembers` | An unknown `__type` member of a union is skipped silently; with `forbidUnknownUnionMembers` any other unknown union member throws `SerializationException` naming the member and the union; otherwise it is reported. Structures are unaffected | RPCv2 CBOR spec ("Deserializers MUST ignore an unrecognized `__type` member" of a union); design A.8 setting, as smithy-java's JSON deserializer does |
| `DefaultCborSerdeProvider.serialize` catches and rethrows `Exception`; `release(serializer, boolean)` ignores the flag | `try`/`finally`; `release(serializer)` | Same behavior, simpler |

**Documents (deferral).** smithy-java writes documents through `Document.serializeContents` and writes structure documents with a `__type` discriminator; it reads maps as `CborDocuments` map documents that parse `__type` with the codec's default namespace. The SDK `Document` from task 1.7 has neither `serializeContents`, structure documents nor discriminators (design B.1–B.3, phase 7). This port therefore:

- writes documents by type over the task 1.7 accessors (scalars, timestamps, blobs, list and map documents; null as CBOR null) and rejects structure and union documents with `SerializationException("Unsupported document type: ...")`;
- reads documents as task 1.7 schema-free documents (integers as `LONG`, floats as `FLOAT` or `DOUBLE` by encoded width, maps as plain map documents);
- does not port `CborDocuments`. It lands with the Protocol_Documents in task 11.5 (Requirement 19.3), and `writeDocument` moves to `serializeContents` with the full document port. Task 7.17 still owns the protocol side of document members. The RPCv2 CBOR specification says document types are not currently supported by the protocol, so CloudWatch and the rpcv2Cbor protocol tests do not depend on this.

**Tests.** Sources: smithy-java `codecs/cbor-codec/src/test` `CborCodecTest` (10 nested classes, 46 test methods), `CborSerializerTest` (5), `CborDepthLimitTest` (6) and `CborDocumentTest` (20), with the helper `CborTestData`. All are in the SDK module under the same names; `CborCodecTest`, `CborSerializerTest` and `CborTestData` are in `cbor.internal` because they use package-private types. Adaptations, in addition to `var`, `List.of`/`Map.of`, `String.repeat`, `HexFormat`, `OutputStream.nullOutputStream` and switch expressions replaced by Java 8 code, and the nested classes' static fixtures moved to the outer class (Java 8 forbids static fields in inner classes):

| Source test | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `CborCodecTest` (all nested classes) | Ported | `CborSerializer.release(s, false)` → `release(s)`. `largeBufferIsDownsizedOnRelease` also asserts the downsized buffer. `MalformedCborTest.bigIntegerWithExcessiveLength` asserts the exception from creating the deserializer, which now detects the overlong byte string; `bigIntegerWithIndefiniteLengthExcessiveChunk` expects `BadCborException` instead of the source's `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` (fixed, above) |
| `CborSerializerTest` (all) | Ported | `ByteBuffer.position(int)` chaining split (it returns `Buffer` on Java 8) |
| `CborDepthLimitTest` (all) | Ported | — |
| `CborDocumentTest`: `convertsNumberToNumber`, `convertsDoubleToNumber`, `convertsTrueToBoolean`, `convertsFalseToBoolean`, `convertsToTimestampWithEpochSeconds`, `convertsToTimestampFailsOnUnknownType`, `convertsToList`, `convertsToMap`, `otherDocumentsReturnSizeOfNegativeOne`, `nullAndMissingMapMembersReturnsNull`, `failToConvert`, `serializeContent`, `checkEquality`, `convertsBigDecimalToDocument`, `canNormalizeCborDocuments` | Adapted | The source builds its input from JSON text with the JSON codec (task 7.1, not ported yet); the port builds the same values with `Document.of` (JSON integers as long documents, decimals as doubles) and writes them with the CBOR codec. `asStringMap` → `asMap`; the type-mismatch message is the task 1.7 format. Hamcrest → AssertJ |
| `CborDocumentTest`: `deserializesIntoBuilder` | Skipped | `Document.deserializeInto` is not ported yet (design B.1, task 11.5) |
| `CborDocumentTest`: `returnsNullWhenGettingDisciminatorOfWrongType`, `findsDiscriminatorForAbsoluteShapeId`, `findsDiscriminatorForRelativeShapeId`, `failsToParseRelativeDiscriminatorWithNoDefaultNamespace` | Skipped | Discriminators and `CborDocuments` are not ported yet (task 11.5, Requirements 19.3, 20.3) |
| testFixtures `CborComparator` | Not ported | Compares CBOR bodies for smithy-java's protocol test harness; the SDK harness compares CBOR with shaded `jackson-dataformat-cbor` trees (task 1.3) |
| fuzz `DeserializationFuzzTest` and its dictionary | Not ported | smithy-java's Jazzer `fuzz` source set has no SDK equivalent (as for `codec-commons`) |

SDK additions: `Rpcv2CborCodecTest` (expected RFC 8949 bytes for shortest-form integers, floats, strings, blobs, tag 1 timestamps with and without a `timestampFormat` trait, tag 2/3 bignums, tag 4 decimals, indefinite-length structures, definite- and indefinite-length lists and maps; a round trip of a structure holding every Smithy type plus a list, map, nested structure and union; empty structures and collections; 100-level nesting; sliced and direct input buffers; definite-length maps, chunked strings, half-precision floats and `undefined` on read; null and unknown members; union `__type` and `forbidUnknownUnionMembers`; 14 malformed payloads; type-mismatch messages naming the ShapeId; out-of-range integers; non-finite timestamps; `close`; `serialize` byte-identical to the serializer path; flush and close of the sink; settings, presets, builder and provider override; extension registration; one codec used by 8 threads), with the schema-driven `SchemaStruct` test model standing in for generated classes. The CBOR round-trip and malformed-input properties are tasks 3.12 and 3.13.

## `core/codecs/json-codec` and the codec-commons number codecs (task 7.1)

**Sources.** smithy-java 1.7.0 (`/Users/alexwoo/java/smithy-java`, commit `142eecf4d`): `codecs/json-codec` (packages `software.amazon.smithy.java.json` and `.json.smithy`) and, from `codecs/codec-commons`, `NumberCodec`, `Zmij` and `DecimalToBinary` (deferred to this task by 3.5).

**Target.** `codec-commons`, package `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.commons.internal`: `NumberCodec` and the SDK addition `ByteArrays` (public, `@SdkInternalApi`), package-private `Zmij` and `DecimalToBinary` (`@SdkInternalApi`). `json-codec`, package `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.json` (`@SdkPublicApi`): `JsonCodec`, `JsonSettings` (extends `CodecSettings`), `JsonSerdeProvider`, `JsonFieldMapper`, `TimestampResolver`. Package `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.json.internal` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `SmithyJsonSerdeProvider`, `JsonSchemaExtensions` and `SmithyJsonSchemaExtensions` (public; the two extensions are registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`), package-private `SmithyJsonSerializer`, `SmithyJsonDeserializer`, `SmithyMemberLookup`, `JsonReadUtils`, `JsonWriteUtils` and the SDK addition `JsonPrettyPrinter`. `json-codec` depends on `annotations`, `schema` and `codec-commons`; the task 1.2 `maven.javadoc.skip`/`mdep.analyze.skip` are removed, and a `maven-enforcer-plugin` `bannedDependencies` execution (`ban-jackson-databind`) fails the build if `com.fasterxml.jackson.core:jackson-databind` or `tools.jackson.core:jackson-databind` is in the dependency tree in any scope (Requirement 8.4). `test-utils` is not a test dependency for that reason (it brings databind); `jqwik-api` is declared because `JsonPrettyPrinterTest` uses it directly. `json-codec` was already in `PortComplianceTest.PORTED_MODULES`; the compliance, `InternalApiBoundaryTest` and `PackageContainmentTest` checks pass with the new classes.

**codec-commons.**

| Big-endian `int`/`long` `VarHandle` views in `NumberCodec`, little-endian `long` view in `Zmij` | `ByteArrays.get/put{Int,Long}{Big,Little}Endian`: plain shift-and-mask expressions; `ByteArrays.rangeEquals` replaces `Arrays.equals(byte[], int, int, byte[], int, int)` | Java 9 APIs (design "Porting conventions"). No racecar E6/E7 loop work here (tasks 9.21, 9.22) |

It is only the slow path; the JSON reader converts numbers with at most 18 significant digits through `DecimalToBinary` first |

`cbor-codec` keeps its private shift helpers (`CborReadUtil`, `CborSerializer`); moving them to `ByteArrays` is a no-behavior refactor left out of this task.

Writes documents by type over the accessors (blobs as base64, timestamps in the default format, null as `null`) and rejects structure and union documents with `SerializationException("Unsupported document type: ...")`. `JsonDocuments` is not ported; `serializeTypeInDocuments` and `defaultNamespace` have no effect yet | Same deferral as `CborDocuments` (task 3.6): the SDK `Document` has no `serializeContents`, structure documents or discriminators until the Protocol_Documents (task 11.5, Requirements 19.2–19.10, 20.2) |
| `close()` of serializers, `flush()` catch `Exception` | Catch `IOException` | Only the sink throws checked exceptions; runtime exceptions propagate unchanged |
| `SmithyJsonSerdeProvider.serialize` catches and rethrows `Exception` to set the pool-release flag | `try`/`finally` with a completion flag | Same behavior |

**Wire format compared with the legacy SDK** (`SdkJsonGenerator` over shaded Jackson 2, `DateUtils`). Unchanged: field order and names (`jsonName` per preset), strings with standard short escapes and unescaped non-ASCII, base64 blobs with padding, `"NaN"`/`"Infinity"`/`"-Infinity"`, `bigInteger` as a JSON number, http-date (`Tue, 29 Apr 2014 18:30:38 GMT`), null sparse elements. Intentional differences. The Smithy protocol tests compare JSON numbers by value (`JsonBodyComparator`, task 1.3), so the number and epoch-seconds spellings compare equal there; `date-time` strings and the `bigDecimal` string-versus-number difference do not (not checked against the generated JSON protocol tests yet; tasks 7.11, 7.13 and 7.21 run them):

| Value | Legacy SDK | JSON codec |
|---|---|---|
| `double`/`float` holding an integer | `1.0`, `1.0E10` | `1`, `10000000000` (integers in `long`/`int` range); `-0.0` → `0` |
| Other `double`/`float` | `Double.toString` of the running JDK (not always shortest before JDK 19) | Shortest round-trip digits on every JDK (Zmij; `Double.toString` spelling on JDK 19+) |
| `epoch-seconds` timestamp | Millisecond precision with three fraction digits: `1398796238.000`, `1398796238.100` | Up to nine fraction digits, trailing zeros dropped: `1398796238`, `1398796238.1`, `1.123456789`; sub-millisecond precision is kept, not truncated |
| `date-time` timestamp | Fraction in groups of three (`ISO_INSTANT`): `...38.100Z` | Trailing zeros dropped: `...38.1Z` |
| `bigDecimal` | JSON string of `toString()` (may use an exponent, API-433): `"1E+3"` | JSON number in plain notation: `1000` (or a string of the plain form with `useStringForArbitraryPrecision`, which also quotes `bigInteger`) |
| `\uXXXX` escapes (control characters, unpaired surrogates) | Upper-case hex: `\u001F`, `\uD83D` | Lower-case hex: `\u001f`, `\ud83d` |
| Pretty print | Jackson `DefaultPrettyPrinter`, system line separator | Same layout, `\n` |

**Tests.** Sources: smithy-java `codecs/json-codec/src/test` and `codecs/codec-commons/src/test`. The ported classes keep their names and packages map `json` → `codecs.json` and `json.smithy` → `codecs.json.internal`; the codec-commons tests go to `codecs.commons.internal`. Common adaptations, not repeated below: `var`, `List.of`/`Set.of`/`Map.of` (`TestCollections` helpers, insertion-ordered), `String.repeat`, text blocks, switch expressions and rules, `instanceof` patterns, `<>` on anonymous classes, `ByteArrayOutputStream.toString(Charset)` and `writeBytes` replaced by Java 8 code; Hamcrest → AssertJ; `schema()` → `sdkSchema()` (OD-1); `asStringMap()` → `asMap()`; `.runtimeCodegen(false)` removed. smithy-java runs most tests per provider (Jackson and native, and every pairing); `ProviderTestBase` keeps `@PerProvider`/`crossProviders` with the native provider only.

| Source test | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| codec-commons `NumberCodecTest`, `DecimalToBinaryTest`, `DecimalToBinaryBoundaryTest`, `ZmijTest`, `ZmijBoundaryTest` (all) | Ported | 174 + 2 + 4 + 3 + 3 runs. `ZmijTest`/`ZmijBoundaryTest` compare with `Schubfach` on every JDK and with `Double.toString`/`Float.toString` only on JDK 19+ (JDK-4511638; `Schubfach.JDK_TO_STRING_IS_SHORTEST`), so they also hold under the `jdk8` profile. `@Tag("stress")` tests run in the normal build, as in smithy-java (only pitest excludes them) |
| codec-commons `Schubfach` (reference formatter) | Ported | `Math.multiplyHigh` (Java 9) → a local signed version over `NumberCodec.unsignedMultiplyHigh` |
| codec-commons `CompactStringAccessTest`, codegen tests, `fuzz`, `jmh` | Not ported | Types not ported (as task 3.5) |
| `JsonDeserializerTest` (all 147 methods) | Ported | 171 runs; the 5 tests `@Disabled` upstream (DateTimeFormatter fallback leniency) stay disabled. `deserializesWithTimestampFormat` passes `TimestampFormatter.format()` (Format-typed setting) |
| `JsonSerializerTest` | Ported (28), adapted (5), skipped (4) | Adapted: `serializesJsonValues` (`serialize` instead of `serializeContents`), `testPrettyPrinting` (SDK pretty printer, same expected text), `smithyProviderPrettyPrintFallsBackToJackson` → `smithyProviderPrettyPrintUsesThePrettyPrinter`, `defaultsToSmithyProvider` (no codegen unwrap), `documentStructAtDepthLimitThrowsSerializationException` (64 nested list documents). Skipped (structure documents and `__type`, task 11.5): `writesDunderTypeAndMoreMembers`, `writesNestedDunderType`, `writesModeledStructureDocumentInsideStructure`, `writesDunderTypeForEmptyStruct` |
| `JsonDocumentTest` | Ported (15), adapted (1), skipped (11) | Adapted: `serializeContent` (`serialize`). Skipped, `JsonDocuments` behaviors (task 11.5): `convertsToTimestampWithEpochSeconds`, `convertsToTimestampWithDefaultStringFormat`, `convertsToBlob`, `deserializesIntoBuilder`, `deserializesIntoBuilderWithJsonNameAndTimestampFormat` (and helper `TestPojo`), `returnsNullWhenGettingDisciminatorOfWrongType`, `findsDiscriminatorForAbsoluteShapeId`, `findsDiscriminatorForRelativeShapeId`, `failsToParseRelativeDiscriminatorWithNoDefaultNamespace`; `onlyEqualIfBothUseTimestampFormat`, `onlyEqualIfBothUseJsonName` (also `@Disabled` upstream, settings-aware equality) |

Skipped: `deserializeShapeWithNestedMissingRequiredFieldReportsFieldName` (required-member validation of generated builders, `PresenceTracker`, not ported; SDK builders do not validate required members) |
| `JsonSchemaExtensionsTest` (8), `ParsingTest` (318 JSONTestSuite files, copied with their MIT `LICENSE`), `SchubfachTest` (22 runs), `JsonDoubleParsingTest`, `JsonReadUtilsTest`, `SmithyJsonSchemaExtensionsTest`, `SmithyMemberLookupTest` | Ported | `JsonSchemaExtensionsTest` reads `JsonSchemaExtensions` from `internal`. `ParsingTest` drops the Jackson cases |
| `JsonWriteUtilsTest` | Ported (5), adapted (3) | `acceleratedWordMasksAcceptExactlyUnescapedAscii`, `forcedInitializationFailureUsesFallback`, `compactStringsDisabledUsesFallback` lose their `CompactStringAccess` assumptions and assertions and run in every build (the char loop is the only path) |
| `SmithyJsonGeneratedFieldTest`, `RuntimeJsonCodegenTest`, `RuntimeCodegenPropertyValidationTest`, helpers `EnumKeyedMapModel`, `StructureEncodedUnionModel` | Not ported | Runtime codegen (D.3) |

SDK additions: `JsonCodecTest` (preset values and output, default settings, `toBuilder`/`updateBuilder`/codec `settings` copy and override, provider override, null arguments, extension registration, number literal read into a string member (including a value a double cannot hold) and a malformed number, type-mismatch and range messages naming the ShapeId, trailing content rejected by `deserializeShape`, union `__type` and `forbidUnknownUnionMembers`, pretty-print layout through both paths and read-back, the wire-format table above, documents by type, document number typing, one codec used by 8 threads, flush/close without closing the sink), `JsonPrettyPrinterTest` (layouts, escaped backslash before a quote, and a jqwik property: pretty output reads back to the same document for any write chunking), `ByteArraysTest` (views against `ByteBuffer`, bounds, `rangeEquals`, `unsignedMultiplyHigh` against `BigInteger`). The JSON round-trip and malformed-input properties are tasks 7.2 and 7.3.

**For the protocol tasks (7.11 awsJson, 7.13 restJson1, 7.17 documents).** Use `JsonSettings.AWS_JSON`/`REST_JSON_1` (`JsonCodec.builder().settings(...)`); for an empty response body, `readStruct` returns with no members (as smithy-java). `JsonCodec.deserializeShape` rejects trailing content; a protocol reading a body through `createDeserializer` should call `close()` for the same check. There is no `MemberSubsetCodec`: HTTP-binding body serde (7.9) must write the body members itself. The `bigDecimal` and timestamp spellings differ from the legacy SDK (table above); if a service needs the legacy string form of `bigDecimal`, that is a protocol-level choice, not a codec setting. Document members (7.17): documents are schema-free task 1.7 documents; a JSON string read into a document stays a string (no base64 or timestamp conversion), structure documents cannot be written, and `__type`/`defaultNamespace` handling arrives with `JsonDocuments` in task 11.5. `NullDocumentProtocolTest` (`{"documentValue": null}`) relies on a null member being treated as absent, which `readStruct` does.

## `core/codecs/xml-codec` (task 7.4)

**Sources.** smithy-java 1.7.0 (`/Users/alexwoo/java/smithy-java`, commit `142eecf4d`), module `codecs/xml-codec`, package `software.amazon.smithy.java.xml`.

**Target.** In `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.xml` (`@SdkPublicApi`): `XmlCodec`, `XmlUtil` (ports) and `XmlSettings` (SDK addition, extends `CodecSettings`). In `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.xml.internal` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `XmlSchemaExtensions` (public, registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`), `XmlReader` and the SDK addition `XmlSerdeFactory` (public), and package-private `SmithyXmlSerializer`, `SmithyXmlDeserializer` (the native byte-level implementation, the default), `XmlSerializer`, `XmlDeserializer` (the StAX implementation), `LazyXmlSerializer`, `EmptyXmlDeserializer`, `XmlInfo`, `XmlMemberLookup`, `XmlWriteUtils` and `XmlErrorCodeParser`. The module depends on `annotations`, `schema`, `codec-commons` and `utils`; the task 1.2 `maven.javadoc.skip`/`mdep.analyze.skip` are removed. The XML API is the JDK's StAX (`javax.xml.stream`, `javax.xml.namespace`), as in smithy-java (no Woodstox or Aalto), so there is no new third-party dependency; the SDK checkstyle `legalPackages` allows `javax.xml.namespace`, `javax.xml.stream` and `javax.xml.stream.events` in `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.xml`, following the `aws-query-protocol` entry. `xml-codec` was already in `PortComplianceTest.PORTED_MODULES`; the compliance, `InternalApiBoundaryTest` and `PackageContainmentTest` checks pass with the new classes.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Everything public or package-private in `xml`; `XmlSchemaExtensions` and its records public | All but `XmlCodec`, `XmlSettings` and `XmlUtil` move to `xml.internal`. The serializer/deserializer choice moves from `XmlCodec` into `XmlSerdeFactory`; `XmlUtil.parseErrorCodeName` delegates to it | `PackageContainmentTest`, as `CborSchemaExtensions` and `JsonSchemaExtensions` (tasks 3.6, 7.1) |
| `XmlCodec.Builder`: `defaultNamespace`, `wrapperElements`, `strictRootElement`, package-private `useNative`; `xmlName` and `timestampFormat` always honored, `date-time` default | Same, plus `settings(XmlSettings)`, `useXmlName`, `useTimestampFormat`, `defaultTimestampFormat`, `forbidUnknownUnionMembers`; `XmlCodec.settings()`, `wrapperElements()`, `strictRootElement()`. `XmlSettings` presets `REST_XML` and `AWS_QUERY_RESPONSE` (both `xmlName` on, `timestampFormat` on, `date-time` default; the protocol supplies the namespace and wrapper elements). `defaultNamespacePrefix` without a `defaultNamespace` throws `IllegalArgumentException` | Requirement 8.3 and design A.8; same settings shape as `JsonSettings`/`CborSettings`. The defaults reproduce smithy-java's fixed behavior |
| Implementation chosen by the `smithy-java.xml-provider` system property (`stax` selects StAX) | Native always, unless the package-private `useNative(false)` builder method (tests) selects StAX. No provider discovery: smithy-java has no XML serde-provider SPI to port | SDK checkstyle forbids `System.getProperty` |
| `XMLInputFactory` with `SUPPORT_DTD`, `javax.xml.stream.isSupportingExternalEntities`, `IS_REPLACING_ENTITY_REFERENCES` and `IS_COALESCING` off | Same four properties (`XmlSerdeFactory.newHardenedInputFactory`), the first two being the legacy `XmlDomParser` settings (`aws-query-protocol`, used by the restXml and query unmarshallers). The native reader is unchanged: a `<!DOCTYPE>` is skipped without being read or resolved, an internal subset is rejected, and only the five predefined entities and character references are resolved; any other entity reference throws | XXE and entity expansion (below) |
| `record`s (`AcquireContext`, `XmlSchemaExtensions.MemberExtension`/`StructExtension`/`ListExtension`/`MapExtension`), `sealed` `XmlReader` and `XmlExtension`, switch expressions, `var` | Final classes with accessors; `XmlReader` abstract with a package-private constructor and `SdkAutoCloseable`; statements | Java 8 |
| `XmlWriteUtils` `CompactStringAccess` Latin-1 path | The `charAt` loop only | Design "Porting conventions" (no `Unsafe`); same output |
| `Arrays.equals(byte[], int, int, byte[], int, int)` in `XmlMemberLookup`; `ConcurrentHashMap.computeIfAbsent` per lookup in `XmlInfo` | `ByteArrays.rangeEquals`; `get` first, then `computeIfAbsent` | Java 9 API; Java 8's `computeIfAbsent` locks the bin even on a hit |
| `XmlSchemaExtensions.resolveMemberXmlName` and `resolveXmlName` (identical) | `resolveXmlName` only | Duplicate code |
| Text escapes `&`, `<`, `>`; attributes also `"` and `'`. A carriage return in text, and a tab or line feed in an attribute, are written raw | Also `&#xD;` for CR in text (both implementations) and `&#x9;`/`&#xA;` in attributes | Behavior fix: XML parsers normalize these characters (XML 1.0 sections 2.11, 3.3.3), so the raw form did not read back as itself. Needed for `SdkRestXmlEscapedCharacters` (`rest-xml-input.json#3`) |
| Value errors: reader messages without the target | Typed reads name the target ShapeId; malformed numbers, blobs and attribute values throw `SerializationException` | Requirement 7.7, same extent as the CBOR and JSON ports |
| `LazyXmlSerializer.close()` releases the pooled serializer on every call | Releases once; a second `close()` does nothing | Releasing twice put one serializer in the pool twice |
| `XmlUtil.parseErrorCodeName` casts to `XmlErrorCodeParser` (`ClassCastException` for an empty body's `EmptyXmlDeserializer`) | `SerializationException` naming the deserializer type; the deserializer is still closed | Codec errors are `SerializationException` |
| `XmlCodec.deserializeShape` (from `Codec`) | Null checks naming the parameter; closes the deserializer after building, so trailing content is rejected; a close failure is added as suppressed to the original exception | Requirement 39.5; as `JsonCodec` |

Unchanged from smithy-java: documents are not supported (`writeDocument` writes nothing, `readDocument` returns `null`); an empty body reads as a structure with no members; unknown elements are skipped and reported through `unknownMember`; root `<ErrorResponse>`, `<Response>` and `<Error>` elements are unwrapped for errors; flattened list and map items may be interleaved with other members (both readers buffer and replay them); no XML declaration is written.

**XXE hardening.** `XmlSecurityTest` runs each case against both implementations: an external entity declared in an internal subset (`<!DOCTYPE Doc [<!ENTITY xxe SYSTEM "file:...">]>`) and an entity declared in an external DTD are rejected with `SerializationException`, and the exception does not contain the referenced file's content; an external DTD reference alone is ignored (the DTD file ends in garbage, so the read would fail if it were loaded); a billion-laughs style internal entity expansion and an undeclared entity (`&nbsp;`) are rejected; the predefined entities and decimal and hex character references are resolved; and the hardened `XMLInputFactory` reports the four properties as off.

**Wire format compared with the legacy SDK** (`XmlProtocolMarshaller`/`XmlGenerator`/`XmlWriter`, `ValueToStringConverter`, `DateUtils`). Unchanged: element names (`locationName` ↔ `xmlName`), wrapped lists (`<member>` or the list member's name) and maps (`<entry><key><value>`), flattened lists and maps, `date-time` (`ISO_INSTANT` spelling, e.g. `2015-01-25T08:00:00.123Z`; fraction in groups of three, sub-millisecond digits kept), `http-date` (`Tue, 29 Apr 2014 18:30:38 GMT`), base64 blobs with padding, `bigDecimal`/`bigInteger` as `toString()` (`1E+3`), integers and booleans, `xmlns` of the operation's namespace on the root element only, a member's `xmlNamespace` declared on that member's element, and an empty structure as a start and an end tag (`<simpleStruct></simpleStruct>`). Intentional differences (the Smithy restXml protocol tests compare bodies with XMLUnit, so the escaping and declaration rows compare equal there; not checked against the generated XML protocol tests yet, tasks 7.14, 7.15 and 7.21 run them):

| Value | Legacy SDK | XML codec |
|---|---|---|
| XML declaration | `<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>` before the root element | None |
| Text escaping | `&`, `"`, `'`, `<`, `>`, CR, LF as `&amp;` `&quot;` `&apos;` `&lt;` `&gt;` `&#x0D;` `&#x0A;`, after first unescaping any of those entities already in the value (so `&amp;` is written as `&amp;` and reads back as `&`) | `&amp;` `&lt;` `&gt;` and `&#xD;`; quotes, apostrophes, tabs and LF raw; the value is never unescaped first (`&amp;` is written as `&amp;amp;`) |
| Attribute values (`xmlAttribute`) | Written raw, unescaped | Escaped: `&amp;` `&lt;` `&gt;` `&quot;` `&apos;` `&#xD;` `&#xA;` `&#x9;` |
| `double`/`float` | `Double.toString`/`Float.toString`: `1.0`, `1.0E10` | Native: integral values in `long` range without a fraction (`1`, `10000000000`, `-0.0` → `0`), others in shortest round-trip form (Zmij, `1.5E-7`, a `float` of `1e10` → `1.0E10`); `NaN`, `Infinity`, `-Infinity`. StAX (tests only): `Double.toString`/`Float.toString` as the legacy SDK |
| `epoch-seconds` timestamp | Millisecond `BigDecimal`, always three fraction digits: `1422172800.000`, `1422172800.100` | `TimestampFormatter.EPOCH_SECONDS.writeString`, the same text as the JSON codec: up to nine fraction digits, trailing zeros dropped (`1422172800`, `1422172800.1`, `1422172800.001`, `1422172800.0000015`); sub-millisecond precision is kept. Reading parses the decimal exactly. The smithy-java formatter's `double` fraction test, which wrote 1 ms after `1422172800` as `1422172800`, was fixed in codec-commons (`fix(codecs): Keep epoch-seconds fractions exact`) |

**Tests.** Sources: smithy-java `codecs/xml-codec/src/test` `XmlCodecTest` (10 methods), `GeneratedModelSerdeTest` (63), `XmlReaderTest` (9) and the helpers `ProviderTestBase` and `PerProvider`. All 82 methods are ported under the same names (`XmlReaderTest` in `xml.internal`), and run against both implementations and, where smithy-java does, every writer/reader pairing (`crossProviders`): 16, 155 and 9 runs. Adaptations, in addition to `var`, `List.of`/`Set.of`/`Map.of`/`String.repeat` (`TestCollections`), text blocks and switch expressions replaced by Java 8 code, Hamcrest → AssertJ and `schema()` → `sdkSchema()`: `GeneratedModelSerdeTest` runs on `XmlTestModel` schemas (the shapes of smithy-java's `model/test.smithy`) with `SchemaStruct` values (enums as strings) instead of generated classes; `XmlCodecTest`'s `TestPojo`/`MapPojo` are hand-written `SerializableStruct`s as in the source.

| Source test | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `XmlCodecTest`, `GeneratedModelSerdeTest`, `XmlReaderTest` (all) | Ported | — |
| fuzz `DeserializationFuzzTest`, `DifferentialXmlFuzzTest` and the dictionary | Not ported | No Jazzer source set (as tasks 3.5, 3.6, 7.1) |
| `model/test.smithy` codegen (`smithyBuild`) | Not ported | Replaced by `XmlTestModel` schemas; the SDK codecs do not generate test classes |

SDK additions: `XmlSecurityTest` (above, 13 runs); `XmlSettingsTest` (defaults, presets, `toBuilder`/`updateBuilder`, value equality, the prefix-without-namespace check, codec `settings` copy and override); `XmlCodecWireFormatTest` (45 runs: the legacy-disposition rows the codec must honor, `rest-xml-input.json#3` escaped characters including the legacy entity-escaped body read back, `#7`/`#18` empty structures, `#16`/`#17` millisecond `date-time` timestamps, `rest-xml-output.json#8` and `ec2-output.json#7` single-element flattened lists, `ec2-output.json#8` flattened list of structures interleaved with another member; the wire-format table above; no declaration; default, prefixed and member namespaces; escaped and renamed attributes; `useXmlName(false)` and `useTimestampFormat(false)`; unknown members; union `forbidUnknownUnionMembers`; error messages naming the ShapeId; query wrapper elements; `parseErrorCodeName` on an empty or non-error body; source buffer position; `serialize` byte-identical to the serializer path without closing the sink; null arguments; extension registration; one codec used by 8 threads). The XML round-trip and malformed-input properties are tasks 7.6 and 7.7.

**For the protocol tasks (7.5 QueryFormSerializer, 7.14 restXml, 7.15 awsQuery/ec2Query).**

- 7.14 restXml: `XmlCodec.builder().settings(XmlSettings.REST_XML).defaultNamespace(<service xmlNamespace>)`, with `strictRootElement(false)` for responses (S3 returns root names that differ from the output shape, such as `<CopyObjectResult>`). The codec writes no XML declaration; if a service needs one, the protocol writes it. Error codes: `XmlUtil.parseErrorCodeName(codec.createDeserializer(body))` (it closes the deserializer); guard an empty body first, which throws. Header, query and label timestamps are protocol-level (`http-date`/`date-time` per binding), not codec settings; `TimestampFormatter.Prelude` has the formatters.
- 7.15 awsQuery/ec2Query responses: `XmlSettings.AWS_QUERY_RESPONSE` and `wrapperElements(Arrays.asList(op + "Response", op + "Result"))` (awsQuery) or `op + "Response"` (ec2Query; no `Result` wrapper). Matching is exact and top-level only. `parseErrorCodeName` handles `<ErrorResponse><Error>` (awsQuery) and `<Response><Errors><Error>` (ec2Query). `ec2-output.json#7` (single-element flattened list) is covered by the codec; the protocol test still has to run end to end.
- 7.5 QueryFormSerializer: does not depend on this module (requests are form-encoded, and the XML implementation classes are package-private). Its naming rules (`xmlName`, `xmlFlattened`, ec2Query's `ec2QueryName` and capitalized `xmlName`) are its own. Epoch-seconds timestamps through `TimestampFormatter.EPOCH_SECONDS` get the exact fraction (the formatter defect is fixed); query timestamps default to `date-time`, so only modeled `epoch-seconds` members use it.

## `core/protocols/aws-query-client-protocol` QueryFormSerializer (task 7.5)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.protocols.client.awsquery.internal` (both `@SdkInternalApi`): `QueryFormSerializer` (a `ShapeSerializer`) and `AwsQuerySchemaExtensions` with its `QueryMemberBinding`, registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`. Ported from smithy-java `aws/client/aws-client-awsquery` (commit 142eecf4d). The client protocols are task 7.15.

**Module.** Declares `annotations`, `utils`, `schema` and `codec-commons`. The task 1.2 javadoc and dependency-analysis skips are removed, and so are the skeleton's `http-client-protocol` and `xml-codec` dependencies, which the serializer does not use; task 7.15 adds them back with the protocols.

**API for task 7.15.** `QueryFormSerializer.forAwsQuery(action, version)` or `forEc2Query(action, version)` writes `Action=<action>&Version=<version>`; then `input.serializeMembers(serializer)` (or `serializer.writeStruct(inputSchema, input)`); then `finish()` returns the body as an array-backed `ByteBuffer` starting at 0 with `arrayOffset() == 0`, without a copy. `finish()` may be called once; a write after it throws `IllegalStateException`. The protocol sets `Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded` (the legacy `QueryParametersToBodyStage` sends `; charset=utf-8`), and fills unset idempotency tokens before serializing: the serializer writes only the values it is given (Smithy `QueryProtocolIdempotencyTokenAutoFill`, `Ec2ProtocolIdempotencyTokenAutoFill`).

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `QueryFormSerializer` package-private in the protocol package (design D.14 Porting_Map: package-private) | Public `@SdkInternalApi` in `.internal`; `AwsQuerySchemaExtensions` also moves to `.internal` | `PackageContainmentTest` requires public `@SdkInternalApi` types in an `internal` package; the 7.15 protocols in the parent package call the factories |
| `acquire(QueryVariant, action, version)` from a `StripedPool`; `finish()` copies a small buffer and releases the serializer to the pool | `forAwsQuery`/`forEc2Query` create a new serializer; `finish()` returns the buffer without copying | `StripedPool`, `NumberCodec` and `TimestampCodec` are `@SdkInternalApi` in `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.commons.internal`, and `InternalApiBoundaryTest` forbids internal APIs across root packages (`protocols` here). Without a pool there is nothing to alias, so no copy is needed |
| Three serializers (top level, list item, map value) with the value methods written out in each | One abstract `ValueSerializer` with the value methods; the subclasses only write keys and push prefixes | Same keys; removes the triplicated code |
| `writeUrlEncoded` reads the Latin-1 array through `CompactStringAccess`; `Action`/`Version` copied unencoded with `String.getBytes(int, int, byte[], int)` | A `charAt` loop with the ASCII fast path; `Action` and `Version` are percent-encoded like values | No compact-string access on Java 8. Operation names and versions are unreserved characters, so the output is the same |
| An unpaired surrogate is encoded as a three-byte sequence (`%ED%A0%BD`) | Encoded as `?` (`%3F`) | Matches the legacy SDK (`URLEncoder` over `String.getBytes(UTF_8)`) and keeps the body valid UTF-8 |
| A list nested in a list, or a map in a list, pushes the element's member name again: `ListOfLists.member.1.member.member.1`, `ListOfMaps.member.1.member.entry.1.key`; in ec2Query `OuterList.1.Member.1` | `ListOfLists.member.1.member.1`, `ListOfMaps.member.1.entry.1.key`, `OuterList.1.1` | Source bug (its test documents the doubled segment). The port matches the legacy `ListQueryMarshaller` paths. No upstream awsQuery or ec2Query request test has a list of lists |
| ec2Query `writeMap` throws `SerializationException` | Maps use the awsQuery map rules in ec2Query | The legacy SDK registers `MapQueryMarshaller` for both protocols, so a model with an ec2Query map keeps working |
| Member names, `xmlFlattened` and list/map member `xmlName` read with `getTrait` (member and target traits merged) | `getDirectTrait` on the member; `timestampFormat` still includes the target's | `xmlName` on a target structure is an XML element name, not a query key; the legacy SDK used the member's `locationName` only |
| `float`/`double` through `NumberCodec` (integral values without a fraction, `1`, `10000000000`; others shortest) | `Float.toString`/`Double.toString` (`1.0`, `1.0E10`, `-0.0`, `NaN`, `Infinity`) | Matches the legacy `ValueToStringConverter`; `NumberCodec` is internal to `codecs` (above) |
| `date-time` through `TimestampCodec.writeIso8601` (fraction with trailing zeros dropped, `08:00:00.1Z`) | `TimestampFormatter.DATE_TIME.writeString` (`Instant.toString`, fraction in groups of three: `08:00:00.100Z`) | Matches the legacy `DateUtils.formatIso8601Date` and the XML codec |
| `epoch-seconds` and `http-date` through `TimestampCodec`; an unknown format through `TimestampFormatter.of` | `TimestampFormatter.of(format).writeString`, resolved once in the extension; an unknown format throws `SerializationException` when a value is written | Same text (epoch-seconds after the codec-commons fix above); public API only |
| `writeDocument` message `Query protocols do not support document types` | The same message plus the schema ShapeId | Errors name the schema |
| — | A value written directly on the serializer with a non-member schema throws `SerializationException` (`must be a structure member`) | The source writes a `null` key and fails with `NullPointerException` |

**Wire format compared with the legacy SDK** (`QueryProtocolMarshaller`, `ListQueryMarshaller`, `MapQueryMarshaller`, `SimpleTypeQueryMarshaller`, then `QueryParametersToBodyStage`, which writes the parameters with `SdkHttpUtils.encodeAndFlattenFormData`). Unchanged: `Action` and `Version` first, then members in member order; nested structure keys joined with `.`; awsQuery lists `Name.member.N` (list member `xmlName` replaces `member`), flattened `Name.N`, an explicitly empty list `Name=`; ec2Query lists always `Name.N` and empty lists omitted; maps `Name.entry.N.key`/`.value` with key/value `xmlName`, flattened `Name.N.key`; ec2Query names (`ec2QueryName`, else capitalized `xmlName` or member name); sparse null list elements skip their index and null map values write only the key; booleans, integers, `Float`/`Double.toString`, padded base64 blobs, `date-time` and `http-date` spellings; UTF-8 percent-encoding with uppercase hex. The Smithy protocol tests compare form bodies as decoded pairs (`FormUrlEncodedBodyComparator`), so the encoding rows compare equal there; the epoch-seconds row is what `QueryTimestampsInput` and `Ec2TimestampsInput` expect.

| Value | Legacy SDK | QueryFormSerializer |
|---|---|---|
| Space | `+` (`encodeAndFlattenFormData` uses `formDataEncode`, plain `URLEncoder`, not `SdkHttpUtils.urlEncode`) | `%20` |
| `*` and `~` | `*` unencoded, `~` as `%7E` | `%2A` and `~` (RFC 3986 unreserved set, as smithy-java and `SdkHttpUtils.urlEncode`) |
| `epoch-seconds` timestamp | `DateUtils.formatUnixTimestampInstant`: three fraction digits, sub-millisecond digits dropped (`1422172800.000`) | Up to nine fraction digits, trailing zeros dropped (`1422172800`, `1422172800.001`) |
| `bigDecimal`, `byte` members | No marshaller registered (the marshalling fails) | `toPlainString()` (`1E+3` → `1000`); `byte` in decimal |
| List of lists, map in a list | `L.member.1.member.1`, `L.member.1.entry.1.key` | Same (smithy-java differs, above) |

**Tests** (`core/protocols/aws-query-client-protocol/src/test/java/.../awsquery/internal/`, 52 runs, with a write-only `SchemaStruct` copied from the codec tests and `QueryTestModel` schemas shaped like the Smithy `AwsQuery`/`AwsEc2` input shapes): `QueryFormSerializerTest` asserts exact bodies for the request cases `QuerySimpleInputParams*`, `AwsQuerySupports*FloatInputs`, `QueryTimestampsInput`, `NestedStructures`, `QueryLists`, `EmptyQueryLists`, `FlattenedQueryLists`, `QueryListArgWithXmlNameMember`, `QueryFlattenedListArgWithXmlName`, `QueryNestedStructWithList`, `QuerySimpleQueryMaps`, `QuerySimpleQueryMapsWithXmlName`, `QueryComplexQueryMaps`, `QueryEmptyQueryMaps`, `QueryQueryMapWithMemberXmlName`, `QueryFlattenedQueryMaps`, `QueryFlattenedQueryMapsWithXmlName`, `QueryQueryMapOfLists`, `QueryNestedStructWithMap` and their ec2Query counterparts (`Ec2Lists`, `Ec2EmptyQueryLists`, `Ec2ListArgWithXmlNameMember`, `Ec2ListMemberWithXmlName`, `Ec2ListNestedStructWithList`, `Ec2Query`, `Ec2QueryIsPreferred`, `Ec2XmlNameIsUppercased`, `Ec2QueryNameDistinctFromXmlNameAndMemberName`, `Ec2TimestampsInput`, `Ec2NestedStructures`, `Ec2ProtocolIdempotencyTokenAutoFillIsSet`), plus integral types, float and double spellings, `bigDecimal`, blobs (buffer position kept), percent-encoding of reserved, control, two-, three- and four-byte and unpaired-surrogate characters and of `Action`/`Version` and member names, long non-ASCII values, millisecond `date-time`, exact epoch-seconds, `http-date`, an unknown format, empty nested structures, lists of lists, sparse lists and maps, list timestamp formats, nested maps, empty list map values, ec2Query maps, documents, a non-member schema, `finish()` once and buffer growth, and the service-loader registration. The behaviors smithy-java's `QueryFormSerializerTest` checks are covered here, with the legacy paths in place of its doubled-member expectations; its pool tests do not apply. The generated `AwsQuery`/`AwsEc2` protocol tests run end to end in tasks 7.15 and 7.21.

## `core/http-binding` (task 7.8)
**Sources.** smithy-java 1.7.0 (`/Users/alexwoo/java/smithy-java`, commit `142eecf4d`), module `http/http-binding` (package `software.amazon.smithy.java.http.binding`), and from `io`, `uri.URLEncoding`.
**Target.** In `software.amazon.awssdk.httpbinding` (`@SdkPublicApi`): `HttpBinding`, `RequestSerializer`, `ResponseSerializer`, `RequestDeserializer`, `ResponseDeserializer`, `HeaderErrorSerializer`. In `software.amazon.awssdk.httpbinding.internal` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `HttpBindingSchemaExtensions` (public, registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`), `HttpBindingSerializer` and `HttpBindingDeserializer` (public, used by the public classes), and package-private `PathSerializer`, `PayloadSerializer`, `PayloadDeserializer`, `StructBodyProxy`, `BasicStringValueDeserializer`, `HttpHeaderDeserializer`, `HttpHeaderListDeserializer`, `HttpPrefixHeadersDeserializer`, `HttpPathLabelDeserializer`, `HttpQueryStringDeserializer`, `HttpQueryParamsDeserializer`, `ResponseStatusDeserializer`, `PrefixConstants`, plus the SDK additions `UriPattern`, `UrlEncoding` (port of `io.uri.URLEncoding`), `StringMapEntrySerializer` and `ValueSkipper`.
**Module.** Declares `annotations`, `utils`, `schema`, `codec-commons` and `http-client-spi`; `json-codec` and `xml-codec` are test-only. The task 1.2 javadoc and dependency-analysis skips and the unused `test-utils`/`jqwik` test dependencies are removed (property tests 7.9 and 7.10 add jqwik back). Only public APIs of other modules are used: `InternalApiBoundaryTest` allows internal APIs only within one root package, and `software.amazon.awssdk.httpbinding` is its own root, so nothing from `codecs.commons.internal` (for example `TimestampCodec`) is used. The internal types live in `.internal` because `PackageContainmentTest` requires a public `@SdkInternalApi` type to be there (as `CborSchemaExtensions`, `JsonSchemaExtensions`).
**Task text.** Task 7.8 names `BindingMatcher`. smithy-java has no `BindingMatcher` at `142eecf4d`; its role (classifying each member by binding trait) is `HttpBindingSchemaExtensions.MemberBinding` and the per-direction plans, which are ported.
| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `HttpBinding` public constructor, `builder()`/`runtimeCodegen`; `HttpBindingCodegen`, `HttpBindingRuntimeCodegenBackend`, `HttpBindingWriter` | `HttpBinding.create()` (one stateless instance); no codegen classes; SDK addition `hasStreamingPayload(schema, response)` | Runtime codegen deferred (design D.3); static factory per SDK guidelines. `hasStreamingPayload` is the task 7.19 hook |
| `MemberSubsetCodec` fast paths (`BodyMemberSubset`, direct-builder reads in `HttpBindingDeserializer`/`PayloadDeserializer`) | Not ported; the body is always written through `StructBodyProxy` and read through a member filter (below) | `MemberSubsetCodec` is not in the SDK `codec-commons` (tasks 3.5, 7.1) |
| `HttpTrait.getUri()` is smithy-model's parsed `UriPattern` | `UriPattern` parses the SDK trait's string once per operation schema (inside `OperationBinding`): literal, label and greedy-label segments, query literals. Malformed patterns (label not a whole segment, two greedy labels, duplicate literal keys) throw `IllegalArgumentException` | The SDK runtime `HttpTrait` keeps the unparsed string (task 1.7) |
| `QueryStringBuilder` writes an encoded query string; `HttpRequest.setUri(endpoint.withConcatPath(path).withQuery(qs))` | Raw query parameters (`LinkedHashMap`, values grouped by name in first-seen order) set with `SdkHttpFullRequest.Builder.rawQueryParameters`; the request percent-encodes them through `SdkHttpUtils.urlEncode` (the same RFC 3986 unreserved set as `URLEncoding`). Path: `uri(endpoint)` then `encodedPath(SdkHttpUtils.appendUri(endpoint raw path, path))` | SigV4 and the rest of the pipeline read raw query parameters. `appendUri` is the joining `HttpClientProtocol` and `RpcV2CborProtocol` use |
| Query literal without `=` (`?tagging`) written as `tagging=`; pre-encoded once | Raw parameter with a null value, written as `tagging` | Matches the legacy `ProtocolUtils.addStaticQueryParametersToRequest` and the `RestJsonConstantQueryString` expectation (`queryParams: ["foo=bar", "hello"]`) |
| `@httpQueryParams` entries checked against the `@httpQuery` names added so far (member order matters) | Entries are collected and added after every member is written; a name written by an `@httpQuery` member or a query literal drops the entry. A null `@httpQuery` member does not block it | Requirement 15.6 independent of member order |
