The same ad hoc run gives awsJson1_0 63 passed, 6 failed and awsJson1_1 125 passed, 3 failed (unchanged from the idempotency fix), and the default run stays at rpcv2Cbor 74 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped.

**For tasks 7.14, 7.15, 7.19 and 7.21.** 7.14 (restXml): extend `HttpBindingClientProtocol` with `XmlCodec`; keep the greedy slash (default), decide `omitEmptyPayload` and `allowEmptyStructPayload` against the legacy XML marshaller, pass an XML `KnownErrorFactory` that reads from the `<Error>` element (the payload parser must unwrap `<ErrorResponse><Error>`), and add the factory to `STAGED_FACTORIES`; the header list rules above already apply. 7.15 (awsQuery/ec2Query) uses `HttpClientProtocol` directly, not this base; idempotency tokens are filled before `serializeRequest`, so `QueryFormSerializer` sees them through `serializeMembers`. 7.19: `HttpBindingClientProtocol.deserializeOutput` already skips the body of a `@streaming` payload output and `createRequest` writes no body for a streaming input; add the request `ContentStreamProvider`/length and the `ResponseTransformer` hand-off there, then remove the streaming gate in `SchemaOperationRouting`. 7.21: enable restJson1 and record `SdkRestJsonNoContentWithBlobPayload` (or `RestJsonHttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody`) and `RestJsonHttpChecksumRequired` in `protocol-test-skips.json` with the reasons above; the default-value cases are the same family as the awsJson and rpcv2Cbor ones.

## `core/protocols/rest-xml-client-protocol` (task 7.14)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.protocols.client.restxml` (`@SdkPublicApi`): `RestXmlClientProtocol` with the nested `Factory`, registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.core.client.protocol.ClientProtocolFactory`. Ported from smithy-java `aws/client/aws-client-restxml` (commit `142eecf4d`) on the task 7.13 `HttpBindingClientProtocol`/`HttpBindingErrorFactory` and the task 7.4 `XmlCodec`. `HttpBindingClientProtocol`, `HttpErrorDeserializer` and http-binding are unchanged.

**Modules.** `rest-xml-client-protocol` declares `annotations`, `utils`, `schema`, `codec-commons`, `xml-codec`, `sdk-core`, `http-client-protocol` and `http-client-spi`; `aws-core` is test-only. Unlike restJson1 it does not declare `http-binding`: it never calls an `HttpBinding` method (no `hasStructPayload` hook), so dependency analysis reports it unused. The task 1.2 javadoc and dependency-analysis skips and the unused `test-utils`/`jqwik` test dependencies are removed. `xml-codec` gains `XmlCodec.Builder.xmlDeclaration(boolean)` and `XmlCodec.xmlDeclaration()` (off by default; `createSerializer` writes `<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>` to the sink first), closing the "XML declaration" row of the task 7.4 wire-format table for restXml.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `RestXmlClientProtocol(ShapeId service)` and `(ShapeId, Schema serviceSchema)`; `Factory` with the implicit constructor | `RestXmlClientProtocol(ProtocolSettings)`; `Factory.INSTANCE`, a public no-argument constructor for `ServiceLoader`, covariant `createProtocol` that ignores the trait (may be null, `noErrorWrapping` included) | As the other ported protocols (design A.11). Both error forms are always read (below), so `noErrorWrapping` changes nothing |
| `XmlCodec.builder().defaultNamespace(xmlNamespace).strictRootElement(false)` | `.settings(XmlSettings.REST_XML).defaultNamespace(<service xmlNamespace or null>).strictRootElement(false).xmlDeclaration(true)`; one codec for requests, responses and errors | The design preset; the declaration matches the legacy `XmlWriter` prolog. The Smithy restXml cases compare bodies with XMLUnit, which ignores the declaration |
| `omitEmptyPayload()` true; `allowEmptyStructPayload` and greedy trimming left at the base defaults | Same | Legacy `XmlProtocolMarshaller`: no body without payload members; a null structure `@httpPayload` writes nothing, so no body; `SimpleTypePathMarshaller.GREEDY_STRING` keeps the leading `/` (`SdkRestXmlGreedyLabelLeadingSlashPreserved`) |
| `headerErrorExtractor(new AmznErrorHeaderExtractor())` | None: `X-Amzn-Errortype` and `X-Amzn-Error-Message` are not read | Requirement 23.2 (restXml: the body `Code` only) and the legacy `AwsXmlErrorProtocolUnmarshaller` |
| `XML_ERROR_PAYLOAD_PARSER.parsePayload`: `XmlUtil.parseErrorCodeName`, `ShapeId.fromOptionalNamespace(ns, code)`, creates the modeled error itself, no message or request ID | `ErrorPayloadParser` returning code, message and request ID from one read of the body with an internal schema (`Code`, `Message`, `RequestId`, `RequestID`), through the codec's `<ErrorResponse>`/`<Error>` unwrapping. When the root element is `<ErrorResponse>` (byte check after an optional BOM, whitespace and processing instructions), a second codec with `wrapperElements(["ErrorResponse"])` reads `<Error>` as a nested structure and the `RequestId`/`RequestID` next to it. `HttpErrorDeserializer` then resolves the code with `ErrorTypeUtils.resolve` and creates the error through `HttpBindingErrorFactory` | The task 3.19 `ErrorPayloadParser` contract. `parseErrorCodeName` returns only the code, so the message would need a second parse, and the codec's own unwrapping cannot see the request ID next to `<Error>`. Legacy reads `<Message>` and the root's `RequestId`/`RequestID` (`AwsXmlErrorUnmarshaller`) |
| Event streams (`AwsEventEncoderFactory`, `AwsEventDecoderFactory`) | Not ported: `UnsupportedOperationException`, reported as `SdkClientException("Unable to (un)marshall <op>: ...")` | Task 7.20 |

**Decisions against the legacy SDK** (`AwsXmlProtocolFactory`, `XmlProtocolMarshaller`, `XmlGenerator`/`XmlWriter`, `XmlProtocolUnmarshaller`, `XmlResponseParserUtils`, `AwsXmlErrorProtocolUnmarshaller`, `AwsXmlErrorUnmarshaller`, `XmlDomParser`).

| Behavior | Legacy SDK | This protocol |
|---|---|---|
| Input without payload members | No body, no `Content-Type` | Same (`omitEmptyPayload`) |
| Body members all null | `<?xml ...?><Root xmlns="..."></Root>` when the input has a root `locationName` (Route 53), else no body | The empty root element in both cases, named by the input structure's `@xmlName` (the C2J converter writes it from `locationName`, task 1.23) or its shape name (`SdkRestXmlNoParamsBody`). No C2J restXml service has body members without a root `locationName` (CloudFront and S3 inputs use payload members) |
| Null structure `@httpPayload` | No body | Same (`allowEmptyStructPayload` false) |
| Root element of a structure payload | The member's `locationName` (the C2J converter sets the target's name) | The member's `@xmlName`, else the target's `@xmlName` or shape name (`HttpPayloadWithXmlName`, `HttpPayloadWithMemberXmlName`) |
| Root namespace | `xmlns` from the input's `xmlNamespace` (or its members') | The root shape's `@xmlNamespace`, else the service's `@xmlNamespace` |
| XML declaration | `<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>` | Same |
| `Content-Type` | `application/xml` when a body is written; `binary/octet-stream` for a blob payload; none for a string payload | `application/xml`; `@mediaType`, else `application/octet-stream` for a blob payload and `text/plain` for a string payload (http-binding, task 7.8; `HttpPayloadTraitsWithBlob`, `RestXmlStringPayloadRequest`) |
| Greedy labels | Leading `/` kept | Same |
| Success with an output without body or payload members | Body not parsed | Same (task 7.13 base) |
| Blob `@httpPayload` response | Raw body | Raw body, never parsed as XML (unit test with XML, non-XML and declaration bodies; `HttpPayloadTraitsWithBlob`). An empty body leaves it unset (`HttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody`), where legacy returns empty `SdkBytes` |
| Error discriminator | `<Code>` of the root's `<Error>` child, so only `<ErrorResponse><Error>`; a bare `<Error>` (S3-style, `noErrorWrapping`) gives the base exception with a null code outside S3's factory | `<Code>` of `<ErrorResponse><Error>` or of a bare `<Error>`, for every service; a root of another name is read leniently as the error element (legacy: base exception) |
| Error code normalization | Exact match against modeled codes | `ErrorTypeUtils.resolve`: anything from the first `:` dropped, `ns#Name` accepted, relative names in the service namespace |
| Message | `<Error><Message>` | Same; null when absent |
| Request ID | The root's `RequestId`, else `RequestID`, else `x-amzn-RequestId`/`x-amz-request-id` | Same order; in the bare form the root is `<Error>`, so its `RequestId`; in the wrapped form a `RequestId` inside `<Error>` is used only when there is none next to it. `x-amz-id-2` is the extended request ID on both paths |
| Unknown code, no `Code`, empty or unparseable body | Base exception from `<Error>` (members and message), code as sent or null | Base exception with the code (or null), message, status and request ID; never a deserialization exception (Requirement 23.5) |
| `X-Amzn-Errortype` header | Not read | Not read |

The task 7.4 codec differences still apply (text and attribute escaping, `float`/`double` spelling, epoch-seconds fractions).

**Runtime protocol selection (codegen).** `ClientProtocolSelection.STAGED_FACTORIES` gains `aws.protocols#restXml` → `RestXmlClientProtocol.Factory`; `FACTORIES` is unchanged. `test/smithy-protocol-tests` depends on `rest-xml-client-protocol`, and no longer on `aws-xml-protocol`: every operation of the restXml Protocol_Test_Services (`RestXml` 65, `RestXmlWithNamespace` 1, `SdkRestXml` 9) has schema serde members, so the generated clients reference no legacy XML marshaller and dependency analysis reported the module unused (as `smithy-rpcv2-protocol` in task 3.34). Route 53 (restXml) does not set `stagedClientProtocols` and generates what it generated before: `LegacyRouteParityTest` (7, including `route53`) passes with `legacy-route-parity/route53.txt` unchanged, and `NoTransformPackageTest` (8) passes.

**Tests.** `rest-xml-client-protocol` `RestXmlClientProtocolTest` (50 runs; `TestShapes` with a service `xmlNamespace`, an input `@xmlName`, an attribute member, an `@xmlName` payload target with its own namespace and an error without a message member; `SchemaStruct` copied from the restJson1 tests): ID, codec settings (`REST_XML` plus the namespace, lenient root, declaration) and exchange; every request binding with the exact body (declaration, `xmlns`, attribute, `xmlName`), `Content-Length` and a zero-copy body; greedy labels kept for 4 values; the empty root element for null body members; no body for a header-only and an empty input; no body for a null structure payload and the target-named root for a set one; a payload with its own `xmlName`/`xmlNamespace`; raw bytes for a blob payload; no namespace without a service `xmlNamespace`; a null label as `SdkClientException`; response body, header and status bindings with a declaration, a namespace and another root name; no `Content-Type` check; an output without members ignoring 3 bodies; empty and missing bodies; structure and blob payload responses (the blob raw for XML, non-XML and declaration bodies); a streaming payload left unread; 4 malformed bodies and an invalid header value as `SdkClientException`; wrapped (with and without declaration, `RequestId`/`RequestID`) and bare errors resolving the modeled error with its header member, status, request ID, extended request ID, code, message and raw body; the request ID headers; the request ID next to `<Error>` over one inside it; `X-Amzn-Errortype` and `X-Amzn-Error-Message` ignored; 7 unresolved bodies as the base exception; another root element; factory discovery, `INSTANCE` and creation. `xml-codec`: `XmlCodecWireFormatTest` declaration written first and read back for both implementations, `XmlSettingsTest` defaults and `toString`. `codegen` `SchemaClientBuilderTest`: restXml selects the staged factory only with `stagedClientProtocols`, and restJson1 wins over restXml.

**Protocol tests, checked outside the suite (for task 7.21).** `test/smithy-protocol-tests` with `-DprotocolTests.enabledProtocols=aws.protocols#restXml`: **196 passed, 1 failed, 0 skipped (197)**, all 75 operations on the new path. The only failure is `SdkRestXmlNoContentWithBlobPayload` (expects the legacy empty `SdkBytes` for an empty blob payload; it contradicts the upstream `HttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody`, which passes), the restXml twin of `SdkRestJsonNoContentWithBlobPayload`. The same run with the restXml projections on the legacy path (no `stagedClientProtocols`, `aws-xml-protocol` added back) gives 142 passed, 55 failed: labels (escaping, greedy, timestamp and NaN/Infinity float labels), headers (boolean, enum, numeric, string, timestamp, NaN/Infinity, null and empty, empty prefix headers), payload traits (structure, `xmlName`, `xmlNamespace`, media type, unions, enum and string payloads), XML attributes, flattened and namespaced maps, `XmlLists`, `XmlNamespaces`, epoch-seconds timestamps, `SimpleScalarProperties`, `RestXmlQueryPrecedence`, `AllQueryStringTypes`, `HttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody` and `SdkRestXmlGreedyLabelLeadingSlashPreserved`, the legacy-route gaps checkpoint 6 recorded. The default run stays at rpcv2Cbor 74 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped; the restJson1 and awsJson runs were not repeated because nothing they use changed (`HttpBindingClientProtocol`, `HttpErrorDeserializer`, http-binding and the JSON codec are untouched).

**For tasks 7.15, 7.19 and 7.21.** 7.15 (awsQuery/ec2Query): `XmlCodec.xmlDeclaration` is off by default, as the query responses need; the awsQuery `<ErrorResponse><Error>...</Error><RequestId>` body has the same shape as restXml's, so the `wrapperElements(["ErrorResponse"])` read here (or a shared helper) gets its request ID, while ec2Query's `<Response><Errors><Error>` puts `RequestID` next to `<Errors>`. 7.19: restXml streaming payloads (S3-style `GetObject`) need nothing protocol-specific; the base hand-off applies, and an `@httpPayload` blob is already read raw. 7.21: enable restXml and record `SdkRestXmlNoContentWithBlobPayload` (or `HttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody`) with the same decision as restJson1.

## `core/protocols/aws-query-client-protocol` client protocols (task 7.15)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.protocols.client.awsquery`: `AwsQueryClientProtocol` and `Ec2QueryClientProtocol` with their nested `Factory` (`@SdkPublicApi`), both registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.core.client.protocol.ClientProtocolFactory`, on the package-private base `QueryClientProtocol` (`@SdkProtectedApi`). In `.internal` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `QueryErrorPayloadParser`. Ported from smithy-java `aws/client/aws-client-awsquery` (commit `142eecf4d`) on the task 3.19 `HttpClientProtocol`/`HttpErrorDeserializer`, the task 7.4 `XmlCodec` and the task 7.5 `QueryFormSerializer`.

**Modules.** `aws-query-client-protocol` adds `xml-codec`, `sdk-core`, `http-client-protocol` and `http-client-spi` (task 7.5 left them out until now), and `aws-core` and `endpoints-spi` for tests only. `http-client-protocol` gains `HttpClientProtocol.buildOutput(builder, response, bodyRequestId)` (`protected final`): a non-null body request ID becomes the response metadata's `AWS_REQUEST_ID` instead of the request ID headers, as in `AwsQueryResponseHandler`; the two-argument `buildOutput` delegates to it with null.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Two independent classes with the same `createRequest`/`deserializeResponse` apart from the variant, the wrapper elements and the error parser | Package-private `abstract class QueryClientProtocol` with `final` `createRequest`, `deserializeOutput`, `createError` and `payloadCodec`; `AwsQueryClientProtocol` and `Ec2QueryClientProtocol` are `final` and only pass the trait ID and the variant | Removes the duplicated code; same pattern as `AwsJsonProtocol` (task 7.11). `@SdkProtectedApi` for the reasons given there |
| `(ShapeId service, String version)` constructors; `Factory` with the implicit constructor and `Objects.requireNonNull(settings.serviceVersion(), ...)` | `(ProtocolSettings)`; `Factory.INSTANCE`, a public no-argument constructor for `ServiceLoader`, covariant `createProtocol` that ignores the trait (may be null). A missing service version throws `NullPointerException("serviceVersion is a required protocol setting for aws.protocols#awsQuery")` (or `#ec2Query`) | As the other ported protocols (design A.11). Generated base builders already pass `serviceVersion` (task 3.23) |
| `payloadCodec()` returns the XML codec | Returns null | Requirement 11.4 and the `ClientProtocol` Javadoc |
| Action from `operation.schema().id().getName()` | `ApiOperation.name()` | The name every other protocol uses (equal for generated operations) |
| `setUri(endpoint)`: the request path is the endpoint path | `POST`, path `SdkHttpUtils.appendUri(endpoint raw path, "/")` | `QueryHostWithPath` and `Ec2QueryHostWithPath` expect `/custom/` for the host `example.com/custom`; the same joining as awsJson (task 7.11) |
| `Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded` through `DataStream.ofByteBuffer` | The same `Content-Type` value, an explicit `Content-Length`, and a body that shares the serializer's array (`contentAsByteBufferOrNull` without a copy). The request body size hint (racecar E1) is not used: `QueryFormSerializer` grows its own buffer from 1 KiB (task 7.5) | The SDK request has no implicit length. Every Smithy request test checks the exact `Content-Type` (see the wire table) |
| Unit input: `serializeMembers` skipped (`UNIT_TYPE_TRAIT` on the input schema) | Same | — |
| Error when `statusCode() >= 300` | `HttpClientProtocol.isErrorResponse` default: any status outside 2xx | As tasks 3.21 and 7.11 |
| Output: a new `XmlCodec` with `wrapperElements([Op+"Response", Op+"Result"])` (ec2Query: `[Op+"Response"]`) per call; `contentLength() == 0` or a Unit output returns `builder.build()` | One codec per operation name, cached in the protocol instance (at most 4096 names; others are created per call), with `XmlSettings.AWS_QUERY_RESPONSE` and `strictRootElement(false)`. Empty body: nothing read. An output without members (Unit included) does not read its members. `errorCorrection()` then `buildOutput` | Codec creation once per operation, not per call; the cap follows `MarshallBufferSizeTracker`. Same output-without-members rule as awsJson; one build path that attaches `sdkHttpResponse` and response metadata |
| No response request ID from the body | A second read of the `<OpResponse>` children with a small schema (`ResponseMetadata` → `RequestId`, and `requestId`; the second wins, as in `QueryProtocolUnmarshaller.parseMetadata`); it skips the result element. A failure there falls back to the request ID headers (DEBUG log) instead of failing the call | Legacy parity for `responseMetadata().requestId()`. Costs one more scan of the body; reading it only when no request ID header is present would avoid it for services that send both |
| `XML_ERROR_PAYLOAD_PARSER`: `XmlUtil.parseErrorCodeName`, then `awsQueryError` codes of `operation.errorSchemas()`, then `ShapeId.fromOptionalNamespace`; creates the error itself. `Ec2ErrorPayloadParser`: shape names only | `QueryErrorPayloadParser.AWS_QUERY`/`EC2_QUERY` return code, message and request ID from one read of the root's children (codec with `wrapperElements(["ErrorResponse"])` or `(["Response"])`). `HttpErrorDeserializer` resolves the code with `useErrorCodeIndex(true)`: `ErrorRegistry.resolveCode` first, then `ErrorTypeUtils.resolve` (anything from the first `:` dropped, `ns#Name`, else the service namespace), for both protocols | The task 3.19 `ErrorPayloadParser` contract. Requirement 23.3 (custom codes for awsQuery and ec2Query); the code index is service-wide (Requirement 23.7), where smithy-java only sees the operation's declared errors |
| `XmlKnownErrorFactory`: `codec.deserializeShape(body, builder)` | `HttpErrorDeserializer.DEFAULT_KNOWN_ERROR_FACTORY` with the protocol's error codec (`AWS_QUERY_RESPONSE`, lenient root), which unwraps `<ErrorResponse><Error>` and `<Response><Errors><Error>` itself | Same members read; no extra class |
| — | The error parser is not shared with restXml's (task 7.14): the query roots are fixed per protocol, so it needs no root-name sniffing, and the restXml parser is private to `rest-xml-client-protocol`. No module has both the XML codec and the `ErrorPayloadParser` SPI to host a shared one | Scope. A shared helper would need `http-client-protocol` to depend on `xml-codec` or a new module |

**Wire format compared with the legacy SDK** (`AwsQueryProtocolFactory`, `AwsEc2ProtocolFactory`, `QueryProtocolMarshaller`, `QueryParametersToBodyStage`, `AwsQueryResponseHandler`, `QueryProtocolUnmarshaller`, `AwsXmlErrorProtocolUnmarshaller`, `AwsXmlErrorUnmarshaller`). Unchanged: `POST`, `Action` and `Version` first, the form keys (task 7.5), `Content-Length`, success classification, the `<OpResponse><OpResult>` (awsQuery) and `<OpResponse>` (ec2Query) result locations, the response metadata request ID (`ResponseMetadata/RequestId`, then `requestId`, else `x-amzn-RequestId`/`x-amz-request-id`), the error element (`<ErrorResponse><Error>`, `<Response><Errors><Error>`), its `Code` and `Message`, the error request ID (the root's `RequestId`, then `RequestID`, then the headers), `x-amz-id-2` as the extended request ID, and the base exception with code, message, status and request ID for an unknown or missing code. The query-compatible awsJson services (SQS) are not this protocol: they stay on `aws-json-client-protocol`. Differences:

| Behavior | Legacy SDK | This protocol |
|---|---|---|
| `Content-Type` | `application/x-www-form-urlencoded; charset=utf-8` (`QueryParametersToBodyStage`) | `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, as smithy-java and the Smithy request tests (64 of the 70 awsQuery and ec2Query request cases check it exactly). Task 7.5 noted both values; the task 7.15 instructions asked for the legacy value, which failed every request test, so the spec value was kept. Services accept both; revisit for SNS in 9.4 if a byte-level parity check needs the legacy header |
| Request path for an endpoint with a path | The endpoint path (`/custom`): `encodedPath("")` joined to the endpoint | `/custom/` (protocol tests above). Endpoints without a path send `/` on both |
| Form encoding of space, `*`, `~` | `+`, `*`, `%7E` | `%20`, `%2A`, `~` (task 7.5). **Open question**, kept as task 7.5 decided: both decode to the same values, and the Smithy tests compare decoded pairs. Decide before 9.4 whether SNS must send the legacy bytes |
| Other form values | See task 7.5 (epoch-seconds fractions, `bigDecimal`, `byte`) | Same as task 7.5 |
| Result element | The first child of `<OpResponse>`, whatever its name (`hasResultWrapper`) | Exactly `<OpResponse><OpResult>`; another name is `SdkClientException("Unable to unmarshall <op>: ...")` |
| Response metadata | Every `ResponseMetadata` child under its element name (`RequestId` as `AWS_REQUEST_ID`), plus all headers | `AWS_REQUEST_ID` and all headers; other `ResponseMetadata` children are not copied |
| Output without members | The body is parsed (a malformed body fails) | Members not read; an unreadable envelope only loses the body request ID |
| Raw blob `@httpPayload` output (`responsePayloadIsBlob`) | The body text as the blob | Not supported (no awsQuery or ec2Query model has one) |
| Error root | awsQuery reads the `Error` child of any root; ec2Query the `Errors/Error` child of any root | Root `ErrorResponse` (awsQuery) or `Response` (ec2Query); another root is the base exception without a code |
| Resolving the code | Exact match against the registered codes (the `awsQueryError` code, else the shape name) | `ErrorRegistry.resolveCode` first, then the shape name. A shape name whose shape has a different `awsQueryError` code also resolves (legacy: the base exception). CloudWatch's `ResourceNotFound` resolves to `DashboardNotFoundError` (its `awsQueryError` code, first in ShapeId order per task 3.15), not to the `ResourceNotFound` shape |
| Reported error code | The `<Code>` text as sent | `ErrorTypeUtils.removeNamespaceAndUri`: the text from the first `:` and any `ns#` prefix dropped (no difference for real query codes) |
| `x-amzn-query-error` | Not read | Read first when the service schema has `awsQueryCompatible` (`HttpErrorDeserializer.protocolSettings`, Requirement 23.2). CloudWatch has the trait, so its awsQuery override (9.5) reads the header; query endpoints do not send it |
| XML values | `XmlDomParser` and the query unmarshallers | Task 7.4 codec (`float`/`double`, epoch-seconds fractions) |

**Runtime protocol selection (codegen).** `ClientProtocolSelection.STAGED_FACTORIES` gains `aws.protocols#awsQuery` → `AwsQueryClientProtocol.Factory` and `aws.protocols#ec2Query` → `Ec2QueryClientProtocol.Factory`; `FACTORIES` is unchanged. Every protocol in `ProtocolUtils.CLIENT_PROTOCOL_PRIORITY` now has a factory, so `SchemaClientBuilderTest`'s "no factory" assertion is gone. `test/smithy-protocol-tests` depends on `aws-query-client-protocol` and no longer on `aws-query-protocol`: every operation of `AwsQuery` (33), `AwsEc2` (25) and `SdkEc2` (3) has schema serde members, so dependency analysis reported the legacy module unused. SNS (awsQuery) and SQS (awsJson1_0 with `awsQueryCompatible`) do not set `stagedClientProtocols` and generate what they generated before: `LegacyRouteParityTest` (7, `sns` and `sqs` with no differences file) and `NoTransformPackageTest` (8) pass, with no file under `legacy-route-parity/` changed. CloudWatch keeps rpcv2Cbor: `CloudWatchProtocolSelectionTest` (4) passes.

**Tests.** `QueryClientProtocolTest` (37 runs, both protocols where it applies; `TestShapes` shaped like generated classes, with an `AwsResponse` output and errors with and without members): ID, null payload codec, exchange and `toString`; method, path, the exact header set and body for awsQuery (`xmlName` key, `%20`) and ec2Query (capitalized keys), with the shared body buffer; Unit input; endpoint paths and `updateServiceEndpoint`; a missing service version; awsQuery `<OpResponse><OpResult>` and ec2Query `<OpResponse>` outputs with the body request ID over the header, unknown elements and HTTP binding traits ignored; the header request ID without a body one; no body; an output without members reading only the request ID, and with another root keeping the header ID; 4 unreadable bodies as `SdkClientException`; a modeled awsQuery error with members, status, body request ID, extended request ID, code, message and raw body; the first ec2Query `<Error>`; custom codes (with a `:` suffix), CloudWatch's duplicate code and an absolute ID for both protocols; an unknown code; 8 bodies without a resolvable code (no body, empty, non-XML, bare `<Error>`, the other protocol's root, no `Code`, empty `<Errors>`) as the base exception; `X-Amzn-Errortype` ignored; factory discovery, `INSTANCE` and creation. `codegen` `SchemaClientBuilderTest`: awsQuery, ec2Query, ec2Query with awsQuery, and awsQuery with restXml select the expected staged factory, and awsQuery and ec2Query keep the legacy builder without `stagedClientProtocols`.

**Protocol tests, checked outside the suite (for task 7.21).** `test/smithy-protocol-tests` with `-DprotocolTests.enabledProtocols=aws.protocols#awsQuery,aws.protocols#ec2Query`: **awsQuery 77 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (77); ec2Query 63 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (63, `AwsEc2` 59 and `SdkEc2` 4)**, all 61 operations on the new path. The first run, with the legacy `Content-Type`, failed 64 of the 70 request cases on that header and the two `*HostWithPath` cases on the path; every response case passed. The legacy path was not run for comparison: `stagedClientProtocols` is module-wide and the restXml projections no longer compile without `aws-xml-protocol` (task 7.14). By construction it sends the `charset` `Content-Type` and the unslashed path, so it would fail at least those 66 request cases. The default run stays at rpcv2Cbor 74 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped.

**For tasks 7.17, 7.19, 7.21, 9.4 and 9.5.** 7.17: nothing to do for the query protocols: `QueryFormSerializer.writeDocument` throws and the XML codec reads no documents, as the protocols define. 7.19: no query operation streams; the legacy raw-blob output above is not ported. 7.21: add `aws.protocols#awsQuery` and `aws.protocols#ec2Query` to `protocolTests.enabledProtocols`; no skips are needed today. 9.4 (SNS): move awsQuery out of `STAGED_FACTORIES` (or opt SNS in) and add the module dependency; decide the `Content-Type`, path and form-encoding rows above first, and expect `legacy-route-parity/sns.txt` to need entries; response metadata keeps only the request ID. 9.5 (CloudWatch): add the dependency and override with `AwsQueryClientProtocol.Factory.INSTANCE.createProtocol(settings, null)` (the generated builder's settings carry `serviceVersion`); custom codes resolve through `CloudWatchOperations`' code index, including `ResourceNotFound` → `DashboardNotFoundError`, and the `awsQueryCompatible` trait makes the override read `x-amzn-query-error` first.

## Document members on the schema path (task 7.17)

SDK addition (smithy-java generated models hold its `Document` directly; SDK models keep Legacy_Document accessors, Requirement 21.4). Already in place before this task: `writeDocument`/`readDocument` for task 1.7 documents in the JSON (7.1) and CBOR (3.6) codecs, including null documents, list and map documents and null elements. Added here:

- `sdk-core` `SchemaSerdeSupport.wrapLegacy(Legacy_Document)` and `unwrapLegacy(Document)` (`@SdkProtectedApi`), backed by the package-private `LegacyDocumentViews`.
- `codegen` `ValueKind.DOCUMENT`: a member, list element or map value whose Java type is `core.document.Document` gets schema serde members. Generated code writes `serializer.writeDocument(M_X, SchemaSerdeSupport.wrapLegacy(this.x))` and reads `SchemaSerdeSupport.unwrapLegacy(deserializer.readDocument())`. `<Svc>SharedSerde` reads a document element or value without the `isNull()` branch, so a null element becomes `Document.fromNull()`, as in the legacy unmarshallers. Classes with document members therefore get schema serde, and their operations route to the new path.
- JSON codec: a `null` document member reaches the member consumer, and `readDocument` keeps exact values for non-integral numbers that a double cannot hold.

**Views (no copies).**

| Direction | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Legacy → schema (`wrapLegacy`) | `null` and `NullDocument` → Java `null` (the task 1.7 Smithy null; the codecs write `null`). Strings and booleans → `Document.of`. Lists and maps → views over the legacy list or map that convert an element or value each time it is read; `size`, `getMember`, `getMemberNames` read the legacy map directly. Numbers → a view that keeps the Legacy_Document and coerces like the schema document of the value: `type()` is the first of integer, long, bigInteger, double and bigDecimal that holds the `SdkNumber` value exactly (Requirement 18.5, as design B.4 specifies), `NaN`/`Infinity` are doubles, integers with more than 1,000 digits stay bigDecimal (so `1E+999999999` is not expanded), and an `SdkNumber` that is not a number throws `SerializationException("A document number is not a valid number")` without the value. `equals`/`hashCode` are `Document.equals`/`Document.hash`. `unwrapLegacy` of a view returns the original Legacy_Document |
| Schema → legacy (`unwrapLegacy`) | Java `null` → `Document.fromNull()`. Scalars → the legacy scalar documents; numbers through the factory of the value's Java type (`fromNumber(int/long/float/double/BigInteger/BigDecimal)`; byte and short as int); blobs → standard base64 with padding; timestamps → epoch seconds with at most 3 fraction digits (Requirement 21.3). Lists, maps, structures and unions → the SDK's own `ListDocument`/`MapDocument` (through `Document.fromList`/`fromMap`) over lazy `AbstractList`/`AbstractMap` views, so `equals`, `hashCode` and `toString` are the legacy implementations (`MapDocument.equals` requires a `MapDocument`; the inner map comparison works against any `Map`). The lazy collections are `Serializable` through `writeReplace` to an `ArrayList`/`LinkedHashMap` copy, so Java serialization of a model with a document member still works |

Converting the same element twice creates two equal objects; no conversion result is cached. `wrapLegacy(unwrapLegacy(d))` of a map or list is a view over a view (no copy), not `d` itself. `DocumentConverter` (task 11.13) can be built on these two methods.

**Compared with the legacy JSON marshallers** (`DocumentTypeJsonMarshaller`, `JsonUnmarshallingParser.parseDocumentValue`, `DocumentUnmarshaller`):

| Behavior | Legacy SDK | Schema path |
|---|---|---|
| Number written | `writeNumber(sdkNumber.stringValue())`: the original string, even an invalid one | The codec's spelling of the value: `fromNumber(1.0)` → `1`, `fromNumber("1E+3")` → `1000`, `fromNumber("1.50")` → `1.5`, `1.00000000000000000001` → `1.00000000000000000001` (bigDecimal, plain notation). Values are equal; an invalid number string fails the request instead of sending invalid JSON |
| Number read | `Document.fromNumber(parser.getText())`: the literal text | `SdkNumber` of the read value: integers as `fromInteger`/`fromLong`/`fromBigInteger`, other numbers as `fromDouble` or, when a double does not hold the value, `fromBigDecimal`. So `stringValue()` is the Java spelling (`1e3` → `1000.0`, `1.50` → `1.5`), and `SdkNumber.equals` (which compares `stringValue()`) differs from the legacy result for such literals. The value is exact |
| `null` member | `Document.fromNull()` | Same: `SmithyJsonDeserializer.readStruct` passes a `null` value of a `DOCUMENT` member to the consumer (other members stay absent, as in smithy-java); `unwrapLegacy(null)` is `fromNull()`. `NullDocumentProtocolTest` passes on the new path for awsJson1_1 and restJson1 |
| `null` element or value in a document list/map, or in a list or map of documents | `Document.fromNull()` | Same |
| `NullDocument` member written | `"doc": null` | Same (`writeDocument(schema, null)` writes `null`) |
| Empty map and list | `{}` and `[]`, read back as empty `MapDocument`/`ListDocument` | Same |
| Model `toString()` | `ToString` with `Document.toString()` | Same: classes that reach a `document` shape (Smithy `Walker` over members, lists, maps and structures) keep the previous `toString` instead of `ToStringSerializer`, which would print the schema value of a number (`1.5` for `1.50`) |

**JSON codec (`SmithyJsonDeserializer`), compared with smithy-java.**

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `readStruct` skips any member whose value is `null` | Skips it unless the member targets a document | Legacy disposition `json-core-output.json#35` (`{"documentValue": null}` reads as `Document.fromNull()`). smithy-java `readStruct` is identical to the 7.1 port here. The check runs only on the `null` path |
| `readDocument` reads every number with a fraction or exponent as a double | A double when the double holds the value (at most 15 significant digits in the normal range, else `BigDecimal.valueOf(d)` compared with the literal); otherwise a big decimal of the literal, so `1.00000000000000000001`, `1e400` and `1e-400` are not rounded, made infinite or zero. A literal whose exponent does not fit a `BigDecimal` keeps the double, as before | Requirement 18.5 ("without loss of precision") and the legacy SDK, which kept the literal. Only literals with more than 15 significant digits, zero, subnormals or huge exponents take the `BigDecimal` path |

The CBOR codec is unchanged: rpcv2Cbor defines no document support (`rpcv2Cbor.NoDocuments`), and its `readStruct` still skips a `null` document member before the member lookup (moving the check would add a lookup for every `null`). CBOR numbers are binary, so the number-spelling rows above do not apply to it.

**Generated code.** CloudWatch (the only Prototype_Service_Set service generated on the schema path) has no document shapes, so its generated code is unchanged; DynamoDB is not generated on the schema path yet. Non-schema services do not run this code. In `test/smithy-protocol-tests`: `RestJson` `DocumentType`, `DocumentTypeAsMapValue`, `DocumentTypeAsPayload` and `OperationWithDefaults`, `SdkRestJson` and `SdkJsonProtocol` `SdkDocuments`, and the rpcv2Cbor `OperationWithDefaults` now route to the new path (`RestJson` 98 of 106 operations new, `SdkRestJson` 10 of 10, `SdkJsonProtocol` 5 of 5, `RpcV2Protocol` 13 of 13). The remaining legacy `RestJson` operations are `JsonMaps`, `JsonUnions`, `SparseJsonMaps` (task 3.7), the three streaming operations (7.19) and the two server-only `Malformed*WithBody` operations.

**Tests.** `sdk-core` `LegacyDocumentViewsTest` (27 runs): null and null documents both ways; scalars; 13 numbers with their 18.5 type and value, the original Legacy_Document back from `unwrapLegacy`, and the `SdkNumber` string in `toString`; Java narrowing coercion; an invalid number; a nested tree (map, list, nested map, bigDecimal, null value and null element) equal to the schema document of the same values both ways with equal hash codes, `size`/`getMember`/`getMemberNames`; the reverse tree equal to the legacy tree both ways with equal `hashCode`, `toString` and `unwrap()`; empty map and list; numbers by value type; blob and timestamp; laziness (a counting legacy number read only on access; keys added to the backing map after wrapping are visible in both directions); `wrap` then `unwrap` identity; Java serialization of an unwrapped tree. `json-codec` `JsonCodecTest`: 9 non-integral literals typed as double or bigDecimal with exact values, and a `null` document member passed to the consumer while a `null` string member is not. `codegen` `SchemaSerdeDocumentMemberTest` (4): the classes with a document member, a list of documents and a map of documents get schema serde members and the operation routes to the new path; golden files `schemaserde/putdocrequest.java` and `schemaserde/docssharedserde.java`; the compiled model package writes the members through `writeDocument`/`writeList`/`writeMap`, round-trips through `ShapeTranscoder` to an equal instance with an equal document, and keeps the legacy `toString` (`Doc={"n": 1.50,"l": [null]}`).

**Protocol tests, checked outside the suite (for task 7.21).** `-DprotocolTests.enabledProtocols=aws.protocols#awsJson1_1,aws.protocols#restJson1`: awsJson1_1 **125 passed, 3 failed** (unchanged; the failures are not document cases), restJson1 **257 passed, 9 failed, 1 skipped** (was 253 passed, 13 failed: `DocumentTypeAsPayloadInput`, `DocumentTypeAsPayloadInputString`, `DocumentTypeAsPayloadOutput` and `DocumentTypeAsPayloadOutputString` now pass on the new path). The remaining restJson1 failures are the task 7.13 list without the four document rows. `NullDocumentProtocolTest` 2 of 2 on the new path. awsJson1_0 63 passed, 6 failed (unchanged). The default run stays at rpcv2Cbor 74 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped.

**For tasks 7.18, 7.21, 11.5 and 11.13.** 7.18 (Property 39): across the wire, numbers keep their value but not their `SdkNumber` string unless it is the Java spelling of the read value (`fromInteger`, `fromLong`, `fromBigInteger`, non-integral `fromDouble`/`fromFloat`, `fromBigDecimal` of a non-integral value in plain notation). A generator of arbitrary `SdkNumber` strings will fail `Legacy_Document.equals` (string-based) under every protocol; CBOR cannot keep spellings at all. Either constrain the generator to such numbers or compare numbers by value; Requirement 21.7 as written needs a decision. 7.21: enable `NullDocumentProtocolTest`'s JSON cases; nothing else document-specific is open. 11.5 (`JsonDocuments`/`CborDocuments`): keep the null-document member rule and the exact non-integral numbers; a settings-aware JSON document may keep the literal text, which would let `unwrapLegacy` return `SdkNumber.fromString(literal)` and match the legacy read exactly. Structure documents written through `wrapLegacy` never occur (Legacy_Documents have no structures). 11.13 (`DocumentConverter`): `toSchemaDocument` and `toLegacy` can delegate to `wrapLegacy`/`unwrapLegacy`, adding the `NullPointerException("document")` checks; `toSchemaDocument(NullDocument)` must return a null-valued document rather than Java `null` if the design's `Documents.NULL` is kept.

## Streaming blob members on the new path (task 7.19)
SDK addition (smithy-java model classes hold a `DataStream` member; SDK model classes do not hold the stream: the client method takes a `RequestBody`/`AsyncRequestBody` or hands the body to a `ResponseTransformer`/`AsyncResponseTransformer`). Requirements 14.1–14.3.
**Streaming input (14.1).** `sdk-core` `StreamingRequestBodies` (package-private, called by `ProtocolMarshalling.marshall` right after `serializeRequest`, inside the measured marshalling) binds the call's body from the interceptor context, where the aws-core context factory puts `params.getRequestBody()`/`getAsyncRequestBody()`; the sdk-core test factory (`BaseClientHandler.invokeInterceptorsAndCreateExecutionContext`) now puts them there too. Only an input whose `ApiOperation.inputStreamMember()` is a blob is bound (event streams are task 7.20). Nothing is read: the sync body's `ContentStreamProvider` becomes the request content; the async body stays on the interceptor context, is sent by the transport from `TransportExecutionAttribute.ASYNC_REQUEST_BODY`, and is restored before each retry, as before. The binding is protocol-independent, as the legacy `StreamingRequestMarshaller` wraps any generated marshaller; `HttpBindingClientProtocol` writes no body or `Content-Type` for the streaming payload (task 7.8). The headers are those of `AbstractStreamingRequestMarshaller.addHeaders`, with the build-time flags read from the schemas:
| Header | Legacy (generated flags) | New path |
|---|---|---|
| `Content-Type` | A header member's value, else `RequestBody.contentType()` (`application/octet-stream` for bytes and streams, the probed type for a file) | Same, with the member's `@mediaType` between the two. Deviation: legacy marshallers ignore `@mediaType` (no C2J model carries it), so `RestJsonStreamingTraitsWithMediaTypeWithBlob` failed on the legacy path; it passes now. A body from `RequestBody.fromFile` keeps its probed type when the member has no `@mediaType` |
| `Content-Length` | A header member's value, else the body's length | Same |
| No length, `requiresLength(true)` | `SdkClientException("This API requires Content-Length header to be set. Please set the content length on the RequestBody.")` | Same message, from the target's `@requiresLength` |
| No length, `transferEncoding(true)` (`v4-unsigned-body` or `unsignedPayload`), not `useHttp2` | `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` | Same, from the operation's `@unsignedPayload` and, for HTTP/2, a protocol trait that lists `h2` in `http` or `eventStreamHttp` (the codegen's `supportsH2`) |
Checksums, compression and signing are not re-implemented: the request the shared stages (`RequestMutationStages`, `RequestSigning`) receive has the same body provider and headers as a legacy marshaller's, so `HttpChecksumStage`, `CompressRequestStage`, the `x-amz-content-sha256`/payload-signing decision of the auth scheme options (`PAYLOAD_SIGNING_ENABLED` from the generated resolver) and the signer run unchanged. This was checked by reading the shared code paths, not with a signing test.
**Streaming output, sync (14.2).** Already in place since task 3.26: `ProtocolResponseHandling.handleStreaming` deserializes the header, prefix-header and status members (the protocol leaves the body unread), runs `afterUnmarshalling`/`modifyResponse`, and calls `ResponseTransformer.transform(output, AbortableInputStream)` as `HttpResponseHandlerAdapter` does. Stream ownership and retries are the legacy ones: the body is closed afterwards unless the transform succeeded and `needsConnectionLeftOpen()` (`toInputStream`) is true; a failure always closes it. A `RetryableException` from the transformer is retried; any other exception becomes a `NonRetryableException` (reported as `SdkClientException("Unable to unmarshall response (...)")`), so a transformer that started reading is not retried. Added tests only.
**Streaming output, async (14.2).** `AsyncApiCallPipeline.execute(..., AsyncResponseTransformer)` replaces the `UnsupportedOperationException`. Per call and attempt:
| Step | Legacy (`BaseAsyncClientHandler`, `AsyncStreamingResponseHandler`, `CombinedResponseAsyncHttpResponseHandler`) | New path |
|---|---|---|
| `prepare()` | Once before `beforeExecution` (issue 1268), again when `EXECUTION_ATTEMPT` changes (`IdempotentAsyncResponseHandler`) | Same (`StreamingOutput.prepare(attempt)`), the per-attempt call just before the request is sent |
| Headers | `afterTransmission`, `modifyHttpResponse`, `beforeUnmarshalling` on the HTTP client thread; success decided on the modified response | Same, in `AsyncResponseHandoff.onHeaders` on the transport thread |
| Success | Response handler on the headers (`afterUnmarshalling`, `modifyResponse`), `onResponse(output)`, then `modifyAsyncHttpResponseContent` and `onStream(publisher)` | Same; `onHeaders` returns `true`, so the SDK transport hands the body off unbuffered. The attempt ends when the transformer's future completes, then the response metrics and `afterExecution` |
| Error status | Buffered by the error handler after `modifyAsyncHttpResponseContent` | Same: `onHeaders` returns `false`, the transport buffers, and the response goes through `modifyAsyncHttpResponseContent` and `ProtocolResponseHandling.handle` (header hooks not run twice). The transformer never sees the error body (14.3) |
| Failure in the header members or their hooks | `exceptionOccurred(e)`, then the body still goes through `modifyAsyncHttpResponseContent` to `onStream` | The body goes through `modifyAsyncHttpResponseContent` and is cancelled unread; the attempt fails with `e` (no `exceptionOccurred` here: the retry or the generated `whenComplete` reports it) |
| Retry | `AsyncRetryableStage` calls `exceptionOccurred(lastException)` before each retry | Same |
| Attempt timeout | Covers the whole stream (the attempt future is the transformer's) | Same |
| Cancelling the call or ending the attempt while streaming | Fails the HTTP client's future, which aborts the request | Same through `TransportExecutionAttribute.ABORTABLE_LISTENER`: `SdkAsyncHttpClientTransport` now gives it an `Abortable` that fails the HTTP client's future, because after a hand-off its own future is already complete. A custom transport without it keeps streaming to the transformer, which the generated `exceptionOccurred` call ends |
| Transport that ignores the hand-off and buffers a success | Not applicable | The buffered body is streamed to the transformer from memory |
`exceptionOccurred` counts can differ from legacy: legacy also forwards the HTTP client's own `onError` for a transport failure (so a retried connection failure is reported twice), and the new path reports it once, at the retry. The generated client still calls `exceptionOccurred` on the returned future's failure, as before. A synchronous failure before the request is sent (serialization, the request hooks) calls `exceptionOccurred` once, as `BaseAsyncClientHandler.doExecute` does.
**Interceptor hook order.** `ApiCallPipelineInterceptorOrderTest.execute_withAStreamingOutputOnTheNewPath_invokesTheSameHooksAsTheLegacyPipeline` (32 cases: sync and async, both transports, `SUCCESS`, `SERVICE_ERROR`, `RETRY_THEN_SUCCESS`, `CLIENT_ERROR_THEN_SUCCESS`, `RETRIES_EXHAUSTED`, `AFTER_TRANSMISSION_FAILURE`, `MODIFY_RESPONSE_FAILURE`, `AFTER_EXECUTION_FAILURE`) compares hooks, contexts, attribute keys, results and attempts with the legacy pipeline. Hooks and contexts are identical (async: `afterUnmarshalling` and `modifyResponse` before `modifyAsyncHttpResponseContent`, as legacy). The one added difference: async streaming attempts carry `TransportAbortableListener` from `beforeTransmission`'s attempt on. Threads differ from the non-streaming new path (difference 4 of task 3.29): for a handed-off body, the header and output hooks run on the transport's thread, as on legacy; `afterExecution` runs on `FUTURE_COMPLETION_EXECUTOR`.
**Routing (gates removed).** `SchemaOperationRouting.legacyReason` no longer keeps streaming input or output operations on the legacy path when the default protocol is `restJson1` or `restXml`; event streams stay legacy (task 7.20). `BaseAsyncClientHandler.execute(params, asyncResponseTransformer)` sends every call with an operation to the new path, with or without a marshaller (the task 3.28 fallback is removed). `SchemaOperationsProtocolSpec` passes `.withRequestBody(requestBody)`/`.withAsyncRequestBody(requestBody)` and `.withResponseTransformer`/`.withAsyncResponseTransformer` with the transformer as the second argument, as the legacy specs do but without a streaming marshaller, and the async call uses the legacy `streamingOutputWhenComplete` (report to `finalAsyncResponseTransformer`, publish metrics at `endOfStreamFuture`). `SchemaSerdeModel` gives classes with a streaming blob member schema serde members: the member is in the schema and `MemberBinding.isStreaming()`; the class does not write it, `getMemberValue` returns null, `setMemberValue` checks the member and ignores the value, and the builder's consumer reads and discards a value a codec delivers (HTTP binding protocols never do). `SdkFieldsFromSchema` already leaves such members out of `sdkFields()`.
**RPC protocols (decision).** Smithy binds a streaming blob only through `@httpPayload`, and smithy-java's awsJson and rpcv2 protocols stream only event streams. No AWS service models a streaming blob in awsJson, rpcv2Cbor, awsQuery or ec2Query: all 40 streaming blob inputs and outputs in the C2J models under `services/` belong to restJson1 or restXml services. The legacy SDK accepts a C2J `payload` streaming blob in awsJson (checkpoint 6), but nothing uses it. So: codegen keeps such an operation on the legacy path when the default protocol is not an HTTP binding protocol (`legacyReason`: `streaming blob members are bound only by HTTP binding protocols, not <protocol>`, also when only the schema has the member), and `AwsJsonProtocol`, `AbstractRpcV2ClientProtocol` and `QueryClientProtocol` throw `UnsupportedOperationException("Streaming blob input/output is not supported by <id>")` (query: "Streaming input/output", since it has no event streams either), reported as `Unable to marshall/unmarshall <op>: ...`, if a runtime protocol override reaches them.
**Generated code.** Services not generated on the schema path are unchanged: codegen 1018 tests with the client golden files, `codegen-maven-plugin` `LegacyRouteParityTest` 7 of 7 and `NoTransformPackageTest` 8 of 8. CloudWatch has no streaming operations; its generated sources are identical before and after. In `test/smithy-protocol-tests`, `RestJson` `StreamingTraits`, `StreamingTraitsRequireLength` and `StreamingTraitsWithMediaType` now route to the new path (101 of 106 sync calls), and their request and response classes gain schema serde members. The restXml protocol test services have no streaming operations.
**Tests.** `sdk-core`: `StreamingRequestBodiesTest` (7: sync and async bodies, header-member and `@mediaType` content types, `@requiresLength`, chunked unsigned payload with and without HTTP/2, no stream); `SyncApiCallPipelineTest` (+4: streaming error not given to the transformer, retry then stream, transformer failure not retried, streaming input bound without reading it); `AsyncApiCallPipelineTest` (+6 replacing the unsupported case: success with the hand-off and the legacy hook order, error buffered, retry with `exceptionOccurred` and a second `prepare`, transformer failure aborting the request, cancellation while streaming aborting it, attempt timeout while streaming then retry); `ClientHandlerPipelineRoutingTest` (2 replaced: async streaming with and without a marshaller on the new path); `ApiCallPipelineInterceptorOrderTest` (+32, above). Module: 1928 + 651 tests. Protocols: restJson1 and restXml streaming request shapes and error responses of a streaming operation; awsJson (both variants) and rpcv2Cbor rejection. `codegen` `SchemaClientClassTest` (+2: a restJson1 service with streaming input and output, routed with the body and the transformer; an rpcv2Cbor streaming operation kept legacy).
**Protocol tests, checked outside the suite (for task 7.21).** `-DprotocolTests.enabledProtocols=aws.protocols#restJson1,aws.protocols#restXml`: restJson1 **258 passed, 8 failed, 1 skipped** (was 257, 9, 1: `RestJsonStreamingTraitsWithMediaTypeWithBlob` now passes; `RestJsonStreamingTraits*` request and response cases pass on the new path); restXml **196 passed, 1 failed** (unchanged). The remaining failures are the task 7.13/7.17 list without the streaming row. The default run stays at rpcv2Cbor 74 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped.
**For tasks 7.20, 7.21 and 9.2.** 7.20: `inputStreamMember()`/`outputStreamMember()` also return event stream members; `StreamingRequestBodies` binds only blobs. The RPC protocols' new checks run after their event-stream checks; when 7.20 removes those, restrict the new checks to blob members. The async streaming machinery (`StreamingOutput`, `StreamingAttempt`, the hand-off and abort) is where an `EventStreamAsyncResponseTransformer` plugs in; `SchemaSerdeModel` and `legacyReason` still exclude event streams. 7.21: no streaming skips are needed for restJson1 or restXml. 9.2 (DynamoDB, SQS): neither has streaming operations, so nothing here applies; S3 (restXml) streaming customizations in `XmlProtocolSpec` are not reproduced by `SchemaOperationsProtocolSpec`.
## Event stream encoders and decoders (task 7.20)
Ports smithy-java `core` `serde.event` (the abstractions) and `aws/aws-event-streams` (the AWS encoding). Requirements 14.4–14.7. **Status: stages (a) and (b)** (the encoder/decoder port, and the protocols' event stream requests, outputs and factories, with unit tests), plus inbound string-header leniency. Stage (c) is not started. Pipeline and codegen integration are not done; every event-stream operation stays on the legacy path behind the existing gates (`SchemaOperationRouting.legacyReason`: `event streams are not supported by the client protocols yet (task 7.20)`; `SchemaSerdeModel` skips events and event streams), so nothing calls the stage (b) code from a client yet. Generated code is unchanged.
**Deferred (task 7.21).** The user deferred stages (c)–(e) to a follow-up; event-stream operations remain on the legacy path only, and `test/smithy-protocol-tests` runs no `@eventStreamTests` cases.
| smithy-java | SDK | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `core.serde.event`: `Frame`, `FrameEncoder`, `FrameDecoder`, `EventEncoder`, `EventDecoder`, `EventEncoderFactory`, `EventDecoderFactory`, `EventStreamingException`, `EventStreamingProtocolException` | `schema` `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.serde.event` (`@SdkPublicApi`), as the design's type table lists | — |
| `EventDecoder.decode` returns `SerializableStruct` | Returns `Object` | An SDK event stream delivers an unknown event type as the generated visitor's unknown-event value (`EventStream.UNKNOWN`), which is not a `SerializableStruct` (Requirement 14.8) |
| `EventDecoder.decodeInitialEvent(frame, EventStream)` | `decodeInitialEvent(frame)` | SDK request and response classes do not hold the event stream: the client method takes a `Publisher` or a response handler |
| `EventEncoderFactory.withFrameProcessor`, `FrameProcessor`, `ProcessingEventEncoder(Factory)` | Not ported | Outbound frames are signed on the encoded bytes by the SDK's existing event stream signing (`EventStreamV4PayloadSigner`/the async request body), unchanged |
| `EventStream`, `EventStreamReader`/`Writer`, `DefaultEventStreamReader`/`Writer`, `ProtocolEventStreamReader`/`Writer`, `EventPipeStream`, `RpcEventStreamsUtil` | Not ported (yet) | smithy-java's stream types are blocking/`Flow`-based and the SDK's public API is `Publisher` plus `EventStreamResponseHandler`; the pipeline integration (stage c) adapts the encoder and decoder to those directly |
| `AwsFrameEncoder`, `AwsFrameDecoder` | `AwsFrameCodec` (internal): `Message.toByteBuffer()` and `MessageDecoder`, without the decoder's `FrameProcessor` | The same `software.amazon.eventstream` library the legacy SDK uses, so framing is byte-identical |
| `HeadersBuilder` keeps headers in a `HashMap` | Case-insensitive name order (`TreeMap`) | The legacy SDK copies event headers from an `SdkHttpFullRequest` (case-insensitive sorted), so an event with only string headers encodes to the same bytes (`AwsEventCodecTest.encode_eventWithOnlyStringHeaders_isByteIdenticalToTheLegacyEncoding`) |
| `eventHeader` members are typed headers (`fromInteger`, `fromTimestamp`, …) | Same | Follows Smithy's event stream spec. **Deviation from the legacy SDK**, which marshals every header member to an HTTP header string and so sends string-typed headers; typed and legacy string headers decode the same for string members only. To review in stage (b) against the protocol tests |
| An event is a union value; `encode` serializes the set union member | `encode` also takes an SDK event (the member's value) when exactly one non-error union member targets its shape, and `AwsEventEncoder.encodeEvent(eventType, event)` names the member | SDK event stream unions are generated as interfaces, and one event shape can back several members (`DefaultEventOne`, `DefaultSecondEventOne`); the member name comes from the generated `sdkEventType()` |
| Decoded events are wrapped in a union through `ApiOperation.outputEventBuilderSupplier()` | Same when that supplier is not null; otherwise (and after `withEventBuilders`) the member's value is returned, built by the per-member builder or the member target's builder | Same reason; the SDK delivers the per-member event class |
| An unknown `:event-type` fails | Returns `withUnknownEvent`'s value when set | Requirement 14.8 (unknown events go to the visitor's default path) |
| `:message-type: exception` returns the error member's value as a union event | Throws the built modeled exception | Requirement 14.6: it is signaled, not delivered. `error` messages throw `EventStreamingException(errorCode, message)` as smithy-java does; stage (c) maps it to the service's generic exception as legacy does |
| `eventPayload` blob/string members are read through the codec deserializer | Read from the raw payload bytes | The encoder writes them raw (bytes, UTF-8), as the Smithy spec says |
| Empty payloads go to the codec | Not read | An empty JSON or CBOR body is not a structure; header-only events have no body |
| `ModeledException` with `schema()` | A `Throwable` that is also a `SerializableStruct` (`sdkSchema()`) | SDK modeled exceptions on the schema path |
**Tests.** `http-client-protocol` `AwsEventCodecTest` (16 unit tests and 1 jqwik property, 50 tries): typed headers and codec payloads; legacy string headers for non-string members, and an unparsable one; header-only events with and without `emitEmptyPayload`; ambiguous events rejected; blob, string and structure `eventPayload`; byte-identical encoding to `AwsClientHandlerUtils.encodeEventStreamRequestToByteBuffer` (the legacy path); decoding a legacy-encoded frame; frames split across buffers decoded in order; per-member builders; unknown events; exception and error messages; initial request and response messages; factories rejecting operations without an event stream. The property's string header values are not empty: the `eventstream` library rejects empty header strings (`IllegalArgumentException: Strings may not be empty`), for the legacy SDK as well.
**Protocols (stage b).** In `http-client-protocol` `HttpClientProtocol` (`@SdkPublicApi`) and its subclasses:

| smithy-java or legacy | SDK | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| smithy-java `HttpBindingClientProtocol.getEventEncoderFactory`/`getEventDecoderFactory` (abstract, per frame type) and `RpcEventStreamsUtil` | `HttpClientProtocol.inputEventEncoderFactory(operation)` (`AwsEventEncoderFactory`), `outputEventDecoderFactory(operation)` (`AwsEventDecoderFactory`) and `sendsInitialEventMessages()`. The defaults throw `UnsupportedOperationException("Event streams are not supported by <protocol>")` and return false (the query protocols keep them). `HttpBindingClientProtocol` (restJson1, restXml) uses its codec and `payloadMediaType()` without `emitEmptyPayload`; `AwsJsonProtocol` (`application/x-amz-json-1.0`/`1.1`) and `AbstractRpcV2ClientProtocol` (`application/cbor`) with `emitEmptyPayload` and `sendsInitialEventMessages() == true`. `RpcEventStreamsUtil` is not ported | One seam on the base class for both protocol families, so the stage (c) pipeline needs no protocol-specific code. The frames are always `AwsEventFrame`s, so `HttpBindingClientProtocol` keeps no frame type parameter |
| An operation has an event stream when `ApiOperation.inputEventBuilderSupplier()`/`outputEventBuilderSupplier()` is not null | `HttpClientProtocol.isEventStream(streamMember)` (the stream member targets a union) and `isStreamingBlob(streamMember)` (targets a blob). The task 7.19 checks of `AwsJsonProtocol` and `AbstractRpcV2ClientProtocol` reject only streaming blobs (same messages) | SDK event stream unions are interfaces with per-member event classes, so stage (d) may leave the union builder supplier null; the decoder factory already handles that |
| Event stream request headers: smithy-java sets `Content-Type: application/vnd.amazon.eventstream` | `putEventStreamHeaders`: `Content-Type: application/vnd.amazon.eventstream` and `Transfer-Encoding: chunked`, no `Content-Length`, for every protocol | As legacy (`JsonProtocolMarshaller` for `hasEventStreamingInput`, then `EventStreamInitialRequestInterceptor.modifyHttpRequest` for RPC). `rpcv2Cbor` keeps `Accept: application/cbor`, as legacy |
| restJson1/restXml event stream input | `RequestSerializer` skips the `@streaming` `@httpPayload` member, so the other members are bound to the request line and headers and the request has no content; the pipeline sends the encoded events as the body | Smithy: the event stream is the payload |
| RPC `initial-request`: legacy marshals the input as a JSON/CBOR body, and `EventStreamInitialRequestInterceptor.modifyAsyncHttpContent` turns that body into an `initial-request` frame (`:message-type`, `:event-type`, `:content-type` from the request's `Content-Type`) prepended to the events | The protocol's request content **is** the encoded `initial-request` frame: the input without the event stream member, through the codec, `{}`/an empty CBOR map when no member is set, with `:content-type` the protocol media type (`initialRequestContent`). The pipeline sends it, then the events | The protocol owns the framing (smithy-java: `RpcEventStreamsUtil.bodyForEventStreaming`). **Interceptor-visible difference:** `afterMarshalling`/`modifyHttpRequest` see the frame and the event stream `Content-Type` (legacy: the JSON/CBOR body and its media type). Header order follows `HeadersBuilder` (case-insensitive); byte identity of the initial frame with legacy is not checked yet |
| RPC `initial-response` | `deserializeOutput` of an event stream output reads no body and returns the error-corrected empty output with the HTTP response; the pipeline decodes `initial-response` with `outputEventDecoderFactory(op).newEventDecoder().decodeInitialEvent(frame)` | As legacy, whose HTTP response handler reads nothing and whose `EventStreamAsyncResponseTransformer` unmarshalls `initial-response` from the stream |
| restJson1/restXml event stream output | Header-bound output members are read from the HTTP response; the body is left unread (`hasStreamingPayload`) | Same as streaming blobs (task 7.19) |

**Header typing (decided: typed).** `eventHeader` members are sent as typed headers (`AwsEventShapeEncoder`, stage a). The Smithy event stream spec and the restJson1 `@eventStreamTests` in `smithy-aws-protocol-tests` 1.73.0 (`META-INF/smithy/restJson1/event-stream.smithy`, for example `BooleanHeaderInput`: `booleanHeader: { boolean: true }` with the exact frame bytes) require typed values; legacy sends every header member as a string, which only matches for `string` members. Inbound, legacy reads every header with `HeaderValue.getString()`, which throws `IllegalStateException` for non-string values (`eventstream` 1.0.1), so services that work with the legacy SDK send string-typed headers. The decoder reads typed headers as their type and also accepts a `string` header for a non-string member, parsed as the legacy header unmarshallers parse an HTTP header string (`Integer.parseInt` and friends, `Boolean.parseBoolean`, the member's `timestampFormat` or `http-date`, base64 blobs), so events that legacy decodes today still decode. A value that does not parse throws `SerializationException("Invalid <type> in event header for member <id>")` without the value. The `eventstream` library's `HeaderType` is package-private, so a string header is recognized by its `HeaderValue` class. `test/smithy-protocol-tests` does not run `@eventStreamTests` cases.

**Tests (stage b).** `http-client-protocol`: `HttpClientProtocolTest.eventStreamFactories_byDefault_areUnsupported`; `HttpBindingClientProtocolTest` (`POST /chat` with an `X-Tag` header and an event stream `@httpPayload`: headers bound, event stream headers, no content, no initial messages; output header read and body left unread). `aws-json-client-protocol` `AwsJsonProtocolTest` (both variants) and `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` `RpcV2CborProtocolTest`: the request content equals the encoder's `initial-request` frame and holds the media type and the member; event stream headers; an event stream output with a non-JSON/CBOR body is not read. The streaming-blob rejection tests are unchanged.

**Remaining (stages c–e).**
- RPC `initial-request` header order (checked, not changed yet): legacy `EventStreamInitialRequestInterceptor` builds the frame headers in a `HashMap`, which encodes them as `:event-type`, `:content-type`, `:message-type` (buckets 11, 11, 13 of 16); `HeadersBuilder` encodes `:content-type`, `:event-type`, `:message-type`. Payload and values are equal; only the header order, and so the bytes and the payload signature input, differ. The Smithy `@eventStreamTests` cover restJson1 only, so matching legacy is allowed: encode the `initial-request` frame's headers in legacy order (a `LinkedHashMap` in that order) in `AwsEventShapeEncoder` and add a byte-identity test against the interceptor's `modifyAsyncHttpContent`.
- (c) Pipeline: async event-stream output through the task 7.19 hand-off (`StreamingOutput`/`StreamingAttempt`), driving the generated `EventStreamResponseHandler` as `EventStreamAsyncResponseTransformer` does (`responseReceived` before events, `onEventStream`, `exceptionOccurred` once, `complete`, request IDs) with the decoder in place of `HttpResponseHandler` adapters (the legacy adapter reads every header with `getString()`, which typed headers do not support); error frames mapped to the service exception with `AwsErrorDetails`; event-stream input as an `AsyncRequestBody` built from the encoded `Publisher`, signed by the existing event stream signing, with hook-order tests against legacy.
- (d) Codegen: schema serde members for event classes and the per-member `Default<Event>` classes (`SchemaSerdeModel.candidate` skips `isEvent()`/`isEventStream()`), the event builder and unknown-event wiring in `<Svc>Operations`, then remove the gate in `SchemaOperationRouting.legacyReason`; keep the generated public API (client methods, `<Op>ResponseHandler`, visitors, event classes) source compatible and check japicmp. None of the prototype services (CloudWatch, SQS, DynamoDB, Secrets Manager, Account, Route 53) has event-stream operations.
- (e) Ad hoc protocol-test runs (restJson1, awsJson1_0/1_1, rpcv2Cbor event-stream cases) and docs.
## `codegen` runtime protocol selection (task 3.23)

SDK addition (smithy-java clients take a protocol from `ClientConfig`, not from generated builders). In `codegen`: `ProtocolUtils.CLIENT_PROTOCOL_PRIORITY` and `ProtocolUtils.resolveDefaultClientProtocol`, `poet.schema.ClientProtocolSelection`, and the generated members below in `BaseClientBuilderInterface`/`BaseClientBuilderClass`. In `core/aws-core` (`software.amazon.awssdk.awscore.client.builder`, `@SdkProtectedApi`): `ClientProtocolSupport`, which the generated builders call; `aws-core` now declares `schema` (it reads the service schema's protocol traits).

| Design (A.11) | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `<Svc>BaseClientBuilder.protocol(ClientProtocol<?, ?>)` default method throwing `UnsupportedOperationException`; `Default<Svc>BaseClientBuilder` stores it as `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` | As designed. The base class method has no `@Override`: the abstract base class does not implement the builder interface, and the sync/async builders inherit it as the implementation of the default method | Requirements 12.1, 16.3. `protocol(null)` leaves the option null, which `lazyOptionIfAbsent` treats as absent |
| One `.option(SdkClientOption.CLIENT_PROTOCOL_FACTORY, <Factory>.INSTANCE)` line in the base builder | In `mergeServiceDefaults`, so a plugin can still replace the factory. `finalizeServiceConfiguration` adds `lazyOptionIfAbsent(CLIENT_PROTOCOL, c -> ClientProtocolSupport.createProtocol(c.get(CLIENT_PROTOCOL_FACTORY), protocolSettings()))`; a generated `private static ProtocolSettings protocolSettings()` sets `serviceSchema(<Svc>ServiceSchemas.SERVICE)`, `serviceVersion(<api version>)` and `baseExceptionBuilder(<Svc>Exception::builder)` | **After the client is built, `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` holds the effective protocol** (the override, or the default created once per client), so the new pipeline (tasks 3.25–3.28) reads one option and never calls a factory per call. The `SdkClientOption.CLIENT_PROTOCOL` javadoc ("null means no override") describes the builder input; `sdk-core` was not editable in this task |
| Trait from `SERVICE_SCHEMA.getTrait(TraitKey.get(Rpcv2CborTrait.class))` | `createProtocol` passes the service schema trait whose `traitId()` equals `factory.id()`, or null if the service schema does not carry it (no "trait built from defaults") | Generic over the factory in the option, so a plugin-replaced factory gets its own trait. Building default traits generically is not possible; `RpcV2CborProtocol.Factory` accepts null |
| Default protocol priority and the codegen failure | `ProtocolUtils.resolveDefaultClientProtocol` picks by trait ID in the 12.3 order; failure is `IllegalArgumentException("Service <id> declares no supported protocol; found: [<sorted trait ids>]")`. The Smithy `resolveProtocol(ServiceIndex, ServiceShape)` that `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilder` calls now delegates to it, so the error is raised while building the IntermediateModel, before any file is written (Requirement 12.4) | The trait order maps one-to-one onto the existing protocol-string order, so the legacy protocol string for every model is unchanged (`ProtocolUtilsTest` cases kept) |
| Every schema-enabled service gets protocol selection | **Phase 2: only services whose default protocol has a `ClientProtocolFactory` class** (`ClientProtocolSelection.FACTORIES`, today `smithy.protocols#rpcv2Cbor` → `RpcV2CborProtocol.Factory`). Of the prototype services only CloudWatch (rpcv2Cbor, awsJson1_0, awsQuery → rpcv2Cbor) qualifies; SQS, DynamoDB, Secrets Manager, Account, Route 53 and SNS keep their legacy builders, with no `protocol(...)` method. The no-supported-protocol failure applies to every Smithy-built service | The other protocol modules are skeletons until phase 7, so referencing their factories would not compile, and a `protocol(...)` setting on a client whose calls still run on legacy marshallers would be silently ignored (violating 12.2). Each 7.x protocol task adds its factory to `FACTORIES`, which gives those services the method and the factory line. `services/cloudwatch` now depends on `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` |
| `build()` throws `IllegalStateException` when `protocol.messageExchange()` differs from the transport's | Generated `validateClientOptions` (called by both `buildClient` methods, after plugins) calls `ClientProtocolSupport.validateMessageExchange(config)`: the transport is `CLIENT_TRANSPORT` (sync) or `ASYNC_CLIENT_TRANSPORT` (async, by `CLIENT_TYPE`); **when unset, it is the SDK HTTP client transport, whose exchange is `HttpMessageExchange.INSTANCE`**. Message: `The client protocol and the client transport exchange different message types. Protocol <protocol> exchanges <exchange>; transport <transport or "(the SDK HTTP client transport)"> exchanges <exchange>.` Exchanges are named by `toString()` (`HttpMessageExchange` names `SdkHttpFullRequest`/`SdkHttpFullResponse`; a third-party exchange its own text) | Requirements 12.5, 13.3 |
| Builders create the default transport from `SYNC_HTTP_CLIENT`/`ASYNC_HTTP_CLIENT` | **Not created by the builder.** Generated service code may not reference `SdkHttpClientTransport`/`SdkAsyncHttpClientTransport` (`@SdkInternalApi` in `sdk-core`; `InternalApiBoundaryTest` forbids cross-module internal use, and the allowlist must not grow), so the default stays "unset" and the pipeline wraps the configuration's HTTP client (tasks 3.26–3.28, "default `SdkHttpClientTransport`"). That code must use `create(httpClient, closeHttpClient=false)`: the configuration already closes `SYNC_HTTP_CLIENT`/`ASYNC_HTTP_CLIENT`. A transport stored in `CLIENT_TRANSPORT` is closed with the configuration (it is `SdkAutoCloseable`) | Keeps the internal adapters internal; the build-time check needs only the default's exchange |
| — | Users cannot yet build a `ClientProtocol` for CloudWatch with the right `ProtocolSettings` without the internal `CloudWatchServiceSchemas.SERVICE` (or `ProtocolSettings.builder().service(id)` without the schema, which loses awsQueryCompatible) | Open for Requirement 12.6/12.7 (tasks 7.x): a public way to obtain the service's settings or to override with a factory |

**Tests.** `codegen`: `ProtocolUtilsTest` (priority for 7 trait combinations including CloudWatch's; exact failure message for an unsupported protocol definition and for no protocol, from both entry points), `poet/builder/SchemaClientBuilderTest` (golden files `test-schema-client-builder-interface.java` and `test-schema-client-builder-class.java` for a Smithy service with rpcv2Cbor, awsJson1_0 and awsQuery; the selection's factory, base exception, service schema and version; an awsJson1_0 default gets no protocol method or `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` reference). Golden files of C2J-built services are unchanged. `aws-core`: `ClientProtocolSupportTest` (trait passed by factory ID, null without it or without a schema, null protocol rejected; no protocol, HTTP protocol with default transport sync and async, mismatch naming both type pairs, transport chosen by client type, configured mismatching transport named). `services/cloudwatch`: `CloudWatchProtocolSelectionTest` (sync and async default `RpcV2CborProtocol` for `com.amazonaws.cloudwatch#GraniteServiceVersion20100801` with `Factory.INSTANCE`; override used; `protocol(null)` resets to the default; a non-HTTP protocol fails sync and async builds with both exchanges in the message). `codegen-maven-plugin`: `legacy-route-parity/cloudwatch.txt` lists the protocol selection members of `CloudWatchBaseClientBuilder` and `DefaultCloudWatchBaseClientBuilder` as the only differences from the legacy C2J route, which has no Smithy model and so no protocol selection.

## `core/sdk-core` new-path protocol steps (task 3.24)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.core.internal.http` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `ProtocolMarshalling` and `ProtocolResponseHandling`, stateless classes with static methods (no per-call or per-client stage objects). They are SDK additions with no smithy-java source; the legacy counterparts are `BaseClientHandler.finalizeSdkHttpFullRequest` (marshalling part), `CombinedResponseHandler`, `BaseClientHandler.decorateResponseHandlers` and `BaseSyncClientHandler.HttpResponseHandlerAdapter`, which are not modified. Nothing calls the new classes yet (tasks 3.25–3.28).

| Design (D.6) or legacy behavior | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `ProtocolMarshalling.marshall(params)` | `marshall(ExecutionContext)`. The operation is `SdkExecutionAttribute.API_OPERATION`, the protocol `CLIENT_PROTOCOL`, the input the interceptor context's request (after `modifyRequest`, as in the legacy handler) | `ClientExecutionParams.withOperation` is task 3.28. Reading the operation from the attribute guarantees the protocol serializes the instance interceptors see (Requirement 30.3); the execution context also carries the interceptor chain and the metric collector. Task 7.19 may add the params when it binds streaming input bodies |
| `serializeRequest(op, input, attrs, UNRESOLVED_URI)` (constant not defined in the design) | `ProtocolMarshalling.UNRESOLVED_URI = https://unresolved.invalid` (reserved `.invalid` TLD, RFC 6761) | A request that skips the endpoint step cannot reach a real host. **Interceptor-visible difference:** legacy marshallers address the request to the client endpoint, so `afterMarshalling`/`modifyHttpRequest` saw the client endpoint host; on the new path they see `unresolved.invalid` until the endpoint step (task 3.25) applies `updateServiceEndpoint`. Task 3.25/3.29 decides whether the endpoint step must also run before these hooks |
| `marshall` = serialize + log | `marshall` runs `beforeMarshalling` → `serializeRequest` (timed as `MARSHALLING_DURATION`, inline `System.nanoTime`, no `Pair`) → request and `RequestBody` (limited to `Content-Length`, unbuffered, as `addHttpRequest`) set on the interceptor context → `afterMarshalling`. `modifyHttpRequest`/`modifyHttpContent`, the `HTTP_REQUEST_URI_BEFORE_MODIFY` snapshot, host prefix and the request-body re-attach stay in the pipeline (tasks 3.25, 3.26) | Keeps the marshalling hooks with the serialization they bracket, mirroring `finalizeSdkHttpFullRequest`; the new path applies the endpoint and host prefix after serialization. The pipeline must not invoke `beforeMarshalling`/`afterMarshalling` itself |
| Log "after protocol selection" | Logged right after the protocol is read from the attributes, before `beforeMarshalling`, so the entry is written even when serialization fails; through `SdkStandardLogger.REQUEST_LOGGER` (`software.amazon.awssdk.request`). Text: `Using protocol <protocol ShapeId> for operation <operation ShapeId>` | Requirements 40.4, 40.5; only the two shape IDs are formatted |
| A non-schema request | `SdkClientException("Unable to marshall <op>: the request <class> is not a schema-based request")`; a missing `API_OPERATION`/`CLIENT_PROTOCOL` or a protocol whose exchange is not `HttpMessageExchange.INSTANCE` is `IllegalStateException` | Client builders already enforce the exchange (task 3.23); the check keeps the cast safe |
| `ProtocolResponseHandling.handle(op, attrs, errorRegistry, request, response)` | `handle(ExecutionContext, op, errorRegistry, request, response)`; the attributes are `executionContext.executionAttributes()` | `afterUnmarshalling`/`modifyResponse` need the interceptor chain and context, which `ExecutionAttributes` does not carry |
| "Sets `sdkHttpResponse` on the builder" | The protocol sets it on the builder (task 3.21 `buildOutput`).
