After `modifyResponse`, the output is copied through `toBuilder()` only if its `sdkHttpResponse` is not the response passed in (a third-party protocol that does not set it, or an interceptor that replaced the output) | Legacy `attachHttpResponseToResult` copies every response after `modifyResponse`; the identity check gives the same final output with no copy in the common case (racecar E4 superseded). **Interceptor-visible difference:** `afterUnmarshalling`/`modifyResponse` now see the output with `sdkHttpResponse` set (legacy: null until after `modifyResponse`) |
| `SdkServiceException` → `Response.failure` | `Response` built with `isSuccess(false)`, the exception and the HTTP response (there is no `Response.failure` factory); interceptors do not run, as in the legacy error handler | `RetryableStageHelper` reads `Response.exception()` the same way |
| Other failures | Protocol: other `SdkException`s (the protocol's `"Unable to unmarshall <op>: <cause>"`) propagate unchanged; a non-SDK runtime exception becomes `SdkClientException("Unable to unmarshall [error ]response (<msg>). Response Code: <n>, Response Text: <t>", cause)`. Interceptors and transformer: classified exactly as `CombinedResponseHandler.handleSuccessResponse` (retryable SDK exceptions, `IOException`, `InterruptedException` rethrown; anything else wrapped with the legacy message) | Legacy wraps unmarshaller failures a second time; the protocol's message already names the operation and the cause, so it is not wrapped again. Interceptor/transformer failures keep the Baseline_Build type and message (Requirement 37.8) |
| `logRequestId`, `UNMARSHALLING_DURATION`, body close | Same as legacy: `SdkStandardLogger.logRequestId` per response; `UNMARSHALLING_DURATION` on the `API_CALL_ATTEMPT_METRIC_COLLECTOR` when deserialization returns an output (legacy times only the success handler); the body is closed after handling, except when a streaming transformer `needsConnectionLeftOpen()` | Unchanged observable behavior |
| "Streaming outputs hand off to the transformer" | Sync: `handleStreaming(..., ResponseTransformer)` runs the interceptors and then `transformer.transform(output, body)` with the legacy `HttpResponseHandlerAdapter` interrupt checks and `NonRetryableException` wrapping; an error response is a failure and the transformer is not called (Requirement 14.3). Async (`AsyncResponseTransformer` through `AsyncResponseHandoff`) is deferred to task 7.19 | The pipeline-side sync hand-off is generic; reading only bound headers for a streaming output is protocol work in 7.19, and the async hand-off needs the 3.27 async response handler |

**Tests** (`core/sdk-core/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/core/internal/http/`, with the stub protocol, operation, shapes and recording interceptor in `ProtocolStepsTestSupport`): `ProtocolMarshallingTest` (operation, input, attributes and `UNRESOLVED_URI` passed to `serializeRequest`; the `modifyRequest` result is serialized; hook order `beforeMarshalling` → serialize → `afterMarshalling` with the request and a length-limited `RequestBody` in the context; no body; one DEBUG entry with the exact text on `software.amazon.awssdk.request` and no member value, once per call, none at INFO; `MARSHALLING_DURATION`; a serialization failure propagates, is still logged and skips `afterMarshalling`; non-schema request; missing attributes; non-HTTP exchange), `ProtocolResponseHandlingTest` (success without a copy and with `sdkHttpResponse`; copy when the protocol does not set it; deserialize arguments; `UNMARSHALLING_DURATION`; `SdkServiceException` → failed `Response` without interceptors; client exception propagation; non-SDK exception wrapping for success and error responses; `afterUnmarshalling` then `modifyResponse` with a replacement output; interceptor failure wrapping and retryable pass-through; streaming hand-off, connection left open, error response not streamed, transformer failure; body closing) and `ProtocolStepsNoGeneratedSerdeTest` (the class files of both classes and their nested classes name no `core.runtime.transform`, `HttpResponseHandler`, `protocols` or `*Marshaller`/`*Unmarshaller` type, Requirement 37.2).

## `core/sdk-core` new-path shared stages and per-client caches (task 3.25)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.core.internal.http` (`@SdkInternalApi`), shared unchanged by the sync and async pipelines (tasks 3.26, 3.27): `RequestMutationStages` (per client configuration and client type), `EndpointResolution` (static), `AuthSchemeResolutionCache`, `UserAgentHeaderCache` and `RequestSigning` (per client), `RetryDecisions` (per call) and `ExecutionAttributesTemplate` (per client). SDK additions modeled on racecar concepts (OD-16); no racecar code. The legacy stages and `RequestPipelineBuilder` graph are not modified (OD-20); `CompressRequestStage`, `HttpChecksumStage`, `SigningStage`/`AsyncSigningStage` (pre-SRA signers, async bodies, unresolved identities) and `RetryableStageHelper` are called unchanged. Nothing calls the new classes yet (tasks 3.26–3.28).

| Design (D.6) or legacy behavior | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| 3.24 open question: `afterMarshalling`/`modifyHttpRequest` saw `unresolved.invalid` | **Decided: address before `afterMarshalling`.** `RequestMutationStages.marshall` passes an addressing step to the new overload `ProtocolMarshalling.marshall(ExecutionContext, UnaryOperator)`, which runs it between `serializeRequest(…, UNRESOLVED_URI)` and `afterMarshalling`: `protocol.updateServiceEndpoint(request, clientEndpoint)` (the configuration's `CLIENT_ENDPOINT_PROVIDER`, as legacy marshallers use, as an `Endpoint` built once per endpoint URI), then the discovered endpoint's host and port (setting `SKIP_ENDPOINT_RESOLUTION`/`IS_DISCOVERED_ENDPOINT`) or the host prefix unless `DISABLE_HOST_PREFIX_INJECTION`, exactly as `BaseClientHandler.modifyEndpointHostIfNeeded`. Without a client endpoint provider the request keeps the `UNRESOLVED_URI` host, and the discovered endpoint or host prefix is still applied to it | Requirements 37.5/30.2: interceptors see the legacy host. Task 3.29 verifies it in the hook-order tests. The other 3.24 difference (`sdkHttpResponse` already set at `afterUnmarshalling`) is unchanged here and left to 3.29 |
| Handler steps after marshalling | `marshall(ExecutionContext, hostPrefix, discoveredEndpoint)` also takes the `HTTP_REQUEST_URI_BEFORE_MODIFY` snapshot, runs `modifyHttpRequestAndHttpContent`, the bearer-token plain-HTTP check (same message), and re-attaches the interceptors' `RequestBody` (sync: when present, and the result is set on the interceptor context as `AmazonSyncHttpClient` does; async: only without an async body, as `BaseAsyncClientHandler`) | Same order and effects as `finalizeSdkHttpFullRequest` + `doExecute` + the HTTP client entry. The host prefix is a parameter (the generated client's resolved `hostPrefixExpression`) until the Requirement 30.4 work reads it from the operation's schema |
| Ten nested mutation stages, then a per-attempt copy for `amz-sdk-request` | `RequestMutationStages.prepare` runs the same mutations as direct calls in the legacy order on one builder: invocation ID, custom headers, custom query parameters, query-to-body, compression (legacy stage), auth (`AuthSchemeResolutionCache`), endpoint (`EndpointResolution`), checksums (legacy stage), `User-Agent` (`UserAgentHeaderCache`), and the first attempt's `amz-sdk-request: attempt=1; max=<n>` (racecar B2.b); `RetryDecisions.startAttempt` returns the prepared request for attempt 1 and a copy with `attempt=<k>` for retries | One builder pass; attempt 1 needs no extra copy. `max` is `RETRY_POLICY.numRetries() + 1`, else `RETRY_STRATEGY.maxAttempts()`, the value `RetryableStageHelper.requestToSend` uses. **Observable difference:** the helper is created with the stamped request, so `RetryPolicyContext.request()` and the DEBUG `Sending Request:` entry list the `amz-sdk-request` header (legacy: absent there) |
| `ApplyTransactionIdStage`: `new Random()` per call | `ThreadLocalRandom` UUID, same format | Per-client stage instance without contention; still not `SecureRandom` |
| `MergeCustomQueryParamsStage` rewrites the query map on every call; `QueryParametersToBodyStage` builds the request twice to test its condition; `MergeCustomHeadersStage` NPEs on a null `ADDITIONAL_HTTP_HEADERS` | Query merge skipped when the request adds none (same result); query-to-body tests the condition on the builder and builds only when it moves the parameters; null headers maps tolerated | Same requests; fewer allocations |
| `EndpointResolutionStage` rewrites scheme, host, port and path on the builder | `EndpointResolution.resolve` keeps the legacy skip conditions, resolver call, auth-property re-apply, `RESOLVED_ENDPOINT`, endpoint headers and metric, and sets the request path relative to the endpoint; after the builder pass `EndpointResolution.apply` calls `protocol.updateServiceEndpoint(request, endpoint)`. When `modifyHttpRequest` moved the request to another scheme/host/port the legacy path-only rewrite is applied and the protocol is not called; when the relative path cannot reproduce the legacy path (an interceptor path that does not start with the client endpoint path while the resolved and client paths are equal) the legacy URI is set on the builder | Design D.6 seam 1 with byte-identical URIs (`EndpointResolutionTest` covers both paths). The URI is applied after the checksum and `User-Agent` mutations, which read only the interceptor context's request, not the builder's URI |
| H7 "auth resolution cached per client" | `AuthSchemeResolutionCache.resolve` reproduces `AuthSchemeResolutionStage` (attribute order, snapshots, merge, `SIGNING_METHOD_UPDATER`, business metrics, existing-scheme identity refresh, failure message and DEBUG entry). When the call's `AUTH_SCHEMES` and `IDENTITY_PROVIDERS` are the client configuration's instances, the auth scheme, `identityProvider(...)` and `signer()` per scheme ID come from a per-client map; otherwise (request-level credentials) the legacy `AuthSchemeResolver.selectAuthScheme` runs | The options (`AUTH_SCHEME_OPTIONS_RESOLVER`) are resolved per call: providers may depend on the request. `signer()`/`identityProvider()` are called once per client and scheme (legacy: per call); a `signer()` that throws is cached as a discarded option |
| H4 "placeholder auth scheme once per client" | `AuthSchemeResolutionCache.seedResolvedChecksumSpecs(attrs, specs)`: when no auth scheme is selected yet it puts the client's `"unset"` placeholder (one completed identity future and failing signer per client) before writing `RESOLVED_CHECKSUM_SPECS`, so the write mapping copies only the option (legacy: new identity, future and signer per call) | No public `InterceptorContext` change. The call-context step of tasks 3.26/3.28 calls it instead of writing `RESOLVED_CHECKSUM_SPECS` directly (`INTERNAL_RESOLVED_CHECKSUM_SPECS` is package-private, so the option copy stays) |
| H9 cached `User-Agent` | `UserAgentHeaderCache`: prefix + client user agent, suffix and the "set in `ADDITIONAL_HTTP_HEADERS`" check computed once per client; a call with no metadata, business metrics, credential provider name or custom API names gets the precomputed string; otherwise the legacy value is built (merging into `BUSINESS_METRICS`, the provider name read from the resolved identity without waiting). When the identity is not resolved yet or failed, the legacy `ApplyUserAgentStage` (held per client) computes the header, waiting or failing as before. Logs on the legacy `ApplyUserAgentStage` logger | Same header value (`UserAgentHeaderCacheTest`, `RequestMutationStagesTest`) |
| Signing (D.6 shared stages, racecar C) | `RequestSigning.sign` (sync SRA, same request, clock, `TIME_OFFSET`, interceptor-context updates as `SigningStage`) and `signAsync`, which signs inline and returns a completed future when there is no async body and the identity is already resolved; a signer failure fails the future, as in the legacy async stage. An identity that is not resolved yet (or failed), a pre-SRA `Signer` and an async body go to the legacy stage (held per client), which waits or fails as before. Each attempt is signed from that attempt's unsigned request | Racecar B2.a is not needed (no re-signing of a signed request). The new classes contain no blocking wait (`ASYNC_BLOCKING_CALL` stays limited to the legacy stages already on the SpotBugs suppression list) |
| Retry decisions | `RetryDecisions` wraps `RetryableStageHelper` (token, backoff, clock skew, final exception); `suggestedDelay` keeps the legacy difference: sync uses `Retry-After` (seconds) or, with 2026 retries, `x-amz-retry-after` of the failed response; async uses `x-amz-retry-after` of the last response only with 2026 retries | Baseline parity. This differs in delay, not in attempts; task 3.31 (Property 50) may decide to unify |
| H8 "skip measurement with no metric publisher" | New `MetricUtils.collectsMetrics(MetricCollector)` (false for null and `NoOpMetricCollector`; additive method on the `@SdkProtectedApi` class). Marshalling, unmarshalling (3.24 steps updated), endpoint resolve, identity fetch and signing durations are neither timed nor reported when it is false | **Hook for 3.26–3.28:** the handlers always create `MetricCollector.create("ApiCall")`; the new path must use `NoOpMetricCollector` when no publisher is configured for H8 to take effect |
| H10 "attributes seeded from a template" | `ExecutionAttributesTemplate.builder(config)` seeds `SERVICE_CONFIG`, `SERVICE_NAME`, `CLIENT_TYPE`, `PROFILE_FILE_SUPPLIER`, `PROFILE_NAME`, `NEW_RETRIES_2026_ENABLED`, `CLIENT_ENDPOINT_PROVIDER`, `CLIENT_CONTEXT_PARAMS`, `DISABLE_HOST_PREFIX_INJECTION`, `SDK_CLIENT`, `SIGNER_OVERRIDDEN`, `REQUEST/RESPONSE_CHECKSUM_*`, `AUTH_SCHEMES` and `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` (from `SdkClientOption.CLIENT_PROTOCOL`); `errorRegistry(…)` adds `ERROR_REGISTRY`; `put(…)` adds module values (aws-core region/signing values, later). `newExecutionAttributes()` and `applyTo(attrs)` (overwrites, the legacy precedence) | No client option holds the service's error registry, so the builder takes it (from `<Svc>Operations.ERROR_REGISTRY`, the registry every generated operation shares). `PROFILE_FILE` (read from its supplier per call) and per-request values stay per call |
| Timeouts, attempt/HTTP metric collectors, `SERVICE_CALL_DURATION`/`TIME_TO_FIRST_BYTE`, `ABORTABLE_LISTENER`, `InterruptMonitor`, default transport | Not in the shared stages: they wrap each attempt and the transport call and belong to the attempt steps of 3.26 (sync) and 3.27 (async). The shared stages read `RequestExecutionContext.attemptMetricCollector()` (signing, unmarshalling via `API_CALL_ATTEMPT_METRIC_COLLECTOR`) and `prepare` starts with `InterruptMonitor.checkInterrupted()` like the legacy first stage | Keeps the shared classes free of scheduling (Requirement 37.6) |

**Tests** (`core/sdk-core/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/core/internal/http/`, fixtures in `ApiCallStagesTestSupport`): `RequestMutationStagesTest` runs the legacy stage chain (`MakeRequestMutableStage` … `MakeRequestImmutableStage` + `RetryableStageHelper.requestToSend`) and `prepare` on the same input, sync and async, for 16 scenarios (custom headers, query merge, query-to-body for query/ec2, resolved endpoints with and without paths, a client endpoint path, an interceptor host change, `User-Agent` with prefix/suffix/API names/metadata/business metrics/provider name, `User-Agent` in client or request headers, no client user agent, required MD5, retry policy, auth scheme resolution), comparing method, URI, query, headers (invocation ID by format), body and the resolved endpoint, auth scheme, business metrics and checksum specs; plus retry-info, protocol call, invocation-ID and interrupt cases, and `marshall` (client endpoint and host prefix visible in `afterMarshalling`/`modifyHttpRequest`, disabled prefix, discovered endpoint, no client endpoint, `modifyHttpRequest`/`modifyHttpContent`, async body not re-attached, bearer token over HTTP). `EndpointResolutionTest` compares URIs and headers with `EndpointResolutionStage` for 11 path/interceptor cases, skip conditions and metrics. `UserAgentHeaderCacheTest` (client-level combinations against the legacy stage, the precomputed value, pending and failed identities), `AuthSchemeResolutionCacheTest` (legacy selection and failure message, placeholder merge, one lookup per client, no caching with request-level providers, separate caches per client, existing scheme, signing-method updater, identity metric only when collecting, placeholder seeding equal to writing the attribute), `RequestSigningTest` (sync and async inline equal to the legacy stages, pending and failed identities, signer failure, no auth schemes, signing metric), `RetryDecisionsTest` (attempt requests equal to the helper's, retry/stop sequence and final exception equal to the helper's, non-SDK wrapping, sync and async suggested delays, retry count, max attempts), `ExecutionAttributesTemplateTest` and `MetricUtilsTest.collectsMetrics`. `ProtocolStepsNoGeneratedSerdeTest` now also checks the seven new classes for generated marshaller and unmarshaller types (Requirement 37.2).

## `core/sdk-core` `SyncApiCallPipeline` (task 3.26)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.core.internal.http` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `SyncApiCallPipeline`, one instance per client configuration (`create(HttpClientDependencies[, TypeRegistry errorRegistry])`), holding the 3.25 stages, the `ExecutionAttributesTemplate` and the transport. An SDK addition modeled on racecar G1 (OD-16); no racecar code. Each call is a fixed sequence of direct method calls (`measureApiCallSuccess` → context factory → failure envelope → `RequestMutationStages.marshall` → API call metrics → API call timeout → request stream closing → `prepare` → retry loop → per attempt: attempt metrics, timeout translation, attempt timeout, `RequestSigning.sign`, `beforeTransmission`, transport, `afterTransmission`/`modifyHttpResponse`, `beforeUnmarshalling`, `ProtocolResponseHandling` → `afterExecution`), each step a re-implementation of the named legacy stage with the same effects. The legacy `RequestPipelineBuilder` graph and stages are not modified (OD-20). Nothing calls the pipeline yet (task 3.28).

| Design (D.6) or legacy behavior | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Legacy handler: `measureApiCallSuccess`, `invokeInterceptorsAndCreateExecutionContext` (`beforeExecution`, `modifyRequest`), marshalling, then `AmazonSyncHttpClient` | `execute(ApiOperation, ClientExecutionParams, Function<ClientExecutionParams, ExecutionContext> contextFactory[, ResponseTransformer])`. The pipeline reports `API_CALL_SUCCESSFUL` and calls the factory, which is the handler's existing context builder (`AwsExecutionContextBuilder`/`BaseClientHandler`; it must use `params.executionAttributes()`) | aws-core's context builder (region, signing and auth attributes) cannot move into `sdk-core`. **For 3.28:** the handler must not wrap the call in its own `measureApiCallSuccess` (it would report the metric twice) |
| Operation and error registry | Explicit `ApiOperation` argument, put as `API_OPERATION` before the factory runs; `ERROR_REGISTRY` is the template's (the `create` argument) or else `operation.errorRegistry()`, also set before `beforeExecution` | `ClientExecutionParams.withOperation` is task 3.28, which may drop the argument |
| H10 template | `ExecutionAttributesTemplate` built once per pipeline and applied to `params.executionAttributes()` before the factory (overwrite; the factory's `putAbsentAttributes` for override attributes then cannot replace these client-level values, the same precedence as its own explicit puts). The factory still writes its values, so the template saves nothing per call until 3.28 trims the factory | The template also carries `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` and `ERROR_REGISTRY`, which no legacy factory sets. `AuthSchemeResolutionCache.seedResolvedChecksumSpecs` (H4) is likewise left to the 3.28 context step, since `AwsExecutionContextBuilder` writes `RESOLVED_CHECKSUM_SPECS` itself |
| H8 | A call without a metric collector gets `NoOpMetricCollector` (legacy: `MetricCollector.create("ApiCall")`, which nobody publishes; generated clients already pass `NoOpMetricCollector` without publishers). With a non-collecting collector, `API_CALL_DURATION`/`SERVICE_ENDPOINT`, `BACKOFF_DELAY_DURATION`, `ERROR_TYPE`, HTTP status/request-ID metrics, `SERVICE_CALL_DURATION`/`TIME_TO_FIRST_BYTE`, `TIME_TO_LAST_BYTE`/throughputs and the response byte-counting stream are skipped | Nothing published changes when metrics are enabled (`metricsEnabled_reportsTheLegacyCallAndAttemptMetrics`). `RESPONSE_BYTES_READ` and `REQUEST_BODY_METRICS` are still set per attempt so interceptors see the legacy attribute keys |
| Failure reporting (`ExecutionFailureExceptionReportingStage` wraps only `AmazonSyncHttpClient`'s graph) | The envelope starts at `RequestMutationStages.marshall`: `modifyException` + `onExecutionFailure` once, then `ThrowableUtils.failure` (the legacy stage plus `AmazonSyncHttpClient`'s checked-exception wrapping). Failures in the context factory are not reported, as in the Baseline | Requirement 37.8 requires `onExecutionFailure` for a serialization failure. **Interceptor-visible difference:** on the Baseline (sync and async) a marshaller failure, or an interceptor failure in `beforeMarshalling`…`modifyHttpRequest`, ends the call without `modifyException`/`onExecutionFailure`; on the new path they run once. For 3.29 to confirm |
| Serialization failures "go to the shared retry decision" | Serialization runs once per call, before the retry loop, and its failure ends the call (the decision for it is "do not retry"; the retry strategy is not asked). The exception is thrown unchanged | The Baseline marshals once outside `RetryableStage` and never retries or counts a marshalling failure; retrying would re-run `beforeMarshalling`…`modifyHttpRequest`, and `RetryableStageHelper` (legacy, not modified) needs the prepared request. **Open for review:** if 37.8 is meant literally, a retryable serialization failure (I/O-caused) would need a re-serializing attempt |
| Transport and deserialization failures | As `RetryableStage`: `SdkException`/`IOException` and failed responses go to `RetryDecisions`; other exceptions end the call unchanged; final exception from `RetryableStageHelper` (same type, cause kept, `numAttempts`, earlier attempts as suppressed exceptions) | Requirement 37.8 |
| Timeouts | `ApiCallTimeoutTrackingStage`, `TimeoutExceptionHandlingStage` and `ApiCallAttemptTimeoutTrackingStage` translations re-implemented as private methods (same `ApiCallTimeoutException`, `ApiCallAttemptTimeoutException`, `AbortedException("Thread was interrupted")`, interrupt-flag handling and `SdkInterruptedException` stream closing); trackers from `TimerUtils` on `SCHEDULED_EXECUTOR_SERVICE`. One `TransportExecutionAttribute.ABORTABLE_LISTENER` per call registers the transport's request with both trackers, read when called | The legacy translations are private to the stages, which cannot be called without the nested-wrapper graph |
| `MakeHttpRequestStage` | `HTTP_METRIC_COLLECTOR` (`MetricUtils.createHttpMetricsCollector`) per attempt; the pipeline writes `API_CALL_ATTEMPT_START_NANO_TIME` before `send` and `HEADERS_READ_END_NANO_TIME` after it when the transport did not (the SDK HTTP client transport writes both itself) | A third-party transport (the 3.33 harness) does not write them, and the TTLB/throughput metrics read them. **Interceptor-visible:** the two `TransportExecutionAttribute` keys are new attributes |
| Default transport | `CLIENT_TRANSPORT` when configured (its exchange must be `HttpMessageExchange.INSTANCE`, else `IllegalStateException`); otherwise `SdkHttpClientTransport.create(SYNC_HTTP_CLIENT, false)` built once in the constructor (the configuration closes the client); neither configured → `IllegalStateException` | 3.17/3.33 hand-off |
| `AfterTransmissionExecutionInterceptorsStage` rebuilds the response with `AbortableInputStream.create(responseBody)` every attempt | The response is copied only when an interceptor replaced the response or its body | Same content (`AbortableInputStream.create` of an `AbortableInputStream` delegates `abort()`); one copy fewer per attempt |
| `StreamManagingStage` | Same wrapping (`ClosingStreamProvider` re-implemented, private in the legacy stage), before `prepare`, so checksum and compression stages read the body through it as before | Same stream lifecycle |
| 3.24/3.25 difference: `sdkHttpResponse` already set at `afterUnmarshalling` | Unchanged | Left to 3.29 |

**Tests** (`core/sdk-core/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/core/internal/http/SyncApiCallPipelineTest.java`, with `ProtocolStepsTestSupport`'s operation and shapes, an `ApiCallStagesTestSupport.HttpStubProtocol` subclass, a scripted `ClientTransport` and stub `SdkHttpClient`s): success (output, sent URI and headers, the 15 hooks in the Baseline order with one attributes instance, contexts at `beforeTransmission`/`afterTransmission`/`afterExecution`, schema attributes visible from `beforeExecution`), 500 then success (attempt hooks per attempt, `attempt=2`), non-retryable 400 (hook sequence ending `modifyException`, `onExecutionFailure`), non-retryable client error (type, message, cause), non-SDK transport exception unchanged, serialization failure, retries exhausted (3 attempts, one `onExecutionFailure`, cause and suppressed attempts) for client and service errors, `afterExecution` failure reported, attempt timeout (3 aborted attempts, `ApiCallAttemptTimeoutException`), API call timeout (`ApiCallTimeoutException`, no retry), interrupted thread (`AbortedException`, flag kept), metrics on (call and attempt metrics, retry count) and off (no-op collectors, unwrapped body, legacy attribute keys kept), default transport over `SYNC_HTTP_CLIENT` (not closed by the transport), configured transport preferred, mismatched or missing transport rejected, streaming output through a `ResponseTransformer`, and the error registry passed to the protocol. `ProtocolStepsNoGeneratedSerdeTest` also covers `SyncApiCallPipeline` (Requirement 37.2).

## `core/sdk-core` `AsyncApiCallPipeline` (task 3.27)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.core.internal.http` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `AsyncApiCallPipeline`, one instance per client configuration (`create(HttpClientDependencies[, TypeRegistry errorRegistry])`), holding the same 3.25 stages, `ExecutionAttributesTemplate` and transport as `SyncApiCallPipeline`, plus `SCHEDULED_EXECUTOR_SERVICE` and `FUTURE_COMPLETION_EXECUTOR`. An SDK addition modeled on racecar G2 (OD-16); no racecar code. Each call is one private `AsyncCall` object whose steps (`start` → `attempt` → `send` → `received` → `handleResponse` → `deserialize` → `decide`/`succeed`/`fail`) re-implement the named legacy async stages; each step starts the next directly, from a future's completion or from a scheduled task. The legacy `RequestPipelineBuilder` graph and stages are not modified (OD-20). Nothing calls the pipeline yet (task 3.28). `transport.ResponseBodyAggregator` is now `public` (still `@SdkInternalApi`) so the pipeline can aggregate a replaced response publisher.

| Design (D.6) or legacy behavior | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Same entry point, operation/error-registry seeding, H10 template, H8 and default-transport rules as 3.26 | Unchanged from 3.26, with `ASYNC_CLIENT_TRANSPORT` (exchange must be `HttpMessageExchange.INSTANCE`) or else `SdkAsyncHttpClientTransport.create(ASYNC_HTTP_CLIENT, false)`; a configuration without `SCHEDULED_EXECUTOR_SERVICE` is rejected with `IllegalStateException` (the legacy `AsyncRetryableStage` needs it too). **For 3.28:** the pipeline reports `API_CALL_SUCCESSFUL`; the async handler must not wrap the call in its own `measureApiCallSuccess` | Same reasons as 3.26 |
| Legacy future shape: `measureApiCallSuccess` → handler `ThrowableUtils.failure` translation → `AsyncApiCallMetricCollectionStage` → `AsyncApiCallTimeoutTrackingStage` → `AsyncExecutionFailureExceptionReportingStage` → `AsyncRetryableStage` → per attempt `AsyncApiCallAttemptMetricCollectionStage`, `AsyncSigningStage`, `AsyncBeforeTransmissionExecutionInterceptorsStage`, `MakeAsyncHttpRequestStage` → `AfterExecutionInterceptorsStage` (about a dozen futures per attempt, each pair joined with `forwardExceptionTo`) | One returned future per call; per attempt the transport's future, plus a signing future only when signing is deferred, an aggregation future only when an interceptor replaced the response publisher, and a signal future only when an API call timeout is configured (H9). Ending the call fails the one in-flight future (transport, signing or aggregation), cancels a pending retry and the API call timer; the transport forwards that failure to the HTTP client's future (its `forwardExceptionTo`) | H9; `forwardExceptionTo` is needed only at the transport boundary |
| Failure reporting and result shape | As 3.26: the envelope starts at `RequestMutationStages.marshall`; `modifyException` + `onExecutionFailure` once; then, as `AsyncExecutionFailureExceptionReportingStage` and the handler, the future fails with `CompletionException(ThrowableUtils.asSdkException(reported))` (`get()` throws `ExecutionException` with the `SdkException` cause). A context-factory failure fails the future with the factory's exception unchanged and is not reported, as in the Baseline handler's `measureApiCallSuccess` | Requirement 37.8; same interceptor-visible difference for marshalling failures as 3.26 (3.29 to confirm) |
| Retry classification (`AsyncRetryableStage`) | Differs from sync on purpose, as the legacy async stage does: every non-`Error` failure of an attempt (including a non-SDK `RuntimeException` from the transport future, which `RetryDecisions` wraps as `SdkClientException("Unable to execute HTTP request: …", cause)`) goes to the retry decision; an `Error` ends the call. A synchronous `SdkException`/`IOException` from `signAsync` goes to the retry decision, anything else thrown before the transport call ends the call. Serialization failures are not retried (3.26 decision) | Same exception types as the legacy async pipeline |
| Retry wait | `decide` schedules the next `attempt` on `SCHEDULED_EXECUTOR_SERVICE` with the decision's delay and restores the call's original `AsyncRequestBody`; a final failure with a non-zero delay is also scheduled; a zero initial-token delay attempts inline | `AsyncRetryableStage.maybeAttemptExecute`/`maybeRetryExecute`; no thread sleeps |
| API call timeout (`AsyncApiCallTimeoutTrackingStage`) | `TimerUtils.timeAsyncTaskIfNeeded` on a signal future; when it fires the call fails with `ApiCallTimeoutException` and `API_CALL_SUCCESSFUL=false`, the in-flight attempt is failed (cancelling the HTTP request), and **no** `modifyException`/`onExecutionFailure` runs | Matches the Baseline: the legacy timeout stage completes the future outside the reporting stage, and `CompletableFuture` skips the `handle` function of a dependent that is already complete, so the reporting stage's interceptor calls never run |
| Cancellation of the returned future | Stops the call as above; `API_CALL_DURATION`/`SERVICE_ENDPOINT` are reported, `API_CALL_SUCCESSFUL` and the interceptors are not | Matches the Baseline for the same `CompletableFuture` reason (`measureApiCallSuccess`'s `whenComplete` dependent is already complete; `AsyncApiCallMetricCollectionStage`'s is not) |
| Attempt timeout (`MakeAsyncHttpRequestStage`) | `timeAsyncTaskIfNeeded` on the transport's future; firing fails it with `ApiCallAttemptTimeoutException`, which the SDK transport forwards to the HTTP client's future, and the failure goes to the retry decision | Same |
| Signing (`AsyncSigningStage`, racecar C) | `RequestSigning.signAsync`; when its future is already complete successfully (resolved identity, no async body), the request is sent directly with no future chained, so, without a `FUTURE_COMPLETION_EXECUTOR`, a call whose transport also completes inline completes on the calling thread | Requirement 37.7 |
| `MakeAsyncHttpRequestStage` request | `Content-Length` from `getRequestWithContentLength` re-implemented (async body length, buffer length, `0` without a body); a body without an in-memory buffer is read once and put back as a byte array (legacy `SimpleHttpContentPublisher` also reads it fully). The async body goes to the transport as `TransportExecutionAttribute.ASYNC_REQUEST_BODY`; `HTTP_METRIC_COLLECTOR`, `API_CALL_ATTEMPT_START_NANO_TIME` and `HEADERS_READ_END_NANO_TIME` as in 3.26. No `ABORTABLE_LISTENER`: the async transport is aborted through its future | Same request bytes and headers |
| Response threads | The response of each attempt is handled on `FUTURE_COMPLETION_EXECUTOR` (inline, with the legacy debug message, when the executor rejects the task or is absent), so `afterTransmission` … `modifyResponse` and `afterExecution` all run there. **Interceptor-visible:** on the Baseline, `afterTransmission` … `modifyResponse` run on the HTTP client's thread and only `afterExecution` on the completion executor | The buffered transport completes once with the whole response; running interceptors and deserialization off the HTTP client's I/O thread avoids blocking it |
| `AsyncAfterTransmissionInterceptorCallingResponseHandler` | Same hooks in the same order with the same contexts (`afterTransmission`, `modifyHttpResponse`/`modifyHttpResponseContent`, `beforeUnmarshalling` with a headers-only response and no `responseBody`, then `modifyAsyncHttpResponseContent` with a publisher). The transport has already buffered the body, so the publisher replays it as one buffer without copying; only a publisher an interceptor substituted is read into a new buffer (`ResponseBodyAggregator`, cancelled when the call ends). **Interceptor-visible timing:** the header hooks run after the whole body has arrived, not when the headers arrive | Requirement 37.5 (order and contexts unchanged); one body copy fewer |
| Attempt metrics (`AsyncApiCallAttemptMetricCollectionStage`, `MakeAsyncHttpRequestStage`) | Same metrics as the Baseline when collecting (`BACKOFF_DELAY_DURATION`, HTTP status/request IDs, `SERVICE_CALL_DURATION` to transport completion, `TIME_TO_FIRST_BYTE` to the headers when known, `TIME_TO_LAST_BYTE`, `READ_THROUGHPUT`, `WRITE_THROUGHPUT`, `ERROR_TYPE` classified from the failure, then its cause, so a wrapped I/O failure is `IO`); skipped when not collecting (H8) | Nothing published changes |
| Streaming output (`AsyncResponseTransformer`) | `execute(…, AsyncResponseTransformer)` throws `UnsupportedOperationException` before any hook runs. **Deferred to 7.19** (streaming blob members), which hands the body to the transformer through the transport's `AsyncResponseHandoff`. **For 3.28:** route async operations with a streaming output to the legacy path until 7.19 | Out of scope for 3.27; `SyncApiCallPipeline` supports `ResponseTransformer` because the sync transport returns the unread stream |

**Tests** (`core/sdk-core/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/core/internal/http/AsyncApiCallPipelineTest.java`, same fixtures as 3.26 with a scripted `AsyncClientTransport`, stub `SdkAsyncHttpClient`s and a test `HttpSigner`): success (output, sent URI and headers, the 16 async hooks in the Baseline order with one attributes instance), contexts and attributes (headers-only `afterTransmission`, body through `modifyAsyncHttpResponseContent`), 500 then success, non-retryable 400 (`CompletionException` around the service exception, hook sequence ending `modifyException`, `onExecutionFailure`), client error type/message/cause, non-SDK transport exception through the retry decision, serialization failure, context-factory failure, retries exhausted for client and service errors, `afterExecution` failure, attempt timeout (3 cancelled HTTP executions), API call timeout (no failure hooks, `API_CALL_SUCCESSFUL=false`), cancellation of the returned future (in-flight transport future cancelled) and during a backoff (scheduled retry removed), inline signing on the calling thread, async request body signed asynchronously, `Content-Length` added, `FUTURE_COMPLETION_EXECUTOR` threads, metrics on (call and attempt metrics, `IO` error type) and off, replaced response publisher (configured transport and SDK transport buffer), default transport over `ASYNC_HTTP_CLIENT` (not closed), configured transport preferred, mismatched/missing transport and missing scheduler rejected, streaming output rejected, and the error registry passed to the protocol. `ProtocolStepsNoGeneratedSerdeTest` also covers `AsyncApiCallPipeline` (Requirement 37.2).

## `core/sdk-core` `PipelineCache` and new-path routing (task 3.28)

New in `software.amazon.awssdk.core.internal.http` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `PipelineCache<P>` (racecar H11, re-implemented; no racecar code), with `forSync`/`forAsync` over `SyncApiCallPipeline.create`/`AsyncApiCallPipeline.create`. Added to the `@SdkProtectedApi` `ClientExecutionParams`: `withOperation(ApiOperation)` and `getOperation()` (additive). `BaseSyncClientHandler` and `BaseAsyncClientHandler` each hold one cache and route on `getOperation()`; `SdkSyncClientHandler`/`SdkAsyncClientHandler` and aws-core's `AwsSyncClientHandler`/`AwsAsyncClientHandler` delegate every `execute` to them, so they route the same way with no change. The legacy `RequestPipelineBuilder` graph, its stages and the legacy branches of the handlers are not modified (OD-20). Nothing generated calls `withOperation` yet (task 3.32).

| Design (D.6) or legacy behavior | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| "Stage graph built once per client configuration" | The cache retains the pipeline of one configuration, compared by identity. A call whose resolved configuration (`params.requestConfiguration()`, else the handler's) is the retained instance reuses its pipeline (one reference comparison). Once a second call has used the retained configuration it stays retained, and a call with any other configuration (a request with plugins, which `updateSdkClientConfiguration` rebuilds per call) builds a pipeline for that call only, as the legacy handlers build their stage graph per call. Until then another configuration replaces it, so a first call with plugins does not lock out the client's configuration | Generated clients pass the same configuration instance on every call without plugins, and it is not the handler's (the client adds `SDK_CLIENT`). A map keyed by configuration would grow with every plugin call |
| Thread safety, lazy build | The retained entry holds a `Lazy` (SDK utility); a thread that loses the `compareAndSet` uses the winner's entry, so concurrent first calls build one pipeline. A build failure (`IllegalStateException` for a missing or non-HTTP transport, or a missing scheduler) is thrown to the call and not cached; the next call tries again | No hand-rolled lazy initialization |
| Pipeline dependencies | The handler's `HttpClientDependencies` for the handler's own configuration; otherwise `toBuilder().clientConfiguration(config)`, as legacy `invoke` does per call, so both paths share the client's `ClockSkewAdjuster`. The cache owns nothing; the handler still closes the dependencies | Same clock-skew state on both paths |
| Error registry | Pipelines are created with `create(dependencies)` (no service registry), so each call seeds `ERROR_REGISTRY` from `operation.errorRegistry()` | Generated operations all return `<Svc>Operations.ERROR_REGISTRY`, so the result is the same; no client option carries the registry |
| `withOperation` | Sets the operation, sets `operationName` to `ApiOperation.name()` and puts `SdkExecutionAttribute.API_OPERATION` in the params' attributes (null is rejected) | Interceptors see the operation on either path (also when an async streaming call goes to the legacy path, below) |
| Routing | Sync, both `execute` methods: `getOperation() != null` → `SyncApiCallPipeline` (streaming output through `ResponseTransformer` included). Async `execute(params)`: same → `AsyncApiCallPipeline` | D.6 |
| Async streaming output (`execute(params, AsyncResponseTransformer)`) | **Until task 7.19:** an operation **with** a marshaller goes to the legacy path; an operation without one goes to `AsyncApiCallPipeline`, whose `UnsupportedOperationException` fails the returned future before any hook runs (`API_CALL_SUCCESSFUL=false`) | `AsyncApiCallPipeline` does not support `AsyncResponseTransformer` yet (3.27). Generated code (3.32) must keep the marshaller and response handlers for async streaming-output operations until 7.19; CloudWatch has none |
| `API_CALL_SUCCESSFUL` | New-path calls are not wrapped in the handler's `measureApiCallSuccess` (the pipelines report it). Only a failure before the pipeline runs (building it) is reported by the handler: sync rethrows, async returns a failed future, as the legacy `measureApiCallSuccess` does | Reported exactly once (`ClientHandlerPipelineRoutingTest`) |
| H8 (`NoOpMetricCollector`, deferred from 3.25) | No handler change: generated clients already pass `NoOpMetricCollector` when no publisher is configured, and both pipelines default a missing collector to `NoOpMetricCollector` before the context factory runs, so the factory's `MetricCollector.create("ApiCall")` fallback is never reached on the new path | H8 takes effect with the existing handlers |
| H10 context-factory trimming and H4 `seedResolvedChecksumSpecs` (deferred from 3.26) | **Not done; deferred to the phase 5 pipeline optimizations (37.9/37.10, measured with the 9.17 paired A/B harness).** The context factory is still the handler's `invokeInterceptorsAndCreateExecutionContext` (aws-core `AwsExecutionContextBuilder`), which re-writes the client-level attributes the template seeded and writes `RESOLVED_CHECKSUM_SPECS` itself (so the placeholder auth scheme is still built per call) | Trimming needs a new-path variant of `AwsExecutionContextBuilder` in aws-core, which the legacy path shares; the values are correct either way (the template and the factory write the same client-level values), only allocations differ |
| CRC32 (`x-amz-crc32`) validation | Not applied on the new path: `AwsSyncClientHandler.addCrc32Validation` wraps the (unused, null) response handler, and `BaseAsyncClientHandler`'s `crc32Validator` runs only on the legacy path | Only DynamoDB sends the header; it must move into the transport or protocol before DynamoDB runs on the new path (phase 5) |

**Tests** (`core/sdk-core/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/core/internal/http/`): `ClientHandlerPipelineRoutingTest` drives `SdkSyncClientHandler`/`SdkAsyncClientHandler` with a stub protocol, sync and async `ClientTransport`s and recording HTTP clients: `withOperation` sets the operation, name and `API_OPERATION` and rejects null; sync calls with an operation (plain and `ResponseTransformer`) reach only the transport and calls without one only the legacy HTTP client; `API_CALL_SUCCESSFUL` reported once on success and on a pipeline-build failure (sync and async); a request configuration gets its own pipeline; async calls with an operation run on the new path, without one on the legacy path; async streaming with an operation and a marshaller runs on the legacy path (which also exposes `API_OPERATION`), without a marshaller fails as unsupported before any hook. `PipelineCacheTest` covers the cache with a counting factory (built once and reused, handler and copied dependencies, retention after reuse and per-call builds for other configurations, a first plugin call not locking out the client's configuration, a failed build retried, 16 concurrent first calls building one pipeline); task 3.30 extends it with the client-level sequential/concurrent sync and async cases. `ProtocolStepsNoGeneratedSerdeTest` also covers `PipelineCache` (Requirement 37.2).

## Interceptor hook order on the new path (task 3.29)

Tests only; no main code changed. `ApiCallPipelineInterceptorOrderTest` (sdk-core) runs each call through `SdkSyncClientHandler`/`SdkAsyncClientHandler` twice, once on the legacy pipeline (marshaller and response handlers that produce the stub protocol's request and outputs) and once with `withOperation` on the new path, over the default SDK HTTP client transport and over a configured `ClientTransport`/`AsyncClientTransport`, with metrics on and off. Both answer from one `ScriptedExchange`. It compares, per hook, the hook sequence, what the hook's `Context` type exposes (the context class, request, HTTP request with header names and `amz-sdk-request`, request bodies, HTTP response, response body/publisher presence, output with its `sdkHttpResponse`, failure context and exception), the `ExecutionAttributes` keys present, the caller's result and the number of attempts. Scenarios: success, service error, client (I/O) error with retries exhausted, retry then success (5xx and I/O), 503 retries exhausted, non-SDK transport exception, and interceptor failures in `modifyRequest`, `beforeTransmission`, `afterTransmission`, `afterUnmarshalling`, `modifyResponse` and `afterExecution`. Every failing call runs `onExecutionFailure` exactly once on both pipelines (except the `modifyRequest` failure, which runs it on neither). The context factory re-writes client-level attributes after the template, so keys are compared, not value provenance. The differences below are applied to the legacy record by `AllowedDifferences`, each with its reason; anything else fails.

| Difference | Outcome | Test |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Endpoint addressing runs before `afterMarshalling` (`ProtocolMarshalling.marshall(ctx, UnaryOperator)`, task 3.25) | **Equal.** `afterMarshalling` and `modifyHttpRequest` see the same HTTP request as on the Baseline: the client endpoint host with the host prefix (`https://data.svc.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/…`), not `UNRESOLVED_URI` | `execute_onTheNewPath_addressesTheRequestBeforeAfterMarshalling` and every scenario |
| 2. `afterUnmarshalling`, `modifyResponse`, `afterExecution` (and failure hooks after them) see the output with `sdkHttpResponse` set | **Allowed difference, kept.** Baseline unmarshallers leave it null and the legacy handler attaches it to a copy after `modifyResponse` that only the caller receives, so no Baseline hook sees it. The new path's protocols set it on the builder (racecar E4 superseded: no `toBuilder` copy). Hooks see strictly more; the caller gets an equal output. Matching the Baseline would need a copy per call | `execute_onTheNewPath_exposesTheHttpResponseOnTheOutputToEveryHookThatSeesTheOutput`; CloudWatch `httpResponsesOnOutputs` |
| 3. Marshalling and early-interceptor failures run `modifyException` + `onExecutionFailure` | **Allowed difference, kept.** Confirmed on the Baseline, sync and async: a marshaller failure, or an interceptor failure in `beforeMarshalling`, `afterMarshalling` or `modifyHttpRequest`, ends the call without either hook (the reporting stage wraps only the HTTP client's stage graph). The new path runs both once, with a `DefaultFailedExecutionContext` holding the thrown exception, because Requirement 37.8 requires `onExecutionFailure` for a serialization failure. The caller gets the same exception and no request is sent on either | `execute_withAnEarlyFailure_isReportedOnceOnTheNewPathOnly` |
| 4. Async: `afterTransmission` … `modifyResponse` run on `FUTURE_COMPLETION_EXECUTOR`; header hooks run after the body is buffered | **Allowed difference (threads and timing only).** Order and contexts are equal. On the Baseline those hooks run on the thread that delivers the HTTP response and only `afterExecution` on the executor; on the new path all of them run on the executor. The timing difference is not observable with a stub that delivers headers and body together and is not asserted | `asyncExecute_onTheNewPath_runsTheResponseHooksOnTheFutureCompletionExecutor` |
| 5. Async API call timeout and cancellation run no failure hooks | **Equal.** Both pipelines end with `ApiCallTimeoutException` (timeout) or `CancellationException` (cancel), run neither `modifyException` nor `onExecutionFailure`, and fail the in-flight HTTP request's future; same hooks, contexts and keys | `asyncExecute_whenTheApiCallTimesOut_…`, `asyncExecute_whenTheCallerCancels_…` |
| 6. Async: a non-SDK `RuntimeException` from the transport goes through retry | **Equal.** Async, both pipelines retry it (3 attempts) and fail with `SdkClientException("Unable to execute HTTP request: …")` with the exception as cause; sync, both end the call after one attempt with it unchanged (`RetryableStage` catches only `SdkException`/`IOException`). One `onExecutionFailure` | `TRANSPORT_RUNTIME_EXCEPTION` scenario |
| 7. (Found here) Async: an interceptor failure in `afterUnmarshalling` or `modifyResponse` | **Allowed difference, kept.** Same type (`SdkClientException`), cause and attempts; the message is the sync Baseline's (`CombinedResponseHandler`: `Unable to unmarshall response (<msg>). Response Code: 200, Response Text: null`), because sync and async share `ProtocolResponseHandling` (Requirement 37.6). The async Baseline gives the raw exception to its retry stage, which wraps it as `Unable to execute HTTP request: <msg>` | `AFTER_UNMARSHALLING_FAILURE`, `MODIFY_RESPONSE_FAILURE` scenarios |
| Attribute keys | **Additive only;** no Baseline key is missing or appears later at any hook. Present on the new path only: `API_OPERATION`, `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` and `ERROR_REGISTRY` from `beforeExecution` (Requirement 30.3; with sdk-core's own context factory also the other template keys, which the aws-core factory writes anyway); sync `TransportExecutionAttribute.ABORTABLE_LISTENER` from `beforeMarshalling`; from the first hook after the first send, `HTTP_METRIC_COLLECTOR`, and async also `ASYNC_REQUEST_BODY` and, with the SDK async transport, its `OwnedResponseBody` | Every scenario |
| Async response publisher | **Equal.** Only `modifyAsyncHttpResponseContent` sees `responsePublisher`; later hooks get the context stored before it, as on the Baseline | Every async scenario |

**CloudWatch.** `CloudWatchInterceptorOrderTest` (`services/cloudwatch`) pins the Baseline_Build record of the generated sync and async clients for `GetDashboard` over stub `SdkHttpClient`/`SdkAsyncHttpClient`s answering in rpcv2Cbor (success, `DashboardNotFoundError` 404, 500 then success, three I/O failures): hook sequence, contexts (marshalled and signed request header names, CBOR request body, response headers, output), the attribute keys written by the aws-core context builder and by each later stage, `sdkHttpResponse` on the outputs, the caller's result and attempts. The generated methods still use the legacy pipeline, so it passes on legacy routing now. It detects the new path by `API_OPERATION` at `beforeExecution` and then allows only the differences above (the three schema keys, the transport keys, `sdkHttpResponse` on outputs). A temporary preview that drove the clients' handlers with `withOperation(CloudWatchOperations.GET_DASHBOARD)` plus the client's protocol metadata, request configuration, auth-scheme and endpoint resolvers passed the same assertions for all eight cases, so the test is expected to pass unchanged after task 3.32 if the generated call passes those parameters (it was not committed). The existing interceptor tests are not modified.

## Stage graph built once per client configuration (task 3.30)

Main code adds only a test seam: `SyncApiCallPipeline` and `AsyncApiCallPipeline` each count completed constructions in a `private static final AtomicLong`, read through a package-private `@SdkTestInternalApi static long constructions()`. The increment runs once per pipeline construction, never on the per-call path. Every stage object of a pipeline (transport, `ExecutionAttributesTemplate`, `RequestMutationStages` with its `AuthSchemeResolutionCache`, `RequestSigning`) is created only by the pipeline constructors, so an unchanged count proves a call constructed no stage objects. The counter is on the stage graph rather than on `PipelineCache.pipelinesBuilt()` because the handlers' cache is private to `core.internal.handler` and that method is package-private to `core.internal.http`: exposing it would need a public test-only member. Because the counter is per class loader, tests compare it before and after (surefire runs the `sdk-core` unit tests in one JVM, one test at a time).

| Requirement 37.4 wording | Tested behavior | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| "When a client is built … construct its stage structure exactly once" | The graph is built once, on the client's first new-path call, and no later call builds one | The configuration a generated client passes with each call is not the handler's (the client adds `SDK_CLIENT`), so the handler cannot build the graph at construction; legacy-only clients also have no protocol or transport to build one with (3.28) |

**Tests** (`PipelineCacheTest`, extended): over `SdkSyncClientHandler`/`SdkAsyncClientHandler` with `withOperation` and a `ScriptedExchange` transport, each passing a client configuration derived from the handler's as generated clients do: 50 sequential calls build one sync (or async) graph on the first call and none after, and none of the other kind; 16 threads released by a start gate, 20 calls each, build one graph; two clients built with different configurations build one graph each and send through their own transports; requests with plugins (a new configuration per call) build one graph per call while the client's graph keeps being reused (sync). The cache-level tests from 3.28 are unchanged except that the concurrent one uses the shared `runConcurrently` helper.

## `codegen` generated client calls through `<Svc>Operations` (task 3.32)

New in `codegen` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `poet.schema.SchemaOperationRouting`, which decides per operation whether the client method runs on the schema-based path, and `poet.client.specs.SchemaOperationsProtocolSpec`, a `ProtocolSpec` that wraps the protocol's legacy spec. `SyncClientClass.getProtocolSpecs` wraps the legacy spec when the routing applies; `ProtocolSpec` gains `default boolean usesProtocolFactory()` (true), which `SyncClientClass` and `AsyncClientClass` check before generating the protocol factory field, its constructor statement and its `init` method. Nothing else in the client generators changes, so every client without routing generates the same code as before: the existing client golden files are unchanged.

| Design (A.18 "Generated client methods") or task | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Routing for "schema-enabled services (CloudWatch in this phase)" | `SchemaOperationRouting.forClient(model, taskParams.getSchemaCodegen())` applies when the client has runtime protocol selection (`ClientProtocolSelection`, task 3.23): today CloudWatch and the rpcv2Cbor Protocol_Test_Services. SQS, DynamoDB, Secrets Manager, Account, Route 53 and SNS keep the legacy calls until their protocol gets a factory (7.x) | Without a `ClientProtocolFactory`, `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` is unset and the new path cannot serialize. One condition for the builder's `protocol(...)` method and for routing, so a configured protocol is never ignored (Requirement 12.2) |
| One private `invoke(operation, request)` per client class | Each method keeps its body up to the `clientHandler.execute` call (request customizer, endpoint discovery, `updateSdkClientConfiguration` with the same configuration instance on every call without plugins, metric publishers and the `NoOpMetricCollector`/`ApiCall` collector, the `SERVICE_ID`/`OPERATION_NAME` metrics, the host prefix expression) and calls `clientHandler.execute(new ClientExecutionParams<I, O>().withOperation(<Svc>Operations.OP)...)` with every other parameter the legacy call sets: protocol metadata, host prefix, discovered endpoint, credential type, long polling, request configuration, input, metric collector, auth scheme options resolver, endpoint resolver, `HTTP_CHECKSUM`/`HTTP_CHECKSUM_REQUIRED`/`REQUEST_COMPRESSION` attributes. Only the marshaller, `JsonOperationMetadata`, response handler, error response handler and exception metadata `switch` are gone. Async methods keep the `whenComplete` metric publishing and `forwardExceptionTo` | `withOperation` (task 3.28) does not yet derive the checksum, compression and host prefix attributes from the schemas (design: "per-operation `putExecutionAttribute` calls no longer needed", Requirement 30.4), so they stay per method until that work; the 3.29 preview showed that `CloudWatchInterceptorOrderTest` passes only with these parameters |
| `withOperation` sets the operation name | The generated call adds `.withOperationName(<client name>)` after `withOperation` only when `ApiOperation.name()` differs from the IntermediateModel's operation name (`SchemaOperationRouting.renamesOperation`); no CloudWatch operation does | The operation name drives auth scheme and endpoint parameters and metrics, which must not change |
| "No per-operation marshaller, response handler or exception-mapper code" | When every operation the client class has methods for is routed, the client also has no protocol factory field, no `init` method and no `createErrorResponseHandler`/`createEventstreamErrorResponseHandler`. CloudWatch: `DefaultCloudWatchClient` drops from 6,995 to 3,629 lines and neither client imports a marshaller, `JsonOperationMetadata`, `HttpResponseHandler`, `ExceptionMetadata` or `SmithyRpcV2CborProtocolFactory` (Requirement 23.6). The sync client does not count event-stream operations, for which it has no methods | The factory's only users were the legacy calls |
| Operations the new path cannot run yet | Kept on the legacy marshaller and response handlers, with the reason in `SchemaOperationRouting.legacyReason`: event-stream input or output (7.20: the protocols reject event streams); streaming input (7.19: no request-body binding on the new path); **streaming output, sync and async** (7.19: the protocols cannot yet deserialize only the bound members; `AsyncApiCallPipeline` also rejects `AsyncResponseTransformer`); an input or output class without schema serde members (task 3.7 exclusions, for example a Smithy structure that is both an operation's input and its output, which the IntermediateModel turns into two classes); and, for every operation, an error class without schema serde members, because the service-wide Error_Registry can resolve it for any operation (23.7) and could not build it. CloudWatch has none of these: all 50 operations are routed, sync and async | `SyncApiCallPipeline` does accept a `ResponseTransformer`, but no protocol can deserialize a streaming output yet, so sync streaming output is gated as well |
| `services/cloudwatch` depends on `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` and defaults to rpcv2Cbor | Already done in task 3.23 (`CloudWatchProtocolSelectionTest`); unchanged. The `smithy-rpcv2-protocol` dependency is removed: the generated code no longer references `SmithyRpcV2CborProtocolFactory`, and `dependency:analyze` fails on an unused declared dependency. `aws-json-protocol` and `protocol-core` stay for the transform package | **Runtime classpath change:** `smithy-rpcv2-protocol` is no longer a transitive dependency of `cloudwatch`. No public CloudWatch type changes (japicmp reports the same 183 modified and 2 new types as in task 3.35) |
| Delete the transform package | Not done: the 50 `<Op>RequestMarshaller` classes are still generated (nothing references them) | Tasks 5.1/5.3 |
| `LegacyRouteParityTest` | `LegacyRouteParity` re-renders the pipeline's `Default<Svc>Client`/`Default<Svc>AsyncClient` of a client with runtime protocol selection from a view of the same IntermediateModel without its Smithy model (`withoutSchemaOperationRouting`), as it already does for model classes with schema serde members; `legacy-route-parity/cloudwatch.txt` is unchanged | The legacy route has no Smithy model and cannot route, so a direct comparison would report every client method. The routed calls are covered by the golden files and `CloudWatchNewPathTest` |

**Tests.** `codegen`: `SchemaClientClassTest` with the fixture `poet/client/schema-routing.smithy` (rpcv2Cbor, awsJson1_0 and awsQuery like CloudWatch; a routed operation with a modeled error and a host prefix, one with request compression and a required checksum, a paginated one, an event-stream operation and one whose input has a `BigInteger` member) and the new golden files `test-schema-sync-client-class.java` and `test-schema-async-client-class.java`; it also checks the routing decisions and reasons, that an error class without schema serde members keeps every operation on the legacy path, that a client whose operations are all routed has no protocol factory, error response handler, marshaller, `HttpResponseHandler`, `ExceptionMetadata` or `JsonOperationMetadata`, that the sync client ignores the event-stream operation for that decision, and that an awsJson1_0-default Smithy service and a C2J service get no routing. `services/cloudwatch`: `CloudWatchNewPathTest`, sync and async over stub `SdkHttpClient`/`SdkAsyncHttpClient`s answering in rpcv2Cbor, for `GetDashboard` (success; `DashboardNotFoundError` 404 with status, request ID and error code; `ResourceNotFoundException`, which `GetDashboard` does not list, through the service-wide registry; an unknown `__type` as `CloudWatchException`), the `ListMetrics` paginator over two pages and `PutMetricData` with the `REQUEST_COMPRESSION` attribute. At every read-only hook an interceptor checks the calling thread's stack: it contains `SyncApiCallPipeline` (sync) or `AsyncApiCallPipeline` (async) and no `RequestPipelineBuilder`/`RequestPipeline*`, legacy stage (`pipeline.stages.*`, except the shared `pipeline.stages.utils` helpers such as `ExceptionReportingUtils`) or `AmazonSyncHttpClient`/`AmazonAsyncHttpClient` frame; `API_OPERATION` is the `CloudWatchOperations` constant and `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` is `RpcV2CborProtocol`; the requests go to `/service/GraniteServiceVersion20100801/operation/<Op>` with `smithy-protocol: rpc-v2-cbor`. `CloudWatchInterceptorOrderTest`, `CloudWatchProtocolSelectionTest` and `SchemaSerdeModelClassTest` pass unchanged, now on the new path.

**Checked outside the suite (for task 3.34).** With `-DprotocolTests.enabledProtocols=smithy.protocols#rpcv2Cbor`, `test/smithy-protocol-tests` runs 89 tests with 6 failures (task 3.33 on the legacy marshallers: 7). `RpcV2Protocol` routes 5 of its 13 operations (`Float16`, `FractionalSeconds`, `GreetingWithErrors`, `NoInputOutput`, `OperationWithDefaults`); the other 8 (`EmptyInputOutput`, `OptionalInputOutput`, `RecursiveShapes`, `RpcV2CborDenseMaps`, `RpcV2CborLists`, `RpcV2CborSparseMaps`, `SimpleScalarProperties`, `SparseNullsOperation`) use one Smithy structure as input and output, so task 3.7 gives their classes no schema serde members and they stay on the legacy marshallers. Of the 6 failures, 3 are on the routed `OperationWithDefaults` (`RpcV2CborClientPopulatesDefaultValuesInInput`, `RpcV2CborClientUsesExplicitlyProvidedMemberValuesOverDefaults`, `RpcV2CborClientPopulatesDefaultsValuesWhenMissingInResponse`: default values) and 3 on the legacy `RpcV2CborSparseMaps`/`SparseNullsOperation` (null sparse-map values); the fractional-timestamp case that failed on the legacy marshallers passes on the new path. The query-compatible services pass. Task 3.34 needs schema serde members for shared input/output structures before the whole suite runs on the new path.

## `services/cloudwatch` public API compared with the Baseline_Build (task 3.35)

**Check.** `services/cloudwatch/pom.xml` adds `cloudwatch` to the root pom's japicmp `includeModules`. The `verify` phase compares the module jar with `software.amazon.awssdk:cloudwatch:${awsjavasdk.previous.version}` (2.49.6). That is the Baseline_Build: the branch's merge base is tag `2.49.6` plus the snapshot version bump, and the module's `service-2.json` is unchanged since then. The check fails if the baseline does not resolve (`ignoreMissingOldVersion=false`) and on any binary or source incompatible change. The failure message names each signature, for example `…CloudWatchBaseClientBuilder.protocol(…ClientProtocol):METHOD_REMOVED` (seen when the comparison was run once with the two jars swapped). Reports are in `target/japicmp/` (`japicmp.*` and `interfaces.*`).

| Root pom japicmp configuration | CloudWatch | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Accepts `METHOD_ADDED_TO_INTERFACE` | Not accepted | A new abstract method on a public client or builder interface breaks customer implementations (Requirement 16.3). New methods must be `default` (`METHOD_NEW_DEFAULT` stays accepted) |
| Accepts `METHOD_ABSTRACT_ADDED_IN_IMPLEMENTED_INTERFACE` | Accepted in the `default` execution. A second execution, `interfaces`, compares `*Client`, `*Builder` and `*Waiter` types without accepting it | japicmp 0.15.6 reports this change for all 166 final model classes that now implement `SerializableStruct`. It does not count `SerializableStruct`'s `default serialize()` as the implementation of `SerializableShape.serialize()`. japicmp reports the same change, correctly, for an interface that starts extending an interface with abstract methods (checked with a two-jar fixture), so the interface types are checked separately |
| `excludes`: `*.internal.*`, … | Same; nothing is excluded by signature | No removed or changed public signature is accepted (list below) |

**Accepted removed or changed public signatures:** none.

**Additions reported against 2.49.6.** All are compatible and are documented in the task 3.7 and 3.23 sections. `javap -protected` over both jars agrees with japicmp: 0 types removed, 0 members removed, and in 182 type headers the only change is the appended interface.

| Addition | Where | Documented in |
|---|---|---|
| `implements SerializableStruct` (and so `SerializableShape`); `public static final Schema SCHEMA`; `sdkSchema()`; `serializeMembers(ShapeSerializer)`; `<T> T getMemberValue(Schema)` | 166 structure, request, response and exception classes (all `public final`) | Task 3.7 |
| `implements SmithyEnum`; `public static final Schema SCHEMA`; `getValue()`; `sdkSchema()` | 16 enums | Task 3.7 |
| `default B protocol(ClientProtocol<?, ?>)` | `CloudWatchBaseClientBuilder` | Task 3.23 |
| New `public final` classes, both `@SdkInternalApi`: `model.CloudWatchOperations` (`SERVICE`, `ERROR_REGISTRY`, one `ApiOperation` per operation) and `model.CloudWatchSchemaIndex` | Model package | Tasks 3.15, 1.27 |
| `internal.CloudWatchServiceSchemas` (`public`) | Excluded by `*.internal.*` | Task 1.27 |

No other type is reported. The `interfaces` execution reports only `CloudWatchBaseClientBuilder` and its new default method.

Builder implementation classes gain `setMemberValue`, `deserialize`, `deserializeMember` and `sdkSchema` through `ShapeBuilder`. They are package-private, so they are not API and japicmp does not compare them. The public `Builder` interfaces are unchanged.

## `codegen` per-operation serde removed for schema-based services (task 5.1)

**Target.** `codegen`: `SchemaOperationRouting.routesEveryOperation(IntermediateModel, SchemaCodegen)` (new), `MarshallerGeneratorTasks`, `BaseClientBuilderClass` (new constructor with the `SchemaCodegen`), `CommonClientGeneratorTasks`, `CodeGenerator`. `services/cloudwatch`: `pom.xml`. Deviations are from design A.15 "Generators removed", D.5, K3/OD-14 and the task text.

A **schema-based service** is one whose client is routed by `SchemaOperationRouting` (task 3.32: runtime protocol selection, today rpcv2Cbor) and where every operation of the model is routed, including the event-stream operations that only the async client has methods for. Today: CloudWatch, the four rpcv2Cbor Protocol_Test_Services of `test/smithy-protocol-tests` (`RpcV2Protocol`, `SdkRpcV2Protocol`, `QueryCompatibleRpcV2Protocol`, `NonQueryCompatibleRpcV2Protocol`), `test/codegen-generated-classes-test`'s `sdkrpcv2` once it generates again (below), and any rpcv2Cbor fleet service of the non-default service profile whose operations are all routed. Every other service generates what it generated before.

| Design (A.15, D.5) or task | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `MarshallerGeneratorTasks` and every `poet/transform` spec removed for schema-based services | `MarshallerGeneratorTasks.hasTasks()` is false for a schema-based service: no `transform` package. The specs (`MarshallerSpec`, `JsonMarshallerSpec`, `QueryMarshallerSpec`, `XmlMarshallerSpec`, `EventStreamJsonMarshallerSpec`) stay | SQS, DynamoDB, Secrets Manager, Account, Route 53, SNS and the other protocol test services still call their marshallers until their protocols get a `ClientProtocolFactory` (7.x, 9.2–9.4) |
| `ProtocolSpec.errorResponseHandler`, `registerModeledExceptions`, `responseHandler`, `initProtocolFactory` and `JsonProtocolSpec`/`QueryProtocolSpec`/`XmlProtocolSpec`/`Ec2ProtocolSpec` removed | Already not called for routed operations, and no protocol factory, `init` or error response handler for a client whose operations are all routed (task 3.32, `SchemaOperationsProtocolSpec`). Checked for CloudWatch: no generated class outside the (now absent) `transform` package imports `protocols.json`, `protocols.core`, `protocols.rpcv2`, `JsonOperationMetadata`, `HttpResponseHandler` or `ExceptionMetadata`. The spec classes stay | Same: the legacy services still use them |
| — | The base client builder of a schema-based service no longer sets `SdkClientJsonProtocolAdvancedOption.ENABLE_FAST_UNMARSHALLER` (`aws-json-protocol`) | Only the legacy JSON protocol factory reads it, and a schema-based service's clients have none. It was the last reference from CloudWatch's generated code to a legacy protocol module |
| `ShapeModelSpec.staticFields` (the `SdkField` constants) and generated `toString` chains removed for schema-based services | No change in this task: task 3.7 already generates `sdkFields()`/`sdkFieldNameToField()` from `<Svc>SharedSerde.SDK_FIELDS` and `toString()` through `ToStringSerializer` for every class with schema serde members, in all seven prototype services. CloudWatch's model package has no `SdkField.builder` and no `ToString.builder` (the four `ToString.builder` users left in the module are the endpoint rules and JMESPath runtime classes). The generators stay for classes without schema serde members | Classes without schema serde members (C2J fixtures, DynamoDB's Kinesis streaming destination shapes, DynamoDB Streams, SNS `ListPhoneNumbersOptedOutResponse.toString`) still need them. Changing which classes get them would change the generated output of services that are not schema-based |
| C2J IntermediateModel builders removed from `codegen` | `CodeGenerator.Builder.models(C2jModels)` and its `IntermediateModelBuilder` branch in `execute()` are removed: `CodeGenerator` takes an `IntermediateModel` only. `IntermediateModelBuilder`, `C2jModels`, `AddShapes`, `AddInputShapes`, `AddOutputShapes`, `AddExceptionShapes`, `AddModelShapes`, `AddOperations`, `AddMetadata`, `AddEmptyInputShape` and `AddEmptyOutputShape` stay in `codegen` | No production code builds an IntermediateModel from C2J any more: `GenerationMojo` and `AwsSdkJavaCodegenPlugin` go through `CodegenPipeline`, and the removed builder method was used only by `CodeGeneratorTest`. The remaining classes are test infrastructure: `ClientTestModels` and about 35 codegen test classes build the C2J golden fixtures with them, and `LegacyRouteParityTest` (`codegen-maven-plugin`) generates the legacy route with them. Moving the fixtures to the Codegen_Pipeline would rewrite every golden file (schema serde members, Schemas) and remove the legacy-route comparison this task relies on; deleting them belongs with retiring those tests. `RemoveUnusedShapes` and `IntermediateModelShapeProcessor` are also used by `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilder` |
| "Update codegen golden-file tests to the new output" | Existing golden files are unchanged, except `poet/builder/test-schema-client-builder-class.java`, whose fixture is a schema-based service (one routed operation): `SchemaClientBuilderTest` now renders it with the model's `SchemaCodegen`, as `CommonClientGeneratorTasks` does, and it loses the `ENABLE_FAST_UNMARSHALLER` statement. New: `poet/client/test-schema-service-sync-client-class.java` and `test-schema-service-async-client-class.java` (the `schema-routing.smithy` fixture without its two legacy operations) | Services generated from C2J are not schema-based, so their golden files must not change |
| — | `services/cloudwatch/pom.xml` drops `aws-json-protocol` and `protocol-core` | Forced in this task: `dependency:analyze` fails on the two unused declared dependencies once the marshallers are gone. **Runtime classpath change:** neither module is a transitive dependency of `cloudwatch` any more. Task 5.3 still adds `NoMarshallerDependencyTest` |

**Accepted removed public signatures (`services/cloudwatch`, japicmp).** The 50 classes `software.amazon.awssdk.services.cloudwatch.transform.<Operation>RequestMarshaller` (each `@SdkInternalApi public`, implementing `software.amazon.awssdk.core.runtime.transform.Marshaller`, with a `(BaseAwsJsonProtocolFactory)` constructor and `marshall(<Operation>Request)`), for the operations AssociateDatasetKmsKey, DeleteAlarmMuteRule, DeleteAlarms, DeleteAnomalyDetector, DeleteDashboards, DeleteInsightRules, DeleteMetricStream, DescribeAlarmContributors, DescribeAlarmHistory, DescribeAlarms, DescribeAlarmsForMetric, DescribeAnomalyDetectors, DescribeInsightRules, DisableAlarmActions, DisableInsightRules, DisassociateDatasetKmsKey, EnableAlarmActions, EnableInsightRules, GetAlarmMuteRule, GetDashboard, GetDataset, GetInsightRuleReport, GetMetricData, GetMetricStatistics, GetMetricStream, GetMetricWidgetImage, GetOTelEnrichment, ListAlarmMuteRules, ListDashboards, ListManagedInsightRules, ListMetricStreams, ListMetrics, ListTagsForResource, PutAlarmMuteRule, PutAnomalyDetector, PutCompositeAlarm, PutDashboard, PutInsightRule, PutLogAlarm, PutManagedInsightRules, PutMetricAlarm, PutMetricData, PutMetricStream, SetAlarmState, StartMetricStreams, StartOTelEnrichment, StopMetricStreams, StopOTelEnrichment, TagResource and UntagResource. Reason: Requirement 17.12; they were internal API. `services/cloudwatch/pom.xml` excludes `software.amazon.awssdk.services.cloudwatch.transform.*` from the default japicmp execution (that package held only these classes in 2.49.6). Everything else is as in task 3.35: 183 modified and 2 new types, all additions.

**CloudWatch generated code.** The `transform` package (50 classes, 2,755 source lines, 217,124 uncompressed class bytes) is gone: 1,184 → 1,134 main classes, 6,061,439 → 5,844,064 uncompressed class bytes, 131,868 → 129,111 generated source lines. The only other change is the two lines of `DefaultCloudWatchBaseClientBuilder`. Model classes and the client classes (`DefaultCloudWatchClient` 3,629 lines, `DefaultCloudWatchAsyncClient` 4,036, since task 3.32) are unchanged. In `test/smithy-protocol-tests` the rpcv2Cbor services lose 16 marshallers (`RpcV2Protocol` 13, the other three 1 each).

**Tests.** `codegen`: `MarshallerGeneratorTasksTest` (no marshaller tasks when every operation is routed; all request marshallers when an event-stream operation keeps the service on the legacy path, for an awsJson1_0-default Smithy model and for a C2J model); `SchemaClientBuilderTest` (the updated golden file, no `ENABLE_FAST_UNMARSHALLER` for a schema-based service, kept for awsJson1_0); `SchemaClientClassTest` (the two new golden files). `codegen-maven-plugin`: `LegacyRouteParity` renders the marshallers of a schema-based service from the same view without the Smithy model that it already uses for the routed client classes, so the comparison still covers them; `legacy-route-parity/cloudwatch.txt` adds the removed builder import and statement. `services/cloudwatch`: `model.BaselineModelParityTest` loads the Baseline_Build jar (`target/baseline/cloudwatch-baseline.jar`, copied by a `maven-dependency-plugin` execution) child-first and, for each of its 167 model classes with a builder, compares `sdkFields()` (order, member name, marshalling type, location, location names, constructor and every trait's accessors, list member and map value fields included), the `sdkFieldNameToField()` keys, and, for an empty instance and one with every member set (nested structures, lists, maps, timestamps, blobs, enums stored as strings), the field values, `toString()` and `hashCode()`. CloudWatch has no sensitive members; redaction through `ToStringSerializer` stays covered by the codegen `SchemaSerdeModelClassTest`. The six other prototype services generate byte-identical sources (compared after a `-P quick` build).

**Not checked.** `test/codegen-generated-classes-test` does not build, independently of this task: the Codegen_Pipeline stops `sdkrpcv2` in stage 4 (Smithy model validation, before any code is generated) on three events, `RuleSetParameter.TestCase.RequiredMissing` and `RuleSetParameter.TestCase.Unused` (endpoint test cases without the required `Region`) and `rpcv2Cbor.NoDocuments` (`AllTypesStructure$MyDocument`).
