## `NoMarshallerDependencyTest` (task 5.3)

**Target.** `test/architecture-tests`: `serde.NoMarshallerDependencyTest`, `serde.MarshallerDependencyScanner`, `serde.MarshallerDependencyScannerTest`, and `ClassFiles` (class-file reading and artifact lookup, moved out of `PortComplianceChecker` so both ASM checks share it). `services/cloudwatch/pom.xml` is unchanged: `smithy-rpcv2-protocol` was dropped in 3.32 and `aws-json-protocol`/`protocol-core` in 5.1. `mvn dependency:tree -pl :cloudwatch` shows none of `protocol-core`, `aws-json-protocol`, `aws-query-protocol`, `aws-xml-protocol`, `aws-cbor-protocol` or `smithy-rpcv2-protocol`, directly or transitively.

| Task text | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Scans the CloudWatch JAR and the listed `sdk-core` new-path classes | Every class of the `SCHEMA_BASED_SERVICES` JARs (today `cloudwatch`), and `SyncApiCallPipeline`, `AsyncApiCallPipeline`, `RequestMutationStages`, `ProtocolMarshalling`, `ProtocolResponseHandling` and `PipelineCache` with their nested and anonymous classes. The classes these call into (`internal.http.transport`, the reused legacy request stages, `BaseClientHandler`'s routing) are not scanned | The task lists the scanned classes. `BaseClientHandler` routes to both paths by design until phase 6 |
| Fails on any reference to `*Marshaller`, `*Unmarshaller`, legacy protocol factories and per-operation response handlers | A reference is any class name in the class file (ASM `ClassRemapper`: the class itself, supertypes, descriptors, generic signatures, instruction operands, `ldc` constants, `invokedynamic` handles and arguments, annotations, `InnerClasses`). Five rules: simple name ends with `Marshaller` or `Unmarshaller`; a package segment `transform`, `marshall` or `unmarshall`; a package of a legacy protocol module (`protocols.core`, `.json`, `.query`, `.xml`, `.cbor`, `.rpcv2`, which covers every protocol factory, `JsonOperationMetadata`, `ExceptionMetadata`, `JsonResponseHandler` and the error unmarshallers); a type that is or extends `HttpResponseHandler` or `TransformingAsyncResponseHandler`, resolved from class files with ASM; and an SDK type whose class file is not on the test classpath, so that a missing dependency cannot hide a handler | The legacy per-operation handlers and their decorators (`CombinedResponseHandler`, `AsyncResponseHandler`, ...) all implement one of the two interfaces, and a hierarchy check also catches handlers that do not follow a naming pattern. String constants (for example `Class.forName` arguments) are not type references and are not checked |
| — | No allowlist | The types the new path shares with the legacy path (`ClientExecutionParams`, `ExecutionContext`, `ExceptionReportingUtils`, the reused request stages, `SdkAsyncHttpResponseHandler`, `aws-core`'s `EventStreamResponseHandler`) match no rule |
| — | `architecture-tests` declares test dependencies on `cloudwatch`, the six legacy protocol modules and `asm-tree` | `cloudwatch` was only transitive (through `cloudwatch-metric-publisher`). The legacy modules let `MarshallerDependencyScannerTest` check that every class of each one matches the legacy-protocol rule |

**Tests.** `MarshallerDependencyScannerTest`: ASM-generated classes that reference a legacy type through a field, a method call, a generic signature, an annotation, a nested class name, an `ldc` class constant, an `invokedynamic` method handle, `checkcast`/`instanceof`, and a scanned class that implements `HttpResponseHandler`; an SDK type not on the classpath; a class that references only shared types (no violation); SQS's legacy `DefaultSqsClient` and `SendMessageRequestMarshaller`; and every class of the six legacy protocol modules.

**CloudWatch, not covered by the rules.** Model classes still implement `SdkPojo` and reference `SdkField`, now built by `SdkFieldsFromSchema` (task 3.7, kept for compatibility), and the clients still call `AwsSyncClientHandler`/`AwsAsyncClientHandler`, which route `withOperation` calls to the new path (task 3.28).

## `test/size-report` (task 5.4)

**Target.** `test/size-report` (artifactId `size-report`, in the root reactor): `SizeReport`, `ClassAttributor`, `MemberCarver`, `ServiceSizes`, `ServiceComparison`, `ModuleComparison`, `ClassFileFingerprint`, `SizeReportWriter`; `scripts/schema-serde/build-size-baseline.sh` and `compiler_config.py`; the generated `docs/design/schema-serde/size-report.md`. `SizeReportDocumentTest` fails when the committed report differs from the one built from the JARs; `-Dsize-report.update=true` rewrites it.

| Task or design text | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Builds Baseline_Build and feature jars with the same JDK and flags | The Baseline_Build is commit `fc8b74e9fe21` (the merge base with `feature/master/smithy-migration`), built by `build-size-baseline.sh` in a worktree with the JDK and Maven that run the script, `-o clean install -P quick -pl <modules> -am`, into a separate local repository (`maven.repo.local.tail` reads the shared one). The JARs go to `~/.cache/aws-sdk-java-v2/size-baseline/<commit>/`, outside `target/`, so `mvn clean` keeps them. The feature JARs come from the reactor or local repository through test-scoped dependencies. The released `2.49.6` JAR that japicmp (3.35) and `BaselineModelParityTest` (5.1) use is not used here | Requirement 36.7 asks for the same JDK and compiler options. The released JAR was built with JDK 17 (`Build-Jdk-Spec: 17`); the same sources compiled with this JDK 21 differ (the 2.49.6 JAR has 951 classes, including a `JmesPathRuntime$1` enum switch map that JDK 21's javac does not emit, against 950, and 1,702,686 compressed bytes against 1,706,662). The merge base is also 17 commits past the `2.49.6` version bump (Smithy codegen work on `feature/master/smithy-migration`), so the 3.35 note that it is "tag 2.49.6 plus the snapshot version bump" is not exact. Those commits change no file under `services/cloudwatch`, and the two JARs' class lists differ only in `JmesPathRuntime$1` |
| Same JDK and flags (checked) | The script fails if the effective `maven-compiler-plugin` configuration of each service differs between the two checkouts (`help:effective-pom`), and records it with the JDK in `build-info.properties`. The report fails if the baseline JDK (`java.version`, `java.vendor`) is not the JDK running the build, if the service JARs' `Build-Jdk-Spec` differ, or if the class files differ in version or optional attributes (`-g`, `-parameters`) | The feature JARs carry no compiler record; these are the checks the artifacts allow |
| Each class in exactly one category; removed exception-mapping methods and registry fields by bytecode attribution (design D.5) | Each class has one category by name (rules in the report's Method section). Two categories are carved out of classes of another category: the static `ErrorRegistry`/`TypeRegistry` fields of `<Prefix>Operations` with their `<clinit>` segment and lambdas (Type_Registries), and the exception-mapping methods of client classes (`ExceptionMetadata` in the descriptor, signature or code, or a `lambda$*(String)Optional` mapper, plus the lambdas they create). A carve's size is the class written by ASM with a fresh constant pool minus the same class without the members, and the class's own category keeps the rest, so the categories add up to the total exactly | D.5 defines both categories by bytecode attribution inside classes that also hold other code. Measuring each carve as a difference of two writes counts the constant-pool entries only those members use, and defining the remainder as the class size minus the carves keeps the sum exact |
| Generated_Model_Class classes | All of the `model` package except `*Copier` and `<Prefix>SharedSerde`: structures, unions, exceptions, builders, enums and nested classes. `<Prefix>SharedSerde` (collection serde) and `*Copier` are Other; `<Prefix>Operations` is Client classes | The glossary entry covers POJOs and builders; D.5's reduction table counts enums as Generated_Model_Class and copiers as Other. `SharedSerde` is neither a model class nor a Schema; the report's "Largest changes" table shows it separately |
| Module JAR sizes (36.6, task 9.27) | Already recorded for CloudWatch's `software.amazon.awssdk` runtime dependencies: added, changed and dropped modules | The JARs are copied for both builds anyway |

**Result for CloudWatch.** Requirement 36.3 fails today: the compressed JAR grows from 1,706,662 to 1,969,660 bytes (+15.4%), and uncompressed class bytes from 5,349,563 to 5,844,064. Removed marshallers (−217,124), exception-mapping methods (−137,682) and client classes (−40,470) are outweighed by Generated_Model_Class classes (+507,412), `CloudWatchSharedSerde` (+285,704, in Other) and Schemas (+93,999). The report records the failure as Requirement 36.8 asks; reducing the size is for task 9.27 and the D.5 reduction opportunities.

**Tests.** `ClassAttributorTest` (the category rules; exception-mapping and registry carves on ASM-generated classes, each equal to the size difference of the class generated with and without the members); `ServiceComparisonTest` (category changes add up to the total change, each change in its category, every class in one category, Requirement 36.3 and the grown categories); `SizeReportTest` (a report from synthetic JARs, JDK mismatch and missing-baseline failures, the service list); `ClassFileFingerprintTest` (with EqualsVerifier); `SizeReportDocumentTest`.

## Racecar decision table (task 9.1)

**Target.** `docs/design/schema-serde/racecar-decisions.yaml` (one entry per racecar phase), `scripts/schema-serde/racecar_decisions.py` (Python 3 standard library only), the generated `docs/design/schema-serde/racecar-decisions.md`, and `RacecarDecisionTableTest` in `test/architecture-tests`.

| Task or design text | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Decisions from the D.6 table | Taken from D.6 and updated with what phases 2–4 implemented. Three decisions change: E14 (Superseded → Deferred: the generated union reader still uses the builder and its `EnumSet`), H4 and H10 (Applied → Deferred: `seedResolvedChecksumSpecs` is not called and the context factory still writes the client-level attributes, task 3.28). The Markdown lists them, and notes where a component differs from D.6's text | The implementation is the source of truth for what is applied |
| YAML source | A YAML subset (flat entries, one JSON literal per line) that the script reads without a YAML library | No script in `scripts/` uses PyYAML; JSON literals are also valid YAML |
| `RacecarDecisionTableTest` fails if the Markdown is stale, a phase id is missing, a decision is outside the vocabulary, or a phase 5 Applied row has no A/B record | The test runs `racecar_decisions.py --check`, which does all of these checks plus required fields and D2/H1/H5 staying Deferred, so the rules live in one place. It also runs `--list-classes` and loads every class an Applied or Superseded row names (`Class.forName` without initialization). The pom passes the script path and `racecar-decisions.python` (default `python3`) as surefire system properties | The classpath check needs the JVM; everything else is the script's, as `SizeReportDocumentTest` checks its generated report against a fresh run. Without the test: `python3 scripts/schema-serde/racecar_decisions.py --check` |
| A row "changed to Applied in this phase" | `applied_in_phase: 5` requires `ab_record`, a path relative to the YAML's directory that must exist (task 9.19 writes `optimizations/<id>.md`) | Phase 2 rows predate the 9.19 procedure |

**Tests.** `RacecarDecisionTableTest` (2 tests). The `--check` failures were tried by hand on mutated copies of the YAML (missing and repeated id, unknown decision, H5 not Deferred, empty reason, phase 5 Applied without a record, edited Markdown) and on an unknown class name.

## `test/architecture-tests` (task 1.19)

`PortComplianceTest` (package `software.amazon.awssdk.archtests.portcompliance`) scans the compiled classes of every module in `PortComplianceTest.PORTED_MODULES` (the 15 artifacts from design "Module layout"; skeleton modules are listed so they are checked as soon as code lands). `PortComplianceCheckerTest` holds the negative fixtures: ASM-generated classes calling `List.of` and `String.isBlank`, referencing `Flow` in a field and in a method body, a record (class version 60), a sealed type (61), a version-53 class, a type with two visibility annotations, a public type without one, plus a javac-compiled unannotated public fixture (`fixtures/UnannotatedPublicPortFixture`).

| Item | Design / task | Implemented | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| Name and location | `Java8ApiGuardTest` in each ported module, run with `animal-sniffer-maven-plugin` (design, Porting conventions) | One `PortComplianceTest` in `test/architecture-tests` (task 1.19) | The task places it in `architecture-tests` with one module list. A per-module plugin would need configuring in 15 poms and could not check annotations, records or sealed types |
| Java 9+ API detection | animal-sniffer `java18` signature (design) or ASM (task) | Both: ASM reads each class; animal-sniffer's `SignatureChecker` (library 1.24) checks every JDK reference against `org.codehaus.mojo.signature:java18:1.0` | The signature is the Java 8 API reference set, and animal-sniffer resolves inherited and covariant members correctly. A hand-rolled check against `ct.sym` would depend on the build JDK. Non-JDK packages referenced by the scanned classes are passed to animal-sniffer as wildcard ignores. JDK prefixes: `java`, `javax`, `jdk`, `sun`, `com.sun`, `org.w3c.dom`, `org.xml.sax`, `org.ietf.jgss`, `org.omg` |
| Extra check | — | Class file major version must be ≤ 52 | Requirement 1.3; catches a module built without `release 8` |
| Visibility annotations | "public types with zero or more than one SDK visibility annotation" | A public top-level type needs exactly one of `@SdkPublicApi`, `@SdkProtectedApi`, `@SdkInternalApi`. Every other type (nested, local, anonymous, non-public) may have at most one. `package-info` and `module-info` are skipped | The annotations have the default `CLASS` retention (no `@Retention` in `core/annotations`), so ASM sees them as invisible annotations. Nested types inherit the enclosing type's visibility, which matches existing SDK code |
| Records / sealed | — | `java/lang/Record` superclass or a `Record` attribute; a `PermittedSubclasses` attribute | — |
| `Flow` | — | Any reference anywhere in the class (supertypes, descriptors, signatures, annotations, method bodies, `invokedynamic`), collected with an ASM `ClassRemapper` | Requirement 1.5 |
| Module discovery | — | Each module is a test-scoped dependency. It is found through its `META-INF/maven/software.amazon.awssdk/<artifactId>/pom.properties` (packaged jar) or a `<artifactId>/target/classes` classpath entry. A listed module that is not on the classpath fails the test | Keeps the list in one Java constant. Adding a module means one constant entry plus one pom dependency |
| Java 8 runtime run (Requirement 1.9) | "Surefire run under the `jdk8` toolchain profile" | New root-pom profile `jdk8`, off by default. It sets Surefire `jdkToolchain` to `[1.8,1.9)` and clears `argLine`, because `jdk-13-plus` adds JDK 13+ JVM flags that a Java 8 JVM rejects. Compilation is unchanged. Run: `mvn test -P jdk8 -pl :schema,:codec-commons,:json-codec,:cbor-codec,:xml-codec,:http-binding,:http-client-protocol,:rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol,:aws-json-client-protocol,:rest-json-client-protocol,:rest-xml-client-protocol,:aws-query-client-protocol,:dynamic-client,:client-mock-plugin,:message-payloads`, with a JDK 8 `<toolchain>` (`<version>1.8</version>`) in `~/.m2/toolchains.xml` or `-t <file>` | Surefire's built-in `jdkToolchain` needs no `maven-toolchains-plugin` and only switches the forked test JVM. No JDK 8 is installed on the dev host, so the Java 8 run itself has not been executed. The wiring was checked: without a matching toolchain the build fails with "Requested toolchain specification did not match any configured toolchain"; with a toolchain entry labelled 1.8 (pointing at JDK 11) the tests forked on that JDK |

**`architecture-tests` and the restricted reactor (OD-7, task 1.1).** The module depended on non-prototype services that the default reactor no longer builds (`s3`, `sts`, `rds`, `iam`, `kinesis`, `transcribestreaming`, `glacier`, `sso`, `cloudfront`, and `s3-transfer-manager`, which needs `s3`). The fix is in `test/architecture-tests/pom.xml` only, and no test was deleted:

- Those dependencies moved into a `fleet-services` profile, matching `services/pom.xml`.
- `CodingConventionWithSuppressionTest` and `NamingConventionWithSuppressionTest` import S3 classes. They are left out of the default test compile (`testExcludes`) and compiled again under `-P fleet-services` (`<testExcludes combine.self="override"/>`).
- Without the fleet, ArchUnit would prune the absent classes' frozen violations from the committed `archunit_store` (`allowStoreUpdate=true`). So the default build copies the store to `target/archunit_store` and points `archunit.freeze.store.default.path` there. `-P fleet-services` uses the committed store.
- The remaining in-reactor dependencies (`emf-metric-logging-publisher`, `iam-policy-builder`, `s3-event-notifications`, `sns-message-manager`, `imds`) resolve once they are installed. The `-P fleet-services` path was checked only through `help:effective-pom`, because the fleet artifacts are not in the local repository.

All of them compile in phase 1; none runs by default.

| Design / task | Implemented | Reason |

Neither JAR is a module dependency, so neither is in `.brazil.json` |
| Projections: JsonRpc10, JsonProtocol, RestJson plus validation services, RestXml, RestXmlWithNamespace, AwsQuery, AwsEc2, RpcV2Protocol, `aws.protocoltests.rpcv2cbor#*` | Those 11 services plus `aws.protocoltests.json10#QueryCompatibleJsonRpc10` | It is a Protocol_Test_Service in the same model as JsonRpc10 and holds the awsJson1_0 `awsQueryCompatible` cases (Requirement 23.2) |
| — | Not generated: `com.amazonaws.glacier#Glacier`, `com.amazonaws.apigateway#BackplaneControlService`, `com.amazonaws.machinelearning#AmazonML_20141212`, `com.amazonaws.s3#AmazonS3` (tagged `aws-service-test`), and `smithy.protocoltests.rpcv2Json#RpcV2JsonProtocol` | The four AWS service models test service-specific customizations (design D.1 retires the API Gateway cases; S3 is out of the prototype). `rpcv2Json` is not a Supported_Protocol |

The generator applies only the entries for its service, or with no `service`. Repeated `(service, id, protocol)` is rejected | One skip file serves 12 services, and the generator validates entries against one service's cases, so every entry in this module's file names its service |

Example: `-DprotocolTests.enabledProtocols=smithy.protocols#rpcv2Cbor` (task 3.34); task 7.21 lists every Supported_Protocol | `none()` keeps the module's untagged tests (`ProtocolTestSkipsFileTest`) running. Generated class names end in a number (`...RequestTests1`), which the root pom's includes do not match. Checked: the default runs only `ProtocolTestSkipsFileTest`; `smithy.protocols#rpcv2Cbor, aws.protocols#awsQuery` runs exactly 72 + 77 generated tests (all fail with the expected "No ProtocolTestHarnessFactory" error until task 3.33) |

The task 1.3 `maven.javadoc.skip` and `mdep.analyze.skip` are removed | The full build (checkstyle, dependency analysis, javadoc) passes |

With one projection nothing changes | Each projection writes its own `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaIndex`; Maven kept only one of the 12 registrations. Covered by `SmithyBuildGeneratorTest.severalProjections_mergeTheirServiceFiles` |

**Skip entries.** One, by category: Smithy IntermediateModel gap, service `rename` not applied (`RestJsonSerializeRenamedStructureUnionValue`, restJson1). No case needed a skip for an event stream (the protocol test models put event-stream cases in `smithy.test#eventStreamTests`, which is not selected) or for a required `httpLabel` on a response case's empty input (no response or error case targets an operation with a path label). Runtime failures found once the harness exists are added in tasks 3.34 and 7.21.

**Codegen fixes found by the real models.** Each was a generation or compile failure. `codegen` (922 tests), `codegen-maven-plugin` (18, including `LegacyRouteParityTest` for the seven prototype modules) and `smithy-protocol-tests-core` (101) pass.

| `ShapeValueLiteralGenerator` (task 1.32) | A sparse list holding only `null` rendered `Arrays.<String>asList(null)`, which passes `null` as the varargs array (NPE at run time; javac warned) | Null elements are cast to the element type: `(String) null` | `requestTest_buildsTheInputFromParamsAndAssertsEveryRequestProperty` (expectation updated) |
| `AddSmithyMetadata` | A service with no `@auth` and no supported auth trait (RestJsonValidation, RpcV2Protocol) had an empty auth list and a null `authType`, and the auth scheme generators failed ("authType must not be null") | Effective auth `NONE` (`smithy.api#noAuth`). An explicit `@auth([])` still gives an empty list, as before | `AddSmithyMetadataTest.noAuthAtAll_isNoAuth`, `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilderTest.serviceWithoutAuth_hasNoAuth` |
| `AddSmithyModelShapes` | A union member targeting `smithy.api#Unit` (RestJson `PlayerAction`) had no shape (stage 5 `UNKNOWN_SHAPE_MEMBER`) | Synthesizes an empty `Unit` model shape, as C2J models declare one | `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilderTest.unionMemberTargetingUnit_getsAUnitModelShape` |
| `AddSmithyExceptionShapes`, `AddSmithyShapes`, `RemoveUnusedShapes` | An error structure that is an event-stream member but not in any operation's `errors` (RestJson `ErrorEvent`) got no exception shape, was marked an event, and was trimmed | Event-stream error structures get exception shapes and are not events (C2J: `exception`, not `event`); `RemoveUnusedShapes` resolves a member type that names an error shape to its exception key | `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilderTest.errorInEventStream_isAnExceptionNotAnEvent` |
| `AddSmithyShapes.buildHttpMapping` | An `@httpPayload` event stream (a `@streaming` union) was marked a streaming payload, so the async client generated `AsyncRequestBody`/`AsyncResponseTransformer` signatures that did not compile | Only a `@streaming` blob is streaming | same test |
| `ShapeInfo.ofSmithy` | `isOrContainsEnum` was true for an `intEnum`, which the IntermediateModel treats as a plain `Integer`, so a union with an `intEnum` member got two `from<Member>(Integer)` factories (JsonRpc10 `MyUnion`) | `intEnum` is not an enum here | `ShapeInfoTest.ofSmithy_intEnumIsNotAnEnum` (replaces `ofSmithy_intEnumIsAlsoOrContainsEnum`) |

**Known gaps, not fixed (generated code compiles; wire behavior is for phases 2 and 4).** The Smithy IntermediateModel does not apply a service `rename` (the skip entry above) and leaves `isGreedy` false for greedy labels (`AddSmithyShapes` TODO). The generated clients still use the legacy marshallers, as all Smithy-route clients on this branch do until phase 2.

## `test/smithy-protocol-tests-core` transport-backed harness (task 3.33)

**Target.** In `software.amazon.awssdk.protocol.smithytests` (test module, no API annotations): `CapturingClientTransport`, `FixedResponseTransport`, `RequestCaptured`, `FixedIdempotencyToken`, `TransportProtocolTestHarnessFactory`, and the package-private `TransportHttpClients`. `test/smithy-protocol-tests` registers the factory in its test `META-INF/services`. No smithy-java source (smithy-java runs protocol tests through `protocol-test-harness`, not ported); the legacy counterpart is `protocol-tests-core`'s mocked `SdkHttpClient`. The protocol test generator and its golden files are unchanged.

| Design (D.1) or task | Implemented | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `CapturingClientTransport` records the request and throws `RequestCaptured` | Implements both `ClientTransport` and `AsyncClientTransport` over `HttpMessageExchange`. Each send reads the body into a `CapturedRequest` and throws (sync) or fails the future (async) with `RequestCaptured`; the async hand-off is never called. Keeps every request (`requests()`) and the first (`firstRequest()`, `awaitFirstRequest(timeout)`) | `RequestCaptured` is a `RuntimeException` without a stack trace, neither `SdkException` nor `IOException`, so no SDK retry condition matches it and the call is not retried (tested with `RetryMode.STANDARD`: one request). The harness returns the capture whenever an exception follows a send, so it does not depend on how a pipeline wraps the exception |
| `FixedResponseTransport` returns `code`, `headers`, `body` (base64-decoded for CBOR) | Takes a `StubbedResponse`; `StubbedResponse.fromTestCase` (task 1.3) already base64-decodes CBOR bodies. One header value per test-case header, a fresh body stream per send. Async: when the hand-off's `onHeaders` returns `true`, the body goes to `onStream` and the future completes with the headers-only response; otherwise the buffered response | Same response semantics as `SdkAsyncHttpClientTransport` (task 3.17). It does not attach an `OwnedResponseBody` (transport-internal methods), so protocol tests read the body through the stream path, not the E11 owned-buffer path |
| Transport installed into the client | The harness applies the generator's configuration, sets `endpointOverride`, then puts the transport in `SdkClientOption.CLIENT_TRANSPORT` and `ASYNC_CLIENT_TRANSPORT` through reflection on `SdkDefaultClientBuilder`'s protected `clientConfiguration`. Builders that do not extend `SdkDefaultClientBuilder` fail with `IllegalArgumentException` | Neither option has a builder or override-configuration setter, and an `SdkPlugin` cannot set an `SdkClientOption`. If a public transport setter is added (tasks 3.23, 9.14), the harness should use it. Tasks 3.23/3.26 must default `CLIENT_TRANSPORT` only when it is absent, or the harness's transport is replaced (the HTTP-client view below would still capture) |
| Capture "at the Client_Transport boundary" (Requirement 32.3) | The harness also sets the client's HTTP client to a view (`TransportHttpClients`) that forwards each request, unchanged, to the same transport: `SdkHttpClient` for sync builders, `SdkAsyncHttpClient` for `SdkAsyncClientBuilder`s | The legacy pipeline sends through the HTTP client and never reads `CLIENT_TRANSPORT`. Until task 3.32 routes calls to the new path, a transport-only harness would let request tests reach the network. With both, no call opens a connection on either path, and the request reaches the transport as the client serialized and signed it. The harness does not show which pipeline ran: `CloudWatchNewPathTest` (3.32) and task 3.34 check that |
| Generated tests use sync clients only | The harness also accepts async builders: `captureRequest` waits up to 10 s for the send before closing the client, so the call may return the future without joining it; `respondWith` returns whatever the call returns (join the future in the call) | For hand-written async tests (Property 50, task 3.34). Async capture reads the request's `contentStreamProvider` only; a streaming async request body passed through the internal `TransportExecutionAttribute.ASYNC_REQUEST_BODY` is not captured on the new path (no generated test needs it) |
| "A fixed `IdempotentUtils` generator hook" | `FixedIdempotencyToken.install()` sets `IdempotentUtils.setGenerator(() -> TOKEN)` and `close()` restores the previous generator once. `captureRequest` installs it around each call, in addition to the generated `@BeforeAll` | Uses the existing `@SdkTestInternalApi` hook; no production API. Installing it in the harness gives hand-written request tests the fixed token too. `TOKEN` (`00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000000`) is the generator's literal; the two must stay equal |
| `ProtocolTestHarnessFactory` registration (task 1.32: "Task 3.33 registers the transport-backed factory") | Registered in `test/smithy-protocol-tests/src/test/resources/META-INF/services`, not in `smithy-protocol-tests-core`'s main resources | `codegen`'s `GeneratedProtocolTestsCompileTest` registers its own `HttpClientHarnessFactory` on a class path that includes `smithy-protocol-tests-core`, and `ProtocolTestHarnesses` rejects two registrations. Other consumers (for example `services/cloudwatch` tests) register it themselves or call the factory directly. Follow-up: the codegen test could use this factory and drop `HttpClientHarnessFactory` |
| `forClient(protocol, ...)`: "passing the protocol lets the factory set a protocol override" | `protocol` is used only in failure messages | Every Protocol_Test_Service in `test/smithy-protocol-tests` declares one protocol, so its client's default is the case's protocol. The override (task 3.23 builder `protocol(...)`) can be added here if a multi-protocol service is added |
| — | Each `captureRequest`/`respondWith` builds a new client and closes it after the call; a close failure is suppressed into the call's failure | Matches "fresh client per call" in `ProtocolTestHarness`; closing releases the executors the SDK creates |

**Tests** (`test/smithy-protocol-tests-core/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/protocol/smithytests/`): `CapturingClientTransportTest` (exchange; method, host, encoded path, decoded query, multi-value header, body; `RequestCaptured` carries the recorded request and has no stack trace; empty body; every send recorded; async failed future without calling the hand-off; await timeout), `FixedResponseTransportTest` (status, headers and base64-decoded CBOR body sync and async; fresh stream per send and recorded requests; empty body; hand-off taking the stream gets the body and a headers-only response; declining hand-off gets the buffered response), `FixedIdempotencyTokenTest` (install and restore; nested installs; second close) and `TransportProtocolTestHarnessFactoryTest`, which runs the harness against `EchoClients`, a hand-written sync and async client on the legacy pipeline (`SdkSyncClientHandler`/`SdkAsyncClientHandler`): the captured request carries the endpoint, path, query, headers, the generator configuration's header and the body, sync and async (joined and not); the idempotency token is fixed during the call and restored after; both transport options hold the capturing transport and a `RetryMode.STANDARD` client sends exactly one request; a failure before the send propagates; a call that sends nothing fails with `AssertionError`; fixed responses (status, headers, CBOR base64 decode) sync and async; error statuses propagate the client exception; a builder that is not an `SdkDefaultClientBuilder` is rejected.

**Checked outside the suite.** With `protocolTests.enabledProtocols=smithy.protocols#rpcv2Cbor` and the factory registered, the 72 generated rpcv2Cbor tests ran through this harness on the legacy `smithy-rpcv2-protocol` marshallers: 65 passed, 7 failed on serialization behaviors of those marshallers (defaults, sparse-map nulls, fractional timestamps), not on the harness; task 3.34 runs them on the new path. That run temporarily left the `restJson1-RestJson` projection out of `smithy-build.json`, because generation of that projection currently fails with `IllegalStateException: Shape smithy.api#Unit has no schema` (`SchemaSerdeModel`, task 3.7), independently of this task.

## rpcv2Cbor protocol tests on the new path (task 3.34)

**Target.** `test/smithy-protocol-tests` runs `smithy.protocols#rpcv2Cbor` by default (`protocolTests.enabledProtocols`). All 13 `RpcV2Protocol` operations, the two query-compatible services' operations and the new SDK-owned `SdkRpcV2Protocol` run on `SyncApiCallPipeline`. Result: **74 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (76)** under `smithy.protocols#rpcv2Cbor`: the 72 generated tests (70 pass, 2 skipped) plus the 4 hand-written `UnknownEnumValueProtocolTest` cases. The module runs 97 tests in total; the rest are untagged module tests, including the new `RpcV2CborNewPathTest`. Four fixes were needed: three runtime or codegen defects, and the legacy `smithy-rpcv2-protocol` dependency, which is now unused.

| Component | Problem | Fix | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SchemaSerdeModel`, `SchemasGenerator`, `SchemaSerdeClassSpec` (task 3.7) | A Smithy structure that several request or response classes represent had no schema serde members, so its operations stayed on the legacy marshallers. Examples: a structure that is both an operation's input and its output (8 of the 13 `RpcV2Protocol` operations), or the input of two operations (DynamoDB `KinesisStreamingDestinationInput`/`Output`). A Root_Schema names one `shapeClass` and one builder | When every class of the shape is a request or response class, each one gets the members and holds its own copy of the structure's schema (`Entry.isSharedShape()`, `SchemasGenerator.classSchemaInitializer`). The copy has the same ShapeId, traits and members (targets read from the partition fields) plus that class's builder supplier and class. The structure's partition field keeps no builder, and `<Svc>Operations` uses each class's `SCHEMA`. A shape that a model class also represents is still excluded | `SchemaSerdeSharedShapeTest` (golden `echorequest.java`, `sharedoperations.java`; compiled package: distinct schemas with the same ID, each building its own class, `ApiOperation` input and output builders) |
| `CborSerializer.writeTimestamp` (task 3.6) | A whole-second timestamp was written as `tag 1` + integer, as smithy-java does. The legacy `SdkRpcV2CborGenerator` and the rpcv2Cbor fixtures write `tag 1` + double, so `RpcV2CborLists` and `...ExplicitlyProvidedMemberValuesOverDefaults` failed, and CloudWatch on the new path sent a different wire value than the Baseline_Build | Always `tag 1` + double-precision epoch seconds, which the protocol allows. Reading still accepts integer and float | `Rpcv2CborCodecTest.timestamps_areTag1EpochSecondsAsDoubles` (expectations changed from integers), `timestamps_readIntegerAndFloatEpochSeconds` |
| `SharedSerdeSpec` (task 3.7) | A null list or map value in a sparse collection was stored by the builder's copier as `DefaultSdkAutoConstructList`/`Map`, so it was written as `[]`/`{}`. A null read from the wire stayed `null`, which differs from what the builder and the legacy unmarshallers (through the setter) store. Cases: `RpcV2CborSerializesSparseSetMapAndRetainsNull`, `RpcV2CborDeserializesSparseSetMapAndRetainsNull` | Sparse collections only: an auto-construct list or map element is written as null, and a null list or map element is read as the auto-construct instance. Dense collections and other element types are unchanged | `SharedSerdeSparseCollectionTest` (golden `sparsesharedserde.java`, compiles) |
| `test/smithy-protocol-tests/pom.xml` | `smithy-rpcv2-protocol` was an unused declared dependency once no generated client referenced `SmithyRpcV2CborProtocolFactory` (`dependency:analyze` failure) | Removed | Module build |

**Skips** (`protocol-test-skips.json`, chosen with the user over populating defaults). `RpcV2CborClientPopulatesDefaultValuesInInput` and `RpcV2CborClientPopulatesDefaultsValuesWhenMissingInResponse` are skipped. Generated model classes do not populate `@default` values: an unset member is null and is neither serialized nor filled in from a missing response member, as in the legacy SDK. smithy-java skips both cases as well. The input case also cannot pass with a strict CBOR comparison: its fixture encodes `defaultTimestamp` as `c1 00` (integer), while the other cases, and the codec, use doubles. The two other default cases (`SkipsTopLevelDefaultValuesInInput`, `IgnoresNonTopLevelDefaultsOnMembersWithClientOptional`) pass. A follow-up decision is needed: either builder fields start at their `@default` value except for `@input`/`@clientOptional` members (NullableIndex `CLIENT`), or the skips stay. The first option would change getters of Smithy-native models; for example, Secrets Manager `ValidateResourcePolicyResponse.policyValidationPassed()` would return `false` instead of `null` when absent.

**Hand-written tests** (package `software.amazon.awssdk.protocol.smithytests` in `test/smithy-protocol-tests`; per-protocol methods carry `@Tag("protocol:<id>")`, so `protocolTests.enabledProtocols` gates them and the summary counts them):

| Test | Design / task | Implemented | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| `UnknownEnumValueProtocolTest` | Through `respondWith` against an SDK-owned client | New SDK-owned service `software.amazon.awssdk.protocoltests.rpcv2cbor#SdkRpcV2Protocol` (`smithy/sdk-protocol-tests/rpcv2-cbor.smithy`, projection `rpcv2Cbor-SdkRpcV2Protocol`, no Smithy cases) with an enum member, an enum list and enum-keyed and enum-valued maps. Four rpcv2Cbor cases for legacy rows #5, #7, #9 and #10, with base64 CBOR bodies: the `...AsString(s)` views keep the wire string; the enum views return `UNKNOWN_TO_SDK_VERSION` for the member, list element and map value | The enum-keyed view leaves an unknown key out. That is the generated map copier's behavior for every SDK model class, and the legacy test asserts only the string view |
| `NullDocumentProtocolTest` | Class added, no rpcv2Cbor case, JSON cases enabled in 7.21 | awsJson1_1 (`SdkJsonProtocol.SdkDocuments`, new `documentValue` member) and restJson1 (`SdkRestJson.SdkDocuments`, new output `SdkDocumentsOutput { documentValue }`) cases for `{"documentValue": null}` | Run once outside the suite with `-Dtest=NullDocumentProtocolTest -DprotocolTests.enabledProtocols=aws.protocols#awsJson1_1,aws.protocols#restJson1`: both pass on the legacy path |
| `RpcV2CborNewPathTest` (untagged) | "Confirm the generated clients take the new path" | For `RpcV2Protocol` (13), `QueryCompatibleRpcV2Protocol`, `NonQueryCompatibleRpcV2Protocol` and `SdkRpcV2Protocol` (1 each), it calls every client operation through `captureRequest`. At `beforeTransmission` it asserts `API_OPERATION` (matching the operation name), `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` as `RpcV2CborProtocol`, a `SyncApiCallPipeline` frame, and no `RequestPipelineBuilder` or legacy stage frame | The generated tests do not show which pipeline ran |

Before this task, the ad hoc runs gave awsJson1_0 63/6/0, awsJson1_1 125/3/0, restJson1 258/8/1, restXml 196/1/0, awsQuery 77/0/0, ec2Query 63/0/0 and rpcv2Cbor 74/0/2 (passed/failed/skipped); the first run of this task reproduced them exactly, so nothing had regressed.

**Fix: a structure that a model class and request or response classes all represent (`codegen`).**

| Component | Problem | Fix | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SchemaSerdeModel` (tasks 3.7 and 3.34) | A Smithy structure that is an operation's input or output and also a member target has a model class besides its request and response classes, and 3.34 kept such shapes out of schema serde. Each class without members kept every structure that reaches it legacy too. awsJson1_1 `KitchenSink` (input and output of `KitchenSinkOperation`, target of `ErrorWithMembers.ComplexData` and of its own recursive members) kept all of `JsonProtocol` legacy, because `ErrorWithMembersException` had no members and an error without them keeps every operation legacy (task 3.32). restJson1 `aws.protocoltests.shared#GreetingStruct` (input of the server-only `MalformedContentTypeWithBody`, value of `JsonMaps`/`SparseJsonMaps` maps) kept `JsonMaps` and `SparseJsonMaps` legacy | When at most one class of the structure is not a request or response class, that class owns the partition field with its builder, so members that target the structure reference it, and every request and response class holds its own copy (`Entry.isSharedShape()`). Two or more non-request classes are still excluded. `SchemasGenerator.classSchemaInitializer` accepts a copy whose member targets the structure itself when the structure has an owning class | `SchemaSerdeSharedShapeTest` (+2): a structure that is an input, an output, its own member target, a list element and an error member gives the model class the partition schema with its builder; the compiled package's request copy, the model class and the error all reference the model class's schema, including the recursive member |

Cases fixed by it, all legacy-marshaller failures: `AwsJson11SparseMapsSerializeNullValues` (null sparse map values were dropped), `serializes_structure_members_with_locationname_traits` (`jsonName` was applied), `AwsJson11ComplexError` (the nested error member was not read), `RestJsonSerializesSparseNullMapValues` (null sparse map values were dropped) and `RestJsonSerializesSparseSetMapAndRetainsNull` (the legacy marshaller wrote the invalid JSON `{"sparseSetMap":{...,"z"}}`). Routing now: every operation of every Protocol_Test_Service is on the new path except restJson1 `JsonUnions` and the event-stream operations. `JsonUnions` stays legacy because its union member `renamedStructureValue` targets `aws.protocoltests.restjson.nested#GreetingStruct`, which has no class: the Smithy IntermediateModel builder does not apply the service `rename` (the existing `RestJsonSerializeRenamedStructureUnionValue` skip). Its other cases pass on the legacy path, so it needs no new skip.

Generated code outside the protocol tests: no prototype service has such a structure (no C2J member, list member, map value or error of CloudWatch, DynamoDB, SQS, Secrets Manager, Account, Route 53 or SNS targets an operation input or output), so their generated code is unchanged: `codegen` 1020 tests, `codegen-maven-plugin` 67 (`LegacyRouteParityTest` 7 and `NoTransformPackageTest` 8 with no list changed). In `test/codegen-generated-classes-test`, the rpcv2Cbor model `sdkrpcv2` has two (`AllTypesStructure`, `NestedContainersStructure`), so their classes and operations now have schema serde members. That module's run has the same 40 failures, test for test, as the checkpoint 6 run (`EndpointDiscoveryAndEndpointOverrideTest`, `HostPrefixTest`, `HttpChecksumInHeaderTest`, `HttpChecksumRequiredTest`, `CustomSdkShapeTest`, `MoveQueryParamsToBodyTest`, `BlobUnmarshallingTest`, `RpcV2CborUserAgentTest` and others), none of them new.

**Hand-written tests.** `UnknownEnumValueProtocolTest` now has one `@Nested` class per Supported_Protocol, tagged with the protocol (JUnit inherits the tag, so Surefire selects the class and the summary counts it under the protocol), running the same four cases (legacy rows json-core-output.json#5, #7, #9 and #10) through `ProtocolTestHarness.respondWith` against an SDK-owned client. New SDK-owned services: `software.amazon.awssdk.protocoltests.json10#SdkJsonRpc10` (`smithy/sdk-protocol-tests/aws-json-1-0.smithy`, projection `awsJson1_0-SdkJsonRpc10`, operations `SdkEnums` and `SdkDocuments`) and `software.amazon.awssdk.protocoltests.query#SdkQuery` (`aws-query.smithy`, projection `awsQuery-SdkQuery`, operation `SdkEnums`), neither with Smithy cases. `SdkJsonProtocol`, `SdkRestJson`, `SdkRestXml` and `SdkEc2` gain an `SdkEnums` operation with an output-only `SdkEnumsOutput` (enum member, enum list, enum-keyed map, enum-valued map). Bodies: JSON for the JSON protocols, the 3.34 base64 CBOR for rpcv2Cbor, `<SdkEnumsOutput>` for restXml, `<SdkEnumsResponse><SdkEnumsResult>` for awsQuery and `<SdkEnumsResponse>` for ec2Query. Result: 4 of 4 on the new path for each of the seven protocols: the `...AsString(s)` views keep `NewValue`, the enum views return `UNKNOWN_TO_SDK_VERSION` for the member, the list element and the map value, and the enum-keyed view leaves the unknown key out (the map copier's behavior for every generated class). `NullDocumentProtocolTest`: awsJson1_0 (`SdkJsonRpc10.SdkDocuments`), awsJson1_1 and restJson1, 3 of 3 on the new path (`{"documentValue": null}` reads as `Document.fromNull()`).

**Skips** (`protocol-test-skips.json`, 16 entries: 3 before this task and 13 added).

| Case | Protocol | Reason (summary) |
|---|---|---|
| `RestJsonSerializeRenamedStructureUnionValue` (task 1.33) | restJson1 | The Smithy IntermediateModel builder ignores the service `rename`, so the renamed union member target has no class |
| `RpcV2CborClientPopulatesDefaultValuesInInput`, `RpcV2CborClientPopulatesDefaultsValuesWhenMissingInResponse` (task 3.34) | rpcv2Cbor | `@default` values are not populated, as in the legacy SDK (chosen with the user) |

| `AwsJson10ClientPopulatesDefaultsValuesWhenMissingInResponse`, `RestJsonClientPopulatesDefaultsValuesWhenMissingInResponse` | awsJson1_0, restJson1 | A member missing from the response stays null; same decision |
| `AwsJson10ClientPopulatesNestedDefaultValuesWhenMissing`, `RestJsonClientPopulatesNestedDefaultValuesWhenMissing` | awsJson1_0, restJson1 | `@default` values of members of nested structures, list elements and map values are not populated on input; same decision |
| `AwsJson10ClientPopulatesNestedDefaultsWhenMissingInResponseBody`, `RestJsonClientPopulatesNestedDefaultsWhenMissingInResponseBody` | awsJson1_0, restJson1 | The same for output; same decision |
| `AwsJson10ClientErrorCorrectsWhenServerFailsToSerializeRequiredValues`, `AwsJson10ClientErrorCorrectsWithDefaultValuesWhenServerFailsToSerializeRequiredValues` | awsJson1_0 | No client error correction: a `@required` output member the response omits stays null instead of taking its zero or `@default` value, as in the legacy SDK; part of the same open `@default` decision |
| `RestJsonHttpChecksumRequired` | restJson1 | SDK checksum behavior: since 2.30.0 the shared `HttpChecksumStage` sends a CRC32 flexible checksum (`x-amz-checksum-crc32`) for `@httpChecksumRequired`, and `Content-MD5` only when no algorithm resolves (`HttpChecksumUtils.isMd5ChecksumRequired`). Both paths fail the case the same way; the protocol's request matches it otherwise |
| `SdkRestJsonNoContentWithBlobPayload`, `SdkRestXmlNoContentWithBlobPayload` | restJson1, restXml | **Behavior change, open question.** On the schema path an empty body leaves a blob `@httpPayload` unset (null), as the upstream `RestJsonHttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody`/`HttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody` require (both pass); the legacy SDK returns empty `SdkBytes`, which these SDK-owned cases (legacy-disposition.csv `rest-core-output.json#6`) expect |

**Not covered.** Event streams: the generator does not select `smithy.test#eventStreamTests`, and event-stream operations stay on the legacy path (task 7.20 stages (c)–(e) deferred by the user). `legacy-disposition.csv` still records `rest-core-output.json#6` as ported to the two skipped SDK-owned cases; it changes once the blob decision is made.

**Open questions.** (1) Empty blob `@httpPayload`: keep null (upstream Smithy, smithy-java) or restore legacy empty `SdkBytes` on the new path (then skip the two upstream cases instead). (2) `@default` population and client error correction: 12 skips across awsJson1_0, restJson1 and rpcv2Cbor wait on one decision (task 3.34). (3) `RestJsonHttpChecksumRequired` is skipped as SDK checksum behavior rather than changed to `Content-MD5`.

**For checkpoint 8.** Build the protocol modules and `smithy-protocol-tests` as checkpoint 8 lists; the default `mvn clean install -pl :smithy-protocol-tests` now runs all seven protocols and should report the table above. `codegen` must be installed first: the fix above is in `codegen`, which the `codegen-maven-plugin` loads from the local repository. `test/codegen-generated-classes-test` has 40 failures that predate this task (same list as checkpoint 6). Properties 21, 23–25 and 39 and the streaming tests are not run by this module.

## Checkpoint 6: Smithy validation for C2J test models

**Target.** Since task 1.22, stage 4 of `CodegenPipeline` validates every converted C2J model and fails on `ERROR` and `DANGER` events. The 30 C2J models of `test/codegen-generated-classes-test` then failed to generate, and once they generated, some tests did not compile. Each failure was either a C2J_Converter or Smithy-route loader gap, fixed with a test, or a C2J form that a test model uses on purpose and that a Smithy model cannot hold, suppressed per service with a recorded reason. Real services stay strictly validated. `test/protocol-tests` is not covered: it is not in the reactor (Requirement 32.9; `test/smithy-protocol-tests` replaces it).

**Converter and loader fixes.**

| Component | Problem | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `C2jInput` | Test models annotate shapes with `//` comments (`endpointproviders`), and stage 1 failed | Java comments enabled, as codegen's C2J loader does |
| C2J_Converter | codegen's C2J loader spells the big number types `bigdecimal`/`biginteger`; the converter knew only camel case (`waiters` failed in stage 3) | Both spellings converted |
| C2J_Converter | codegen reads rule-set parameter types ignoring case; Smithy accepts only `stringArray` (`stringarray`'s `StringArray` failed assembly) | Parameter type normalized to `stringArray` |
| C2J_Converter | 25 test models ship an empty `endpoint-tests.json`; as `@endpointTests` every parameter is reported unused (DANGER) and every required one missing (ERROR) | Omitted; codegen generates no endpoint tests for an empty suite either way |
| C2J_Converter | A `location: querystring` map was bound with `@httpQuery`, which Smithy does not allow on a map (also used by apigateway, amp, route53globalresolver) | Bound with `@httpQueryParams`; a `locationName` on such a member is recorded as a gap |
| C2J_Converter | C2J can mark a member streaming instead of its blob target; the converter put `@streaming`/`@requiresLength` on the member, where Smithy does not allow them | The member targets a streaming blob (the C2J blob, or a `<Name>Streaming` copy when non-streaming members also use it); optional streaming members get `@default("")`, as in S3's published model |
| C2J_Converter | Seven REST test models have an event stream output member and no `payload`; Smithy requires `@httpPayload` on it | `@httpPayload` added when the event stream is the structure's only member without an HTTP location (published AWS models always name the payload) |
| `AddSmithyOperations` | An operation whose `@auth` lists several schemes or sigv4a got the auth type `V4A`, which has no legacy signer ("Unsupported authtype for AWS Request: V4A") | Such operations get no auth type, as their C2J auth list did |
| `AddSmithyMetadata` | Without `@auth`, only sigv4 or else bearer became the service auth list; the converter omits `@auth` when a C2J auth list is already in Smithy's order (`environmenttokenprovider`: `[sigv4, httpBearerAuth]`), and sigv4a (`sigv4aonly`) was never listed | Every applied sigv4, sigv4a and bearer scheme, in `ServiceIndex` order; `NONE` if there is none |
| `test/codegen-generated-classes-test/pom.xml` | The rpcv2Cbor test client (`sdkrpcv2`) now generates and runs on the new path (task 3.23), and did not compile without `RpcV2CborProtocol` | Depends on `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` |
| `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilder` | The `endpointParameters` and `customOperationContextParams` customizations were not applied (`endpointproviders`, `restjson`: `OperationContextParametersTest`, `MapEndpointParamTest` did not compile) | Both routes call the same `IntermediateModelBuilder` methods |
| `AddSmithyShapes.buildHttpMapping` | An RPC protocol ignores HTTP bindings, so a streaming C2J `payload` member (kept as `@httpPayload`) was not the payload, and the awsQuery test client had no `RequestBody`/`AsyncResponseTransformer` overloads (`AsyncOperationCancelTest` did not compile) | A top-level `@httpPayload` streaming blob is the payload in every protocol, as on the C2J route. `ProtocolTestGeneratorTest`'s awsJson1_0 `@httpPayload` stream case is now generatable (`rpcProtocolHttpPayloadStream_isPassedAsARequestBody`) |
| `AddSmithyOperations` | An operation `@auth([])` (the converter's form of C2J `authtype: none`) stayed authenticated with no auth type and was signed (`NoneAuthTypeRequestTest`) | Unauthenticated, auth type `NONE`, auth `[NONE]`, as `authtype: none` on the C2J route |

**Suppression mechanism.** `customization.config` key `smithyValidationSuppressions`: a list of `{id, shapes?, reason}` (`SmithyValidationSuppression`, applied by `SmithyValidationSuppressions` in stage 4).

- Only C2J models, and only in modules whose POM sets the `codegen-maven-plugin` parameter `allowSmithyValidationSuppressions` (no user property, so not settable from the command line). `GenerationMojo` rejects it for modules whose parent is `services`. Without it, a model with suppressions fails generation. Smithy's own `@suppress` and metadata suppressions cannot be used: they never apply to `ERROR` events.
- `reason` is required; `shapes` must be absolute shape IDs, and an entry with shapes matches only events on those shapes. `id` matches as `ValidationEvent.containsId` (so `HttpMethodSemantics.UnexpectedPayload` is specific).
- `Model`, `Target` and `ResourceCycle` cannot be suppressed, and loading is checked with validation disabled first. With suppressions, validation runs in Smithy's legacy mode so that a suppressed critical validator (`TraitTarget`, `TraitValue`) does not stop the others.
- An entry that matches no `ERROR` or `DANGER` event is stale and fails generation. Each applied entry is logged as a warning with the service, event count and reason.
- `C2jTestModelsPrepareTest` (`codegen-maven-plugin`) runs stages 1 to 5 on every C2J model of the module, as `GenerationMojo` does, so a converter change that breaks one fails there.

**Suppressed events** (each also recorded in the model's `customization.config`):

| Event ID | Models | Why a suppression, not a fix |
|---|---|---|
| `SdkServiceId` | 15 models (`query`, `xml`, `h2`, `stringarray`, ...) | Test service IDs contain "Amazon"/"Aws" or end in "Service"; the generated client and package names derive from them and the tests use those names |
| `ExclusiveStructureMemberTrait` | 7 (shape `IdempotentOperationStructure`) | An idempotency token in a header, a URI label and the query string at once, to test each binding |
| `HttpMethodSemantics.UnexpectedPayload` | 8 | GET/DELETE operations with body members, to test that the SDK marshals them as C2J defines |
| `HttpUriConflict` | 6 | Test operations share request URIs; the client never routes by URI |
| `RuleSetParameter.RuleSet.UnmatchedName` | `bearerauth`, `mixedauth`, `mixedauthservice`, `multiauth`, `endpointproviders` | Shared test rule sets declare parameters the model never binds; in `endpointproviders`, `StaticStringParam` vs `staticStringParam`, which codegen matches ignoring case |
| `TraitValue` | `customresponsemetadata`, `serviceconfig` (one shape each) | Lowercase response checksum algorithms; the SDK matches them ignoring case |
| `TraitTarget` | `tostring` (one member), `documenttype` (two members) | A member-level C2J `sensitive` flag; a `RegistryName` URI label member on an operation without that label |
| `HttpLabelTrait`, `HttpPayload` | `documenttype` (same members) | Same `RegistryName` binding |
| `StreamingTrait` | `endpointproviders`, `waiters` (one shape) | A `@requiresLength` streaming structure used as both input and output |
| `WaitableTrait` | `waiters` (one shape) | Test waiters with only failure and retry acceptors |
| `RuleSetAuthSchemes` | `endpointbasedauth` | A sigv4a `signingRegionSet` that Smithy's validator rejects, to test endpoint-based auth resolution |
| `RuleSetTestCase`, `RuleSetParameter.TestCase.RequiredMissing`, `RuleSetParameter.TestCase.Unused` | `stringarray` | Endpoint test cases with an empty `stringArray` and without the required `Region` |
| `rpcv2Cbor.NoDocuments` | `sdkrpcv2` (one member) | A document member, to test documents in rpcv2Cbor |

None of these hides a converter gap that a prototype service hits: the prototype services are validated without suppressions, and `LegacyRouteParityTest` shows their generated sources unchanged.

**Open: Smithy-route behavior gaps in `test/codegen-generated-classes-test`.** The module generates and compiles, and 1633 of its 1673 tests pass. The 40 that fail, in 9 classes, are C2J-route behaviors that the C2J_Converter or the Smithy IntermediateModel does not carry yet. None of them is suppressed or skipped, and none affects the seven prototype services (`LegacyRouteParityTest`):

| Test class (failures) | Evidence | Likely gap |
|---|---|---|
| `EndpointDiscoveryAndEndpointOverrideTest` (16) | Requests go to `https://endpointoverride/`, expected `/DescribeEndpoints`, `/TestDiscoveryOptional` | RPC `requestUri` other than `/`: the converter keeps it only in the gap marker (`operation.http`), and the Smithy route sends to `/` |
| `HttpChecksumInHeaderTest` (4), `HttpChecksumRequiredTest` (2) | rest-xml body checksums differ (`0/Va+Gm0...` vs `FB/utBbw...`, `caVLcw==` vs `YP74zg==`); JSON cases pass | The rest-xml request body differs (root element or namespace) |
| `HostPrefixTest` (4) | Host `localhost`, expected `123-foo.localhost`; invalid prefix not rejected | C2J `endpoint.hostPrefix` does not reach the operation model |
| `BlobUnmarshallingTest` (8) | Four rest-xml cases (sync and async, two content lengths): a present blob payload fails with "Could not parse XML response" at [1,1], and a missing one is null instead of empty `SdkBytes`; JSON cases pass | A rest-xml blob `@httpPayload` response is read as an XML document, not as the raw body |
| `MoveQueryParamsToBodyTest` (2) | Body `Action=AllTypes`, expected `Action=QueryService.AllTypes` | The C2J `targetPrefix` of an awsQuery service is only in the metadata marker |
| `SensitiveDataRedactedTest` (1) | `ListOfListOfSensitiveString=[[Value]]` is not redacted | Sensitivity of a nested list's element target is not propagated |
| `CustomSdkShapeTest` (1) | The request body has no `SdkPartType` | The `customSdkShapes` customization is not applied on the Smithy route |
| `RpcV2CborUserAgentTest` (2) | `BadCborException: unexpected end of payload` for `OperationWithNoInputOrOutput` | New path: an empty rpcv2Cbor response body is not read as an empty structure (`rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol`) |

## Phase 5 behavior policy (user decision)

The prototype services that tasks 9.2–9.4 move to the schema path (SQS, DynamoDB, Secrets Manager, Account, Route 53 and SNS) keep the new path's Smithy-aligned behavior everywhere. The new path does not reproduce the legacy behavior below for any service. Each row is an accepted behavior change. A module test that asserts the legacy behavior is listed with this section as its reason in `docs/design/schema-serde/excluded-tests.md` (task 9.2). This closes the open questions of tasks 7.15 and 7.21.

| # | Behavior | Legacy SDK | Schema path (kept) | Where recorded | Protocols |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Form encoding of space, `*` and `~` in query request bodies | `+`, `*`, `%7E` | `%20`, `%2A`, `~` (RFC 3986 unreserved set, as smithy-java and `SdkHttpUtils.urlEncode`) | Tasks 7.5, 7.15. Both forms decode to the same values | awsQuery, ec2Query (SNS) |
| 2 | Query request `Content-Type` | `application/x-www-form-urlencoded; charset=utf-8` | `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, as the Smithy request tests and smithy-java | Task 7.15 | awsQuery, ec2Query (SNS) |
| 3 | Request path for an endpoint with a path (`https://example.com/custom`) | `/custom` | `/custom/` (`QueryHostWithPath`, `Ec2QueryHostWithPath`) | Task 7.15 | awsQuery, ec2Query (SNS) |
| 4 | Empty response body for a blob `@httpPayload` output member | Empty `SdkBytes` | Unset (null), as the upstream `RestJsonHttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody` and `HttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody` require (both pass) | Task 7.21. The SDK-owned `SdkRestJsonNoContentWithBlobPayload` and `SdkRestXmlNoContentWithBlobPayload` stay skipped, with "accepted behavior change (user decision)" in `protocol-test-skips.json`. `legacy-disposition.csv` row `rest-core-output.json#6` changes from `ported` to `retired` for both protocols (Requirement 32.10 allows only `ported` or `retired`, and the case it was ported to no longer describes SDK behavior) | restJson1, restXml |
| 5 | `@default` values and client error correction | Not populated: an unset member is null and is not sent; a `@required` output member the response omits stays null | Not populated, as legacy (no change from the legacy SDK; a deviation from the Smithy protocol tests). The 12 skips stay | Tasks 3.34, 7.21 | rpcv2Cbor, awsJson1_0, restJson1 (skips); all protocols (behavior) |
| 6 | Event-stream `eventHeader` members | String-typed headers | Typed headers (`fromInteger`, `fromTimestamp`, …), as the Smithy event stream spec. The decoder also accepts a legacy string header for a non-string member | Task 7.20 (header typing decided: typed) | Event-stream operations once they run on the new path (task 7.20 stages (c)–(e)) |
## `services/sqs` and `services/dynamodb` on the schema path (task 9.2)
**Target.** `core/sdk-core`: `Crc32ResponseValidation` (new), `SyncApiCallPipeline`, `AsyncApiCallPipeline`. `services/sqs`: `customization.config`, `pom.xml`. `services/dynamodb`: `dynamodb/customization.config`, `dynamodbstreams/customization.config`, `pom.xml`. Guard tests in `codegen-maven-plugin` and `test/architecture-tests`. `docs/design/schema-serde/excluded-tests.md` (new).
| Task text or earlier note | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| CRC32 (`x-amz-crc32`) not applied on the new path (task 3.28 row); must move into the pipeline, protocol or transport | In the pipeline: `Crc32ResponseValidation.validate` runs before `ProtocolResponseHandling.handle` for every non-streaming response (success and error) and for the buffered error responses of streaming calls, sync and async. It reuses `Crc32Validation` (checksum of the body as received when `CRC32_FROM_COMPRESSED_DATA_ENABLED`, DynamoDB; otherwise of the decompressed body; gzip decoding; the "no content" case), so the exception type (`Crc32MismatchException`) and messages are the legacy ones. The whole body is read and checked before the protocol parses it, and handed on as an `OwnedResponseBody` so the protocol does not copy it again; an async transport-owned body that is not gzip is checked in place without a copy | One place for both pipelines and every protocol, as the legacy client handlers do it for every protocol. The protocol SPI has no hook for response decoding, and the transport has no client configuration |
| Same behavior as legacy `Crc32Validation` | Same checks and exceptions. One ordering difference: legacy validates when the unmarshaller closes the stream, so a body that is both malformed and mismatching fails as an unmarshalling error there, and as `Crc32MismatchException` here | New (not a policy row). The new order reports the transport error, which is the cause |
| A mismatch is retryable | `Crc32MismatchException.retryable()` is true, as before, but the standard retry strategy (`SdkDefaultRetryStrategy`) retries an `SdkClientException` only if it is a `RetryableException`, an `IOException` cause or a timeout, so neither path retries it with the default strategy | Unchanged behavior: both paths run the same retry strategy on the same exception. `DynamoDbNewPathTest` asserts one attempt |
| SQS (awsJson1_0 + awsQueryCompatible) through the converter, on the new path | `"stagedClientProtocols": true` (task 7.11): the generated builder selects `AwsJson1Protocol.Factory`, all 23 operations are routed and no `transform` package is generated. `x-amzn-query-mode: true` is sent and the `x-amzn-query-error` code is the reported error code (modeled class from `__type`), as legacy. `MessageMD5ChecksumInterceptor` and the batch manager are unchanged and their tests pass | `FACTORIES` stays as it is (task 7.11) |
| SQS module dependencies | `aws-json-client-protocol` replaces `aws-json-protocol` and `protocol-core` (`dependency:analyze` reports both unused). **Runtime classpath change:** neither is in `sqs`'s runtime tree any more; it gains `aws-json-client-protocol` and `http-client-protocol`. japicmp (SQS is in the root `includeModules`) excludes `software.amazon.awssdk.services.sqs.transform.*`: the 23 `@SdkInternalApi` `<Operation>RequestMarshaller` classes are removed (Requirement 17.12); the rest of the diff is 85 modified classes, 3 modified enums, 1 modified interface and 2 new classes, all compatible | As CloudWatch in task 5.1 |
| DynamoDB (awsJson1_0, benchmark baseline) on the new path | `"stagedClientProtocols": true`: all 57 DynamoDB operations are routed (the Kinesis streaming destination operations too, since task 3.34) and `services.dynamodb.transform` is gone. Endpoint discovery (`EndpointDiscoveryTest`) and the account-ID endpoint (`DynamoDbNewPathTest`: `123456789012.ddb.us-west-2.amazonaws.com`) work on the new path | |
| DynamoDB and DynamoDB Streams both route (`routesEveryOperation`) | **Not met for DynamoDB Streams.** Streams shares DynamoDB's module and model package (`shareModelConfig`); `SchemaCodegen` generates no schemas for a model sharer (task 1.27), so the Streams clients keep the legacy marshallers (`services.dynamodb.streams.transform`, 4 classes) and the legacy JSON protocol. Its `customization.config` also sets `stagedClientProtocols`, which has no effect until it has schemas. Generated Streams code is unchanged (`legacy-route-parity/dynamodb.txt` lists only the two DynamoDB builder files) | Blocked on codegen: schemas for a model-sharing service (its own shapes in the shared package, its `Operations` class and service schema, and the shared classes' schemas whose ShapeIds are in DynamoDB's namespace). Planned for phase 6 (task 1.27 note). No D.2 fallback applies: DynamoDB is the benchmark addition (Requirement 33.4), not a protocol slot |
| DynamoDB module dependencies | `aws-json-client-protocol` added; `aws-json-protocol` and `protocol-core` kept, used by the Streams clients | `dependency:analyze` passes |
| Existing module tests pass, exclusions listed | SQS 119 tests, DynamoDB 65 tests, all passing, no exclusions. `PutItemRequestMarshallerTest` is rewritten to the same wire assertion through the client (it called the removed `PutItemRequestMarshaller`); recorded in `excluded-tests.md` | |
| Fallbacks recorded | None used | |
**Guard tests.** `NoTransformPackageTest.SCHEMA_BASED_MODULES` gains `sqs`; `dynamodb` stays unlisted because Streams is not schema-based (the legacy-module test still passes for it, as its Javadoc foresees for a mixed module). `NoMarshallerDependencyTest.SCHEMA_BASED_SERVICES` gains `sqs` (already a dependency of `architecture-tests`); `dynamodb` cannot be listed while its JAR holds the Streams legacy classes. `MarshallerDependencyScannerTest` takes its real legacy fixture from DynamoDB Streams instead of SQS. `legacy-route-parity/sqs.txt` and `dynamodb.txt` list the builder changes (the protocol factory option, `protocol(ClientProtocol)`, `protocolSettings()`, and the removed `ENABLE_FAST_UNMARSHALLER`), as `cloudwatch.txt` does. The size report is unchanged (CloudWatch only; task 9.27 extends it).
**Behavior differences.** Phase 5 policy rows 1–4 are query, restJson1 and restXml behaviors and do not apply to awsJson1_0; row 5 (no `@default` population) is unchanged from legacy. New: the CRC32 ordering row above. No SQS or DynamoDB module test asserts a different legacy behavior.
**For tasks 9.3, 9.4, 9.17 and 9.27.** 9.3/9.4: opt in with `stagedClientProtocols` per service, add each module to `SCHEMA_BASED_MODULES`, `SCHEMA_BASED_SERVICES` and a parity list (`LegacyRouteParity.main <module>` prints it, run from `codegen-maven-plugin` with its test classpath); a service in the root japicmp `includeModules` needs the `transform.*` exclude; add rows to `excluded-tests.md`. CRC32 now applies on the new path for every service, but only DynamoDB sends the header. 9.17: DynamoDB is on the new path for the benchmark, Streams is not; SQS's runtime tree no longer has the legacy JSON modules, DynamoDB's still does (Streams). 9.27: the SQS and DynamoDB JARs lose their `transform` packages (SQS 23 classes; DynamoDB 57 operations' marshallers) and gain schemas; DynamoDB keeps `aws-json-protocol` and `protocol-core` until Streams moves.

## `services/cloudwatch` protocol overrides (task 9.5)
**Target.** `services/cloudwatch`: `pom.xml`, `CloudWatchProtocolOverrideTest` (new). `docs/design/schema-serde/size-report.md` (regenerated). No codegen or runtime change, and no regeneration: the generated builder's `protocol(ClientProtocol)` (task 3.23) already accepts any protocol.
| Task text or earlier note | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| CloudWatch depends on `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol`, `aws-json-client-protocol` and `aws-query-client-protocol` (Requirement 33.2, A.11) | `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` stays `compile` (the generated base builder references `RpcV2CborProtocol.Factory`). The other two are **`runtime`** scope. No CloudWatch class references them, so `dependency:analyze` would report them unused at `compile` scope; the root pom sets `ignoreNonCompile`, so at `runtime` scope it passes ("No dependency problems found") without an `ignoredUnusedDeclaredDependencies` entry | They are loaded through `ServiceLoader` (`ClientProtocolFactories.forId`), which needs them on the runtime class path only. A user who writes `AwsJson1Protocol.Factory.INSTANCE` in their own code declares the protocol module themselves, as for any class they compile against |
| `protocol(...)` overrides to awsJson1_0 and awsQuery work with no regeneration | Both work, sync and async. The builder takes one `ClientProtocol` instance (design A.11; there is no `protocol(ShapeId)` or factory setting, and none was added). A user creates it from the factory class or by protocol ID: `ClientProtocolFactories.forId(ShapeId.from("aws.protocols#awsQuery")).createProtocol(settings, null)`. Both factories ignore the trait argument (null is accepted). After `build()`, `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` is the override instance, and the `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` execution attribute of every call is the same instance | Requirements 12.1, 12.2 |
| How a user obtains `ProtocolSettings` (open since task 3.23) | **Still open; reported, not solved.** The settings the generated builder uses (`protocolSettings()`: `CloudWatchServiceSchemas.SERVICE`, `serviceVersion("2010-08-01")`, `CloudWatchException::builder`) are private, and every route to the service schema is `@SdkInternalApi` (`CloudWatchServiceSchemas`, `CloudWatchOperations.SERVICE`, design 1244). With public API only (`ProtocolSettings.builder().service(ShapeId.from("com.amazonaws.cloudwatch#GraniteServiceVersion20100801")).serviceVersion("2010-08-01").baseExceptionBuilder(CloudWatchException::builder)`) the override works but the settings have no `awsQueryCompatible` trait: the awsJson1_0 override sends no `x-amzn-query-mode` and ignores `x-amzn-query-error` (the error code is then `DashboardNotFoundError`, not `ResourceNotFound`); the class is still resolved from `__type`. A forgotten `serviceVersion` fails awsQuery creation with the task 7.15 `NullPointerException`. The tests use the internal schema, as the generated builder does | Design A.11 specifies only the builder setting. Fixing it needs public API beyond the design (for example a builder overload taking a `ClientProtocolFactory` or protocol ID that the builder resolves with its own settings, or a public settings accessor); left for an API review |
| awsJson1_0 wire shape (task 7.11) | `POST /`, `X-Amz-Target: GraniteServiceVersion20100801.GetDashboard`, `Content-Type: application/x-amz-json-1.0`, `x-amzn-query-mode: true`, body `{"DashboardName":"my dashboard"}`; no `smithy-protocol` header | |
| awsQuery wire shape (task 7.15, SNS in 9.4) | `POST /`, `Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded` (no charset, policy row 2), body `Action=GetDashboard&Version=2010-08-01&DashboardName=my%20dashboard` (`%20`, policy row 1) and `Action=ListMetrics&Version=2010-08-01&Namespace=AWS%2FEC2`; no `x-amzn-query-mode` or `X-Amz-Target`. Policy row 3 (endpoint path) is not exercised: CloudWatch's endpoints have no path | Phase 5 policy rows 1–3 |
| rpcv2Cbor default, for comparison | `POST /service/GraniteServiceVersion20100801/operation/GetDashboard`, `smithy-protocol: rpc-v2-cbor`, `Content-Type: application/cbor`, `x-amzn-query-mode: true` | Unchanged; `CloudWatchNewPathTest` (12) and `CloudWatchInterceptorOrderTest` (8) pass |
| Outputs (Requirement 12.6) | `GetDashboard` and `ListMetrics` (two metrics and `NextToken`) give member-equal outputs (`equalsBySdkFields`) for all three protocols, with the request ID from `x-amzn-RequestId` (JSON, CBOR) or `<ResponseMetadata><RequestId>` (awsQuery). `equals()` of a whole response also compares `sdkHttpResponse` and the response metadata, so it differs between any two calls | Property 27 (task 9.6) must compare members, not `equals()` |
| Modeled errors (Requirement 12.7) | A 404 missing dashboard throws `DashboardNotFoundErrorException` with code `ResourceNotFound`, status 404, message and request ID for each protocol. rpcv2Cbor and awsJson1_0 read `x-amzn-query-error: ResourceNotFound;Sender` first (`awsQueryCompatible`; `__type` alone gives `DashboardNotFoundError`); awsQuery reads `<Code>ResourceNotFound</Code>`, which the code index resolves to `DashboardNotFoundError` (first of the two shapes with that code, task 7.15) | Same type and code only when the JSON/CBOR response carries the header, as a query-compatible service sends it |
| Schema extensions | `aws-query-client-protocol` registers `AwsQuerySchemaExtensions` and `json-codec`/`xml-codec` their own providers, now on CloudWatch's runtime class path. Extensions are computed on first use per key, so the default rpcv2Cbor client computes none of them; the per-schema extension array is sized by the registry, so it gains slots | No behavior change for the default |
**Size report.** `SizeReportDocumentTest` failed after the dependency change and `size-report.md` is regenerated (JDK 21.0.2, baseline in `~/.cache`). From this task: the runtime-modules table gains `aws-json-client-protocol` (17,521 B), `aws-query-client-protocol` (47,263 B), `json-codec` (78,641 B) and `xml-codec` (123,693 B), and the CloudWatch JAR grows by 254 B (the embedded `pom.xml`). The same regeneration also picks up drift from the phase 7 tasks, which changed modules without regenerating the report (it was last written in task 5.4): `codec-commons` 43,039 → 75,617, `http-binding` 8,106 → 101,960, `http-client-protocol` 37,868 → 83,455, `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` 15,440 → 16,096, `schema` 218,802 → 224,109, `sdk-core` 1,222,861 → 1,260,355. The CloudWatch class count and uncompressed bytes are unchanged.
**Tests.** `CloudWatchProtocolOverrideTest` (20): wire shape and output of `GetDashboard`, output and wire body of `ListMetrics`, and the modeled error, each for rpcv2Cbor (default), awsJson1_0 (factory class) and awsQuery (factory by ID), sync and async (18); both IDs resolve to the factories of the runtime dependencies (1); the awsJson1_0 override with public-only settings (1, records the gap above). Module total 302, no exclusions. japicmp (both executions) and `dependency:analyze` pass; CloudWatch's public API is unchanged.
**For tasks 9.6, 9.27 and 9.28.** 9.6: create the overrides as this test does (internal service schema settings, or the public-only settings with the header gap above); compare outputs with `equalsBySdkFields`; for errors, send `x-amzn-query-error` with the JSON and CBOR responses, or the error codes differ (`DashboardNotFoundError` vs `ResourceNotFound`) while the exception type is the same. Error codes that map to two shapes (`ResourceNotFound`) resolve to `DashboardNotFoundError` on every protocol. 9.27: CloudWatch's runtime tree now also has `aws-json-client-protocol`, `aws-query-client-protocol`, `json-codec` and `xml-codec` (see the size report); a per-protocol size comparison should count them as override cost, not default cost. 9.28: no public API change for CloudWatch in this task; the `ProtocolSettings` gap above is the open API question for Requirement 12.

## Prototype dependency trees (task 9.8)

**Target.** `services/pom.xml` (the `ban-smithy-runtime` enforcer execution and the `schema-serde.ban-smithy.skip` property, default `true`); the seven Prototype_Service_Set `pom.xml` files (`sqs`, `secretsmanager`, `account`, `route53`, `sns`, `cloudwatch`, `dynamodb`), each setting the property to `false`; `test/architecture-tests`: `dependencies.DependencyTreeTest`, `dependencies.ReactorDependencyGraph`, and the `dependency-tree.root` surefire property.

| Task or requirement text | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `maven-enforcer` rule on every prototype service module banning `software.amazon.smithy:*` from compile and runtime scopes (Requirement 1.6) | One execution in `services/pom.xml` `<build><plugins>`, inherited by every service and skipped unless the module sets `schema-serde.ban-smithy.skip` to `false`. `bannedDependencies` with `searchTransitive` and the excludes `software.amazon.smithy:*:*:*:compile` and `:runtime`. Plugin version 3.5.0, as json-codec's `ban-jackson-databind` (task 7.1). `provided` and `test` scopes are allowed | Opting in per module keeps the rule to the Prototype_Service_Set that Requirement 1.6 names; the fleet services (`-P fleet-services`) inherit it switched off. Requirement 1.6 names only `smithy-model`; banning every Smithy artifact is the task's wording and stricter |
| Codegen's Smithy dependencies | Not seen by the rule: they are dependencies of `codegen-maven-plugin` (a build plugin of the `generated-service` profile), not of the service module | Verified: `mvn -o validate` passes on all seven modules |
| `DependencyTreeTest` asserting `dynamic-client` is the only published module depending on `smithy-model` (Requirement 1.7) | Reads the reactor's `pom.xml` files with DOM (no Maven run; about 0.4 s), following every `<module>` including profile modules, with parent-inherited and profile dependencies and `dependencyManagement` scopes. A published module is one managed by `bom/pom.xml`. The walk is transitive over compile and runtime edges, with Maven's rules for optional dependencies and exclusions. Artifacts outside the reactor are not expanded; every `software.amazon.smithy` artifact except `smithy-utils` counts as carrying `smithy-model` | Running `mvn dependency:tree` per module in a test is slow and needs a network or a full local repository. Requirement 1.6's real resolution for the prototypes is the enforcer rule above |
| "other than the codegen modules, the codegen Maven plugin and test modules" (Requirement 1.7) | Allowlist with reasons: `codegen`, `codegen-c2j-to-smithy` (codegen modules), `codegen-maven-plugin`. Unpublished modules (every `test/` module except `client-mock-plugin`) are not checked. `client-mock-plugin` is published and users put it on their classpath, so it is checked as a runtime module. A test fails if an allowlist entry is no longer published or no longer reaches `smithy-model` | `codegen-lite` and `codegen-lite-maven-plugin` are published codegen modules but do not depend on Smithy, so they need no entry |
| "declares a compile-scope or runtime-scope dependency" (Requirement 1.7) | **Interpretation, flagged:** the test is transitive, so a module that reaches `smithy-model` through another reactor module fails, including through `dynamic-client`. Requirement 1.7's literal text checks only declared (direct) dependencies | Requirement 1.6 and design K9 intend no Smithy on runtime class paths other than the Dynamic_Client's. A future module that builds on `dynamic-client` needs a decision and an allowlist entry |
