The post-interceptor snapshot is absent, the same `SelectedAuthScheme`, another scheme with the same option, an equal copy, or a copy with changed, cleared, added and removed properties. The pre-interceptor snapshot is absent in some cases, and the current scheme is null in 1%. Each call starts from the previous call's result and post-interceptor snapshot with a few values changed. Both implementations must leave the same selected scheme: the same instance when nothing was re-applied, otherwise the current identity future and signer with an equal scheme ID, signer properties (including null-valued entries) and identity properties. A failure must have the same exception type. The test also checks that every outcome (unchanged, replaced, failed) occurs. Three example tests check that the current option is not copied when both snapshots hold the same option or equal values, and that it is copied once for several changes. Four mutations each fail the tests: skipping null values, `putSignerPropertyIfAbsent` instead of `putSignerProperty`, dropping the null check, and a new copy for each change. Module run (`mvn verify -pl :sdk-core`, nothing installed): 1,940 JUnit tests (6 skipped) and 651 TestNG tests (272 skipped), 0 failures, with the existing tests unchanged. Checkstyle, SpotBugs and japicmp pass. The auth and endpoint tests in `codegen-generated-classes-test` (`EndpointInterceptorTests`, `EndpointAuthSigningPropertiesTest`, `MultiAuthSigningPropertiesTest`, `Sigv4aSigningRegionSetTest`, `RequestOverrideAuthSchemeAndEndpointProviderTest`, `SdkPluginTest`, `IdentityResolutionOverrideTest`, `LegacySignerOverrideTest`) also pass against the candidate: 218 tests, 1 skipped. S3's `ExecutionAttributeBackwardsCompatibilityTest`, which racecar ran, is not in this branch's default reactor and was not run.

**Smoke run.** e2e only (E13 has no JMH target), v2-sync and v2-async small-get, about 6 minutes; `E13-smoke.md` is marked PRELIMINARY. A probe client built like the harness's showed both snapshots holding the same placeholder option on every DynamoDB GetItem and PutItem call, so every measured call takes the early return. Allocation, the target metric in `targets.json`, was lower in both pairs on both clients: −356 B/op (−0.6%) on v2-sync against a 0.9% floor, and −296 B/op (−0.4%) on v2-async against a 0.4% floor. The script's verdict is REVERT (sync within the floor), which does not apply under the user's decision. Racecar's profile put the removed subtree at 360 B/op (sync) and 225 B/op (async), the same size as the saving here, but two repetitions of 300 operations cannot resolve it. CPU is noise (application CPU −2.7% and +3.9%, floors 12.2% and 13.1%). The full measurement should add small-put and more repetitions, or profile the method's allocation directly, as racecar did.

## Size_Report for the Prototype_Service_Set (task 9.27)

**Target.** `test/size-report` reports all seven Prototype_Service_Set services (`size-report.services`: SQS, Secrets Manager, Account, Route 53, SNS, CloudWatch, DynamoDB, each a test-scoped dependency). `build-size-baseline.sh` defaults to the seven modules. New in the tool: `ReductionOpportunity` (Requirement 36.5), `ServiceComparison.modelVersionDifferences`, per-entry compressed sizes in `JarContents`, and `ClassAttributor.locate`. The report gains a cross-service category table, a model-version-differences table per service, the runtime modules split into schema-serde modules and other modules, and the size-reduction opportunities. The Baseline_Build (`fc8b74e9fe21`) and the feature build (`ad0a9abcf24`, `-o clean install -P quick -pl <seven services> -am` from a clean worktree of the commit) were both compiled with JDK 21.0.2 (Oracle) and the same compiler options.

| Task or design text | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Append the reduction-opportunity table (design D.5) with measured deltas | The table is generated into `size-report.md` from `ReductionOpportunity`, and `SizeReportDocumentTest` keeps it current. Each row selects the feature build code it would remove and reports, per service, the estimated change in compressed JAR bytes and the measured uncompressed bytes and class count. Whole classes are measured exactly (entry data plus local and central headers). Carved members are estimated at the compression ratio of their class's entry. The rows select disjoint code, so the report adds them up and gives the JAR change and the Requirement 36.3 verdict with all of them applied | Hand-copied numbers in the design would go stale the next time codegen changes. The design (`design.md`) is not in the repository, so the committed record of Requirement 36.5 is the report |
| D.5 rows | Kept: generic `equals`/`hashCode` (now with `equalsBySdkFields`), schema-driven copier, generic paginator (with D.5's 20–40% estimate applied to the measured paginator bytes, since only part of each paginator class goes away), `sdkFieldNameToField` overrides (with `sdkFields`, which delegates to the same `SdkFieldsFromSchema`), and enum boilerplate. Added, from what grew: a generic list and map serde instead of the `<Prefix>SharedSerde$<Shape>Serde` classes, a runtime-built `<Prefix>SchemaIndex`, folding `InnerDeserializer` into `BuilderImpl`, no covariant `ShapeBuilder` bridges, package-private `M_*` constants (no `access$NNN()` accessors), and moving DynamoDB Streams' remaining legacy marshallers to the schema path. Dropped: "share prelude-targeting member constants in `<Svc>SharedSerde`" | The member constants are `M_*` fields in each model class, and which of them target prelude shapes is not visible in the bytecode, so that row cannot be measured from the JARs. Row 5 measures the overhead those constants do cause (the synthetic accessors) |
| — | Estimates leave out the one-time cost of shared runtime code an opportunity needs (for example a generic collection serde in `schema`) | That code is in a runtime module, not the service JAR, and does not exist yet |
| Failing marker with grown categories for every service whose JAR grows | All seven services fail Requirement 36.3: SQS +144,859 (+16.8%), Secrets Manager +96,026 (+12.8%), Account +76,335 (+16.1%), Route 53 +340,365 (+15.7%), SNS +191,128 (+17.3%), CloudWatch +263,335 (+15.4%), DynamoDB +389,317 (+13.3%). Each lists Schemas, Type_Registries, Generated_Model_Class classes and Other as the grown categories | Reported as measured. With every opportunity applied, the estimates put every service below its Baseline_Build JAR (from −8,517 to −353,045 bytes), which the opportunity table shows as "Meets" |
| — | Account's native Smithy model is newer than the Baseline_Build's C2J model (task 9.3): the feature JAR has `SendPhoneNumberVerification` and `VerifyPhoneNumber` (request and response classes) and `PhoneNumberVerificationStatus`, 21 classes and 87,209 uncompressed bytes (25,769 bytes of compressed entry data). The report lists them in Account's model-version-differences table and keeps them in the totals | The verdict uses the JARs as built. Subtracting those classes' JAR entries still leaves Account about 45 KB larger than the Baseline_Build, so the verdict does not depend on the model difference |
| Module rows for `schema`, codecs, `http-binding` and protocol modules (0 B baseline) | A "Schema-serde modules" table: `schema`, `codec-commons`, `cbor-codec`, `json-codec`, `xml-codec`, `http-binding`, `http-client-protocol` and the five client protocols, each added (0 B in the Baseline_Build), 852,327 bytes together. Other modules are listed with their status. `aws-json-protocol` and `protocol-core` are unchanged dependencies because DynamoDB Streams keeps the legacy path (task 9.2) | Requirement 36.6 covers the modules the feature build adds or changes. `dynamic-client`, `message-payloads` and `client-mock-plugin` are also new, but no prototype service depends on them at runtime, so they are not in the report. Adding them would need a test-scoped dependency for each; that is open |
| — | Before this task the shared `~/.m2` held candidate JARs: `http-auth-aws` was the `opt/F` build (195,179 bytes against 180,503 from this branch). The feature JARs were rebuilt from a clean worktree of `ad0a9abcf24`. The report shows `http-auth-aws` unchanged against the Baseline_Build, and the `json-codec` JAR has no `skipWhitespaceSlow` (the `opt/E7` method) | The candidates of tasks 9.20–9.26 are not merged |

**Changes from the previous report.** The CloudWatch JAR is 83 bytes larger than in the task 9.5 report (1,969,997), from the embedded `pom.xml`: task 9.8 added the `schema-serde.ban-smithy.skip` property to the service POMs. Its class bytes and class count are unchanged. `codec-commons` (75,617 → 77,420), `json-codec` (78,641 → 77,946) and `xml-codec` (123,693 → 122,936) changed in task 9.9 (codec serialize and deserialize).

**Tests.** `ReductionOpportunityTest` (on ASM-generated classes: whole-class selection, member carves, `InnerDeserializer` keeping its `accept` methods, a class selected whole not measured again by a member row, the legacy remainder, a service with none of the code, the paginator range). `ServiceComparisonTest` covers the model-version differences. `SizeReportTest` covers the new sections and the module kinds. `mvn -o clean install -pl :size-report` (checkstyle and `dependency:analyze` included): 63 tests, 0 failures, and `SizeReportDocumentTest` matches the committed report. `dependency:analyze` now ignores `protocol-core` as well as `schema`: the attribution names `ExceptionMetadata` as a descriptor string, and DynamoDB brings `protocol-core` onto the test classpath.

## API compatibility of the prototype services (task 9.28)
**Baseline.** japicmp compares each module with the released `software.amazon.awssdk:<artifactId>:${awsjavasdk.previous.version}` (2.49.6), as the root pom, CloudWatch (task 3.35) and `BaselineModelParityTest` already do. The other option was `<oldVersion><file>` on the Baseline_Build jars in `~/.cache/aws-sdk-java-v2/size-baseline/fc8b74e9fe21/jars/` (from `build-size-baseline.sh`, which has all nine jars). It was not taken: every `mvn verify` of these modules would then depend on a machine-local directory that a script must build first. The released jars resolve in any build that can reach Maven Central.
| Item | Result |
|---|---|
| Released 2.49.6 jars in `~/.m2` before this task | `sdk-core`, `cloudwatch`, `sqs` only. The offline build could not resolve `http-client-spi`, `dynamodb`, `secretsmanager`, `account`, `route53` or `sns`; `http-client-spi` (already in the root `includeModules`) was not compared, without failing the build, because the root sets `ignoreMissingOldVersion=true`. The six jars were fetched once with `mvn dependency:get -Dartifact=software.amazon.awssdk:<a>:2.49.6 -Dtransitive=false`; `mvn -o` resolves them since |
| Released jar against the Baseline_Build jar | japicmp 0.15.6 CLI (`-m -a PACKAGE_PROTECTED`, both class paths the Baseline_Build jars) reports "No changes" for all nine modules: `http-client-spi`, `sdk-core` and the seven services. The 17 commits between tag `2.49.6` and the merge base `fc8b74e9fe21` change codegen only (the Smithy IntermediateModel path and the unused `defaultEnableSocketTimeout2026` customization key) |
| Missing baseline | Each of the nine modules sets `ignoreMissingOldVersion=false`. With `-Dawsjavasdk.previous.version=2.49.99`, `http-client-spi` and `sns` fail in `japicmp:cmp (default)` with "The following artifacts could not be resolved" |

**Configuration.** The six service modules other than CloudWatch now have CloudWatch's configuration (task 5.1). The default execution drops the root's `METHOD_ADDED_TO_INTERFACE` acceptance, so an abstract method on a public client or builder interface fails (Requirement 16.3). It accepts `METHOD_ABSTRACT_ADDED_IN_IMPLEMENTED_INTERFACE`, which japicmp reports for every final model class that now implements `SerializableStruct`. The second execution, `interfaces`, compares `software.amazon.awssdk.services.<pkg>.*Client`, `*Builder` and `*Waiter` without that acceptance. Those patterns also match nested model `Builder` interfaces and the DynamoDB Streams types. Before this task SQS used the root configuration unchanged.

It has no `combine.children="append"`, so it replaces the root's excludes and `*.internal.*` is compared too. "Schema members" are the additions listed for CloudWatch in task 3.35: `implements SerializableStruct` (and `SerializableShape`), `SCHEMA`, `sdkSchema()`, `serializeMembers(ShapeSerializer)` and `getMemberValue(Schema)` on each structure, union and exception class; `implements SmithyEnum`, `SCHEMA`, `getValue()` and `sdkSchema()` on each enum; `default protocol(ClientProtocol<?, ?>)` on `<Svc>BaseClientBuilder` (the 1 interface in each `interfaces` report); and the two new `@SdkInternalApi` classes `model.<Svc>Operations` and `model.<Svc>SchemaIndex`.

**Source-compatibility fixtures (`test/schema-serde-source-compat`, Requirement 16.8).** The design's name `schema-source-compat-tests` became `schema-serde-source-compat`, the name the task 10 checkpoint uses. The fixtures (`src/test/java/…/sourcecompat/fixtures`) use only Baseline_Build APIs. `BaselineCompileTest` compiles them with `javax.tools` (`--release 8`) against the Baseline_Build jars of `build-size-baseline.sh` plus `reactive-streams`, with no feature jar on the class path. It fails, naming the script, when the Baseline_Build is missing. A second test checks that a source using `MetricAlarm.SCHEMA` does not compile there. Maven compiles the same sources against the feature build. The `*SourceCompatTest` classes call each fixture once per client call through `client-mock-plugin`. The plugin replaces the transport and generates each response with the client's protocol from a value the fixture built. The fixtures' HTTP clients fail if called, so no test needs the network. DynamoDB Streams is on the legacy route, so its test returns a JSON body from a stub HTTP client.
| Service | Clients | Requests | Response getters | Exceptions | Paginator | Waiter |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| All | Sync and async builders: `region`, `credentialsProvider`, `overrideConfiguration` (object and consumer), `endpointOverride`, `httpClient`, `addPlugin`. Sync calls send the override header to the override endpoint, and the override interceptor runs once | Builder and consumer-builder overloads, `toBuilder` with an override and a round trip back to an equal request | Checked against literal values, and for the main sync call also against the same getters on the value the mock returned | Caught by type with a service-exception fallback; `statusCode()`, `awsErrorDetails().errorCode()`; built with `builder().message(…).statusCode(…)` | | |
| CloudWatch | rpcv2Cbor | `DescribeAlarms` (enum, enum list) | List of nested structures, enums with `xxxAsString`, nested list, timestamp, boolean, `hasCompositeAlarms` | `DashboardNotFoundErrorException` from `GetDashboard`, plus `toBuilder` | `describeAlarmsPaginator().metricAlarms()`, 2 pages | `waitUntilAlarmExists` |
| SQS | awsJson1_0 (`checksumValidationEnabled(false)` on the sync client) | `ReceiveMessage`, `GetQueueAttributes(QueueAttributeName.ALL)` | Enum-keyed maps with `attributesAsStrings`, map of nested structures with a blob | `QueueDoesNotExistException` from `GetQueueUrl` | `listQueuesPaginator().queueUrls()`, 2 pages | None in the model |
| DynamoDB | awsJson1_0 (`endpointDiscoveryEnabled(false)`), and `DynamoDbStreamsClient` | `GetItem` (`Map<String, AttributeValue>`, enum), `PutItem`, `DescribeTable` | `AttributeValue` of each kind (S, N, SS, M, L, BOOL, B) and `type()`, nested structures, enum | `ConditionalCheckFailedException` from `PutItem` | `scanPaginator().items()`, 2 pages | `DynamoDbWaiter.builder().client(…)`, `waitUntilTableExists` |
| Secrets Manager | awsJson1_1 | `GetSecretValue`, `DescribeSecret`, `ListSecrets` with a `Filter` | Sensitive strings, blob, list, timestamp, `Map<String, List<String>>`, nested structure, `toString` redaction | `ResourceNotFoundException` | `listSecretsPaginator`, page iteration | None in the model |
| Account | restJson1 (`Region.AWS_GLOBAL`) | `GetAlternateContact`, `GetContactInformation`, `ListRegions` (enum varargs) | Nested structures, enums with `xxxAsString` | `ResourceNotFoundException` | `listRegionsPaginator().regions()`, 2 pages | None in the model |
| Route 53 | restXml (`Region.AWS_GLOBAL`) | `GetHostedZone` (label), `ChangeResourceRecordSets` (nested consumer builder), `GetChange` | Nested structures, string list, list of structures with an enum, timestamp | `NoSuchHostedZoneException` | `listHostedZonesPaginator()` (no-argument overload), 2 pages | `waitUntilResourceRecordSetsChanged` |
| SNS | awsQuery | `Publish` (map of structures), `GetTopicAttributes`, `ListSMSSandboxPhoneNumbers` | `Map<String, String>` with `hasAttributes`, list of structures with an enum | `NotFoundException` | `listSMSSandboxPhoneNumbersPaginator().phoneNumbers()` (sync), `listTopicsPaginator().topics().subscribe(…)` (async publisher) | None in the model |
| Legacy_Document | — | — | `mapBuilder`/`listBuilder` (each `put`/`add` kind), every `from*` factory, `is*`/`as*`, `asNumber().intValue()`/`longValue()`/`bigDecimalValue()`/`doubleValue()`, `unwrap`, `accept(DocumentVisitor)`, `equals`, `toString` | — | — | — |
None of the seven services has a document member, so the Legacy_Document fixture uses the `Document` API directly.

Route 53 returns the hosted zone and change IDs without their `/hostedzone/` and `/change/` prefixes, because `Route53IdInterceptor` (also in the Baseline_Build) strips them from responses. The Route 53 test therefore compares with literal strings, not with the value the mock returned. Module run: 44 tests, 0 failures (`mvn -o clean verify -pl :schema-serde-source-compat` with checkstyle, SpotBugs and `dependency:analyze`).

## Checkpoint 10: batch-put-large allocation

**Finding.** The checkpoint 10 smoke A/B (run `20261020-135020`, null `20261020-134208`; 2 repetitions × 300 operations, v2-sync, `--metrics`) measured e2e DynamoDB `batch-put-large` at 358,882 B/op on the Baseline_Build (`fc8b74e9fe21`) and 433,899 B/op on the feature build (`b93a9b5e66c`): +75,017 B/op (+20.9%) against a null floor of 84 B. The scenario is one BatchWriteItem of 25 `BenchmarkItems.largeItem` rows (each a medium item plus 12 200-character strings: string, number, boolean, map and list attributes), a 104,740-byte request body. The response is 23 bytes (`{"UnprocessedItems":{}}`) and is only unmarshalled.

**Method.** The harness ran 40,000 measured operations per build after the usual warm-up. async-profiler 4.4 (`asprof -e alloc --alloc 16k --total -d 6 -o collapsed`) was attached after the warm-up line. Each profile's bytes were scaled so its total equals that run's `alloc_bytes_per_op` (baseline 358,620, feature 433,627), so the per-site numbers carry about ±3% sampling error. JMH `-prof gc` on `SchemaMarshallBenchmark` (`AWS_JSON_1_0`, `LARGE`) was the cross-check.

**Breakdown (B/op).**

| Category | Baseline_Build | Feature | Feature + fix |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Request body buffers (JSON writer buffers and the body array) | 244,023 | 368,382 | 126,410 |
| Request marshalling, other | 48,962 | 3,327 | 4,463 |
| Signing | 24,165 | 24,309 | 22,573 |
| Response unmarshalling | 163 | 369 | 334 |
| HTTP client (Apache5) | 15,428 | 15,227 | 14,880 |
| Metrics | 8,643 | 8,681 | 8,389 |
| Other | 17,236 | 13,332 | 12,185 |
| Total (measured) | 358,620 | 433,627 | 189,234 |

| Site | Feature − baseline (sampled) | Exact per call |
|---|---:|---:|
| `SmithyJsonSerializer.grow` (`Arrays.copyOf`) | +240,186 | 245,824: arrays of 16,384, 32,768, 65,536 and 131,072 bytes |
| `ByteBufferOutputStream.<init>` from `HttpClientProtocol.newRequestBodyBuffer` | +121,469 | 117,848: the E1 hint, 104,740 + 1/8 |
| `SmithyJsonSerializer.<init>` (the serializer's own buffer) | +6,590 | 8,208 |
| `SdkByteArrayOutputStream.write` growth under Jackson's `_flushBuffer` (legacy only) | −242,547 | The legacy body array and its growth chain |
| `Arrays$ArrayItr` in `JsonProtocolMarshaller.doMarshall` (legacy only) | −42,695 | — |
| `SdkByteArrayOutputStream.<init>` (legacy only) | −1,121 | — |
| All other sites | −6,875 | — |

**Cause.** `SmithyJsonSerializer`, ported from smithy-java, wrote the whole document into its private buffer, starting at 8 KB and doubling, and wrote it to the sink only on `close()`. `AwsJsonProtocol.createRequest` passes a sink, the `ByteBufferOutputStream` that `HttpClientProtocol` presizes from the operation's previous body (racecar E1). So each large request allocated the serializer's growth chain (8 K → 128 K) and then a second, presized copy of the body. The legacy path also grew a buffer (`SdkByteArrayOutputStream`, 242.5 KB of growth arrays here), but it handed that final array to the request without a further copy, and E1 had no effect on the serializer.

**Suspects ruled out.**

| Suspect | Result |
|---|---|
| `toByteArray` or `ByteBuffer.wrap` copy of the serialized body | Not on the request path. `SmithyJsonSerializer.extractResult` (`Arrays.copyOf`) serves only the sink-less `Codec.serialize`. `finishRequestBody` shares the `ByteBufferOutputStream` array through `toContentStreamProvider()` |
| Body copied again for signing, `Content-Length` or checksums | No. SigV4's payload SHA-256 streams the body through `ChecksumUtil.readAll` with the same small read buffer in both builds (3.9 and 4.6 KB sampled; the frames differ only in lambda class names). Signing is 24.2 KB on both builds |
| CRC32 response validation | Not visible: the response is 23 bytes, and response unmarshalling is under 0.4 KB/op on both builds |
| Per-`AttributeValue` boxing or intermediate maps in the schema serializer (`SharedSerde`) | No. Marshalling allocates 3.3 KB/op outside the body buffers, against 49 KB/op on the legacy path |
| The legacy path streamed directly where the new path materializes | Both materialize the body. The difference was the extra serializer buffer and its growth, as described under Cause |

**Fix (`400f3fe99f2`, `perf(json-codec)`).** With a sink, `SmithyJsonSerializer.grow` now writes the buffered bytes to the sink and reuses the buffer. It grows the buffer only when a single value does not fit in it. Callers write at `pos` after `grow` returns and never refer back to earlier positions, so draining at that point is safe. `JsonPrettyPrinter` keeps its state across writes, so pretty output is unchanged. The output bytes are unchanged; the sink receives them in chunks of up to 8 KB instead of in one write. If serialization fails partway, the sink may hold a prefix of the document; protocols discard the body buffer on failure. The sink-less pooled path is unchanged. Deviation from smithy-java, recorded in the class Javadoc.

| Item | Result |
|---|---|
| Tests | `JsonSerializerTest`: a 120 KB document of nested maps and lists, with ASCII and multi-byte strings, written through a sink matches `Codec.serialize` byte for byte, in more than one write, and no write is larger than 8,192 bytes. A test with a 20,000-character string written after an 8,000-character one covers draining followed by growth. The first test fails on the previous code (one write of the whole document) |
| Module runs | `json-codec` (`mvn -o install`, with checkstyle and SpotBugs): 948 tests, 0 failures, 5 skipped. `http-client-protocol` 103 and `aws-json-client-protocol` 68 tests, `dynamodb` 65 tests, all 0 failures |
| JMH cross-check (`SchemaMarshallBenchmark`, `AWS_JSON_1_0`, 3 × 2 s, 1 fork, `-prof gc`) | `LARGE`: 871,640 → 363,512 B/op (−508,128; the 16 K → 256 K growth chain is 507,984 bytes); 144.9 ± 14.8 → 137.0 ± 2.4 µs/op. `SMALL`: 10,416 → 10,360 B/op |
| Other codecs | `CborSerializer` and `SmithyXmlSerializer` have the same grow-then-write-on-close buffer. Not changed: no prototype scenario measures them, and each needs its own check that nothing writes back into earlier positions. Changed in the follow-up below |

**Smoke A/B after the fix** (run `20261020-142028`, same settings: 2 repetitions × 300 operations, warm-up 200, v2-sync, `--metrics`, the same JVM options; the arms are the checkpoint 10 baseline and feature jars plus a harness jar built against `400f3fe99f2`).

| Scenario | Body bytes | Baseline_Build B/op | Feature B/op | Feature + fix B/op | Fix vs feature | Fix vs baseline |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| batch-put-large | 104,740 | 358,910 | 433,978 | 188,018 | −245,960 | −170,892 (−47.6%) |
| batch-put | 38,390 | 224,445 | 228,588 | 113,760 | −114,828 | −110,685 (−49.3%) |
| small-put | 498 | 68,098 | 69,464 | 68,955 | −509 | +858 (+1.3%) |

The batch-put-large and batch-put savings equal the removed growth chains (245,824 and 114,736 bytes). small-put's body never fills the 8 KB buffer, so the fix cannot change it. Its −509 B/op (pairs −187 and −831) is run-to-run and build variance: the fix jar's rebuilt modules were compiled incrementally and contain a few different synthetic classes. For batch-put-large, application CPU was 560.3 / 408.8 / 373.9 µs/op and `MarshallingDuration` 264.9 / 75.4 / 66.3 µs (baseline / feature / feature + fix). At two repetitions these CPU numbers are noise.

**Still open.**

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| small-put stays above the Baseline_Build (+858 B/op in this run, +1,154 at checkpoint 10; null floor 106 B) | A profile of small-put (baseline 67,513, feature + fix 69,272 B/op) puts +5.6 KB/op in the request body buffers, almost all of it the serializer's per-call 8 KB buffer (8,208 B; the legacy Jackson generator's buffer does not appear per call in the baseline profile). About 3.9 KB/op saved elsewhere offsets part of it. Resolved in the follow-up below (`24cd2b3ed74`: a 1 KB buffer for serializers with a sink) |
| `perf-report.md` | Not regenerated: this rerun covered 3 of the 5 scenarios, so the report's feature numbers predate `400f3fe99f2`. Regenerated in the follow-up below |
| Cold start (first call 1.82× slower on the feature build) | Not investigated (user decision) |

### Follow-up: the sink buffer size, CBOR and XML

**small-put: options for the initial buffer.** With draining, the buffer of a serializer that has a sink only sets how many `write` calls the sink receives, so its size is a per-call allocation with no other use. Options:

| Option | Result |
|---|---|
| (a) A smaller fixed buffer for serializers with a sink (512 B, 1 KB, 2 KB) | Measured below. Chosen at 1 KB |
| (b) Size the buffer from the sink: `ByteBufferOutputStream.array().length - size()`, the room `HttpClientProtocol` presized from the last body, clamped to 256 B…8 KB | Measured below (measurement build only). The same small-put allocation as 512 B, but large bodies keep the 8 KB buffer, and the serializer has to know the sink's type |
| (c) Write directly into the presized body buffer's array | Not measured. `ByteBufferOutputStream` is `@SdkPublicApi` and has no way to commit bytes written into `array()`, so this needs a public API change |
| (d) Pool serializers that have a sink, as `SmithyXmlSerializer` does | Not measured. Once (a) removes the 8 KB array, pooling could save at most the 1 KB buffer plus the serializer's own objects, about 1.2 KB. It needs a wrapper like `LazyXmlSerializer`, so that a write after `close()` cannot reach a serializer the pool has handed to another thread, and the wrapper adds a delegation step to every write |

JMH `SchemaMarshallBenchmark` (`-prof gc`, 2 forks × 3 × 1 s). The variants ran from one measurement build of json-codec placed before `benchmarks.jar` on the class path, with a system property selecting the buffer; "8 KB control" is that build at 8 KB, the same code as HEAD.

| Variant | AWS_JSON_1_0 SMALL B/op | µs/op | AWS_JSON_1_1 SMALL B/op | REST_JSON_1 SMALL B/op | AWS_JSON_1_0 LARGE B/op | µs/op | AWS_JSON_1_1 LARGE B/op | REST_JSON_1 LARGE B/op |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| HEAD (`53b2ee53299`, 8 KB) | 10,388 | 0.869 | 10,440 | 10,936 | 363,585 | 137.1 | 363,513 | 364,077 |
| 8 KB control | 10,360 | 0.743 | 10,360 | 11,048 | 363,685 | 150.6 | 363,713 | 386,533 |
| 512 B | 2,708 | 0.521 | 2,760 | 3,336 | 355,833 | 139.9 | 355,933 | 356,377 |
| 1 KB | 3,220 | 0.708 | 3,204 | 3,880 | 367,645 ± 34,712 | 163.4 ± 105.7 | 356,517 | 368,129 ± 34,430 |
| 2 KB | 4,216 | 0.652 | 4,216 | 4,904 | 357,541 | 156.7 | 357,541 | 380,393 |
| Sized from the sink (b) | 2,680 | 0.423 | 2,732 | 3,368 | 363,685 | 149.1 | 363,657 | 375,313 ± 34,478 |

SMALL falls by the buffer-size difference in every protocol. LARGE allocation falls by about 7 KB, the 8 KB buffer, for (a), and not for (b). REST_JSON_1 LARGE and the 1 KB LARGE row vary by about 22 KB between forks: the 8 KB control shows the same spread (364,077 vs 386,533) for code identical to HEAD, so the spread is run-to-run, not the buffer. The time columns are noise at these settings: the control is 10% slower than HEAD at LARGE for the same code.

e2e: the harness jar built against `400f3fe99f2`, with its json-codec classes replaced by the measurement build and the variant chosen by system property, against the Baseline_Build jar; 3 interleaved repetitions × 2,000 operations, warm-up 1,000, v2-sync, `--metrics`. B/op are means over the 3 repetitions; application CPU and `MarshallingDuration` are means over repetitions 2 and 3, because repetition 1 was still compiling:

| Arm | small-put B/op | small-put CPU µs | batch-put-large B/op | batch-put-large CPU µs | batch-put-large `MarshallingDuration` µs |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Baseline_Build | 67,404 | 52.6 | 358,655 | 437.3 | 209.0 |
| HEAD (8 KB) | 69,089 | 45.5 | 187,785 | 345.3 | 63.5 |
| 512 B | 61,341 | 46.3 | 180,123 | 341.1 | 61.9 |
| 1 KB | 61,877 | 45.5 | 180,654 | 344.2 | 63.1 |
| 2 KB | 62,685 | 45.0 | 181,825 | 347.4 | 63.2 |
| Sized from the sink (b) | 61,259 | 45.4 | 187,745 | 347.1 | 64.0 |

**Choice (`24cd2b3ed74`, `perf(json-codec)`).** A serializer created with a sink starts with a 1 KB buffer; the sink-less serializer that `Codec.serialize` pools keeps 8 KB. small-put goes from +1,685 to −5,527 B/op against the Baseline_Build in this run, and batch-put-large loses another 7.1 KB/op. No variant changed CPU or marshalling time measurably. 512 B and (b) save 536 and 618 B/op more on small-put. 1 KB was chosen because it is a fixed constant with no coupling to the sink's type; it drains half as often as 512 B on large bodies; and a non-ASCII string, whose reservation is 6 bytes per character, fits without growing the buffer up to about 170 characters instead of 85. The constant is private; the tests restate it.

| Item | Result |
|---|---|
| Tests (`JsonSerializerTest`) | The large-document test now requires every write to be at most 1 KB, and fails on `53b2ee53299`. New: a `ByteBufferOutputStream` sink; a small document reaches the sink in one write on `close()`; and a boundary sweep. The sweep moves a pad string from 320 bytes before the end of the buffer to 2 bytes past it, one byte at a time, followed by a structure with string, int, boolean, double and timestamp members, a structure with every number type, a string, a blob and a tail. The string is ASCII, escaped (`"`), two-byte or a surrogate pair, at 0, 1, about 1/6 of the buffer (the 6-byte reservation boundary), the buffer size −3 to +1, and 2 × the buffer + 1 characters. Every case equals `Codec.serialize` byte for byte |
| Module runs | `json-codec` 954 tests, 0 failures, 5 skipped (`mvn -o install`, checkstyle and SpotBugs) |

**CBOR (`1ea51d0276c`, `perf(cbor-codec)`).** `CborSerializer` had the same buffer as the JSON serializer before `400f3fe99f2`: with a sink, a 4 KB buffer that doubled up to the whole document and was written to the sink on `close()`. `DefaultCborSerdeProvider.newSerializer` creates one per call, so the 4 KB is also a per-call allocation. Check for writes back to earlier positions: every write reserves its full size with `ensureCapacity` and then writes from `pos`. Lists and maps with a known size get a definite-length header written up front from that size; structures and lists or maps of unknown size are indefinite-length and end with a break byte, so no container header is backpatched. The one backpatch is a non-ASCII text string's length header (`encodeUtf8TextStringRewind`/`finishTextStringRewind`). It is rewritten inside that string's own `5 + 3 × length` reservation, with no capacity check between the reservation and the rewrite, so a drain never separates them. The drain-to-sink change is therefore safe for the whole buffer, and it is applied as in JSON, with a 1 KB initial buffer for serializers with a sink. The sink-less pooled path keeps 4 KB.

| Variant (JMH, `RPCV2_CBOR`) | SMALL B/op | SMALL µs/op | LARGE B/op | LARGE µs/op |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| HEAD | 6,264 | 0.596 | 720,457 | 81.6 |
| Drain, 4 KB | 6,264 | 0.589 | 204,265 | 76.7 |
| Drain, 512 B | 2,680 | 0.421 | 200,777 | 101.7 ± 5.1 |
| Drain, 1 KB (kept) | 3,192 | 0.402 | 201,193 | 78.2 |
| Drain, 2 KB | 4,216 | 0.678 | 202,313 | 82.0 |

The 512 B LARGE time was 30% above the other variants in this run. Not investigated, but it is a further reason to stay at 1 KB.

**XML (`a2a15cc5486`, `perf(xml-codec)`).** `SmithyXmlSerializer` grew its buffer the same way. Check for writes back to earlier positions: every write appends at `pos`. A start tag is left open (`pendingClose`) while attributes are appended, then closed by appending `>`, and no element start offset is kept, so draining is safe and the change is applied unchanged. The initial size stays 4 KB: `LazyXmlSerializer` takes the serializer, buffer included, from a `StripedPool` and returns it on `close()`, so the buffer is not allocated per call. Before the change, a document over 16 KB also cost a new 4 KB buffer per call, because the pool drops buffers above 16 KB. JMH `REST_XML`: SMALL 4,660 → 4,664 B/op (unchanged), LARGE 1,840,682 → 796,050 B/op, 331.8 → 323.1 µs/op (noise).

| Item | Result |
|---|---|
| Tests (`Rpcv2CborCodecTest`) | Large document through a sink equals `Codec.serialize`, in more than one write, none above 1 KB (fails on `53b2ee53299`); a value larger than the buffer after a near-full buffer (`ByteBufferOutputStream` sink); a small document in one write; and the boundary sweep with the `AllTypes` structure (every member type, including a non-ASCII string), pad from 400 bytes before the buffer end to 2 past it, strings ASCII, two-byte, three-byte or surrogate pair, at 0, 1, 23, 24 (CBOR header sizes), the `5 + 3 × length` reservation boundary ±1, the buffer size −1, 0 and +1, and twice the buffer size + 1 |
| Tests (`XmlSerializerSinkBufferTest`, new) | `XmlCodec` has no sink-less path (`Codec.serialize` also writes through a sink), so the expected output is assembled member by member: each member serialized alone, which fits in the buffer, and the large pad and value strings as literal markup. Large document (4,000 list members, over 100 KB) in writes of at most 4 KB (fails on `53b2ee53299`); a 20 KB value after a near-full buffer; a small document in one write; and the boundary sweep with attributes, a structure of every number type, a blob, a list, a map with escaped keys and values and a namespaced structure, pad from 600 bytes before the buffer end to 2 past it, strings ASCII, escaped (`&`), two-byte, three-byte or surrogate pair |
| Module runs | `cbor-codec` 226 and `xml-codec` 285 tests, 0 failures (`mvn -o install`). `http-client-protocol` 103, `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` 23, `aws-json-client-protocol` 68, `aws-query-client-protocol` 89, `rest-xml-client-protocol` 52 and `rest-json-client-protocol` 47 tests, 0 failures. `smithy-protocol-tests` 923 tests, 16 skipped: awsJson1_0 68/0/6, awsJson1_1 132/0/0, restJson1 264/0/7, restXml 200/0/1, awsQuery 81/0/0, ec2Query 67/0/0, rpcv2Cbor 74/0/2 (passed/failed/skipped, the same as task 7.21). `cloudwatch` 302 and `dynamodb` 65 tests, 0 failures |
| Not changed | `QueryFormSerializer` (awsQuery, ec2Query) starts at 1 KB, grows by half, and hands its own array to the request body without a copy, so it has no second copy to remove |

The xml-codec checkstyle run first failed on `NonJavaBaseModule` in files this change does not touch: the `build-tools` 1.0 jar in `~/.m2` had been replaced at 13:50 by a build without the xml-codec `java.xml` allowance (`3c07dd8d584`). Reinstalling `build-tools` from this branch fixed it.

## `core/schema` enhanced Document interface (task 11.1)

**Target.** `core/schema`, package `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.serde.document`: `Document` (new `serializeContents`, `getDiscriminator`, `expectDiscriminator`, `createDeserializer`, `deserializeInto`, `asShape`, `of(SerializableShape)`), `Documents` (every implementation now holds a `Schema` and implements `serializeContents`; new `StructureDocument` and `LazyStructure`), and the new `DocumentParser` (`@SdkProtectedApi`), `DocumentDeserializer` (`@SdkProtectedApi`, non-final so codecs can extend it in task 11.5) and `DiscriminatorException` (`@SdkPublicApi`). Source: smithy-java `core/src/main/java/software/amazon/smithy/java/core/serde/document/` (`Document`, `Documents`, `DocumentParser`, `DocumentDeserializer`, `DocumentUtils.getMemberValue`, `DiscriminatorException`; commit `142eecf4d`). The task 1.7 rows above describe the scalar and container documents; this section lists only what changed in this task.

`LazyStructure` (a structure document over a `SerializableStruct` that parses the members only when they are first read) is ported here because smithy-java's `DocumentParser.writeStruct` creates it, and `Document.of(SerializableShape)` is in this task. That covers the "typed documents over `SerializableStruct` (no copying)" part of task 11.5; the JSON and CBOR Protocol_Documents remain task 11.5.

| Task, design or source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Task text: numeric `asByte`…`asBigDecimal` "with exact coercion rules", read as "throw on out-of-range or fractional values" | JLS 5.1.2/5.1.3 casts, no throw, as before (`asInteger()` on long 4294967297 is 1, on double 1.9 is 1, on NaN is 0, on +Infinity is `Integer.MAX_VALUE`). Only `asBigDecimal()` on a NaN or infinite float/double throws (task 1.7) | **User decision.** Requirement 18.4, design B.1, Property 29, smithy-java and the existing code all specify casts. Exact coercion would also make `asShape` reject values smithy-java accepts |
| Task text names `discriminator()` and `asStringMap()` | `getDiscriminator()` and `asMap()` | Document SEP names from Requirements 18.3/18.6 and design B.1; `asMap()` has been in use since task 1.7 |
| `serializeContents(ShapeSerializer)` is abstract | A default method that writes the value of `type()` through the matching `as<Type>` method with the `PreludeSchemas` schema of that type; lists and maps are written as untyped list and map documents. Structure, union and other types throw `SerializationException("A <type> document must implement serializeContents")`. Every `Documents` class overrides it | `sdk-core` `LegacyDocumentViews` (`NumberView`, `ListView`, `MapView`) implement `Document` and must keep compiling without change; the default gives them correct contents. The task 1.17 row above assumed only `Documents` implemented the interface |
| Records holding a non-null `Schema`; factories pass `PreludeSchemas.X`, `LIST_SCHEMA` and `STR_MAP_SCHEMA` (static fields of `Documents`) | `AbstractDocument` holds the schema, null for documents created by the `Document.of` factories. `schema()` resolves null to `PreludeSchemas.getSchemaForType(type())`, or to the document list and map schemas, which live in a holder class (`ContainerSchemas`) | `PreludeSchemas` creates documents while it initializes (`DefaultTrait(Document.of(0.0))` for the primitive schemas), so a factory that reads a `PreludeSchemas` field could see null during that initialization. Resolving lazily removes the ordering dependency |
| `type()` hard-coded per record (`IntegerDocument` is `INTEGER` even with an intEnum schema, `StringDocument` is `STRING` for enums, `StructureDocument` is `STRUCTURE` for unions); `NumberDocument` uses `schema.type()` | Same: the type is fixed by the write method or factory, not by the schema (`writeInteger` gives `INTEGER`, `writeBigInteger` gives `BIG_INTEGER`, a union gives `STRUCTURE`) | Kept Smithy-aligned. Requirements 19.1 and 19.7 say `type()` is the Schema's ShapeType, which differs for enums, intEnums and unions; see the open question below |
| `LazyStructure.discriminator()` returns `schema.id()` | Returns `schema.memberTarget().id()` when the schema is a member, else `schema.id()` | A nested structure is written with its member schema, so the source reported the member ID (`com.example#Outer$child`) instead of the structure's ShapeId (Requirement 18.11) |
| `LazyStructure.serializeContents` calls `struct.serialize(serializer)` (the struct's root schema) | `serializer.writeStruct(schema, struct)` with the schema the document was created with | Same call for a top-level structure; a nested structure keeps its member schema, which serializers need for the member name |
| `LazyStructure.serializeMembers` calls `getDocument().serialize(serializer)` | `struct.serializeMembers(serializer)` | The source writes the whole document as one `writeDocument` call where member writes are expected |
| `LazyStructure.getMemberValue` goes through the parsed document and `asObject()` | `struct.getMemberValue(member)` | Returns the struct's typed value (an enum stays an enum) without parsing |
| `LazyStructure.equals`: another `LazyStructure` or a `Document` compared with the parsed `StructureDocument` record (`equals` includes the schema) | `Document.equals(this, o)` and `Document.hash(this)`, as for every other implementation (task 1.7): type and members, not schema | Consistent equality and hash across implementations |
| `StructureDocument.serializeMembers` calls `value.serialize(serializer)` (one `writeDocument(PreludeSchemas.DOCUMENT, …)` per member) | Calls `value.serializeContents(serializer)`; the member documents hold their member schemas. A member that targets `document` is written with `writeDocument(memberSchema, value)`. Null values are skipped | The source loses the member schema and so the member name. The source notes the method is not called in its own code paths |
| `StructureParser.after` stores every member result, including null | Clears the parser after each member and stores only non-null results | Design B.2 and Requirement 19.7: only set members, `getMember` returns null for the rest |
| `StructureDocument`/`StringMapDocument.getMemberNames()` return `Set.copyOf(keySet())` | The unmodifiable key set, in written order | Java 8, and Requirement 18.8 (same as task 1.7) |
| `DocumentUtils.getMemberValue` uses `SchemaUtils.validateMemberInSchema` | `Documents.getMemberValue`: `SchemaUtils.notAMember` when the container has no member of that name, else `SchemaUtils.validateSameMember` (`IllegalArgumentException`) | The SDK `SchemaUtils` port has those two methods (task 3.7) |
| Record `toString` prints every value | `Document(<type>: <value>)` as in task 1.7, but a document whose schema has `@sensitive` (on the member or its target) prints `*** Sensitive Data Redacted ***`. Structure documents print their members, so sensitive members are redacted at every depth | SDK rule: `toString` never shows sensitive fields. Documents created from modeled shapes now carry member schemas |
| `DocumentParser` `public final`; `DocumentDeserializer` `public` | `@SdkProtectedApi` | Used by codec and SDK modules, not by customers, who call `Document.of` and `asShape` |
| `DiscriminatorException extends RuntimeException` | Same, with `serialVersionUID` | Matches `SerializationException` |
| `DocumentDeserializer.parseDiscriminator(String, String)` | Not ported yet | Discriminator parsing and normalization are task 11.9 |
| `DocumentDeserializer.readNull` message `"Attempted to read non-null value as null"` | `"Attempted to read a non-null <type> document as null"` | Names the type |
| Coercion message `"Expected an integer document, …"` (task 1.7 port printed `"Expected a integer document"`) | `"Expected an integer document, but found <type>"`; other types keep `"Expected a <type> document"` | Restores the source text |
| `BlobDocument.serializeContents` passes the stored buffer | Passes a duplicate | A serializer that reads the buffer cannot move the document's position (same rule as `asBlob()`, task 1.7) |
| `Document.of(SerializableShape)` with null | `NullPointerException` from `Validate.paramNotNull(shape, "shape")` | SDK null-argument style |
| `ofNumber`, `ofObject`, `compare`, `asDataStream`, `asEventStream`, `of(Schema, DataStream)`, `of(Schema, EventStream)`, `DataStreamDocument`, `EventStreamDocument`, `DocumentUtils.serializeNumber` | Not ported | Not in the task text. `ofNumber` needs the Requirement 18.5 promotion rule (task 11.13 converts `SdkNumber` with its own view). The stream types do not exist in `core/schema` (task 13.13) |

**Open questions.**

- `type()` for enum, intEnum and union values: smithy-java reports `STRING`, `INTEGER` and `STRUCTURE`, and this port keeps that. Requirements 19.1 and 19.7 (task 11.5) say `type()` equals the Schema's ShapeType (`ENUM`, `INT_ENUM`, `UNION`). Changing it makes a typed enum document unequal to a JSON string document with the same value.
- Union documents have no discriminator, as in smithy-java. Requirement 19.7 says every Serializable_Struct document reports its ShapeId; Requirement 18.11 mentions only structure documents.
- The JSON, CBOR and XML serializers still reject `STRUCTURE`/`UNION` documents in `writeDocument`; writing them (with `__type`) is task 11.9.

**Tests.** `DocumentTest` (71 runs, was 30): boundary narrowing (`Integer.MAX_VALUE + 1`, 128 to byte, `Short.MAX_VALUE + 1`, `Long.MIN_VALUE` to int); floating-point truncation toward zero, saturation (`1e20` to long, int and bigInteger) and `Double.MAX_VALUE` to float; NaN and ±Infinity to integral types; non-finite float and double to bigDecimal with exact messages; bigInteger above 2^64 and bigDecimal fractions, `1e400` and `1.00000000000000000001` to double; 19 coercion failures with exact messages, including numeric strings (`"12"`, `"1.5"`, `"NaN"`) that are not coerced to numbers; `getDiscriminator`/`expectDiscriminator` on untyped documents; `serializeContents` of every scalar factory (method, prelude schema, value) and of list and map documents; the default `serializeContents` on a custom implementation and its failure for a structure; `serialize` as one `writeDocument`. New `TypedDocumentTest` (28) with hand-written generated-like shapes (`DocumentTestShapes`: a structure with string, integer, long, list with a null element, map, nested structure, timestamp, blob, document and sensitive members; a union with string, integer and document members): type and discriminator; member names in written order; unset and unknown members null; member values; the nested structure's discriminator is its ShapeId; members parsed once and only on first read; union documents; scalar and null shapes; copying a document through `Document.of`; `asShape` round trips (structure, three union members) with one `errorCorrection` call; `asShape` from a map document ignoring unknown members and coercing a long to int; a string document with a structure builder and a mistyped member, with exact messages; `serialize` and `serializeContents` of structure, nested structure, union and member documents with the expected schemas; equality and `NUMBER_PROMOTION` on structure members; `getMemberValue`; sensitive redaction in `toString`; non-string map keys; `DocumentParser.clearResult`; `DocumentDeserializer` reads, `containerSize`, `readNull` and a null value. Properties 29–31 (tasks 11.2–11.4) are not written (earlier decision).

**Module runs** (`mvn -o clean install`): `schema` 654 tests (was 585), checkstyle, SpotBugs and japicmp clean; `json-codec` 954 (5 skipped), `cbor-codec` 226, `xml-codec` 285, `http-binding` 151; `http-client-protocol` 103, `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` 23, `aws-json-client-protocol` 68, `aws-query-client-protocol` 89, `rest-xml-client-protocol` 52, `rest-json-client-protocol` 47; all 0 failures. `sdk-core` installed with `-P quick` and its `core.schema` tests (`LegacyDocumentViewsTest`, `SchemaSerdeSupportTest`, `SdkFieldsFromSchemaTest`, 45) pass; the first `sdk-core` build failed test compilation on classes outside this change (`core.Response`, `core.signer`), an IDE rewrite of `target/`, and passed when repeated. `codegen` `SchemaSerdeDocumentMemberTest`, `TraitInitializersTest` and `SchemaRuntimeTypeTest` (16) pass. No serializer or deserializer changed, so the protocol test suites were not rerun. The legacy `software.amazon.awssdk.core.document.Document` is unchanged.
## JSON and CBOR Protocol_Documents (task 11.5)

**Target.** `json-codec`: new `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.json.JsonDocuments` (`@SdkProtectedApi`), and `SmithyJsonDeserializer.readDocument` now returns its documents. `cbor-codec`: new `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.cbor.CborDocuments` (`@SdkProtectedApi`), and `CborDeserializer.readDocument` now returns its documents. `schema`: new `DocumentDeserializer.parseDiscriminator(String, String)`. Sources: smithy-java `codecs/json-codec/src/main/java/software/amazon/smithy/java/json/JsonDocuments.java`, `codecs/cbor-codec/src/main/java/software/amazon/smithy/java/cbor/CborDocuments.java` and `DocumentDeserializer.parseDiscriminator` (commit `142eecf4d`). This section replaces the reading half of the document deferrals above (`CborDocuments`, task 3.6; `JsonDocuments`, task 7.1). The serializers still write documents by type and reject structure and union documents; writing those and their `__type` member is task 11.9.

**Typed documents.** The task's first bullet ("schema-backed typed documents over `SerializableStruct`, no copying") is `Documents.LazyStructure` from task 11.1. Checked against Requirement 19: 19.7 (`type()` is `STRUCTURE`, the discriminator is the structure's ShapeId, unset members are null) and 19.8 (`Document.of` then `asShape` round trip, `TypedDocumentTest`) hold. Nothing was added. The `type()` row below supersedes 19.1 and 19.7 where they differ.

| Task, requirement or source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Requirements 19.1 and 19.7: `type()` is the Schema's ShapeType (`ENUM`, `INT_ENUM`, `UNION`), and every Serializable_Struct document reports its ShapeId as discriminator | `type()` is `STRING` for enums, `INTEGER` for intEnums and `STRUCTURE` for unions, and union documents have no discriminator, as in smithy-java and task 11.1 | **User decision.** Supersedes Requirements 19.1 and 19.7 where they differ. Closes the first two task 11.1 open questions. A typed enum document equals a JSON or CBOR string document with the same value |
| Numeric coercion | JLS 5.1.2/5.1.3 casts with no throw on overflow, in every JSON and CBOR document. Only `asBigDecimal()` on a NaN or infinite float or double throws `SerializationException("Cannot convert a non-finite double document (NaN) to a bigDecimal")` | **User decision**, as in task 11.1 |
| Records `ByteDocument`, `ShortDocument`, `IntegerDocument`, `LongDocument`, `FloatDocument`, `DoubleDocument`, `NumberDocument` (big numbers) | `IntegralDocument` (type plus a `long`), `FloatingPointDocument` (type plus a `double`), `BigIntegerDocument`, `BigDecimalDocument`, as in `Documents` | Java 8 has no records. A long holds every byte, short and integer value exactly, and a double holds every float exactly, so the casts give the source's results |
| `asBigDecimal()` of a byte through long is `BigDecimal.valueOf((double) value)`; of a big integer, `BigDecimal.valueOf(value.doubleValue())` | `BigDecimal.valueOf(long)` and `new BigDecimal(BigInteger)` | The source loses digits above 2^53 (`9007199254740993` becomes `9007199254740992`). Same as `Documents` (tasks 1.7, 11.1) |
| `JsonDocuments.of(Number, JsonSettings)` is the only number factory | Also `of(int, …)`, `of(long, …)`, `of(double, …)`, `of(BigInteger, …)` and `of(BigDecimal, …)`; `of(Number, …)` keeps the source's per-class types | The deserializer creates numbers without boxing |
| `asTimestamp()` of a JSON number with `epoch-seconds`: `format.readFromNumber(n.doubleValue())`, which is `(long) (d * 1000)` milliseconds | Exact: integral numbers through `Instant.ofEpochSecond`; doubles through `BigDecimal.valueOf(d)`, the shortest decimal form, which is the JSON text the deserializer kept as a double; big decimals and big integers exactly. Digits past nanoseconds are dropped, as `SmithyJsonDeserializer` drops them. Out-of-range values throw `SerializationException("Epoch seconds out of range: <value>")`, and NaN or infinity throws `SerializationException` | Requirement 19.11 round trip. The JSON serializer writes up to nine fraction digits and the JSON deserializer reads them exactly; the source truncates to milliseconds and can lose one (`1.001` reads as 1000 milliseconds) |
| `asTimestamp()` of a JSON string: the `DateTimeParseException` of `date-time` and `http-date` escapes | Wrapped in `SerializationException("Expected a date-time timestamp, but found \"nope\"")` | Requirement 19.10: a `SerializationException` that names the default format. Wrong-type values still throw codec-commons `TimestampSyntaxError` (`Expected a NUMBER value for a EPOCH_SECONDS timestamp, but found String`), as the JSON deserializer does; it names the format by its enum constant |
| `JsonDocumentDeserializer.readStruct` loops over the schema's members and looks each up with `fieldMapper().memberToField(member)`; unknown fields are never reported | Loops over the document's fields in document order, resolves each with `fieldMapper().fieldToMember(container)` (the target schema for a member), and handles unknown fields as `SmithyJsonDeserializer` does: reported for structures (`__type` included); for unions, `__type` is skipped and other fields are reported, or rejected with `Unknown member <name> encountered for union <id>` when `forbidUnknownUnionMembers` is set. Null values are skipped | Reading a JSON document behaves like reading the same bytes with the codec. The base `DocumentDeserializer` (task 11.1) also reports unknown members |
| `JsonDocumentDeserializer.readTimestamp`: `TimestampResolver.readTimestamp(asObject(), resolve(schema))` | The `asTimestamp()` rules above, with the format the resolver gives for the member (its `timestampFormat` trait when `useTimestampFormat` is on, else the default) | Exact epoch seconds, same errors (Requirement 19.6) |
| `JsonDocumentDeserializer` does not override `readFloat`, `readDouble`, `readBigInteger` or `readBigDecimal` | `readFloat` and `readDouble` also read the strings `"NaN"`, `"Infinity"` and `"-Infinity"`. `readBigInteger` and `readBigDecimal` also read strings when `useStringForArbitraryPrecision` is on. `asDouble()` and `asBigDecimal()` on a string document still throw | Requirement 19.11: the JSON serializer writes those values as strings, so in the source a non-finite float member, or an arbitrary-precision member with that setting, does not round trip. Requirement 18.7 allows no string-to-number coercion in the `as<Type>` methods |
| SDK addition of task 7.1: `SmithyJsonDeserializer.readString` returns the literal text of a JSON number | Not in the document deserializer: a string member whose document is a number throws `Expected a string document, but found integer` | The literal text is gone once the number is parsed (`1e20` would read back as `1.0E20`). Same as smithy-java |
| Record `hashCode()` (value and settings) with `equals` through `Document.equals` | `Document.hash(this)` and `Document.equals(this, o)`; settings are not compared. `CborDocuments.MapDocument.hashCode()` (`values.hashCode()`) likewise | A hash code consistent with equals and with the 11.1 documents: a JSON or CBOR document equals the `Document.of` document of the same type and value. smithy-java's `onlyEqualIfBothUseTimestampFormat` and `onlyEqualIfBothUseJsonName` are `@Disabled` upstream and are not ported |
| Each record implements `serializeContents`: lists write elements with `serialize`, map values with `serializeContents`, with a `PreludeSchemas.DOCUMENT` container schema and a `STRING_MAP_KEY` key schema | No override: the default `Document.serializeContents` (task 11.1) writes the value of `type()` with its prelude schema, and lists and maps as untyped list and map documents whose elements and values are written with `serialize` | One implementation with the same output. Map values written with `serialize` reach the serializer through `writeDocument`, as list elements do, which task 11.9 needs for nested structure documents |
| `MapDocument.discriminator()` parses `__type` on every call | Parsed on the first call and cached (a volatile field with a no-discriminator sentinel). A parse failure is not cached and throws on every call | Design B.3 |
| A `__type` that is not a string gives no discriminator | Same | smithy-java. Requirement 20.8 (task 11.9) asks for an error; see the open questions |
| `CborDocuments` has only a map document; other values are untyped documents | A document for every value the CBOR codec decodes: boolean, integer (`long`), float or double (by encoded width, as task 3.6), bignum (`bigInteger`), decimal fraction (`bigDecimal`), text string, byte string (`blob`), tag 1 (`timestamp`), array and map | Requirement 19.3 and design B.2. CBOR has native blobs and timestamps, so no value converts to another type: `asTimestamp()` on an integer and `asBlob()` on a text string throw |
| `CborDocumentSerializer` (sic) only overrides `deserializer` | `CborDocumentDeserializer`, which also overrides `readStruct` with the `CborDeserializer` rules for unknown members: `__type` of a union is skipped, other unknown union members are rejected when `forbidUnknownUnionMembers` is set | Reading a CBOR document behaves like reading the same bytes. The base deserializer reports a union's `__type` as an unknown member |
| `JsonDocuments`, `CborDocuments` are `@SmithyInternalApi` public classes; records are package-private with public accessors such as `settings()` | `@SdkProtectedApi` public classes in `codecs.json` and `codecs.cbor` (design B.2), so the codecs' `internal` deserializers and other SDK modules can create them. Implementations are package-private nested classes; `settings()` is package-private on their base class | `PackageContainmentTest` restricts `@SdkInternalApi` types to `internal` packages; customers read the documents through `Document` |
| Record `toString` | `JsonDocument(<type>: <value>)` and `CborDocument(<type>: <value>)`, the `Documents` format with the codec's name | Shows which codec read a document |
| Null arguments to the factories | `NullPointerException("value must not be null")` and `("settings must not be null")` through `Objects.requireNonNull` | The codecs do not depend on `utils` (as `TimestampResolver`) |
| Task 11.1 listed `DocumentDeserializer.parseDiscriminator` for task 11.9 | Ported in this task, with the source's messages; `ShapeIdSyntaxException` is the SDK's | The JSON and CBOR map documents read `__type` with it. Task 11.9 keeps the writing side and Requirement 20.8 |

**Wire form.** Reading the JSON written for a structure as a document and writing that document gives the same bytes with `JsonSettings.REST_JSON_1` and `JsonSettings.AWS_JSON` (`JsonDocumentsTest`). The JSON values are always equal, with two differences in the text:
- A double of 1e7 or more is written with an exponent (`1398796238.12` becomes `1.39879623812E9`). This is the serializer's form for every double, for example an epoch-seconds timestamp written with `useTimestampFormat` off.
- With `useStringForArbitraryPrecision`, a number too precise for a double (a big integer or big decimal document, such as an epoch-seconds timestamp with nine fraction digits) is written as a string. smithy-java does the same: `serializeContents` of a big number calls `writeBigDecimal`.

For CBOR, the values are equal, but structures are written as indefinite-length maps (`0xbf`) and map documents with their length (`0xb3` for 19 members). Writing a CBOR document read from document bytes gives the same bytes.

**Tests.** `JsonDocumentTest` now ports the 9 smithy-java methods that were skipped (task 7.1 row above): `convertsToTimestampWithEpochSeconds`, `convertsToTimestampWithDefaultStringFormat`, `convertsToBlob`, `deserializesIntoBuilder` and `deserializesIntoBuilderWithJsonNameAndTimestampFormat` (with `SchemaStruct` and the source's `TestPojo` schema), and the four discriminator tests (`failsToParseRelativeDiscriminatorWithNoDefaultNamespace` also asserts the `DiscriminatorException`, which the source only provokes by reading). `serializeContent` calls `serializeContents` again. 62 runs. `CborDocumentTest` likewise ports `deserializesIntoBuilder` and the four discriminator tests (57 runs). New `JsonDocumentsTest` (59 runs) covers: settings held by every value and nested value; base64 `asBlob` (valid, empty, invalid, on a number); `asTimestamp` with `epoch-seconds` for ten numbers (integral, long, millisecond and nanosecond fractions, `1.001`, more than nine fraction digits, negative fractions, exponent), out-of-range numbers, strings with the epoch default, `date-time` and `http-date` strings, unparseable strings and numbers with string formats (exact messages), and booleans, lists and maps; numeric narrowing; exact `asBigDecimal`; `of(Number)` types; coercion failure messages; equality and hash codes against `Document.of` documents, `NUMBER_PROMOTION`, and settings not compared; `toString`; discriminator caching, absent and non-string `__type`, an invalid ShapeId on every call, and nested objects; `asShape` round trips of a 23-member structure (every simple type, NaN, -Infinity, three timestamp formats, list, map, nested structure with `jsonName`, union, enum, intEnum, document) under seven settings (the two presets, defaults, `useTimestampFormat` off, `date-time` and `http-date` defaults, strings for arbitrary precision); same bytes for the presets and equal values for the other settings; the wire-form differences above; `jsonName` and `timestampFormat` only when the settings enable them; union and structure unknown-member rules; non-finite and arbitrary-precision strings; a document member keeping its JSON document; mismatch errors; null arguments. New `CborDocumentsTest` (18): document types and settings for every CBOR value, half and single floats from raw bytes, byte strings and tag 1 (double and integer), independent blob buffers, no cross-type coercions, numeric narrowing and `NaN` to bigDecimal, equality and hash codes, `toString`, discriminators, an `asShape` round trip of a 19-member structure, the definite-length map difference, same bytes for documents, unknown members, mismatch errors, null arguments. `TypedDocumentTest` adds three `parseDiscriminator` tests (31). Properties 32–34 (tasks 11.6–11.8) are not written.

**Module runs** (`mvn -o clean install`, checkstyle and SpotBugs included): `schema` 657 tests (was 654); `json-codec` 1021 (5 skipped, as before; was 954), then `JsonDocumentsTest` alone after adding the `1.001` case (59); `cbor-codec` 249 (was 226); `http-client-protocol` 103, `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` 23, `aws-json-client-protocol` 68, `rest-json-client-protocol` 47; all 0 failures. `smithy-protocol-tests` (`clean install -pl :smithy-protocol-tests`, 923 tests, 16 skipped): awsJson1_0 68/0/6, awsJson1_1 132/0/0, restJson1 264/0/7, restXml 200/0/1, awsQuery 81/0/0, ec2Query 67/0/0, rpcv2Cbor 74/0/2 (passed/failed/skipped), unchanged.

**Open questions.**
- Requirement 20.8 (task 11.9) says a `__type` that is not a string is an invalid discriminator. smithy-java and this port return no discriminator.
- JSON documents with `useStringForArbitraryPrecision`: a number that the reader keeps as a big decimal for precision (not because a model said `bigDecimal`) is written back as a string. Writing raw numeric documents as numbers would need the document to remember that it came from a JSON number; smithy-java does not.
- CBOR integers stay `long` documents and floats stay `float` or `double` by encoded width (task 3.6, smithy-java). Requirement 18.5 reports the narrowest of `integer`, `long`, `bigInteger`, `double` and `bigDecimal` for documents without a schema, so a CBOR `1` does not equal a JSON `1` without `NUMBER_PROMOTION`.

## Document serialization and discriminators (task 11.9)

**Target.** `json-codec` `internal.SmithyJsonSerializer.writeDocument` and `cbor-codec` `internal.CborSerializer.writeDocument` now write structure documents; `schema` `DocumentDeserializer.parseDiscriminator` rejects member ShapeIds. Sources: smithy-java `SmithyJsonSerializer.writeDocument` and `SerializeDocumentContents`, `JacksonJsonSerializer.SerializeDocumentContents`, `CborSerializer.writeDocument` and `SerializeDocumentContents`, `JsonSettings.serializeTypeInDocuments` (commit `142eecf4d`). This closes the third task 11.1 open question and the writing half of the task 11.5 deferral. `xml-codec` is unchanged: smithy-java's XML serializers write nothing for a document, and so does the SDK port (see the `XmlCodec` Javadoc). restXml, awsQuery and ec2Query have no document members (Requirement 20.1 lists only awsJson1_0, awsJson1_1, restJson1 and rpcv2Cbor).

**Behavior.**
- `writeDocument` calls `Document.serializeContents` with the serializer itself, as smithy-java does, instead of the by-type switch over the `as<Type>` accessors that tasks 3.6 and 7.1 used. A null document is still written as null. Typed documents now pass their own schemas: a timestamp document taken from a member with `@timestampFormat` (`Document.of(x).getMember("created")`) is written in that format when the writer's `useTimestampFormat` is on, where the switch used the prelude schema and the default format.
- A document whose `type()` is `STRUCTURE` is written through a serializer that accepts one `writeStruct`. That call writes the structure's members with the writing codec's rules (field names from `jsonName` when the settings use it, `timestampFormat` when `useTimestampFormat` is on, the default timestamp format otherwise). JSON writes `"__type":"<namespace#Name>"` first when `serializeTypeInDocuments` is on and the document has a discriminator. CBOR always writes it when there is a discriminator; `CborSettings` has no such setting, as in smithy-java.
- Only the document's own structure gets `__type`. A structure nested in its members is written as an ordinary structure. A document nested in its members (a `document` member, or a list or map document element) goes through `writeDocument` again and gets its own `__type`.
- A document is written with the writing codec's settings, never the settings it was read with. JSON and CBOR Protocol_Documents have no `serializeContents` of their own (task 11.5). The default writes each value with its prelude schema, so a CBOR blob or timestamp document written by JSON is base64 or the JSON codec's default timestamp format. A typed structure written by another codec is re-walked through `serializeMembers` with that codec's settings.

| Task, requirement or source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| smithy-java writes `__type` from the schema passed to `writeStruct` (`structSchema.id()`), so a union document gets its union's ShapeId | Writes `Document.getDiscriminator()`, and nothing when it is null. A union document has no discriminator (task 11.5 user decision) and is written without `__type` | Requirement 20.6. For a structure document (`Documents.LazyStructure`), `getDiscriminator()` is the same ShapeId as smithy-java's, including for a nested structure written with its member schema |
| smithy-java `SerializeDocumentContents` throws `IllegalStateException("Unexpected schema type: …")` when a structure document writes something other than a structure | `SerializationException("A structure document must write a structure, but wrote a value of type <type> with schema <id>")` | The codec's exception type, with the type and schema |
| smithy-java reads the discriminator (or schema) whatever the settings | JSON reads `getDiscriminator()` only when `serializeTypeInDocuments` is on | With the setting off, a document whose discriminator cannot be read is still written |
| Requirement 20.2: a structure Document's Discriminator is written as an absolute ShapeId "even when the Discriminator was read in relative form or was read by a different Client_Protocol" | A Protocol_Document (a JSON or CBOR map document, `type()` `MAP`) is written as the map it was read as: its `__type` entry keeps the text it was read with (relative or absolute, valid or not), and `serializeTypeInDocuments` does not add or remove it. The absolute form is written for documents whose `type()` is `STRUCTURE`, which includes a Protocol_Document converted with `asShape` and `Document.of` | smithy-java (keep Smithy-aligned). Rewriting the entry would change map data, would need the discriminator read (and so could throw) for every map with a `__type` key, and would break the SEP assertion that writing the document read from a serialization gives that serialization when the serialization has a relative `__type`. See the open questions |
| Requirement 20.8: a `__type` that is not a string is an invalid Discriminator | No discriminator, as in task 11.5 | smithy-java (keep Smithy-aligned). The SEP `serdeTests` suite (task 11.12) is not in the workspace, so no SEP case contradicts this yet; revisit when it is added |
| smithy-java accepts a member ShapeId (`ns#Name$member`, or relative `Name$member`) as a discriminator: Smithy's `ShapeId.fromOptionalNamespace` parses members | `parseDiscriminator` throws `DiscriminatorException("A document discriminator must name a shape, not a member: <text>")` | The requirements glossary defines the Discriminator as the ShapeId of the document's concrete shape, and Requirement 20.2 writes it as `namespace#Name`. A member ID never names a type a registry can build. Requested by the task instructions, which described this as smithy-java's behavior; smithy-java accepts members |

**Tests.**
- `TypedDocumentTest` (schema), 8 new runs: six malformed discriminators with the exact message and a `ShapeIdSyntaxException` cause (`""`, `#Foo`, `com.example#Foo#Bar`, `com..example#Foo`, `1Foo`, `com.example#Foo$`); a relative name keeping its name and taking the default namespace; absolute and relative member IDs rejected.
- `JsonSerializerTest` (json-codec) ports the four smithy-java document tests: `writesDunderTypeAndMoreMembers`, `writesNestedDunderType`, `writesModeledStructureDocumentInsideStructure` and `writesDunderTypeForEmptyStruct`. `serializesJsonValues` now calls `serializeContents`, as in the source.
- New `JsonDocumentSerializationTest` (json-codec, 13 runs):
  - a typed structure under `REST_JSON_1`, `AWS_JSON`, and `http-date` without `useTimestampFormat` or `__type`: jsonName, the `timestampFormat` trait and the default format follow the writer
  - a timestamp document taken from a typed structure: its member's `date-time` format with `useTimestampFormat` on, epoch seconds with it off
  - no `__type` with `serializeTypeInDocuments` off, and none for union documents
  - nested documents in a `document` member and a list
  - reading the written JSON back gives a Protocol_Document with the same discriminator, the same bytes and the same shape
  - a Protocol_Document read with `AWS_JSON` and written with `REST_JSON_1` keeps its keys, values and relative `__type`, with or without `serializeTypeInDocuments`
  - a Protocol_Document converted to a shape is written with jsonName and an absolute `__type`
  - a Protocol_Document with an invalid `__type` is written unchanged
  - the error for a structure document that writes a string
  - a failing discriminator throws only when `__type` is written
- New `CborDocumentSerializationTest` (cbor-codec, 8 runs; `json-codec` is now a test-scope dependency of `cbor-codec`):
  - exact bytes for a structure document (`__type` first) and a union document (no `__type`)
  - reading back: member order, discriminator, tag 1 timestamp and byte string regardless of the `timestampFormat` trait, no `__type` on the nested structure, `__type` on a nested document
  - a JSON-read document written by CBOR gives the bytes of the same values (base64 and timestamp strings stay strings, `__type` stays relative) and reads back with CBOR's default namespace
  - a CBOR-read document written by JSON writes base64 and the JSON default timestamp format (epoch seconds, then `date-time`)
  - typed structures from a CBOR document (written as JSON with jsonName) and from a JSON document (written as CBOR), both with an absolute `__type`
  - the error for a structure document that writes an integer

Properties 35 and 36 (tasks 11.10, 11.11) are not written.

**Module runs** (`mvn -o clean install`, checkstyle and SpotBugs included): `schema` 665 tests (was 657); `json-codec` 1038 (5 skipped; was 1022 with the task 11.5 `1.001` case), then `JsonDocumentSerializationTest` alone after adding the timestamp member case (13); `cbor-codec` 257 (was 249); `http-client-protocol` 103, `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` 23, `aws-json-client-protocol` 68, `rest-json-client-protocol` 47; all 0 failures. `smithy-protocol-tests` (923 tests, 16 skipped): awsJson1_0 68/0/6, awsJson1_1 132/0/0, restJson1 264/0/7, restXml 200/0/1, awsQuery 81/0/0, ec2Query 67/0/0, rpcv2Cbor 74/0/2 (passed/failed/skipped), unchanged.

**Open questions.**
- Requirement 20.2, relative or cross-protocol discriminators: this port writes a Protocol_Document's `__type` as read (smithy-java). If the SEP `serdeTests` expect a relative `__type` to be written as absolute, the map documents would need a `serializeContents` that rewrites the entry with the reader's default namespace.
- Requirement 20.8, non-string `__type`: no discriminator (smithy-java), not `DiscriminatorException`.
- Member ShapeIds are rejected as discriminators, which smithy-java accepts.

## SEP `serdeTests` suite (task 11.12)

**Fixtures (OD-11).** `core/codecs/json-codec/src/test/resources/new-document-type-test-model.json` and `new-document-type-test-cases.json` are copied unchanged from the AwsDrSeps package (`seps/accepted/shared/schema-serde/`), checkout at HEAD `ff7741679f689f91cad2bfa9e6312cf3cc7691ec`. Both files were last changed in `1b997f468a624f219c58bc84b2ccc5cd5568a061` (2025-11-07, "move Schema & Serialization Decoupling SEP from in-progress to accepted"). SHA-1: test model `c080558bc14f81d7ea24689392617640af0d78c6`, test cases `d57bf1ba98756ce902631beacaa9225e8c8ff4d0`. The SEP text next to them is the same file as the workspace reference copy of `document-types-and-type-registries.md`.

**Target.** New json-codec tests `DocumentSerdeTestSuite` and `SmithyJsonAstSchemas`. The json-codec `pom.xml` adds `**/DocumentSerdeTestSuite.java` to the surefire includes (appended to the parent's patterns, which do not match `*TestSuite`). No main code changed.

**Suite.** A `@TestFactory` with one container per case and one test per SEP assertion (6 cases, 36 tests). The setup is the SEP's: the case's `serialized` node (read from the fixture as a JSON document) is written by the case's codec with `PreludeSchemas.DOCUMENT` to give `canonicalSerialization`; that is read with the subject schema into `canonicalDataObject`, read as a document into `documentFromSerialization`, and `Document.of(canonicalDataObject)` gives `documentFromDataObject`. The fixture files are read from the classpath, and a missing file fails with a message naming the SEP directory to copy it from.

| SEP or design | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Design B.3: subject schemas built with the Schema_Converter as a test-scope dependency | `SmithyJsonAstSchemas` (test code) builds `Schema`s from the JSON AST read with the JSON codec. It supports simple shapes, `enum`, `intEnum`, `structure`, `union`, `list` and `map`, prelude and model targets, recursion (`SchemaBuilder` targets, then `resolve()`), and the `jsonName`, `timestampFormat`, `xmlName` and `enumValue` traits. Any other shape type, trait or target throws `IllegalArgumentException` naming the shape | The Schema_Converter is task 13.1 and does not exist yet. json-codec bans Jackson databind in every scope, and `smithy-model` is not a json-codec dependency. Replace with the Schema_Converter when task 13.1 lands |
| SEP: the data object is the generated class (`OmniWidget`) | `SchemaStruct`, the json-codec test stand-in for a generated class (values by member index, compared with `equals`) | No generated classes in json-codec |
| SEP case `settings` | `defaultNamespace` → `defaultNamespace`, `jsonName` → `useJsonName`, `timestampFormat.useTrait` → `useTimestampFormat`, `timestampFormat.default` → `defaultTimestampFormat`. Settings a case omits keep the `JsonCodec` defaults (`useJsonName` and `useTimestampFormat` off, `epoch-seconds`, no default namespace, `serializeTypeInDocuments` on). An unknown setting or a codec other than `json` throws | The SEP does not say what an omitted setting means |
| Assertion 1: no assertion beyond a successful setup | Also asserts that reading `canonicalSerialization` reported no unknown members | Otherwise a member dropped by a wrong `jsonName` mapping would leave the other assertions comparing data objects that lost the same data |
| Assertions 4–6: the serialization "is" `canonicalSerialization` | Compared as exact text | All six cases give identical text, including number forms |
| Assertion 6: writing `documentFromDataObject` gives `canonicalSerialization` | With the case's codec (`serializeTypeInDocuments` on), the output is `canonicalSerialization` with a leading `"__type":"smithy.example#OmniWidget"`. With the same settings and `serializeTypeInDocuments` off, it is exactly `canonicalSerialization`. Both are asserted | A document created from a structure has a discriminator, and Requirement 20.2 and the SEP's "Typed Document Serialization" section write it as an absolute `__type`. The SEP's `canonicalSerialization` (assertion 4, the data object written directly) has no `__type`, so the SEP's literal assertion 6 holds only with the type off. See the open questions |
| The `deserialized` member of each case | Not asserted | The SEP calls it a reference for building data objects and lists no assertion for it. In "default settings with populated members with initial values" its timestamps keep the serialized forms (`0`, an `http-date` string), unlike the convention the SEP states for it |

**Results.** 36 of 36 pass, none skipped, with no code changes. A mutation check (the converter dropping `timestampFormat` traits) failed the 18 tests of the three cases with format-specific timestamps, as expected.

**Task 11.9 open questions.** The SEP cases settle none of them. The only `__type` in the cases is the absolute `smithy.example#OmniWidget` inside a `document` member, which is written as read in every representation. No case has a relative `__type` (Requirement 20.2), a non-string `__type` (Requirement 20.8) or a member ShapeId. The SEP text does bear on Requirement 20.2: "When serializing a document, implementations MUST always serialize it as an absolute shape ID" ("Typed Document Serialization", item 2).

**Module runs** (`mvn -o clean install -pl :json-codec`, checkstyle and SpotBugs included): 1075 tests, 0 failures, 5 skipped (was 1039). No main code changed, so other modules and the protocol test suites were not rerun.

**Open questions.**
