- Assertion 6 and `__type`: should the SEP cases name `serializeTypeInDocuments`, or should `canonicalSerialization` include the subject's `__type`? The suite asserts both forms.
- Requirement 20.2 for Protocol_Documents with a relative `__type` (task 11.9): the SEP text quoted above asks for absolute IDs when serializing, which supports rewriting the entry. That conflicts with SEP assertion 5 for a serialization with a relative `__type`, which no current case has.

## `DocumentConverter` (task 11.13)

**Target.** New `sdk-core` `software.amazon.awssdk.core.document.DocumentConverter` (`@SdkPublicApi`, final, static methods `toSchemaDocument(Document)` and `toLegacy(schema Document)`). Source: smithy-java `aws/sdkv2/aws-sdkv2-shapes` `DocumentConverter`, `AwsJsonProtocols`, `SdkJsonDocumentVisitor` and `SdkJsonDocumentParser` (commit `142eecf4d`). The task 7.17 views move from the package-private `core.schema.LegacyDocumentViews` to the public `@SdkInternalApi` `core.schema.internal.LegacyDocumentAdapter`, renamed because checkstyle's `PluralEnumNames` rejects public class names that end in "s". `SchemaSerdeSupport.wrapLegacy`/`unwrapLegacy` and `DocumentConverter` both call it. The Legacy_Document (`core.document.Document` and `core.document.internal.*`) is unchanged.

**Delegation.** `DocumentConverter` and the `SchemaSerdeSupport` methods share one implementation and differ only in how they handle nulls:

| | `SchemaSerdeSupport` (generated code) | `DocumentConverter` (customers) |
|---|---|---|
| `null` argument | `wrapLegacy(null)` is `null`; `unwrapLegacy(null)` is `Document.fromNull()` (a Smithy null read by a codec) | `NullPointerException("document")` both ways (Requirement 21.6, through `Validate.notNull`) |
| `Document.fromNull()` at the top level | `null`, which codecs write as `null` | `LegacyDocumentAdapter.NULL`, a null-valued document |

`SchemaSerdeSupport` cannot delegate to `DocumentConverter` because generated code needs the codecs' Java `null`. A public `DocumentConverter` that delegated to the `@SdkProtectedApi` `SchemaSerdeSupport` would still need a null-valued document that `unwrapLegacy` recognizes at any depth, and only the views class can provide that, so both call the internal class. Moving it to an `internal` package, where `PackageContainmentTest` requires public `@SdkInternalApi` types to be, makes it reachable from `core.document`.

| Task, design or source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| smithy-java: an interface with one implementation per protocol (`AwsJsonProtocols`: awsJson1_0, awsJson1_1, restJson1) found through `ServiceLoader`; restJson1 keys structure members by `jsonName` and writes timestamps in their `timestampFormat` | One final class with static methods and no protocol. Structure members use Smithy member names, and timestamps are always epoch seconds | Design B.4 and Requirements 21.3/21.5 (as the design's porting map records). The schema Document does not depend on a protocol |
| smithy-java `sdkToSmithy`: an eager copy into `JsonDocuments`; numbers become `bigDecimalValue()` (the `SdkNumber` is lost) | Lazy views (task 7.17). A number keeps its Legacy_Document, so `toLegacy` returns the same `SdkNumber` (Requirement 21.2). Its `type()` is the first of integer, long, bigInteger, double and bigDecimal that holds the value exactly (Requirement 18.5, design B.4), so the narrowest type is used, not always bigDecimal | Design B.4 ("lazy view", "keeps the original `SdkNumber`") |
| Design B.4: the top-level null becomes `Documents.NULL`; smithy-java `sdkToSmithy` returns Java `null` | `LegacyDocumentAdapter.NULL`, a singleton: `type()` is `DOCUMENT`, `asObject()` is `null`, `getMember` is `null`, and every other `as<Type>` throws the usual `SerializationException`. `serializeContents` writes `writeNull(PreludeSchemas.DOCUMENT)`, `toString` is `Document(null)`, and it equals only itself and other `DOCUMENT` documents whose `asObject()` is `null`. `toLegacy` returns `Document.fromNull()` for it at any depth. Nested null elements and values stay Java `null` | **User decision.** `core/schema` has no null document and no null `ShapeType`; adding `Documents.NULL` there would change the schema module and the codecs. With Java `null`, `toLegacy(toSchemaDocument(fromNull()))` would throw under Requirement 21.6 and break Requirement 21.2 for a top-level null |
| Design B.4 (Document → Legacy numbers): `fromNumber(SdkNumber.fromBigDecimal(...))`, or the narrower factory when the value is integral | The factory of the value's Java type, as `unwrapLegacy` already did: byte, short and int through `fromNumber(int)`, then `long`, `float`, `double`, `BigInteger` and `BigDecimal`. A double `1.0` becomes `"1.0"`, `1e20` becomes `"1.0E20"` | No behavior change for generated code. The JSON codec reads `1.0` as a double, and the legacy unmarshaller kept the literal `1.0`, so this matches it more often than `"1"` would. The values are equal either way (Requirement 21.5) |
| Design B.4 timestamps: `BigDecimal.valueOf(epochMilli, 3).stripTrailingZeros()` | Same digits, computed as `epochSecond + (nano / 1_000_000) / 1000` and then `stripTrailingZeros`, with scale 0 for whole seconds (`1000`, not `1E+3`). Sub-millisecond digits are dropped toward negative infinity, as `Instant.toEpochMilli()` drops them (`-1 ns` → `-0.001`) | `toEpochMilli()` throws `ArithmeticException` for instants beyond about ±292 million years. The new form converts every `Instant` (`Instant.MAX` → `31556889864403199.999`). This also changes `unwrapLegacy`, but only for inputs that used to throw |
| smithy-java blobs: `ByteBufferUtils.base64Encode` | `BinaryUtils.toBase64` of a copy of `asBlob()`, standard alphabet with padding; the document's buffer does not move | Requirement 21.3 |
| Requirement 21.5: structures and unions become maps of the set members with no Discriminator | `asMap()` of the structure or union document. `getDiscriminator()` is never written. A Protocol_Document read from JSON with a `__type` field has `type()` `MAP`, so it keeps `__type` as an ordinary key | Only structure documents have a discriminator to drop. A JSON map is data |

**Behavior to know.** `Document.of(struct)` builds a structure document by writing the struct's members. A `document` member that holds a `toSchemaDocument` view is copied through `serializeContents` at that point. Its numbers keep their values but get the schema type's spelling (`"1.50"` becomes the double `1.5`), and `toLegacy` gives a new tree, not the original Legacy_Document. Converting the view directly does not do this. Generated classes with `document` members write them with `writeDocument(wrapLegacy(x))` and are not affected (task 7.17).

**Tests.** New `sdk-core` `DocumentConverterTest` (47 runs):
- `NullPointerException("document")` from both methods.
- The top-level null document: type, `asObject`, `getMember`, equality and hash code, `toString`, a coercion failure, conversion back to `fromNull()`, and serialization as `writeDocument` then `writeNull(DOCUMENT)`.
- Strings and booleans.
- 9 legacy numbers (int, `Long.MIN_VALUE`, a 30-digit big integer, `0.25`, `"1.50"`, `"2.0"`, `"1E+3"`, a 36-digit big decimal, `-1E-24`), each with its type and exact value, the `SdkNumber` string in `toString`, and the same `Legacy_Document` back.
- Java narrowing (a double above `Integer.MAX_VALUE` saturates; a long wraps).
- A nested map and list with nulls: order, keys, size, element types and Java `null` elements.
- Laziness.
- A round trip that returns the same instance, and a round trip through copied schema containers that is equal under `Legacy_Document.equals`, `hashCode` and `toString` and keeps a 30-digit number's string.
- `toLegacy` of strings, booleans and 9 numbers of every Java type (string forms and values).
- Blobs: empty, `AA==`, `+/8=`, `aGkh`, and the buffer not moved.
- 11 timestamps: whole, milliseconds, sub-millisecond truncation, negative fractions, `Instant.MAX`, `Instant.MIN`.
- Lists and maps with nulls, and empty containers.
- A structure from `Document.of(struct)` with integer, string, a `date-time` timestamp written as epoch seconds, blob, nested structure, list, map and document members, an unset member absent and the discriminator dropped, in written order.
- A union.
- The error for a type with no Legacy_Document form (`Unsupported document document`, the existing message).

The existing `LegacyDocumentViewsTest` (27) and `SchemaSerdeSupportTest` (7) are unchanged and pass. Properties 37 and 38 (tasks 11.14, 11.15) are not written.

**Module runs** (from a `/tmp` worktree of HEAD with this change, because the IDE was rewriting the workspace's `target/` and the workspace build failed to compile unrelated test classes): `sdk-core` `mvn -o clean install` with checkstyle, SpotBugs and japicmp: 1983 tests (6 skipped) and the 651-test TestNG suite (272 skipped), 0 failures. japicmp passes: the only `core.document` change is `NEW CLASS: PUBLIC(+) FINAL(+) software.amazon.awssdk.core.document.DocumentConverter`, and `core.document.Document` is not listed. `schema-serde-source-compat` 44 tests (`LegacyDocumentSourceCompatTest` 2) and `codegen` `SchemaSerdeDocumentMemberTest` 4 (generated document members round-trip through `wrapLegacy`/`unwrapLegacy`) pass. CloudWatch has no document members, so it does not run this code, and it was not rerun.

**Open questions.**
- `LegacyDocumentAdapter.NULL` is a top-level value only. A schema list built by hand that contains it is not `Document.equals` to the same list with a Java `null` element, although codecs write both as `null`.
- Requirement 21.7 (task 7.18) is unchanged by this task: across the wire, numbers keep their value but not always their `SdkNumber` spelling (task 7.17 notes).

## Type registries (task 11.16)

**Target.** `schema`: `TypeRegistry.deserialize(Document)` (a new default method) and its override in the package-private `ComposedTypeRegistry`. The rest of `TypeRegistry`, `ErrorRegistry` and `DefaultTypeRegistry` is unchanged from task 3.14. `codegen`: the new package-private `poet.schema.TypeRegistrySpec`, `SchemaRuntimeType.TYPE_REGISTRY`, and in `SchemaCodegen` the new `typeRegistryClass()`, `typeRegistryShapes()`, `Builder.serviceShapes(Collection<ShapeId>)` and the package-private `builderFor(IntermediateModel, TraitInitializers)`, which `create` now calls. Generated for every schema-enabled service: `<Svc>TypeRegistry` (public, final, `@SdkPublicApi`, model package) with `public static final TypeRegistry INSTANCE` and a private constructor. Source: smithy-java `core/src/main/java/software/amazon/smithy/java/core/serde/TypeRegistry.java` and `codegen/codegen-core/.../generators/TypeRegistryGenerator.java` (commit `142eecf4d`). smithy-java generates no package-level type registry. `CodegenUtils.getSharedRegistryFileName` names a `model.SharedTypeRegistry`, but nothing calls it, and its generated registries are the private per-client error `TYPE_REGISTRY` fields.

**The `shapes` property.** Requirement 22.6 and design A.18 fill the Primary_Type_Registry from the service shape's `shapes` property. No model can list shapes today:
- Smithy's `ServiceShape` has no such member, in the build's smithy-model 1.71.0 or in the reference checkout (1.74.0, `main`). The JSON AST loader accepts only `version`, `operations`, `resources`, `rename`, `errors`, `traits` and `mixins` on a service.
- The C2J converter (`C2jToSmithyConverter.buildService`) has no C2J input to read for it.

The Document SEP (`document-types-and-type-registries.md`) anticipates this: "If it is not available during implementation, temporarily consider the set of shapes to be empty." So every service gets `INSTANCE = TypeRegistry.empty()`, including all seven prototype services. `SchemaCodegen.Builder.serviceShapes` is the one input the property will feed. `SchemaCodegen.create` does not set it yet, and the codegen tests set it.

| Task, design or source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| smithy-java `deserialize`: `createBuilder(document)` with `document.discriminator()`; a null discriminator gives a null builder and `UnsupportedOperationException("No shape registered for document discriminator: null")` | `document.expectDiscriminator()` first, which throws `DiscriminatorException("<type> document has no discriminator")` before any builder is created. Then `createBuilder(id, SerializableStruct.class)`. An unregistered ID throws `UnsupportedOperationException("No shape registered for document discriminator: <namespace#Name>")`. Otherwise it returns `document.asShape(builder)` | Requirements 22.3 and 22.4, design B.5. `ShapeId.toString()` is the absolute ID |
| smithy-java `createBuilder(Document)` default method | Not ported | Not in design B.5. `deserialize` reads the discriminator itself |
| smithy-java `deserializeStrict(Document)` | Not ported | Not in Requirement 22 or design B.5. Generated builders apply error correction in `asShape`, and nothing in the SDK needs the strict form. It can be added later as a default method without breaking anything |
| smithy-java `compose`: the anonymous registry uses the default `deserialize` over its composed `createBuilder(Document)`, so a registry that overrides `deserialize` loses that override when composed | `ComposedTypeRegistry.deserialize` checks the discriminator (`DiscriminatorException` if absent), then delegates to `first.deserialize` when `first.contains(id)`, otherwise to `second.deserialize` | Requirement 22.5 resolves `deserialize` from the first registry that contains the ID, by the same `contains` rule as the task 3.14 lookups |
| Null `document` | `NullPointerException("document must not be null.")` from `Validate.paramNotNull` | SDK null-argument style |
| `ErrorRegistry.deserialize` | The inherited default; no override | Error registries hold `putLazyType` entries, which the default resolves |
| Design A.18: `<Svc>TypeRegistry.INSTANCE` in the model package, filled with `putLazyType` | As specified. Entries are the listed shapes of type `structure`, in ShapeId order. Unions, enums and other types, and `smithy.api#Unit` (a structure with `@unitType`), are skipped. A listed ID that is not in the model fails generation with `SchemaCodegenException` (`... lists <id>, which is not in the model`), and so does a listed structure outside the service closure (`... which is not in the service closure and has no schema`). Each entry is `putLazyType(ShapeId.fromParts("<namespace>", "<Name>"), () -> <Svc>Schemas<N>.<FIELD>)` with the schema field's ID | Requirements 22.6 and 22.7. Like `ERROR_REGISTRY` (task 3.15), the supplier reads the partition field, so creating the registry and calling `contains` load no partition and no model class. Resolving a shape initializes its partition, which loads (but does not initialize) the classes named by that partition's `shapeClass(X.class)` literals. The partitions do not hold schemas for shapes outside the closure, so a listed shape outside it cannot be registered yet. The property does not exist, so the namespace is a literal and there is no `NS` constant |
| Requirement 22.6: "a public static member that takes no arguments" | A `public static final` field, `INSTANCE`, on a separate `@SdkPublicApi` class. The client interfaces get no accessor | Design A.18. A field on `<Svc>Operations`, as `ERROR_REGISTRY` is, would put a public API member on an `@SdkInternalApi` class. The SEP prefers static access from the client (`EventBridge.registry()`), but the design does not ask for a client method, and leaving the client interfaces alone keeps the `interfaces` japicmp execution unchanged |
| — | Services whose `customization.config` has `shareModelConfig` (they share another service's model package) get no `<Svc>TypeRegistry`, because they get no schema classes at all (`SchemaCodegen.isApplicable`) | Same scope as every schema class |

**Size.** `<Svc>TypeRegistry` adds one class per service: 717 to 772 uncompressed bytes, depending on the name length, and 589 to 645 compressed JAR bytes. Its name matches the Size_Report's `<Prefix>*TypeRegistry` rule (task 9.27), so it counts under Type_Registries. `size-report.md` is regenerated. Besides the new class, its runtime module table now also shows the `schema`, `json-codec`, `cbor-codec`, `xml-codec` and `sdk-core` growth from tasks 11.1 to 11.13, which did not regenerate the report because they did not change any service JAR: all runtime modules went from +777,614 to +834,177 bytes.

**Tests.** `schema` `TypeRegistryTest` (22 runs, was 14):
- Ports of the two smithy-java methods that task 3.14 skipped, `deserializesDocumentsFromRegistry` (eager `putType`) and `registersLazyTypes` (adapted to `putLazyType`).
- SDK tests: a new builder per call, with the same result as `asShape`; `DiscriminatorException` for map, string and integer documents, with no builder created; `UnsupportedOperationException` with the exact message for an unregistered ID and for `empty()`; composition resolving `deserialize` from the first registry that contains the ID, in both orders, falling through to the second, with errors for no discriminator and for an ID neither registry contains, and with neither input changed; an overridden `deserialize` kept under `compose`; null documents.

The existing tests already covered first-wins `compose` for lookups, `compose(empty(), r)` and `compose(r, empty())` returning `r`, null results for unregistered IDs, and immutability (`build_isNotAffectedByLaterBuilderChanges`); they are unchanged. `ErrorRegistryTest` (9, was 8) adds `deserialize` of an error document, an unregistered document and a document with no discriminator. `codegen` gets the new `TypeRegistrySpecTest` (5):
- The golden file `EmptyTypeRegistry.java` from `SchemaCodegen.create`.
- With `serviceShapes`, a list holding two structures (one an error), a union, an enum, a string and `smithy.api#Unit` registers exactly the two structures, and `RegistryTypeRegistry.java` is the golden file.
- The model package compiled with `--release 8` through `ModelPackageCompiler`. `INSTANCE` is the same on every read. `contains` is true for the two structures and false for the unregistered shapes, the partition, `Thing` and the exception class stay unloaded until a shape is resolved, and `deserialize(Document.of(thing))` returns an equal, distinct `Thing`.
- The two generation failures.

`SchemaGeneratorTasksTest` checks that `DemoTypeRegistry.java` is written with `TypeRegistry.empty()`, and `SchemaRuntimeTypeTest` checks the new runtime type. Property 39 (task 11.17) is not written.

**Module runs** (`mvn -o`; the services and the test modules were built from a `/tmp` worktree of the codegen commit, because the IDE was rewriting `services/sqs/target/` in the workspace and test compilation failed on a class file it had moved):
- `schema` `clean install`: 674 tests (was 665), with checkstyle, SpotBugs and japicmp.
- `codegen` `clean install` with the new and affected test classes, then the full suite: 1025 tests.
- The seven prototype services (`clean install -pl :sqs,:secretsmanager,:account,:route53,:sns,:cloudwatch,:dynamodb`): CloudWatch 302, DynamoDB 65, Route 53 19, SQS 119, SNS 13, Secrets Manager 6, Account 8 tests, all passing. Both japicmp executions (`default` and `interfaces`) pass for each service. The only new entry is `NEW CLASS: PUBLIC(+) FINAL(+) ...model.<Svc>TypeRegistry` with `NEW FIELD ... TypeRegistry INSTANCE`.
- `codegen-generated-classes-test`: 1673 tests. The 40 failures in 9 classes match the checkpoint 6 list above (`EndpointDiscoveryAndEndpointOverrideTest` 16, `HostPrefixTest` 4, `HttpChecksumInHeaderTest` 4, `HttpChecksumRequiredTest` 2, `BlobUnmarshallingTest` 8, `MoveQueryParamsToBodyTest` 2, `SensitiveDataRedactedTest` 1, `CustomSdkShapeTest` 1, `RpcV2CborUserAgentTest` 2). One more failure, `AsyncRetrySetupTest` scenario 8 (WireMock received no request in 90 s), passed on a rerun of the class (48 tests) and is a timing flake.
- `schema-serde-source-compat`: 44 tests.
- `size-report`: `SizeReportDocumentTest` flagged `size-report.md` as expected. The report was regenerated with `-Dsize-report.update=true`, and then all 63 tests pass.

**Open questions.**
- The Primary_Type_Registry is empty for every service until Smithy adds the service `shapes` property. When it does, `SchemaCodegen.create` should pass `service.getShapes()` (or the equivalent) to `serviceShapes`. A listed structure outside the operation closure will also need a schema field, because the partitions hold only closure shapes; until then such a listing fails generation.
- OD-18 suggests that a customization could also register every structure lazily for document-heavy services. That is not implemented. With `putLazyType` it costs one `putLazyType` call and one lambda per structure.
- As with `ERROR_REGISTRY`, an entry reads the partition field. A structure that several request or response classes share (task 3.7) has no shape class or builder on its partition field, so resolving it through the registry throws `IllegalStateException` (`... has no shape class that implements SerializableStruct`). Operation inputs and outputs are not normally listed in `shapes`, so this does not affect the registry today.

## No per-shape document code (task 11.18)

**Target.** `codegen-maven-plugin` tests, package `software.amazon.awssdk.codegen.pipeline`, next to `NoTransformPackageTest`: the new `NoPerShapeDocumentCodeTest`, the scanner it uses, `PerShapeDocumentCodeScanner`, and the scanner's own test, `PerShapeDocumentCodeScannerTest`. `PerOperationSerdeScanner.javaFiles` and `read` become package-private so the new scanner reuses them. No production code changes; Codegen already generates no per-shape document code (design B.6), and the test keeps it that way.

**What is scanned.** Design B.6 says "generated sources" and Requirement 24.1 says the output of Codegen for a Prototype_Service_Set service. The test does what `NoTransformPackageTest` does: each of the seven `LegacyRouteParity.PROTOTYPE_MODULES` is generated through the `CodegenPipeline`, as `GenerationMojo` does, into a temporary directory, and `src` and `tests` are scanned. This checks the code the current codegen produces, not a stale `services/*/target` tree or a hand-maintained fixture. No prototype service has a member that targets a `document` shape, so the Legacy_Document exemption never appears in their output. The test therefore also scans the codegen golden files (`codegen/src/test/resources/software/amazon/awssdk/codegen/poet`, 293 `.java` files). The codegen generator tests compare these files with generator output, and they include model classes with `document` members (`putdocrequest.java`, `docssharedserde.java`, which call `SchemaSerdeSupport.wrapLegacy`).

**Rules** (matched after comments are removed with `LegacyRouteParity.withoutComments`; string literals are kept; each rule is reported once per file, with its first match):

| Rule | Pattern | Covers (Requirement 24.1) |
|---|---|---|
| `DOCUMENT_METHOD` | the identifier `toDocument` or `fromDocument`, declared, called or referenced (`Foo::toDocument`) | per-shape `toDocument`/`fromDocument` methods, and fields of that name |
| `DOCUMENT_CONVERSION_TYPE` | a declared type (`class`/`interface`/`enum`) named `*DocumentConverter`, `*DocumentSerializer` or `*DocumentDeserializer` with any prefix, or a reference to such a type whose name has a prefix before `Document` | per-shape converter classes, serializers and deserializers |

**Exempt list** (Requirement 24.1's three exemptions; design B.6 calls them "the exempt registries"):

| Exemption | How it is applied |
|---|---|
| Primary_Type_Registry (Requirement 22) | In a file named `<Svc>TypeRegistry.java`, the declaration `TypeRegistry INSTANCE = ...;` is removed before matching |
| Error_Registry (Requirement 23) | In a file named `<Svc>Operations.java`, the declaration `ErrorRegistry ERROR_REGISTRY = ...;` is removed before matching |
| Legacy_Document handling of `document` members (Requirement 21) | Not matched by construction. Generated code calls `SchemaSerdeSupport.wrapLegacy`/`unwrapLegacy`, which may delegate to the runtime `core.document.DocumentConverter`. A bare `DocumentConverter` or `DocumentDeserializer` reference is not reported; only a declaration or a prefixed name is |

The rest of `<Svc>TypeRegistry` and `<Svc>Operations` is still scanned. `NoPerShapeDocumentCodeTest` also checks that each module's output contains both registry declarations, and so do the golden files (`RegistryTypeRegistry.java`, `OpsOperations.java`). If a registry is renamed, the test fails instead of silently scanning the renamed registry as ordinary code.

| Task, design or source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Task text and design B.6: `*DocumentConverter`/`*DocumentSerializer` types | Also `*DocumentDeserializer` | Requirement 24.1 names "per-shape Document serializers or deserializers" |
| Design B.6: "outside the exempt registries" | Only the registry field declarations are exempt, not the whole `<Svc>TypeRegistry` or `<Svc>Operations` class | `<Svc>Operations` holds every operation's schema-side wiring as well, so exempting the whole file would hide code that is not a registry |
| — | The Legacy_Document exemption is a property of the rules (bare runtime names are allowed), not a removed region | The handling is a call to shared runtime methods inside each model class's `serializeMembers`/`deserialize`, not a separate declaration that could be cut out |
| — | Names are matched exactly (`toDocument`, `fromDocument`), not variants such as `fooToDocument` or `toFooDocument` | The design names exactly these. Broader patterns would also match runtime names such as `DocumentConverter.toSchemaDocument` |

**Negative tests.** `PerShapeDocumentCodeScannerTest` (19 runs) feeds synthetic sources to the scanner:
- Each forbidden form is reported: a `toDocument` method, a `fromDocument` static method, a `Foo::toDocument` reference, a `fromDocument` field, declared `FooDocumentConverter`/`FooDocumentSerializer`/`FooDocumentDeserializer`/`DocumentConverter`/`DocumentSerializer` types, and prefixed references.
- A model class with Legacy_Document handling, documentation and line comments that mention the forbidden names, and references to the runtime `DocumentConverter` and `DocumentDeserializer` is not reported.
- A forbidden name inside an exempt declaration is not reported. The same text in another file, or in a file named just `TypeRegistry.java` or `Operations.java`, is reported, and so is a `toDocument` method outside the declaration in an exempt file.
- A temp-dir tree is reported by relative path.

The real scan was also checked by hand: appending `class Injected { Object toDocument() { return null; } }` to the golden file `putdocrequest.java` made `codegenGoldenFiles_containNoPerShapeDocumentCode` fail with `model/schemaserde/putdocrequest.java: DOCUMENT_METHOD: toDocument`. The file was then restored.

**Fix to task 11.16's parity test.** `LegacyRouteParityTest` failed for all seven modules with `src/.../model/<Svc>TypeRegistry.java: only generated by the pipeline`. Task 11.16 added the class but did not add it to `LegacyRouteParity.SCHEMA_GENERATOR_OUTPUT`, the list of schema classes that only the pipeline can generate, and task 11.16 did not run the `codegen-maven-plugin` module. `TypeRegistry` is added to that pattern in a separate commit. The seven parity lists are unchanged.

**Module run** (`mvn -o clean install -pl :codegen-maven-plugin`, in the workspace): 94 tests, all passing, 0 Checkstyle violations, SpotBugs clean. That includes `NoPerShapeDocumentCodeTest` with 8 runs (7 modules plus the golden files, 6.7 s), `PerShapeDocumentCodeScannerTest` with 19, `LegacyRouteParityTest` with 7 and `NoTransformPackageTest` with 8.

**Open questions.**
- Requirements 24.2–24.4 are runtime properties (`Document.of` and `asShape` use only `serializeMembers` and `deserialize`, with no opt-in). Task 11.18 does not test them; design B.2 says they hold by construction.
- A member named `toDocument` or `fromDocument` in some future service model would generate a getter that `DOCUMENT_METHOD` reports. No prototype service has one. If such a member appears, the rule could skip getters whose name matches a modeled member.

## S3 Event Notifications on schemas (task 11.19)

**Target.**
- `codegen`: a types-only mode of the Schema_Generator. `SchemaCodegen.Builder.typesOnly(boolean)` generates only the partition classes and `<Prefix>SchemaIndex`. `SchemaCodegen.Builder.rootShapes(Collection<ShapeId>)` adds closure roots beyond the service's operations and errors (`SchemaFieldOrder.create` gets an overload with `rootShapes`). The new `pipeline.TypesOnlySchemaCodegen` assembles and validates the Smithy files as stage 4 does (any `ERROR` or `DANGER` event fails), then writes the classes and the `META-INF/services` entry. Supporting changes: `SchemaGeneratorTasks.serviceFileTask(Path, SchemaCodegen)` is now public and `IntermediateModel.defaultFileHeader()` is new.
- `codegen-maven-plugin`: the goal `generate-types` (`TypesGenerationMojo`), configured with `service`, `rootShapes`, `packageName`, `classPrefix` and `smithyModelDirectory` (default `src/main/smithy`).
- `services-custom/s3-event-notifications`:
  - `src/main/smithy/s3-event-notification.smithy`. The build generates `internal.S3EventNotificationSchemas` (18 schemas) and `internal.S3EventNotificationSchemaIndex` from it.
  - The 15 model classes implement `SerializableStruct`, with `sdkSchema()`, `serializeMembers` and `getMemberValue`.
  - The new internal `S3EventNotificationSerde` holds the root schemas, the 15 `ShapeBuilder`s (one per structure) and the write helpers.
  - `CodecS3EventNotificationReader` and `CodecS3EventNotificationWriter` implement the unchanged internal `S3EventNotificationReader`/`S3EventNotificationWriter` interfaces, whose `create()`/`builder()` now return them.
  - `DefaultS3EventNotificationReader` and `DefaultS3EventNotificationWriter` moved unchanged (pure renames) to `src/test`.
  - `json-utils` moved to test scope. `schema` and `json-codec` are new compile dependencies; `third-party-jackson-core` stays, because the reader still constructs its `JsonParseException`.
- `build-tools`: the SpotBugs `MS_EXPOSE_REP` exclusion that named `DefaultS3EventNotificationWriter` now names `CodecS3EventNotificationWriter`, which has the same `create()` singleton.

**Codegen.**

| Task, design or source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Design D.7: "Generate Schemas only (types-only codegen mode)" | Partition classes plus `<Prefix>SchemaIndex` and its `ServiceLoader` entry. No service schemas, operations, type registry, shared serde, intermediate model, model classes or protocol classes. `typesOnly` with `intermediateModel`, `internalPackage` or `serviceShapes` set fails with `IllegalArgumentException`. In this mode, `operations()`, `serviceSchemasClass()`, `typeRegistryClass()` and `typeRegistryShapes()` throw `IllegalStateException` | The index costs one small class and makes the shapes resolvable by ShapeId (`SchemaIndex`). The other classes need operations or generated model classes |
| — | Configured on a separate plugin goal, not through `customization.config` or the `generate` goal | `customization.config` describes an IntermediateModel and a client. None of its keys apply, and the library needs a package (`...s3.internal`) that IntermediateModel naming cannot produce |
| Closure (Requirement 10.1): operations and service errors | Plus `rootShapes` and every shape they reach. A root that is not in the model fails with `SchemaCodegenException` | A Smithy service has no `shapes` property (task 11.16), and this service has no operations |
| — | The Smithy model needs a service shape (`S3EventNotifications`, no operations) | `SchemaCodegen` takes the namespace constant, the trait policy and the Javadoc from it |
| — | Generated into `...eventnotifications.s3.internal`, not the model package. The partition class is package-private, so `S3EventNotificationSerde` republishes the root schemas as `public static final` fields | `EqualsAndHashCodeTest` runs `EqualsVerifier.forPackage("...s3.model", true)`, which would check every class in the model package and its subpackages, including generated classes that do not override `equals`. The builders are in `internal` for the same reason |
| — | The schema field of shape `S3` is `SOFTWARE_AMAZON_AWSSDK_EVENTNOTIFICATIONS_S3_S3` | Existing `SchemaFieldOrder` naming rule: a field name equal to the shape name is qualified |

**Model and serde.** The Smithy members are in the order `toJson` writes them, so `serializeMembers` writes in ascending member index. `x-amz-request-id` and `x-amz-id-2` are `@jsonName` members (`xAmzRequestId`, `xAmzId2`), and both codecs use `useJsonName(true)`. `S3EventNotificationRecordList`, `S3ObjectAnnotationList` and `AwsGeneratedTags` are `@sparse`.

| Task, design or source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `eventTime`, `lifecycleRestorationExpiryTime` (Java `Instant`) | Smithy `String` members. They are read with `Instant.parse` and written with `DateTimeFormatter.ISO_INSTANT` | The JSON codec's `date-time` writer drops trailing fraction zeros (`.1Z`, task 7.1), while the previous writer wrote `ISO_INSTANT` (`.100Z`). `Instant.parse` also keeps the previous reader's accepted inputs and its `DateTimeParseException` |
| `SerializableStruct.serializeMembers`: "unset members are not written" | Members the previous writer always wrote are written as `null` when null (`writeNullable`). Event data, `objectAnnotation`, `hasObjectAnnotation` and empty or null `awsGeneratedTags` are left out. An owner identity without a principal ID is written as `null`, as before | `toJson` output must be unchanged (Requirement 38.6). Other codecs and `Document.of` therefore see explicit nulls too |
| `getMemberValue` | Returns the `ISO_INSTANT` string for the two instants, matching the member's schema type | Same reason as the string members |
| Design D.7: "get `ShapeBuilder`s" | 15 internal builders behind `S3EventNotificationSerde.notificationBuilder()`. No public builder accessor, and the schemas have no `builderSupplier`/`shapeClass` | Requirement 38.6 keeps the public API unchanged. A public accessor is an open question |
| Previous reader messages | Each typed read maps a codec `SerializationException` to `IllegalArgumentException` with the previous message and the JSON field name: `'%s' was not a string`, `expected '%s' to be an object, but was not.`, `... an array ...`, `... numeric, but was not`, `... boolean, but was not`, and `Records[]`/`objectAnnotation[]` for elements. The codec exception is the cause | Same exception type and message for type errors. A top-level `null` gives null records; any other non-object root gives `expected 'Records' to be an object, but was not.` |
| `awsGeneratedTags` values | Read as documents. A non-string value throws `The value of tag '%s' in 'awsGeneratedTags' was not a string, but was: %s`, with the value spelled as `JsonNode.toString()` spelled it (`[1, "a", null]`, `{"a": "b"}`) | `ShapeDeserializer.readString` accepts a JSON number, and Requirement 38.6 keeps the exception type for these values. Numbers are spelled in the codec's form (`1.5` for `1.50`) |
| Malformed JSON: `UncheckedIOException` caused by the shaded Jackson `JsonParseException` | After any failure, the input is checked with `readDocument()`. If it is not well-formed up to the end of its first value, the reader throws `UncheckedIOException(new JsonParseException(null, <codec message>, <SerializationException>))`. Otherwise it rethrows the failure, with a bare `SerializationException` as `IllegalArgumentException` | The previous reader built the whole tree before reading members, so malformed input always gave `JsonParseException`, even when a type error or bad instant came earlier in the input. Jackson's message and its subclasses (`JsonEOFException` for a truncated input) are not reproduced |
| `JsonNodeParser.DEFAULT_JSON_FACTORY`: `ALLOW_JAVA_COMMENTS`, UTF-8 byte order mark, only the first value read | After a failure, a leading BOM is removed and `//` and `/* */` comments outside strings are replaced with spaces, and the input is read again. An unterminated block comment is left to fail. After the value, `close()`'s trailing-content error is ignored | `fromJson` must accept what it accepted before. The extra scan runs only on inputs that already failed |

**Known differences** (each is asserted in `S3EventNotificationParityTest`):
- A JSON number where a string is expected is read as its text (`"eventVersion": 2.5` gives `"2.5"`). The previous reader threw `IllegalArgumentException`. This is the codec's `readString` behaviour (task 7.1).
- `"size": 1.5` throws `IllegalArgumentException` (`expected 'object' to be an object, but was not.`), where the previous reader threw `NumberFormatException`, a subclass. The codec reads `1` as the long and then fails on `.5` as object syntax.
- `\uXXXX` escapes of control characters use lower-case hex (`\u001f`, not `\u001F`), and supplementary characters are written as UTF-8 rather than escaped surrogate pairs (`\uD83D\uDE00`). These are the task 7.1 codec differences. S3 sends object keys URL-encoded, so they do not occur in S3's own notifications.
- `GlacierEventData` without `restoreEventData` is written as `"glacierEventData":{}`, and reads back equal. The previous writer threw `JsonWriter.JsonGenerationException` ("Current context not Object but Array"), because it closed the object twice.
- An `S3Bucket` with a null `ownerIdentity`, and a null element in `objectAnnotation`, are written as `null`. The previous writer threw `NullPointerException`.

Not asserted:
- Pretty output always ends lines with `\n`. The previous writer used Jackson's system line separator, so the output differs on Windows. The existing writer tests expect `\n`.
- The previous reader detected UTF-16 and UTF-32 input; the codec reads UTF-8 only.
- Jackson's read limits (string and number length, nesting depth 1000) are replaced by the codec's (depth 1000).
- With duplicate keys, both readers keep the last value. The previous reader validated only the last occurrence, while the codec reads, and can reject, every occurrence.
- When one input has two type errors, the codec reports the first in input order. The previous reader reported the first in its own field order.

**`toJsonPretty`.** `JsonPrettyPrinter` (task 7.1) reproduces Jackson's `DefaultPrettyPrinter` byte for byte on all 50 valid samples, and the existing pretty-print tests pass unchanged.

**Parity test.** `S3EventNotificationParityTest` (135 runs) reads every file under `src/test/resources/samples`:
- `valid/`, 50 files. 23 are the JSON strings of `S3EventNotificationReaderTest` and `S3EventNotificationWriterTest`, extracted once with a script that decodes the Java literals. The other 27 cover the S3 User Guide event shapes (put, restore, replication, lifecycle transition, Intelligent-Tiering), every event-data member null, empty or set, object annotations, root `null`, empty and whitespace-only input, unknown members at every level, absent, null and empty `Records` and null records, nested nulls, escapes and non-ASCII text, instant fractions (`.100Z`, `.123456Z`, `.123456789Z`, none), `awsGeneratedTags` with null, empty and duplicate keys, long extremes, duplicate members, comments, a byte order mark and trailing content.
- `invalid/`, 28 files: 4 from the reader test (malformed JSON and the three `awsGeneratedTags` errors) and 24 new ones (syntax errors, every wrong-type message, bad instants, and type or instant errors before a syntax error).

Each valid sample is checked three ways:
- `fromJson` from the bytes, the string and an `InputStream` equals the old reader's result.
- `toJson` and `toJsonPretty` equal the old writer's output.
- The compact output reads back equal with both readers.

Each invalid sample must throw the same exception class as the old reader. For `UncheckedIOException`, both causes must be `JsonParseException`s; otherwise the messages must be equal. One more run checks the sample counts, and six runs cover the known differences.

**Size.** The JAR grows from 45,476 bytes (2.49.6) to 80,344, and its classes from 88,755 to 169,096 uncompressed bytes (22 to 42 classes). The largest new classes are `S3EventNotificationSerde` (24.6 KB), whose lambdas and helpers are counted with it, the 15 builders and their base class (2.4 to 6.0 KB each, 48.6 KB in total), and the `serializeMembers`/`getMemberValue` code in the model classes. The removed reader and writer were 43.1 KB. Design D.7 estimated a decrease of about 15 KB; the measured change is an increase of about 80 KB uncompressed. Task 11.23's Size_Report rows replace this estimate in the Library_Integration_Analysis.

**Dependencies and compatibility.**
- `dependency:analyze`: no problems.
- `architecture-tests`: 77 runs, 0 failures; the 11 skipped are `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest`, as before. `DependencyTreeTest` passes: `schema` and `json-codec` do not reach `smithy-model`, and the codegen plugin is a build plugin, not a dependency. Requirement 1.7 is read as covering runtime modules.
- japicmp: the module was already in the root `includeModules`. Against the released 2.49.6 JAR (fetched once with `mvn dependency:get`), it reports 15 modified classes. Each change is additive: `SerializableStruct` and `SerializableShape` as new interfaces, plus `sdkSchema()`, `serializeMembers(ShapeSerializer)` and `getMemberValue(Schema)`. No `!` markers, and every change is binary and source compatible. `internal` is excluded.

**Module runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2). The module, `codegen` and `architecture-tests` were built from a `/tmp` worktree of HEAD with the changes copied in, because the IDE was writing its own compiled classes (with "Unresolved compilation problems") into the workspace's `target/classes`.
- `codegen` `clean install`, with `SchemaCodegenTest` (13 runs, 2 new) and the new `TypesOnlySchemaCodegenTest` (3), and then with every test in `poet.schema`, `pipeline` and `emitters` (102).
- `codegen-maven-plugin` `clean install`: 94 tests.
- `s3-event-notifications` `clean install`: 174 tests. These are `S3EventNotificationReaderTest` 19, `S3EventNotificationWriterTest` 13, `S3BucketTest` 6 and `EqualsAndHashCodeTest` 1 (all unmodified), plus `S3EventNotificationParityTest` 135. Checkstyle, SpotBugs, `dependency:analyze`, Javadoc and japicmp also ran.

**Open questions.**
- Should the library publish a `ShapeBuilder` (for example `S3EventNotification.shapeBuilder()`) and give the schemas a `builderSupplier`, so that `MessagePayloads` (task 11.20) and type registries can read notifications with any codec? Either change is additive, but it adds public API.
- Should `json-codec` reject a fraction for `readLong`/`readInteger` instead of reading the integer part? The error type is the same either way, but the message names the enclosing object.
- The instants are Smithy strings. If the codecs' `date-time` writer kept `ISO_INSTANT`'s fraction groups, they could become `timestamp` members with `@timestampFormat("date-time")`.
- The size increase contradicts D.7's "decrease" estimate. A table-driven builder, or reading through `Document`, would be smaller. Task 11.23's benchmark should decide whether the streaming parse is worth the size.

## Typed message payloads (task 11.20)

**Target.** `services-custom/message-payloads`, package `software.amazon.awssdk.messaging.payload` (an SDK addition with no smithy-java source):
- `MessagePayloads`: `public final`, `@SdkPublicApi`, with a private constructor and the four static methods of design D.7: `serialize(SerializableShape, Codec)`, `deserialize(String, Codec, ShapeBuilder<T>)`, `deserialize(String, Codec, TypeRegistry)` and `deserialize(String, Codec, TypeRegistry, ShapeId)`.
- `MessagePayloadException`: `public final`, `@SdkPublicApi`, extends `SdkClientException`. It has the nested `enum Cause { MALFORMED, NON_CONFORMING, UNKNOWN_DISCRIMINATOR }`, `cause()`, and the SDK exception builder pattern (`builder()`, `toBuilder()`, a nested `Builder` with `cause(Cause)`). `retryable()` is inherited (`false`).
- The pom drops the skeleton's `maven.javadoc.skip` and `mdep.analyze.skip`. Compile dependencies: `annotations`, `schema`, `codec-commons`, `json-codec`, `cbor-codec`, `sdk-core` and `utils`. `xml-codec` is a test dependency. The module was already in `services-custom/pom.xml`, `bom`, `bom-internal`, the root `jdk8` profile list and `PortComplianceTest.PORTED_MODULES` (task 1.2). There is no japicmp baseline, because the module is new.

**Behavior.**

| Task, design or source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Design D.7: "`serialize` writes `__type` when the codec's settings have `serializeTypeInDocuments`" | A structure (a `SerializableStruct` whose schema type is `STRUCTURE`) is serialized as `Document.of(value)` when the codec is a `JsonCodec` with `serializeTypeInDocuments()` on, or an `Rpcv2CborCodec`. The codec then writes `__type` with the absolute ShapeId first and the members after it, the same way it serializes the structure itself. Everything else is serialized directly. This applies to every `serialize` call, because `serialize` has no registry parameter | `__type` is written only for documents (task 11.9). The CBOR codec has no setting and always writes it, as smithy-java does. The XML codec has no documents. Unions are left out because `Document.of(union)` is a schema-less structure document with no discriminator |
| Design D.7: "JSON/XML: UTF-8 text. Binary codecs (CBOR): standard base64 text" | `Rpcv2CborCodec` is binary: `java.util.Base64.getEncoder()` on serialize and `getDecoder()` on deserialize (RFC 4648 alphabet, padding optional on input, no line breaks or URL-safe characters). Every other codec is text (`serializeToString`, UTF-8 bytes on input) | `Codec` has no way to tell whether a codec is binary. The SDK has three codecs, and CBOR is the only binary one |
| D.7: "MALFORMED: the codec failed to parse" | Base64 that the decoder rejects is `MALFORMED`, with the `IllegalArgumentException` as cause. With a registry, a `SerializationException` while reading the body as one document (`readDocument()` followed by `close()`, which rejects trailing content) is `MALFORMED` | Requirement 38.5 |
| D.7: "NON_CONFORMING: a `SerializationException` from schema mismatch" | Builder and caller-ShapeId paths call `codec.deserializeShape(bytes, builder)`, the codec's own path. A `SerializationException` there means either a syntax error or a schema mismatch, so the body is then read again as a document. If that also fails with a `SerializationException`, the result is `MALFORMED`; otherwise it is `NON_CONFORMING`. With a registry, a `SerializationException` from `registry.deserialize(document)` is `NON_CONFORMING`, and the message names the discriminator | The codecs report both kinds of failure with the same exception type. The second read runs only after a failure. If it fails with an exception other than `SerializationException`, the codec cannot be checked this way, and the result is `NON_CONFORMING` |
| D.7: "UNKNOWN_DISCRIMINATOR: `UnsupportedOperationException` or `DiscriminatorException`" | As specified, from `registry.deserialize(document)`. A body with no `__type` (including a non-string `__type`, a list or a string), a `__type` that is not a valid absolute ShapeId, and an unregistered `__type` are all `UNKNOWN_DISCRIMINATOR`. A body that reads as no document (JSON `null`, and any XML body) is also `UNKNOWN_DISCRIMINATOR`, with no cause. With a caller ShapeId, `registry.createBuilder(shapeId, SerializableStruct.class)` returning null gives `UNKNOWN_DISCRIMINATOR` ("No shape registered for ShapeId: ...") before the body is read | `TypeRegistry.deserialize` throws `DiscriminatorException` for a missing discriminator (task 11.16), and the design maps that exception to `UNKNOWN_DISCRIMINATOR`, so a missing `__type` is not `NON_CONFORMING` |
| — | Other exceptions (for example from a user's builder) are not wrapped. `serialize` failures propagate as the codec's `SerializationException` | `Cause` describes deserialization only |
| D.7: "the body is parsed once into a Protocol_Document and `registry.deserialize(document)` is called" | As specified. The caller-ShapeId variant reads the body with the codec's deserializer into the registry's builder, like the builder variant, and ignores any `__type` in the body | Reading directly is the codec's own path (task 3.9 convenience methods). The caller's ShapeId takes precedence over the body |
| Null arguments | `NullPointerException("<parameter> must not be null.")` from `Validate.paramNotNull` for every parameter, checked before any work | SDK null-argument style (Requirement 39.5) |
| Design module table: `message-payloads` depends on `schema` | Also `codec-commons` (`Codec`), `json-codec` and `cbor-codec` (the text/binary and `__type` checks), `sdk-core` (`SdkClientException`, which D.7 names as the superclass) and `utils` (`Validate`) | The table lists only the main dependency. `sns-message-manager`, the task 11.22 user, already depends on `sdk-core` |

**Unions.** A union is written without `__type`: smithy-java and the SDK write no discriminator for unions, and union documents have none (`type()` is `STRUCTURE`). So `deserialize(body, codec, registry)` reports a union body as `UNKNOWN_DISCRIMINATOR` ("... has no discriminator"). A union body can be read with its builder, or with `deserialize(body, codec, registry, unionShapeId)` when the union class is registered. Both are tested.

**Known behavior** (each is asserted):
- An empty JSON body read into a shape is an empty structure, because the JSON codec reads empty input that way (an empty HTTP body). Through a registry, the same body is `MALFORMED`, because it holds no document. SQS and SNS do not accept empty message bodies.
- XML syntax errors are `NON_CONFORMING`, not `MALFORMED`. The XML codec's `readDocument()` returns null without reading, so the second read cannot detect them.

**Tests.** `MessagePayloadsTest`, 65 runs (21 `@Test` and 13 `@ParameterizedTest` methods), over the hand-written shapes in `PayloadTestShapes`: structures `OrderPlaced` (string and integer members) and `OrderCancelled`, the union `Notice`, and a `TypeRegistry` that holds all three. These were chosen over generating schemas through the task 11.19 types-only mode, because they exercise the same API with no build step.
- Serialization: the exact JSON text with `__type` first; no `__type` with `serializeTypeInDocuments(false)` and for a union; base64 CBOR whose decoded bytes equal `CBOR.serialize(Document.of(value))` and carry the discriminator; XML as UTF-8 text with no `__type`.
- Round trips through the builder, the registry and the caller ShapeId for JSON and CBOR; unions through the builder and the caller ShapeId; an XML builder round trip. Also hand-written JSON without `__type`, a `__type` of another shape ignored by the builder path, and a caller ShapeId that differs from the body's `__type`.
- `MALFORMED`: truncated, invalid and trailing-content JSON, and the empty body through the registry; invalid base64 (an illegal character, a single character, a line break, a URL-safe character) and truncated CBOR. Each is checked through all three variants where it applies, with the cause type.
- `NON_CONFORMING`: wrong member types, a JSON array and a string for a structure, a CBOR map with a wrong member type, and an XML syntax error.
- `UNKNOWN_DISCRIMINATOR`: missing, non-string, relative and unregistered `__type` (JSON and CBOR); a union body through the registry; JSON `null`; an XML body through the registry; an unregistered caller ShapeId.
- The 12 null-argument cases. The exception builder (`cause`, message, cause throwable, `toBuilder`, `SdkClientException` superclass) and the missing-`Cause` error.

Property 51 (task 11.21) is not written.

**Size.** The JAR is 17,135 bytes, and its classes are 24,907 uncompressed bytes (7 classes). `MessagePayloads` is 10,983 bytes and the exception with its builder is 13,778. Design D.7 estimated at most 10 KB. Most of the excess is the SDK exception builder pattern (the `Builder` interface and `BuilderImpl`, 8.7 KB).

**Module runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, in the workspace):
- `clean install -pl :message-payloads --am -P quick` once, then `clean install -pl :message-payloads`: 65 tests, 0 Checkstyle violations, SpotBugs 0 bugs, `dependency:analyze` "No dependency problems found", Javadoc built. japicmp skips the module (it is not in `includeModules`).
- `test -pl :architecture-tests`: 77 runs, 0 failures. The 11 skipped are `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest`, as before. `DependencyTreeTest` (5) and `PortComplianceTest` (15, which includes `message-payloads`) pass.

**Open questions.**
- Binary detection is `instanceof Rpcv2CborCodec`. A third-party binary codec would be treated as text, and its bytes would be corrupted by UTF-8 decoding. Should `Codec` say whether its output is text (for example a `mediaType()` or `isBinary()` default method), or should `MessagePayloads` take an explicit encoding?
- Should `serialize` failures be wrapped in `MessagePayloadException`? `Cause` has no value for them today.
- Should the base64 decoder accept MIME-style line breaks (`Base64.getMimeDecoder()`)? It is strict now.
- XML errors cannot be split into `MALFORMED` and `NON_CONFORMING`, because the XML codec reads no documents. A schema-less well-formedness check would need a codec-level hook.
- Should an empty body be `MALFORMED` for every variant, instead of following the JSON codec's empty-structure rule on the builder path?

## SNS Message Manager typed messages (task 11.22)

**Target.** `services-custom/sns-message-manager`:
- `SnsNotification` (public, final) gains three public methods that decode `message()` through `MessagePayloads` (task 11.20):
  - `<T extends SerializableShape> T message(Codec codec, ShapeBuilder<T> builder)`
  - `SerializableStruct message(Codec codec, TypeRegistry registry)`, which reads the `__type` discriminator from the body
  - `SerializableStruct message(Codec codec, TypeRegistry registry, ShapeId shapeId)`, with a ShapeId the caller supplies

  The class Javadoc gains a "Typed messages" section with an example and the dependency note.
- `pom.xml`: `message-payloads` is a new optional dependency. `schema` and `codec-commons` are declared as compile dependencies; `sdk-core` already brought both, so the dependency closure is unchanged. `json-codec` is a test dependency.
- `SnsMessageUnmarshaller`, `SignatureValidator`, `DefaultSnsMessageManager`, `SnsMessage` and the other message classes are unchanged. The envelope is parsed and the signature validated over the message string exactly as before.

**Behavior.**

| Task, design or source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Design D.7 row 2: "Add a typed-payload accessor through `MessagePayloads`". No name or signature is given | Three overloads of `message`, one per `MessagePayloads.deserialize` variant, with the same parameter order after the body | The getter is already `message()`. The overloads read as "the message, as a typed value" and need no new term. `MessagePayloads` defines the semantics, so each overload is a null check plus one call |
| Task: "the parsed SNS message" | `SnsNotification` only, not the base class `SnsMessage` | Only a notification carries the publisher's message. `SubscriptionConfirmation` and `UnsubscribeConfirmation` carry SNS's own English text, which no codec can read. `SnsMessageManager.parseMessage` returns `SnsMessage`, so callers check the type first, as they already must to confirm subscriptions. Adding the methods to `SnsMessage` would also be additive (it is an abstract class, not an interface) |
| Null or absent `Message` (design silent) | All three return `null` after their argument checks | `message()` returns `null` when the envelope has no `Message`. `MessagePayloads` would throw `NullPointerException("body must not be null.")`, which names a parameter the caller did not pass. A notification that passes signature validation normally has a `Message`, because the signature covers it. `SignatureValidator` signs the text `null` when it is absent (`StringJoiner.add(null)`), so in practice `null` comes from notifications built with the builder |
| Null arguments | `NullPointerException("<parameter> must not be null.")` from `Validate.paramNotNull` for `codec`, `builder`, `registry` and `shapeId`, checked before the null-message check | Same messages as `MessagePayloads`. A null argument fails the same way whether or not the notification has a message |
| D.7 Requirement 38.5 causes | `MessagePayloadException` propagates unchanged, with its `Cause` and the codec or registry exception as its cause | It already extends `SdkClientException`, the exception type the manager throws today |
| D.7 row 2, size: "Neutral (library); `message-payloads` is a separate small module" | `message-payloads` is `<optional>true</optional>` | Customers who never call the new methods get no new artifacts: the published pom adds no transitive dependency. Customers who call them add `message-payloads`, which brings `json-codec` and `cbor-codec`. Every type in the new signatures (`Codec`, `ShapeBuilder`, `SerializableShape`, `SerializableStruct`, `TypeRegistry`, `ShapeId`) is in `schema` or `codec-commons`, which `sdk-core` already brings. So linking `SnsNotification` and reflecting over it (`getDeclaredMethods`, as EqualsVerifier and bean tools do) never loads `message-payloads` classes. `MessagePayloadException` appears only in Javadoc, not in a `throws` clause, for the same reason |
| — | No caching: each call decodes the message again. `equals`, `hashCode` and `toString` are unchanged | The class stays an immutable value of the envelope fields |

**Without `message-payloads`.** A one-off check (not committed) ran a small program on the module's runtime classpath with the `message-payloads`, `json-codec` and `cbor-codec` JARs removed. Parsing a notification with `SnsMessageUnmarshaller`, `message()`, `toString`, `equals`, `hashCode` and `SnsNotification.class.getDeclaredMethods()` all worked. `message(codec, builder)` on a notification with no message returned `null`, and on a notification with a message threw `NoClassDefFoundError: software/amazon/awssdk/messaging/payload/MessagePayloads`. The class Javadoc documents this.

**Tests.** `SnsNotificationTypedMessageTest` (17 runs: 8 `@Test` and 1 `@ParameterizedTest` method with 9 cases) and `TypedMessageTestShapes`, a minimal copy of `PayloadTestShapes` with the structures `OrderPlaced` and `OrderCancelled` and a registry that holds both. Each notification is signed with SHA256withRSA (signature version 2) by a key generated in the test, and parsed and validated by `DefaultSnsMessageManager`, whose stubbed `CertificateRetriever` returns the public key.
- The message string is unchanged by parsing, and `MessagePayloads.serialize` output (with `__type`) and a hand-written body without `__type` both validate.
- Builder: bodies with and without `__type`. Registry: two shapes told apart by `__type`. Caller ShapeId: a body without `__type`, and a body whose `__type` names another shape.
- Failures, each checked for `MessagePayloadException`, its `Cause` and its cause type: `MALFORMED` (truncated JSON) through all three methods; `NON_CONFORMING` (a string for the integer member) through all three; `UNKNOWN_DISCRIMINATOR` for an unregistered `__type` (cause `UnsupportedOperationException`), no `__type` (`DiscriminatorException`) and an unregistered caller ShapeId (no cause).
- A notification with no message returns `null` from all three methods. Null arguments throw `NullPointerException` naming the parameter.

The test manager is given a mock `SdkHttpClient`. Without one, `DefaultSnsMessageManager` builds the default HTTP client, and `DefaultSdkHttpClientBuilder` caches the HTTP service it loads first in a static field. That made two existing `DefaultSnsMessageManagerTest` tests, which select their own service through a system property, fail when the new class ran first in the same JVM.

**Dependencies and compatibility.**
- `dependency:analyze`: "No dependency problems found".
- `architecture-tests` (`clean test`): 77 runs, 0 failures; the 11 skipped are `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest`, as before. `DependencyTreeTest` (5) and `PortComplianceTest` (15) pass. `sns-message-manager` is not a ported module, so `PORTED_MODULES` is unchanged.
- japicmp: the module is in the root `includeModules`, but offline builds had never downloaded its 2.49.6 baseline, so `cmp` warned and compared against nothing. With the released 2.49.6 JAR fetched once (`mvn dependency:get`), `japicmp:cmp` reports one modified class, `SnsNotification`, with the three new methods. All are binary and source compatible, and there are no other changes.
- Size: the JAR grows from 48,543 bytes (2.49.6) to 49,355. `SnsNotification.class` grows from 3,218 to 5,602 uncompressed bytes. The other differences are the embedded pom (+1,088 bytes) and the two enums `SignatureVersion` and `SnsMessageType` (+49 bytes each). Their sources have not changed since the module was introduced, so that difference comes from compilation.

**Module runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, in the workspace):
- `clean install -pl :sns-message-manager`: 100 tests (83 existing, unmodified, plus 17 new), 0 Checkstyle violations, SpotBugs 0 bugs, `dependency:analyze` clean, Javadoc built.
- `japicmp:cmp -pl :sns-message-manager` against 2.49.6, as above.
- `clean test -pl :architecture-tests`, as above.

**Open questions.**
- Should the methods also be on `SnsMessage`, so a caller can decode without checking the type? They would then fail on confirmation messages with `NON_CONFORMING` or `MALFORMED`.
- `message(...)` overloads the getter. Would a distinct name (`messageAs`, `typedMessage`) read better in IDE completion?
- With the optional dependency, a missing `message-payloads` shows up as `NoClassDefFoundError` at the first call. Should the methods check for it and throw an `SdkClientException` that names the artifact? Or should the dependency be compile scope, which adds `message-payloads`, `json-codec` and `cbor-codec` to every SNS Message Manager user?
- HTTP/S notifications can carry `MessageAttributes`, which `SnsMessageUnmarshaller` ignores. Exposing them would let callers pick the caller-ShapeId overload's ShapeId from an attribute, as with SQS. That would be a separate additive change to the envelope model.

## Library_Integration_Analysis (task 11.23)

**Target.**
- `scripts/schema-serde/library_integration_analysis.py` writes `library-integration-analysis.md` from `library-integration.yaml` (the seven D.7 subjects), the Libraries rows of `size-report.md` and `library-integration-jmh.json` (the JMH JSON result of `S3EventNotificationBenchmark`). Modes: write (default), `--check` and `--self-test` (17 unit tests on fixture data), as in `racecar_decisions.py` and `paired_ab_summary.py`. The YAML uses the `racecar-decisions.yaml` subset; `racecar_decisions.parse` now takes the allowed keys so that both scripts share the parser.
- `test/architecture-tests`: `LibraryIntegrationAnalysisTest` runs `--check` and `--self-test`. The process handling it shares with `RacecarDecisionTableTest` moved to the new package-private `PythonScript`.
- `test/size-report`: a "Libraries (Requirement 38.2)" section for `s3-event-notifications`, `sns-message-manager` and `message-payloads` (`size-report.libraries`, each a test-scoped dependency; new `LibraryComparison`). `build-size-baseline.sh` gains `--libraries` (default: the three libraries) and records `baseline.libraries` and `baseline.absentLibraries` in `build-info.properties`.
- `test/sdk-standard-benchmarks`: `S3EventNotificationBenchmark` (`read` and `write`; `implementation` `LEGACY`/`SCHEMA`; `sampleSet` `ALL_SAMPLES`/`OBJECT_CREATED_PUT`), `S3EventNotificationSampleSet`, and `S3EventNotificationBenchmarkTest` (4 runs).

| Task, design or source | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| "measured rows from `size-report.md`" | The size report measures the libraries; the script parses the generated Libraries rows. The script does not read JARs | The size report already builds a same-JDK Baseline_Build (Requirement 36.7) and checks the JDK. Measuring JARs in the script would need a second baseline, or the released 2.49.6 JARs, which the release pipeline built with JDK 17 |
| Tasks 11.19 and 11.22 measured against the released 2.49.6 JARs | The Baseline_Build (`fc8b74e9fe21`, JDK 21.0.2): `s3-event-notifications` 45,490 bytes (2.49.6: 45,476), `sns-message-manager` 48,622 (2.49.6: 48,543). The feature JARs are unchanged (80,344 and 49,355) | Same JDK and compiler options as the feature build |
| — | `message-payloads` has no module at the Baseline_Build commit. `build-size-baseline.sh` skips it and records it in `baseline.absentLibraries`; the report shows it as "new", with 0 bytes in the Baseline_Build | The report must tell a library that is new from one the baseline forgot to build. `SizeReport` fails if a reported library is in neither list |
| — | The libraries are excluded from the runtime-module tables. Their `software.amazon.awssdk` dependencies stay in them; today every one is also a dependency of a reported service, so the module tables are unchanged | The library rows report each library's own JAR |
| Benchmark: reach the old reader and writer, which are in the library's test scope | The benchmark build copies `DefaultS3EventNotificationReader.java` and `DefaultS3EventNotificationWriter.java` from `services-custom/s3-event-notifications/src/test/java` (`maven-resources-plugin` `copy-resources`) into `target/generated-sources/s3-event-notifications-legacy`, added with `build-helper` `add-source`. The valid samples are added as resources from the library's `src/test/resources/samples/valid` (`add-resource` with `targetPath`) | Least invasive: the library's build and published artifacts do not change, and no code is duplicated in the repository, so the copy cannot drift. A test JAR would add an artifact to a published module, and no module in this repository builds one. A checked-in copy would be 714 duplicated lines |
| D.7 row 1: "a JMH benchmark on the test-suite samples" | One operation reads, or writes, every notification of the sample set once: all 50 valid samples (`ALL_SAMPLES`), or the S3 User Guide's `ObjectCreated:Put` event alone (`OBJECT_CREATED_PUT`). Setup fails if the two implementations read a sample to different values. `LEGACY` calls the old classes the way the old `fromJson` and `toJson` did (a new reader per call, one shared writer); `SCHEMA` calls the public `fromJson(String)` and `toJson()`. `toJsonPretty` is not benchmarked | The parity test (task 11.19) proves both implementations agree on every sample, so both sides do the same work. The sample set includes edge cases (comments, a byte order mark, trailing content) that send the new reader down its slow retry path, so the typical event is reported separately |
| — | The enum is `S3EventNotificationSampleSet` | Checkstyle's `PluralEnumNames` rejects `S3EventNotificationSamples` |
| D.7 row 1, compatibility "None" | `additive`, listing the 15 model classes, with `d7_compatibility: "none"` and a note | The serde entry points are unchanged, but each model class gains `SerializableStruct`, `SerializableShape`, `sdkSchema()`, `serializeMembers` and `getMemberValue` (japicmp, task 11.19). Requirement 38.2(c) asks for every affected public type |
| D.7 row 1, size "Decrease … about −15 KB" | Kept as the estimate; the measured row is an increase (+34,854 compressed, +76.6%), explained in `size_note` | The script requires a note when the measured direction differs from the expected one |
| D.7 row 3, size "estimate ≤ 10 KB" | Kept; measured 17,135 bytes, explained in `size_note` | Same direction (increase), but over the estimate (task 11.20) |
| D.7 row 2, size "Neutral" | Measured +733 bytes (+1.5%), neutral by the script's rule | — |
| D.7 row 7 | Kept as Defer, with a note that `Codec.deserializeShape(String, ShapeBuilder)` already exists in `codec-commons` and that this repository's CloudWatch C2J model has no `mediaType` on `DashboardBody` | `Documents` in `schema` is internal, so only the schema-less helper would be new API |
| Measured directions (not in the design) | Size: increase for a new library, neutral when the compressed JAR change is at most 2% of the Baseline_Build JAR, otherwise the sign. Time: from the SCHEMA/LEGACY ratio of every operation and sample set; neutral within 5%, otherwise decrease, increase or mixed | Requirement 38.2 asks for a direction; the thresholds are this task's choice and are printed in the analysis's Method section |
| User decision "tooling only, defer measurements" | The committed JMH result is a smoke run (`-wi 1 -i 2 -f 1 -w 1s -r 1s -prof gc`). The script marks any result below the benchmark's class defaults (5 warmup and 5 measurement iterations, 2 forks) PRELIMINARY and prints the full-run command | The flag comes from the JMH settings recorded in the result, not from a hand-set value |
| `perf-reference.json` is a curated file | `library-integration-jmh.json` is JMH's own `-rf json` output, unedited (it includes the local JVM path) | The script reads JMH's format, so a full run replaces the file with no conversion step |
| Requirement 38.1 vocabulary "prototype in phase 3" | Kept as written, although the prototypes are tasks 11.19 to 11.22 | The recommendation names the requirement's category, not this plan's phase numbers |

**Recommendations.** Prototype in phase 3: S3 Event Notifications, SNS Message Manager (typed payload only), SQS message bodies. Defer to a later phase: Lambda event payloads, DynamoDB Enhanced Client, `mediaType` JSON strings. Do not adopt: IAM Policy Builder.

**Smoke benchmark (PRELIMINARY).** JDK 21.0.2, 1 warmup and 2 measurement iterations of 1 s, 1 fork, `-prof gc`:

| Operation | Sample set | LEGACY µs/op | SCHEMA µs/op | LEGACY B/op | SCHEMA B/op |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `read` | `ALL_SAMPLES` (50 per op) | 240.5 | 55.9 | 352,286 | 178,160 |
| `read` | `OBJECT_CREATED_PUT` | 1.866 | 0.945 | 7,992 | 3,896 |
| `write` | `ALL_SAMPLES` | 59.7 | 28.2 | 189,256 | 169,640 |
| `write` | `OBJECT_CREATED_PUT` | 5.561 | 1.029 | 3,917 | 3,344 |

The `LEGACY` `write` of one event (5.6 µs) costs more than the per-event average of the 50-sample pass (1.2 µs), which points to too little warmup. Only the direction is usable.

**Size report changes.** Besides the new section and two sentences: `sdk-core` is 1,262,849 bytes, 1 byte more than in the last report, from a later rebuild with no source change (the JAR in `~/.m2` dates from the task 11.20 builds). The entry CRCs, which exclude `META-INF/maven/`, decide its status, which stays "changed". After the baseline rebuild every Baseline_Build service and module JAR has the same size as before.

**Runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2):
- `scripts/schema-serde/build-size-baseline.sh` (defaults): 1:08 min.
- `clean install -pl :size-report -Dsize-report.update=true` in the workspace: 66 tests (3 new in `SizeReportTest`), 0 Checkstyle violations, `dependency:analyze` clean; then the regenerated report.
- `sdk-standard-benchmarks` and `architecture-tests` were built in a `/tmp` worktree of HEAD with the changes copied in, because the IDE holds files in the workspace's `target/classes` (`clean` failed to delete them). `clean install -pl :sdk-standard-benchmarks -P quick`, then `clean install -pl :sdk-standard-benchmarks -Dtest=S3EventNotificationBenchmarkTest`: 4 tests, 0 Checkstyle violations. Then the smoke run above from `target/benchmarks.jar`.
- `clean install -pl :architecture-tests -Dtest=LibraryIntegrationAnalysisTest,RacecarDecisionTableTest`: 4 tests, 0 Checkstyle violations.
- `library_integration_analysis.py --check` and `--self-test` (17 tests); `racecar_decisions.py --check`; `optimization_experiment.py --self-test` (7 tests, which imports `racecar_decisions`).

**Open questions.**
- Are the neutral bands (2% for size, 5% for time) the right ones? A full JMH run should report its error, and the time rule could use it instead.
- S3 Event Notifications stays "Prototype in phase 3", conditioned in the rationale on a smaller builder or a Document-based reader, because its JAR grows by 77%. Should the recommendation be "Defer" until the size is fixed?
- Row 7: with `Codec.deserializeShape(String, ShapeBuilder)` already public, is there anything left to add, or is "Do not adopt" (no new API needed) the better recommendation?
- The committed JMH JSON contains the local JVM path. Should the script accept a trimmed copy instead?

## `core/dynamic-client` Schema_Converter (task 13.1)

**Sources.** smithy-java `dynamic-schemas` (commit `142eecf4d`): `SchemaConverter`, `StructDocument`, `ContentDocument` (with its nested record `SchemaDocument`) and `SchemaGuidedDocumentBuilder`.

**Target.** `core/dynamic-client`, package `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient.schema`: `SchemaConverter` and `StructDocument` (`@SdkPublicApi`), `ContentDocument` and `SchemaGuidedDocumentBuilder` (package-private and `@SdkInternalApi`, like `Documents`: Checkstyle's `MissingSdkAnnotation` needs an annotation on every top-level class, and `PackageContainmentTest` exempts package-private classes). Package `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient.schema.internal` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `DefaultTraitInclusion` (the task 1.25 constants), `RuntimeTraitInclusion` (the runtime port of the policy computation) and `ModelConversions` (smithy-model shape IDs, nodes and traits to their runtime forms).

The `pom.xml` drops the skeleton's Javadoc and dependency-analysis skips and adds `utils`, `schema` and `codec-commons`. Dependency analysis now runs. `aws-core`, the six protocol modules and `smithy-rules-engine` are declared for tasks 13.4 to 13.10 but not used yet, so they are listed in `ignoredUnusedDeclaredDependencies` (appended to the parent's list), with a comment to remove each one when it is used. New test dependencies: `smithy-aws-traits` (the protocol and auth trait definitions) and `json-codec` (codec round trips).

**Requirement 1.7.** `dynamic-client` was already the only published runtime module allowed to depend on `smithy-model` (`DependencyTreeTest` exempts it by name), so no allowlist entry was needed. `codegen` gains a test-scope dependency on `dynamic-client`; `DependencyTreeTest` does not follow test scope, and `codegen` is in its build-time list anyway.

**Shared trait lists (task 1.25).** `DefaultTraitInclusion` is the output of codegen's `DefaultTraitInclusionSpec` for `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient.schema.internal.DefaultTraitInclusion`, checked in and reformatted: the generated text has 2-space indentation and lines of more than 300 characters, which Checkstyle rejects in `src/main`. The values are unchanged. Codegen's `DefaultTraitInclusionSpecTest.dynamicClientConstants_matchTheCodegenLists` fails when the checked-in lists and codegen's `DefaultTraitInclusion` differ. The check lives in `codegen` because codegen is the source of the lists, and because a `dynamic-client` test dependency on `codegen` would make the reactor cyclic once `codegen` tests use the converter (below).

**Cache, recursion and lock.**
- Each converter holds a `ConcurrentHashMap<ShapeId, Schema>`, keyed by the model's ShapeId (the smithy-model ID, not the original ID a schema may carry). A cache hit is a lock-free read.
- A miss takes the converter's lock (one `Object` per converter) and converts the shape and every shape it reaches that is not cached yet. New schemas go into a `pending` map. They are added to the cache only when the whole conversion succeeds, so a failure caches nothing (Requirement 25.8). A second thread that misses on the same shape waits for the lock and then finds the published schema (Requirement 25.9).
- Recursion follows smithy-java: aggregate shapes are built with `SchemaBuilder`s kept in `recursiveBuilders`; a member whose target is still in the `building` set targets that builder, which closes the cycle with a deferred member. The three collections are fields guarded by the lock and are cleared after every conversion, whether it succeeded or not. A deferred member reads its builder's built schema, which is the schema the converter caches, so every member target is the cached instance (Requirement 25.5). The converter does not call `Schema.resolve()`: `resolve()` returns a copy, and deferred members would then point at the copy. The class Javadoc tells callers not to call it.
- Structure and union schemas get a builder supplier that calls `getSchema` again, so it always returns the cached schema. It runs only after the conversion has been published.
- The lock is held while `TraitFactories.create` runs. A cache miss on the thread that holds the lock (a third-party `TraitFactory` calling back into the same converter) throws `IllegalStateException` instead of starting a nested conversion, which would clear the state of the one in progress.

| Source or design | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Module `dynamic-schemas` | Merged into `dynamic-client` | Design C.1 and Requirement 1.7: the only runtime module with `smithy-model` |
| `SchemaConverter(Model)` attaches every trait | Adds `SchemaConverter(Model, ServiceShape)`. Both apply the default Trait_Inclusion_Policy through `RuntimeTraitInclusion` (defaults, plus each protocol and auth definition trait on the service and its `traits` list, minus constraint traits and hard exclusions, which include the `com.amazonaws.c2j` namespace). The one-argument constructor includes the protocol and auth traits of every service in the model, which with one service is that service's policy. A member gets only its own included traits, sorted by trait ID | Requirement 25.3 and design C.1. The two-argument constructor checks that the service is in the model |
| `convertTraits` copies smithy-model `Trait`s | `TraitFactories.create(id, ModelConversions.document(trait.toNode()))`, so a trait with a typed runtime class becomes that class and any other becomes a `GenericTrait` | Requirement 25.4. The node mapping is codegen's `DocumentLiterals` mapping (an integral number is an `int` document when it fits, else `long`, else `BigInteger`; `BigDecimal` stays; other numbers become `double`; objects keep member order), so converted and generated trait values are equal |
| `ConcurrentMap<Shape, Schema>` filled while converting (`putIfAbsent` per shape) and `Collections.synchronizedMap` builders, with no conversion lock | Cache keyed by ShapeId, filled only after a conversion succeeds, conversions serialized by a lock per converter (see above) | Requirements 25.8 and 25.9. In smithy-java a failure leaves the shapes converted before it cached, and their deferred members can point at builders that are never built. Two threads converting one recursive group at the same time can build two schemas for a shape: `putIfAbsent` keeps one, but member targets of the other group can point at the losing instance |
| `getSchema(Shape)` only | Also `getSchema(ShapeId)`, which throws `IllegalArgumentException("Shape <id> is not in the model")`. `getSchema(Shape)` rejects a shape that is not the model's shape with that ID | Design C.1 (`getSchema(ShapeId)`, an error naming the missing ID). The cache is keyed by ShapeId, so a foreign shape with the same ID would otherwise get the model shape's schema |
