| A member that targets a missing shape fails in `Model.expectShape` | `IllegalArgumentException("Shape <target> targeted by member <member> is not in the model")` | Requirement 25.8: the error names the missing ShapeId |
| A member ShapeId or a resource shape throws `UnsupportedOperationException` | A member ShapeId returns that member of the container's schema (`getSchema(ns#S$m)` is `getSchema(ns#S).member("m")`). A resource becomes `Schema.createResource` | Requirement 25.1 covers every shape type in the ShapeType enumeration |
| Enum values from `getEnumValues().values()` into a `HashSet`; a `string` with the legacy `@enum` trait becomes a string schema | Values from each member's `enumValue` trait in declaration order (`LinkedHashSet`), as the Schema_Generator does; a `string` with `@enum` becomes `Schema.createEnum` with the trait's values | Requirement 25.7: the generated schemas do the same |
| Prelude shapes are converted like model shapes | The prelude simple shapes that have a `PreludeSchemas` constant (`Blob` through `Document` and the seven `Primitive*` shapes) return that constant. `smithy.api#Unit` is still converted, so it has a `StructDocument` builder | The Schema_Generator targets the `PreludeSchemas` constants. A converted `PrimitiveByte` would carry `DefaultTrait(Document.of(0))` (an integer document, from the node) where the constant has `Document.of((byte) 0)`, so the trait values would differ (Requirement 25.7) |
| `schemaId`: the original ID, or the shape's own ID when the original is `smithy.api#Unit` | Same, and a shape synthesized from `smithy.api#Unit` also gets the `unitType` trait when it does not have it | Requirement 25.6; the Schema_Generator adds the trait (task 1.27, `SchemaFieldOrder.isSyntheticUnit`) |
| `getSchemaIndex().getSchema(id)` calls `expectShape` and throws for an unknown ID | Takes the SDK `ShapeId`, converts on demand, and returns null for a shape or member that is not in the model | The SDK `SchemaIndex.getSchema` contract (task 1.11): null for an unknown ID |
| `createDocumentBuilder(Schema, ShapeId)` takes a smithy-model ShapeId | Takes the SDK `ShapeId`; both overloads reject null. Builder suppliers of a converter created with a service pass the service's ShapeId, otherwise the schema's | The documents are runtime types. The ID is passed down but not read, as in smithy-java, where `convertDocument` only forwards it; the Javadoc says it is reserved for relative discriminators |
| `StructDocument.type()` is the schema's type (`UNION` for unions); `ContentDocument.type()` is the schema's type (`ENUM`, `INT_ENUM`) | `STRUCTURE` for unions, `STRING` for enums, `INTEGER` for intEnums. Union documents have no discriminator; a structure document's discriminator is the structure's ShapeId, and the target's ShapeId when the schema is a member | **User decision** (the task 11.5 rule). The member case is the task 11.1 `LazyStructure` fix |
| `StructDocument.serialize` and `ContentDocument.serialize` write the contents (`writeStruct`, `writeString`, ...); the source notes this breaks the `Document` invariant on purpose | Both write one `writeDocument(PreludeSchemas.DOCUMENT, this)`, as every `Document` (Requirement 18.1) and the task 11.1 `StructureDocument`/`LazyStructure`, which are also `SerializableStruct`s. `serializeContents` writes `writeStruct(schema, this)`; `serializeMembers` writes each member's contents with its member schema | **User decision**: the documents follow the task 11.1 and 11.5 rules. To write a `StructDocument` as a structure, call `serializeContents` or `serializer.writeStruct(doc.sdkSchema(), doc)`. See the open questions |
| Members, list elements and map values written with `value.serialize(serializer)` | Written with the member schema: `writeDocument(member, value)` when the member targets `document`, else `value.serializeContents(serializer)` | Follows from the row above; the same calls reach the serializer as in smithy-java |
| `equals`: none on `StructDocument`; `ContentDocument` compares schema and value | `Document.equals(this, o)` and `Document.hash(this)` on all three classes: type and value, not schema | Task 11.1 rule: equality is the same across `Document` implementations, so a `StructDocument` member equals `Document.of` of the same value |
| No `toString` | `Document(<type>: <value>)`, with `*** Sensitive Data Redacted ***` when the schema (member or target) has `@sensitive`; structure documents print their members, so redaction applies at every depth | Task 11.1 rule and the SDK rule that `toString` never shows sensitive values |
| `convertDocument` stores `delegate.asNumber()` (any `Number` class) for numeric members | Converts to the member's type with the `as<Type>` method of that type (`asByte()` ... `asBigDecimal()`), that is with JLS 5.1.2/5.1.3 conversions | **User decision** (JLS coercion). `asNumber()` then returns the class that matches `type()`, and equality and hash codes see the converted value. smithy-java kept, for example, a long 4294967297 in an integer member, which compared as 4294967297 and serialized as 1. Range checks are task 13.7 |
| `ContentDocument.as<Type>` casts the value; a wrong type throws `ClassCastException` | Checks the type and throws `SerializationException("Expected an integer document, but found string")`, the `Documents` message | Requirement 18.7 |
| `ContentDocument.asBigDecimal()` of a byte to long is `BigDecimal.valueOf(double)`; non-finite floats throw `NumberFormatException` | `BigDecimal.valueOf(long)`, `new BigDecimal(BigInteger)`; a NaN or infinite float or double throws `SerializationException("Cannot convert a non-finite double document (NaN) to a bigDecimal")` | Same as `Documents` (tasks 1.7, 11.1): no digits lost above 2^53 |
| `asList()`, `asStringMap()` return the stored collections; `asBlob()` returns the stored buffer | Unmodifiable views, created once with the document; `asBlob()` and the serializer get a duplicate of the buffer | The `Document` contract (unmodifiable results, a blob buffer with its own position) |
| `asStringMap()`, `discriminator()`, `schema()` | `asMap()`, `getDiscriminator()`, `sdkSchema()` | SDK `Document` and `SerializableStruct` names (tasks 1.17, 11.1) |
| `StructDocument.asObject()` override | Not ported; `Document.asObject()` gives the same maps | Less code; raw union scalars are already `asObject()` results |
| `getMemberValue` uses `SchemaUtils.validateMemberInSchema` | `SchemaUtils.notAMember` when the schema has no member of that name, else `SchemaUtils.validateSameMember` | The SDK `SchemaUtils` (task 3.7), as `Documents.getMemberValue` |
| `mapView` is a plain field | `volatile` | Safe publication of the lazily built view |
| `ContentDocument.SchemaDocument` record whose `type()` is the schema's (`DOCUMENT`) | A nested final class whose `type()` and `getDiscriminator()` are the wrapped document's; it also delegates `createDeserializer()` | Java 8 has no records. `type()` must be the data model type (task 11.1); a nested typed document keeps its discriminator |
| `Document.of(schema, DataStream)`, `Document.of(schema, EventStream)`, `asDataStream()`, `asEventStream()`; streaming blob and event stream members | Not ported. Converting a document for, setting, or reading a member whose schema has `@streaming` throws `UnsupportedOperationException("Streaming member <id> (<type>) is not supported by StructDocument")` | `core/schema` has no stream documents (task 11.1); dynamic streaming is task 13.13 |
| `convertTimestamp` with smithy-java `TimestampFormatter.Prelude` | `codec-commons` `TimestampFormatter.Prelude.EPOCH_SECONDS.readFromNumber` and `DATE_TIME.readFromString(value, false)` | Same behavior (a number is epoch seconds, a string is `date-time`); the SDK's tightened rules are task 13.7 |
| Unknown members are dropped by `StructDocument.of` | Same | smithy-java behavior; the `SerializationException` for unknown members is task 13.7 (Requirement 28.10) |
| `SchemaGuidedDocumentBuilder.build()` passes its values array to the document | Passes a copy | Later `setMemberValue` or `deserialize` calls on the builder would otherwise change a built, supposedly immutable document |
| `setMemberValue` converts with `Document.ofObject(value)`; a null value fails in the conversion | A private conversion for `Document`, any other `SerializableShape` (through `Document.of`), `String`, `Boolean`, the eight `Number` types, `byte[]`, `ByteBuffer`, `Instant`, `List` and string-keyed `Map`; other types throw `IllegalArgumentException`. A null value clears the member | `core/schema` does not port `Document.ofObject` (task 11.1). `AwsEventShapeDecoder` passes `SerializableShape`s |
| `LinkedHashMap.newLinkedHashMap(size)` | `new LinkedHashMap<>((int) Math.ceil(size / 0.75))` | JDK 19 API |
| Switch expressions, `yield`, records, pattern-matching `instanceof` | Switch statements, final classes and casts | Java 8 |
| `SchemaConverterBench` (JMH) | Not ported | No JMH source set in `dynamic-client` |

**Tests.** smithy-java's `dynamic-schemas` tests (51 methods in four classes) are not ported method by method; the task asked for new unit tests. `dynamic-client` (78 tests, JUnit 5 and AssertJ, Smithy IDL fixtures under `src/test/resources/.../dynamicclient/schema/`):
- `SchemaConverterTest` (44): every shape type through a member of one structure (21 cases: ShapeId, type, target ShapeId, target is the cached instance); members in declaration order with their indexes; enum, intEnum and legacy `@enum` values in order; list and map members; service, operation and resource; member ShapeIds; prelude shapes are the `PreludeSchemas` constants except `Unit`; typed traits (`required`, `httpHeader`, `httpQuery`, `jsonName`, `http`, `restJson1`, `sigv4`, `error`, `httpError`, `timestampFormat`, `streaming`); omitted traits (`documentation`, `length`, `range`, `pattern`, `tags`, a `com.amazonaws.c2j` trait, `input`); member traits merged with target traits but kept apart from direct traits; a custom protocol definition whose listed trait has no typed class becomes a `GenericTrait` whose document equals the node, in member order, while an unlisted trait is omitted; the policy with one service, all services and no service; cache identity through `getSchema(ShapeId)`, `getSchema(Shape)` and the schema index, and no sharing between converters; `visit` and null for unknown IDs; errors for unknown shapes and members, foreign shapes and services; a member target missing from the model (exact message, nothing cached, a retry fails the same way, other shapes still convert); `StructDocument` shape builders; self, mutual (both orders), list, map and union recursion with identical target instances.
- `SchemaConverterConcurrencyTest` (1): 25 rounds, each with a new converter and 8 threads of a bounded pool released together by a latch, each converting the recursive shapes in a different order; every thread gets the cached instance and recursive targets are identical. `@Timeout(60)`, `Future.get` with a 10-second timeout, and the pool is shut down and awaited after the test.
- `SchemaConverterOriginalShapeIdTest` (3): a renamed shape uses the original ID for itself and its members and is cached by its model ID; a shape synthesized from `Unit` keeps its ID and gets `unitType`; a shape without the trait keeps its ID.
- `StructDocumentTest` (21): member conversion for every type; JLS numeric conversion (4294967297 to integer is 1, 1.9 to long is 1, 300 to byte is 44); timestamps from timestamps, epoch seconds and `date-time`; unknown members ignored; argument and coercion errors with exact messages; streaming members rejected; union documents (type, no discriminator, one member, `asObject`, `getMemberValue`); unmodifiable views in schema order; coercion errors of member documents; equality and hash codes; sensitive redaction; `getMemberValue`; `serialize` as one `writeDocument` and `serializeContents` as `writeStruct`; `serializeMembers` with member schemas (blob, intEnum, enum, list, map, document, union, nested structure); JSON round trips through the document builder for a 23-member structure (same text when written again) and for each union member; sparse and dense lists; `asShape` with the document builder; `setMemberValue` with plain values, null and a `SerializableShape`; a union builder with no value.
- `RuntimeTraitInclusionTest` (5) and `ModelConversionsTest` (4): the policy for one, two and no services, `includedTraits`, `isHardExcluded`; the node-to-document mapping for every node type, member order and nulls, typed and generic traits, ShapeId conversion both ways.

`codegen` (test scope only):
- `DefaultTraitInclusionSpecTest.dynamicClientConstants_matchTheCodegenLists` (above).
- `SchemaConverterEquivalenceTest`, a spot check of Requirement 25.7 (Property 42 is the optional task 13.2 and is not written). It prepares SQS from its C2J files with the Codegen_Pipeline, generates and compiles its model package and schemas, and compares the converter with the generated schemas through the closure of `SendMessage` input and output (12 shapes) and the generated `SERVICE` and `SEND_MESSAGE` schemas: ShapeId, type, traits (as sets of runtime traits), enum values in order, member names and order, member ShapeIds, indexes and traits, and target ShapeIds. It also checks that `RuntimeTraitInclusion` gives the same effective set as `TraitInclusionPolicy.forService` for SQS. All equal.
- `PrototypeServices`: `PrototypeErrorRegistryTest`'s prepare code, moved to a helper the new test shares. No behavior change.

**Runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, from a `/tmp` worktree of HEAD with the changes copied in):
- `clean install -pl :dynamic-client`: 78 tests, 0 failures; Checkstyle, SpotBugs, Javadoc and `dependency:analyze` clean.
- `clean test -pl :codegen -Dtest=DefaultTraitInclusionSpecTest,SchemaConverterEquivalenceTest,PrototypeErrorRegistryTest`: 14 tests, 0 failures. The rest of the codegen suite was not run; no codegen main code changed.
- `clean test -pl :architecture-tests`: 79 tests, 0 failures, 11 skipped (the disabled `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest` methods). `PortComplianceTest` now checks real `dynamic-client` classes; `DependencyTreeTest` and `PackageContainmentTest` pass unchanged. After the last main-code change (the re-entrancy guard), `dynamic-client`, the three codegen classes and `PortComplianceTest`, `DependencyTreeTest` and `PackageContainmentTest` (22 tests) were run again, with 0 failures.
- `schema` and the codecs did not change, so they were not rerun.

**The json-codec `SmithyJsonAstSchemas` stand-in (task 11.12).** It can now be replaced with the Schema_Converter in principle, but not as design B.3 describes. A `json-codec` test dependency on `dynamic-client` makes the Maven reactor cyclic, because `dynamic-client` depends on `json-codec` (through `aws-json-client-protocol` and `rest-json-client-protocol`, and directly in test scope), and Maven orders the reactor by every scope. `json-codec`'s test dependencies are unchanged. To use the converter, `DocumentSerdeTestSuite` would have to move to a module that may depend on both, such as `dynamic-client`'s tests or a test module.

**Open questions.**
- `StructDocument.serialize` writes a document, as the task 11.1 documents do. Code that passes a `StructDocument` where a structure is expected must call `writeStruct(doc.sdkSchema(), doc)` or `serializeContents`, not `serialize`. Tasks 13.7 (`DynamicRequest` delegates to `serializeMembers`, so it is not affected) and 13.14 (`MockQueue` with `StructDocument` outputs) need to check their call sites. Is that the intended reading of the user decision, or should `StructDocument` keep smithy-java's struct-style `serialize`?
- Requirement 41.1 asks for smithy-java's tests to be ported method by method. Should the 51 `dynamic-schemas` test methods be ported in a follow-up? Of their subjects, only the stream documents and constraint validation (`Validator`) are out of scope here.
- `DocumentSerdeTestSuite` and the reactor cycle above: move the suite, or keep `SmithyJsonAstSchemas`?
- `RuntimeTraitInclusion` needs the protocol and auth trait definitions in the model. A model assembled without `smithy-aws-traits` on the class path keeps, for example, `aws.protocols#restJson1` as an unknown trait (if unknown traits are allowed), and its `traits` list (`httpHeader`, `httpQuery`, ...) is then not included. Codegen behaves the same. Task 13.4 should decide whether `dynamic-client` depends on `smithy-aws-traits` at runtime.

## `DynamicClient` construction (task 13.4)

**Sources.** smithy-java `client/dynamic-client` (commit `142eecf4d`): `DynamicClient` and its `Builder`, `settings/ModelSetting`, `settings/ServiceIdSetting`. The protocol detection of `plugins/DetectProtocolPlugin` is task 13.5; `DynamicOperation` and the operation lookup are task 13.7; `DocumentException` and the error registry are task 13.9.

**Target.** `core/dynamic-client`, package `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient`:
- `DynamicClient` (`@SdkPublicApi`, `@ThreadSafe`, implements `AwsClient`): `builder()`, `service()` (an `ApiService` over the converted service schema; the same instance on every call), `serviceName()`, `serviceClientConfiguration()`, `close()`. Package-private `model()`, `serviceShape()`, `schemaConverter()`, `syncClientConfiguration()` and `asyncClientConfiguration()` for tests and the later tasks.
- `DynamicClient.Builder` (`@NotThreadSafe`) extends `AwsClientBuilder`, `AwsSyncClientBuilder` and `AwsAsyncClientBuilder`, and adds `model(Model)`/`model()`, `serviceId(ShapeId)`/`serviceId()` (smithy-model `ShapeId`, as `SchemaConverter.getSchema`), `protocol(ClientProtocol)`, `authSchemeResolver(DynamicAuthSchemeResolver)` and `endpointProvider(DynamicEndpointProvider)`. The implementation is a private nested class extending `AwsDefaultClientBuilder`.
- `DynamicServiceClientConfiguration` and its `Builder`: the plugin and user view of the configuration, a copy of the generated `<Service>ServiceClientConfiguration` with `authSchemeResolver()` in place of `authSchemeProvider()`.
- `DynamicEndpointProvider` and `DynamicEndpointParams`, `DynamicAuthSchemeResolver` and `DynamicAuthSchemeParams`: SDK additions (design C.2 and the "SDK additions" list). The builder options need their types now; their default implementations (endpoint rules, model auth) are task 13.5.
- Package `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient.internal` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `DynamicServiceMetadata` (service resolution and the names the configuration derives from the service) and `DynamicServiceClientConfigurationBuilder` (the write-through plugin builder, a copy of the generated one).

**`smithy-aws-traits` at runtime (the task 13.1 open question).** Decision: a compile-scope dependency of `dynamic-client`. Reasons: AWS service models do not assemble without the `aws.api`, `aws.auth` and `aws.protocols` trait definitions (unless unknown traits are allowed, which loses the protocol traits' `traits` lists that `RuntimeTraitInclusion` reads), and `DynamicServiceMetadata` reads `ServiceTrait` and `SigV4Trait`, as codegen's `AddSmithyMetadata` and `DefaultSmithyNamingStrategy` do. Requirement 1.7 is about `smithy-model`: `smithy-aws-traits` brings `smithy-model`, `smithy-diff` and `smithy-protocol-traits`, all below `dynamic-client`, which `DependencyTreeTest` already exempts by name. No other published module depends on `dynamic-client` (codegen's dependency is test scope), so no allowlist entry was needed; `DependencyTreeTest` passes unchanged.

**`pom.xml`.**
- `smithy-aws-traits` moves from test to compile scope (above).
- New compile dependencies used by the new code: `sdk-core`, `regions`, `endpoints-spi`, `http-client-spi`, `http-auth-spi`, `identity-spi`, `retries-spi`. `aws-core` is used now and leaves `ignoredUnusedDeclaredDependencies`; the six protocol modules and `smithy-rules-engine` stay there (tasks 13.5 to 13.10).
- `apache5-client` and `netty-nio-client` at runtime scope, as `services/pom.xml` declares for generated clients, so that an unset HTTP client resolves to the same default (Requirement 26.5).
- Test scope: `smithy-aws-endpoints` (the Account model uses `aws.endpoints#standardPartitionalEndpoints`) and `equalsverifier`.

| Source or design | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `DynamicClient.Builder extends Client.Builder` with `ModelSetting` and `ServiceIdSetting`; one `ClientConfig` | `DynamicClient.Builder` extends `AwsClientBuilder`, `AwsSyncClientBuilder` and `AwsAsyncClientBuilder`; the implementation extends `AwsDefaultClientBuilder` | Requirement 26.5 and design C.2: the options and defaults of a generated builder. `build()` is final in `SdkDefaultClientBuilder`, so the work is in `buildClient()` |
| Design C.2 `httpClient(SdkHttpClient)` and `asyncHttpClient(SdkAsyncHttpClient)` | `httpClient(SdkHttpClient)`, `httpClient(SdkAsyncHttpClient)`, `httpClientBuilder` for both and `asyncConfiguration`, inherited from the SDK's sync and async builder interfaces; no `asyncHttpClient` | The names of generated sync and async builders (Requirement 26.5), and one way to set each option. See the open questions |
| One client configuration | `buildClient()` resolves two: `syncClientConfiguration()` and `asyncClientConfiguration()`, as a generated sync and a generated async builder would from the same settings. Each resolves its HTTP client (the configured one, one built from the configured builder, or the default); plugins run once per configuration. `close()` closes both. If either fails, or the message-exchange check fails, the configurations already resolved are closed | The SDK's sync and async pipelines and handlers each need their configuration (task 13.10), and Requirement 27.8 asks the builder to fail on a protocol and transport mismatch, so the configurations cannot be resolved lazily at the first call. Cost: a client resolves both default HTTP clients even when only one form is used (open question) |
| `model(Model)` throws `NullPointerException("model cannot be null")`; a missing model fails in `context().expect(MODEL)` | `model(null)` and `serviceId(null)` unset the value; `build()` throws `NullPointerException("A Smithy model is required")` | Requirement 26.7 and design C.2 (the message). SDK builder setters accept null |
| No `serviceId` and several services: `NullPointerException("No `service` set, and the model contains multiple services: " + serviceShapes)`; none: `NullPointerException("`service` is not set, and the model contains no services")` | Still `NullPointerException`: `"No serviceId is set, and the model contains multiple services: [a#A, b#B]. Set serviceId to one of them."` and `"No serviceId is set, and the model contains no services"` | Requirements 26.3 and 26.4 (silent on the type, so smithy-java's is kept). The list holds the ShapeIds sorted, not the `ServiceShape`s in set order, so the message is stable; it names the builder option |
| An unknown `serviceId`: `model.expectShape(id, ServiceShape.class)` throws smithy-model's `ExpectationNotMetException` | `IllegalArgumentException("Service ns#X is not in the model. The model contains the services [...]")`, and for a shape of another type `"Shape ns#X is a structure, not a service. ..."` (`"The model contains no services."` when there are none) | Requirement 26.9 (the message includes the ID), and the SDK error message guideline: say what is wrong and how to fix it |
| `Builder.service()` (deprecated alias of `serviceId()`) | Not ported | Deprecated in smithy-java |
| `new SchemaConverter(model)` | `new SchemaConverter(model, service)` | Design C.1: the trait policy of the resolved service |
| The `ApiService` goes into the client configuration (`configBuilder().service(...)`) | `DynamicClient.service()`; the SDK configuration has no service option | Design C.2 and Requirement 26.6. Task 13.7 passes it to each `ApiOperation` |
| `DynamicClient extends Client` | `implements AwsClient` (design C.2 says `SdkAutoCloseable`, which `AwsClient` extends) | `serviceName()` and `serviceClientConfiguration()` as generated clients, and `SdkClientOption.SDK_CLIENT` is an `SdkClient` (task 13.10) |
| — | `serviceName()` is the signing name, or the endpoint prefix when the model has no signing name | Generated clients return their signing name (`SERVICE_NAME`); a model without `aws.auth#sigv4` and `arnNamespace` has none |
| — | The builder hooks come from the model: `serviceName()` (the SDK ID in PascalCase without `Amazon`/`Aws` and `Service`), `signingName()` (`sigv4` name, else `arnNamespace`, else empty) and `serviceEndpointPrefix()` (`endpointPrefix`); a service without `aws.api#service` uses its shape name as SDK ID and its lower-cased shape name as endpoint prefix | The same derivation as codegen (checked against the generated Account, Secrets Manager and SQS builders). Codegen rejects a service without the trait; a dynamic client also serves non-AWS models |
| — | `pascalCase` and `splitOnWordBoundaries` are private copies of `utils`' `CodegenNamingUtils` | `InternalApiBoundaryTest`: `CodegenNamingUtils` is `@SdkInternalApi` in another module |
| — | `finalizeServiceConfiguration` adds the service-specific endpoint override settings (`AWS_ENDPOINT_URL_<SDK ID>`, `aws.endpointUrl<Service>`, the profile's service section), as generated builders | Requirement 26.5 (the same default endpoint override sources) |
| `EndpointResolver.resolveEndpoint(EndpointResolverParams)` returns an `Endpoint` | `DynamicEndpointProvider extends EndpointProvider` returns `CompletableFuture<Endpoint>` and has a `Consumer<DynamicEndpointParams.Builder>` overload; `DynamicEndpointParams` holds the operation, the input and the call's `ExecutionAttributes` (for smithy-java's `Context`) | The shape of the generated `<Service>EndpointProvider`, and `ENDPOINT_PROVIDER` holds an `EndpointProvider`. sdk-core's pipeline `EndpointResolver` is synchronous; task 13.10 adapts. See the open questions |
| `AuthSchemeResolver.resolveAuthScheme(AuthSchemeResolverParams)` | `DynamicAuthSchemeResolver extends AuthSchemeProvider` returns `List<AuthSchemeOption>` and has a consumer overload; `DynamicAuthSchemeParams` holds the protocol ID, the operation and the call's `ExecutionAttributes` | The shape of the generated `<Service>AuthSchemeProvider`; `AUTH_SCHEME_PROVIDER` holds an `AuthSchemeProvider` |
| — | Both params classes are final classes with builders, `toBuilder`, `equals`, `hashCode` and `toString` (the operation ID, no input values) | SDK POJO rules. They hold the call's attributes, not a copy, so they carry no thread-safety annotation |
| — | `putAuthScheme` stores the scheme; `mergeServiceDefaults` sets `AUTH_SCHEMES` to the configured ones | Generated builders add theirs to the built-in schemes. The model-derived schemes are task 13.5 |
| — | `DynamicServiceClientConfiguration` inherits `equals` and `hashCode` from `AwsServiceClientConfiguration` | As the generated configuration classes |

**Seams left for later tasks.**
- **13.5 (protocol, auth, endpoint, transport).** `DefaultBuilder.mergeServiceDefaults` is where the default `ENDPOINT_PROVIDER` (a rules-evaluating `DynamicEndpointProvider`), `AUTH_SCHEME_PROVIDER` and the built-in `AUTH_SCHEMES` go, as `merge` only fills unset options; `finalizeServiceConfiguration` is where `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` gets a `lazyOptionIfAbsent` from the fixed priority list, as generated builders do with `ClientProtocolSupport.createProtocol`. `buildClient()` already runs `ClientProtocolSupport.validateMessageExchange` on both configurations, so a selected protocol is checked against the configured or default transport. `DynamicServiceMetadata.service()` gives the `ServiceShape` for `ServiceIndex`; the service schema is ready before the configurations are resolved. The one WARN per missing auth scheme belongs in `buildClient()`.
- **13.7 (operations).** `model()`, `serviceShape()`, `schemaConverter()` and `service()` hold what `DynamicOperation` needs; the per-name operation cache is a field to add to `DynamicClient`.
- **13.10 (pipeline).** `syncClientConfiguration()` and `asyncClientConfiguration()` are the inputs of `AwsSyncClientHandler` and `AwsAsyncClientHandler`; `close()` then closes the handlers. `SDK_CLIENT` is not set: putting the client into the configuration that `close()` closes would make `close()` call itself. Generated clients set it on a copy that the handler does not own.
- **13.14 (Mock_Plugin).** Plugins run on a `DynamicServiceClientConfigurationBuilder`, whose `SdkClientConfiguration.Builder` field is the one `ClientConfigurationAccess` looks for. A plugin runs twice per client, once per configuration.

**Tests** (54 new; `dynamic-client` now has 132, JUnit 5 and AssertJ; fixtures `weather.smithy`, `two-services.smithy` and `no-service.smithy` under `src/test/resources/.../dynamicclient/`):
- `DynamicClientServiceResolutionTest` (13): no model and an unset model (exact message), the only service, no services and several services (exact messages, sorted IDs), a selected service among several and the only service by ID, an unknown ID, a non-service shape and an ID in a model without services (exact messages), `service()` (type `service`, ShapeId, name, root schema, the converter's cached instance, same instance on each call), and the builder's model.
- `DynamicClientBuilderTest` (23): region configured and from the default chain (`aws.region`), credentials configured and the default provider, configured HTTP clients used and not closed, HTTP client builders whose clients `close()` closes once, the default sync and async HTTP clients, `CLIENT_TYPE`, future completion executor and retry strategy per configuration, `protocol`, a protocol with another message exchange (the build fails naming both, and closes the HTTP client it created), `authSchemeResolver`, `endpointProvider`, `endpointOverride`, the default regional endpoint, the service-specific endpoint system property, override configuration (timeout and interceptor in both configurations), a plugin (runs twice on a `DynamicServiceClientConfiguration.Builder`, its region and timeout changes apply), `putAuthScheme`, the derived names, `serviceName()` without a signing name, builder getters, builder changes after `build()` not reaching the built client, and a build after a failed build.
- `DynamicClientAccountModelTest` (3): the Account prototype service from its single-file JSON AST (`services/account/model/model.json`, skipped if absent): service ShapeId, type and protocol and auth traits, the names of the generated Account builder, the default endpoint, and resolution by ID.
- `DynamicServiceMetadataTest` (6): the generated names of Secrets Manager and SQS, prefix and suffix removal, the `arnNamespace` signing name, a service without `aws.api#service`, a null model.
- `DynamicResolverParamsTest` (9): `EqualsVerifier` for both params classes, values and `toBuilder` round trips, `toString`, default attributes, required values, and the consumer overloads.

**Runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, from a new `/tmp` worktree of HEAD with the changes copied in):
- `clean install -pl :dynamic-client --am -P quick` once, to refresh the reactor dependencies.
- `clean install -pl :dynamic-client`: 132 tests, 0 failures; Checkstyle, SpotBugs, Javadoc and `dependency:analyze` clean.
- `clean test -pl :architecture-tests`: 79 tests, 0 failures, 11 skipped (the disabled `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest` methods). The first run failed `InternalApiBoundaryTest` on `CodegenNamingUtils`, fixed by the private copy above. `PortComplianceTest`, `PackageContainmentTest` and `DependencyTreeTest` pass.
- `clean test -pl :codegen -Dtest=DefaultTraitInclusionSpecTest,SchemaConverterEquivalenceTest`: 5 tests, 0 failures (codegen's test classpath now has the new `dynamic-client` dependencies).

**Open questions.**
- Two configurations mean two HTTP clients per dynamic client (by default an Apache5 and a Netty client) even when only `call` or only `callAsync` is used. The alternative, resolving the async configuration at the first async call, needs a frozen copy of the builder state (the SDK builder has no copy) and would move protocol and transport mismatches from `build()` to the first call, against Requirement 27.8. Keep both eager?
- Design C.2 names `asyncHttpClient(SdkAsyncHttpClient)`; the port uses the SDK's `httpClient(SdkAsyncHttpClient)` overload. Should the design be updated, or should `asyncHttpClient` be added as well?
- `DynamicEndpointProvider` returns a `CompletableFuture<Endpoint>` like generated providers. smithy-java's resolver and sdk-core's pipeline `EndpointResolver` are synchronous. Keep the generated-provider shape?
- AWS models published with `aws.endpoints` traits (Account has `standardPartitionalEndpoints`) assemble only with `smithy-aws-endpoints` on the class path. It is test scope here; should `dynamic-client` depend on it at runtime too?
- No service and several services keep smithy-java's `NullPointerException`; an unknown ID throws `IllegalArgumentException`. Is `NullPointerException` the wanted type for "the model contains no services", or should all three be `IllegalArgumentException`?
- Design C.2's class sketch (`implements SdkAutoCloseable`, `asyncHttpClient`) differs from the port in the two ways above; update the design?

## Dynamic protocol, auth, endpoint and transport resolution (task 13.5)

**Sources.** smithy-java `client/dynamic-client` and `client/client-core` (commit `142eecf4d`): `DetectProtocolPlugin`, the dynamic client's auth scheme resolution, and `client/client-rulesengine` (`RulesEngineBuilder`, `EndpointRulesPlugin`). Not a method-by-method port: the SDK has its own builder pipeline (task 13.4), and the work follows design C.2 and Requirement 27.

**Target.** Package `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient.internal` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `DynamicProtocolSelection`, `DynamicAuthSchemes`, `ModelAuthSchemeResolver` and `RulesEndpointProvider`. `DynamicClient`'s builder uses the 13.4 seams:
- `mergeServiceDefaults` adds the built-in `AUTH_SCHEMES` (the `putAuthScheme` ones replace them) and the default `ENDPOINT_PROVIDER` with `merge`, so a configured `endpointProvider` wins.
- `finalizeServiceConfiguration` adds `AUTH_SCHEME_PROVIDER` and `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` with `lazyOptionIfAbsent`, so a configured resolver or protocol wins. Both are lazy: the protocol reads the client type and transport, and the resolver reads `AUTH_SCHEMES`, so a plugin that sets a transport or replaces the auth schemes changes them on the next configuration build.
- `buildClient()` fails when neither configuration has a protocol, runs `ClientProtocolSupport.validateMessageExchange` on both, closes what it created on failure, and logs the missing auth scheme warnings.
- One `BuildState` per build holds what both configurations share: the protocol selection and `ProtocolSettings` (the converted service schema, the service version and `AwsServiceException::builder`), the effective auth schemes, and one endpoint provider instance.

**Protocol priority list.** `rpcv2Cbor`, `awsJson1_0`, `awsJson1_1`, `restJson1`, `restXml`, `awsQuery`, `ec2Query`. This is Requirement 12.3 and codegen's `ProtocolUtils.CLIENT_PROTOCOL_PRIORITY`. `dynamic-client` cannot depend on `codegen` (Requirement 1.7), so it keeps its own copy. `codegen`'s `ProtocolUtilsTest.dynamicClientProtocolPriority_matchesTheCodegenList` fails if the two lists differ, as `DefaultTraitInclusionSpecTest` does for the trait lists (task 13.1).

**Which protocols the dynamic client may select.** All seven. Codegen's `ClientProtocolSelection.FACTORIES`/`STAGED_FACTORIES` split, with `stagedClientProtocols` as the opt-in, decides only whether a generated client leaves its legacy marshallers. A dynamic client has no legacy marshallers, and design C.2 only requires that the factory be available. `ClientProtocolSelection` is in `codegen` and maps IDs to JavaPoet `ClassName`s, so it is not reused at runtime. Generated builders reference their factory directly, and so does the dynamic client.

| Source or design | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `DetectProtocolPlugin` takes the first protocol trait found by `ServiceLoader` discovery (its TODO notes the order problem) | The fixed priority list, with direct references to the seven `Factory.INSTANCE`s. The traits come from `ServiceIndex.getProtocols`, so the order of traits in the model does not matter either | Requirement 27.1 (DC-23). Design C.2 says "whose factory is available (`ClientProtocolFactories`)". Direct references avoid a `ServiceLoader` scan at build. They also make `dependency:analyze` see the six protocol modules as used, so they leave `ignoredUnusedDeclaredDependencies` |
| Design C.2: "If a transport is configured, also require a matching `messageExchange`" | Candidates are created in priority order, and the first whose `messageExchange()` equals the transport's is returned. If none matches, the first candidate is returned, so that `validateMessageExchange` fails and names both components (Requirement 27.8) | Requirement 27.2 is only about traits. A transport mismatch is reported as a mismatch, not as "no protocol" |
| — | The transport's exchange is read as `validateMessageExchange` reads it: `CLIENT_TRANSPORT` or `ASYNC_CLIENT_TRANSPORT` by client type, else `HttpMessageExchange.INSTANCE` (the SDK HTTP client transport). The pipeline resolves the transport from the same options (`SyncApiCallPipeline.transport`) | Requirement 27.7 and the task: the same transport resolution as generated clients. The four-line lookup is repeated in `DynamicClient`, because `ClientProtocolSupport` exposes it only inside `validateMessageExchange` and a lazy value sees a `LazyValueSource`, not a configuration. No aws-core change |
| Design C.2: no protocol trait matches → `build()` throws listing the traits, or "none found" | `IllegalStateException("Service <id> declares no protocol that the dynamic client supports. Protocol traits on the service: none found. Supported protocols: [...]. Set a protocol with DynamicClient.Builder.protocol(...).")`, with `[<sorted trait IDs>]` in place of `none found` when the service has unsupported ones. Thrown after the configurations are resolved, so a protocol set by a plugin also counts | Requirement 27.2. `IllegalStateException`, as for the mismatch |

**Auth mapping.** Effective schemes come from `ServiceIndex.getEffectiveAuthSchemes(service, operation, NO_AUTH_AWARE)` for every operation in `TopDownIndex.getContainedOperations(service)`, computed once at build. An operation without schemes gets `[smithy.api#noAuth]`, and `optionalAuth` appends `noAuth` (Requirement 27.3).

| Scheme | SDK `AuthScheme` | `AuthSchemeOption` signer properties |
|---|---|---|
| `aws.auth#sigv4` | `AwsV4AuthScheme.create()` | `SERVICE_SIGNING_NAME` from the trait, `REGION_NAME` from the call's `AwsExecutionAttribute.AWS_REGION`, `PAYLOAD_SIGNING_ENABLED=false` for `aws.auth#unsignedPayload` |
| `aws.auth#sigv4a` | `AwsV4aAuthScheme.create()`, only when `software.amazon.awssdk.http.auth.aws.crt.HttpAuthAwsCrt` loads (checked once per class loader with `Lazy`) | `SERVICE_SIGNING_NAME`, `REGION_SET` from `AWS_SIGV4A_SIGNING_REGION_SET` or the region, `PAYLOAD_SIGNING_ENABLED=false` for `unsignedPayload` |
| `smithy.api#httpBearerAuth` | `BearerAuthScheme.create()` | none |
| `smithy.api#noAuth` | `NoAuthAuthScheme.create()` | none |
| anything else (`httpBasicAuth`, `httpDigestAuth`, `httpApiKeyAuth`, custom) | none, unless registered with `putAuthScheme` (builder or plugin) | none (scheme ID only) |

| Source or design | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| — | `ModelAuthSchemeResolver` returns, in model order, the options of the operation's schemes that are in `AUTH_SCHEMES`, then moves the `auth_scheme_preference` schemes first, as the generated `Preferred<Service>AuthSchemeProvider` does | Requirements 27.3 and 27.4; same profile behavior as generated clients |
| Design C.2: a call to an operation with no usable scheme fails before sending | The resolver throws `SdkClientException("Cannot call operation <id>: none of its auth schemes has an implementation. Missing auth schemes: [...]. Register an implementation with DynamicClient.Builder.putAuthScheme, or configure an authSchemeResolver.")`. Auth scheme resolution runs before signing and sending. Task 13.10 adds the call path; this task tests the resolver | Requirement 27.4 |
| — | The resolver keeps the key set of the configuration's `AUTH_SCHEMES` map, not a copy | `DynamicServiceClientConfigurationBuilder.putAuthScheme` (like the generated one) adds to that map in place, which no lazy value sees |
| Design C.2: one WARN per missing scheme at build | `buildClient()` logs one WARN per missing scheme ID, sorted, through the SDK `Logger` of `DynamicClient`. The WARN names the scheme and the service, lists the operations that cannot be called (or says each has another scheme), and says how to fix it (for sigv4a: add `http-auth-aws-crt`). It is logged once per build, not per call, and only when the client uses the model's resolver. The sync configuration's `AUTH_SCHEMES` decides | Requirement 27.4 and the logging guidelines (warn once; the client still works). With a configured resolver the model's schemes are not used (27.6). Plugins run once per configuration, so the two configurations normally hold the same schemes |
| sigv4a via http-auth-aws-crt | Without the CRT module, sigv4a is a missing scheme: it gets a WARN, and the resolver moves on to the next scheme (sigv4 in `Sigv4aFirst`) instead of selecting a scheme whose signer fails at signing time | The task: the module is optional in `http-auth-aws` |

**Endpoints.** `smithy-rules-engine` 1.71.0 (the pinned `smithy.version`) has `RuleEvaluator.evaluate(EndpointRuleSet, Map)` and `RuleEvaluator.evaluate(EndpointBddTrait, Map)` (checked with `javap`). Its classes, and those of `smithy-jmespath`, `smithy-model-jmespath` and `smithy-aws-endpoints`, are Java 8 class files (major version 52).

**OD-10 resolved:** `endpointBdd` is supported. A service with `endpointRuleSet` uses it, which is what generated clients compile. A service with only `endpointBdd` uses the BDD. The Account model has both. Its 23 endpoint test cases without an `Endpoint` parameter pass with each of them; the BDD path is tested by removing the rule set trait.

| Source or design | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Parameter precedence | Applied least specific first, so later bindings replace earlier ones: the evaluator applies parameter defaults, then built-ins, `operationContextParams`, `contextParam` and `staticContextParams` | The Smithy rules engine specification ([parameters, "Binding parameter values"](https://smithy.io/2.0/additional-specs/rules-engine/parameters.html)) |
| Design C.2 built-ins: region, FIPS, dualstack, endpoint override | `AWS::Region`, `AWS::UseFIPS`, `AWS::UseDualStack` from the call's `AwsExecutionAttribute`s. `AWS::Auth::AccountId` comes from the selected auth scheme's `AwsCredentialsIdentity.accountId()`, waited for with `thenApply`, not `joinLikeSync`. `AWS::Auth::AccountIdEndpointMode` and `AWS::S3::UseGlobalEndpoint` come from their attributes. `SDK::Endpoint`, the other S3 built-ins, `AWS::STS::UseGlobalEndpoint` and `AWS::Auth::CredentialScope` are not bound (rule defaults apply) | The generated `EndpointResolverUtils` binds the same set. The generated code blocks on the identity, and SpotBugs' `ASYNC_BLOCKING_CALL` rejects that in `dynamic-client`. The S3 built-ins come from `S3Configuration`, which a dynamic client does not have |
| Requirement 27.6, design C.2: a static endpoint skips evaluation | With an endpoint override (`CLIENT_ENDPOINT_PROVIDER.isEndpointOverridden()`), the provider returns the override URI and evaluates nothing, so `SDK::Endpoint` is never bound. Without rules and without an override, the call fails with `SdkClientException("No endpoint provider or endpointRuleSet available for service <id>: its model has neither a smithy.rules#endpointRuleSet nor a smithy.rules#endpointBdd trait. Configure an endpointOverride or an endpointProvider on the DynamicClient builder.")` | Requirements 27.6 and 27.9. **Differs from generated clients**, which pass the override as `SDK::Endpoint` and let the rules decide. For example, FIPS with a custom endpoint is an error there, and S3 can put the bucket into the custom host. See the open questions |
| `contextParam` | Read from the input's member of that name. The input is converted to a `Document` (itself if it is one, else `Document.of(input)`) and then to a smithy-model `Node` | Members are keyed by Smithy member names (Requirement 28.3) |
| `operationContextParams` (JMESPath) | `JmespathExpression.parse(path)` once per operation (cached per operation ShapeId), evaluated over the input `Node` with smithy-model-jmespath's `NodeJmespathRuntime`. The SDK's `JmesPathRuntime` is generated per service and works on POJOs | `smithy-jmespath` 1.71 has an evaluator and comes with `smithy-rules-engine`. Both are declared because the code uses them |
| — | `Document` to `Node`: a blob becomes its base64 text and a timestamp its epoch seconds. A value whose type does not match the parameter (`string`, `boolean`, `stringArray`) fails with `SdkClientException` naming the parameter and the binding | Endpoint parameters are strings, booleans and string lists, so no rule reads blobs or timestamps |
| — | A required parameter without a default and without a value fails with `SdkClientException` naming the parameter | Generated parameter builders reject it (`Validate.notNull`) |
| Result | An error rule throws `SdkClientException` with the rule's message. An evaluator failure becomes `SdkClientException("Unable to resolve the endpoint of <op> from the endpoint rules of <svc>: <cause>")`. An endpoint becomes an SDK `Endpoint`: the URL, every header value, `authSchemes` as `AwsEndpointAttribute.AUTH_SCHEMES` (`SigV4AuthScheme`/`SigV4aAuthScheme` with `signingName`, `signingRegion`/`signingRegionSet`, `disableDoubleEncoding`), and `metricValues` as `METRIC_VALUES`. Other properties and auth scheme names (`sigv4-s3express`) are skipped with a DEBUG log | Design C.2 and Requirement 27.9. Generated providers handle the same properties (`CodeGeneratorVisitor`). Using `AUTH_SCHEMES` to update the signer properties is the pipeline's job (task 13.10) |
| `clientContextParams` | Not bound | No builder or configuration option sets a client context parameter by name: `CLIENT_CONTEXT_PARAMS` is keyed by typed per-service keys. See the open questions |
| — | `RulesEndpointProvider` parses the rule set once at build and keeps it in a final field | `EndpointRuleSetTrait.getEndpointRuleSet()` caches it in a non-volatile field |

**`pom.xml`.**
- New compile dependencies: `http-auth` and `http-auth-aws` (the built-in schemes and signer properties), `smithy-jmespath` and `smithy-model-jmespath`.
- `smithy-aws-endpoints` moves from test to runtime scope. It provides the AWS rules functions and built-ins (`aws.partition`, ...) through `ServiceLoader`, and AWS models with `aws.endpoints` traits need it to assemble. This answers the 13.4 open question.
- The six protocol modules and `smithy-rules-engine` leave `ignoredUnusedDeclaredDependencies`. `http-client-protocol` stays until task 13.10.
- Test scope: `log4j-api`, `log4j-core` and `log4j-slf4j-impl` for `LogCaptor`.

**Tests** (45 new; `dynamic-client` now has 177, JUnit 5 and AssertJ). New fixtures: `internal/auth.smithy`, `internal/endpoint-params.smithy` and `no-protocol.smithy`.
- `DynamicProtocolSelectionTest` (12): each of the seven protocol traits alone gives its protocol class and ID. The CloudWatch trio in both orders gives rpcv2Cbor, and restXml plus awsJson1_1 in both orders gives awsJson1_1, with candidates in priority order. A transport of another exchange gets the first candidate. With no protocol trait: no candidates, null selection, and the exact `none found` message. With two unsupported protocol definitions, the message lists them sorted. The priority list itself is also checked.
- `ModelAuthSchemeResolverTest` (12):
  - effective schemes for default, operation-level, empty and optional auth;
  - the built-in schemes with and without the sigv4a signer, and the sigv4a signer absent on the test class path;
  - sigv4 options (signing name, region, no payload property) and sigv4a options (signing name, region set from the region or from `AWS_SIGV4A_SIGNING_REGION_SET`);
  - bearer and noAuth options without signer properties, and `optionalAuth`;
  - `unsignedPayload`;
  - schemes without an implementation skipped; the no-usable-scheme failure (exact message); a registered implementation used;
  - preferences; an operation of another service;
  - `missingSchemes`.
- `RulesEndpointProviderTest` (12):
  - the Account endpoint tests from its `smithy.rules#endpointTests` trait through the rule set and through the BDD (23 of 26 cases each);
  - SQS's `endpoint-rule-set.json` and `endpoint-tests.json` on a synthetic service (44 of 48 cases). Cases with an `Endpoint` parameter are skipped (see above);
  - Account regions with FIPS and dual-stack;
  - endpoint headers, `authSchemes` and `metricValues` attributes;
  - an error rule's message;
  - `contextParam`, `staticContextParams` over `contextParam`, `operationContextParams` (string and `stringArray` through JMESPath projections), and `contextParam` over `operationContextParams`;
  - an endpoint override skipping the rules;
  - a service without rules, with and without an override.
- `DynamicClientResolutionTest` (9):
  - the selected protocol in both configurations;
  - the `none found` build failure, closing the HTTP client it created;
  - a configured protocol on a model without protocol traits;
  - a plugin-set transport of another message exchange failing the build and naming the protocol and the transport;
  - the default endpoint provider with and without rules, the same instance in both configurations;
  - the default resolver and built-in schemes;
  - exactly two WARNs for the auth fixture (sigv4a without CRT, httpBasicAuth) with exact messages (`LogCaptor`);
  - no WARN when the schemes come from `putAuthScheme` on the builder or from a plugin;
  - no WARN with a configured resolver.
- Changed: `DynamicClientBuilderTest.builder_changedAfterBuild_doesNotChangeTheBuiltClient` now expects the selected `AwsJson1Protocol` (it expected no protocol), and the second client's configured stub protocol.
- `codegen`: `ProtocolUtilsTest.dynamicClientProtocolPriority_matchesTheCodegenList` (above).
- Comparing against the generated Account client's endpoint provider was not cheap. A `dynamic-client` test dependency on `services/account` would make the reactor cyclic through codegen's test dependency on `dynamic-client`. The model's own `endpointTests` cases, which the generated client's endpoint tests also come from, are used instead.

**Runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, from a new `/tmp/aws-sdk-135-wt` worktree of `ac43c50580d` with the changes copied in):
- `clean install -pl :dynamic-client --am -P quick` once.
- `clean install -pl :dynamic-client`: 177 tests, 0 failures. Checkstyle, SpotBugs, Javadoc, `dependency:analyze` and japicmp are clean. SpotBugs first flagged `ASYNC_BLOCKING_CALL` on a `joinLikeSync` of the identity, now replaced by `thenApply`.
- `clean test -pl :codegen -Dtest=ProtocolUtilsTest,DefaultTraitInclusionSpecTest,SchemaConverterEquivalenceTest`: 30 tests, 0 failures. No codegen main code changed.
- `clean test -pl :architecture-tests`: 79 tests, 0 failures, 11 skipped (the disabled `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest` methods).
- `aws-core` and `sdk-core` did not change.

**Open questions.**
- **Endpoint override.** Requirement 27.6 says a static endpoint is used "without evaluating `endpointRuleSet` or `endpointBdd`", so the dynamic client sends FIPS or dual-stack calls to a custom endpoint and never puts an S3 bucket into a custom host. Generated clients pass the override as `SDK::Endpoint` and evaluate the rules (the Account rules then reject FIPS with a custom endpoint). Should the dynamic client do the same when the rule set declares an `SDK::Endpoint` parameter? That would change Requirement 27.6.
- **Client context parameters.** Design C.2 lists `clientContextParams` as a binding source, but there is no way to set one on a dynamic client. Add a builder method such as `clientContextParam(String name, Object value)`, backed by keys created per name?
- **Endpoint auth schemes.** The endpoint's `AUTH_SCHEMES` attribute is set, but nothing applies it to the selected auth option yet (signing name and region overrides from the endpoint, as generated `ResolveEndpointInterceptor`s do). Task 13.10?
- **Bearer tokens.** `httpBearerAuth` maps to `BearerAuthScheme`, but the builder has no `tokenProvider` option. `IDENTITY_PROVIDERS` holds only the credentials provider, so a bearer call fails at identity resolution. Add `tokenProvider` to the builder, as generated bearer clients have it?
- **Warnings per build.** The missing-scheme WARN is logged once per build, as Requirement 27.4 says. A process that builds many clients for one model logs it each time. Log once per model and scheme instead?

## Dynamic operations and document calls (task 13.7)

**Sources.** smithy-java `client/dynamic-client` (commit `142eecf4d`): `DynamicClient.call`/`getOperation`/`createStruct`, `DynamicOperation`, and `dynamic-schemas` `StructDocument.convertDocument` for the coercion. Not a method-by-method port: the calls go through the SDK client handlers, and the coercion follows design C.3 and Requirement 28.

**Target.** Package `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient`:
- `DynamicOperation` (`@SdkPublicApi`, `@ThreadSafe`) implements `ApiOperation<DynamicRequest, DynamicResponse>`: operation, input, output and error schemas, effective auth scheme IDs, the client's `ApiService`, `inputBuilder()` and `outputBuilder()`.
- `DynamicRequest extends AwsRequest implements SerializableStruct` and `DynamicResponse extends AwsResponse implements SerializableStruct` (`@SdkPublicApi`, `@Immutable`): `document()`, builders with `document(StructDocument)`, `toBuilder`, `equals`/`hashCode`/`toString`. `sdkSchema()` is the document's schema; `serializeMembers` and `getMemberValue` delegate to the document, so `serialize` is the `SerializableStruct` default `writeStruct(sdkSchema(), this)` (the task 13.1 open question does not affect them). `sdkFields()` is empty.
- `DynamicClient`: `call(op)`, `call(op, Document)`, `call(op, Document, AwsRequestOverrideConfiguration)`, the three `callAsync` forms, `getOperation(String)` and `createStruct(ShapeId, Document)`.
- Package `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient.schema`: `StructDocument.coerce(Schema, Document, ShapeId)` (a public static method annotated `@SdkInternalApi`) and the package-private `StrictDocumentCoercion`.
- Package `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient.internal`: `DynamicCallResolvers`, the call's `AuthSchemeOptionsResolver` and `EndpointResolver`.

**Split between 13.7 and 13.10.** Calls need a call path, so 13.7 wires the shared handler path, and 13.10 keeps the features it lists.
- **13.7 (done).** `DynamicClient` creates an `AwsSyncClientHandler` over its sync configuration and an `AwsAsyncClientHandler` over its async configuration. `close()` closes the handlers, which close the configurations. Each call does what a generated client's operation method does on the schema route: per-call plugins from the override configuration (`updateSdkClientConfiguration`), the metric publishers of the call or the client, `SERVICE_ID` (the SDK ID) and `OPERATION_NAME` on the call's metric collector, and `clientHandler.execute(new ClientExecutionParams<>().withOperation(op).withRequestConfiguration(...).withInput(request).withMetricCollector(...).withAuthSchemeOptionsResolver(...).withEndpointResolver(...))`. `withOperation` routes the call to `SyncApiCallPipeline`/`AsyncApiCallPipeline` and puts the operation in `SdkExecutionAttribute.API_OPERATION` before `beforeExecution`. The async form adds the generated client's `whenComplete` publishing and `forwardExceptionTo`.
- **Auth and endpoint resolvers** (`DynamicCallResolvers`) are the counterparts of a generated client's `resolveAuthSchemeOptions` and `resolveEndpoint`. The request's override configuration can replace the auth scheme provider (it must be a `DynamicAuthSchemeResolver`). The endpoint provider comes from `SdkInternalExecutionAttribute.ENDPOINT_PROVIDER`, which already holds the request's override when it has one; it must be a `DynamicEndpointProvider`. Both read the operation from `API_OPERATION`.
- **Left to 13.10**, and named in the `DynamicCallResolvers` Javadoc:
  - the host prefix of the `endpoint` trait;
  - the signer properties of the endpoint's `authSchemes` (the 13.5 open question);
  - the endpoint's metric values;
  - `withProtocolMetadata`, `SDK_CLIENT` (still unset, see task 13.4) and `API_METADATA` (the user agent's `api/` segment);
  - idempotency token, compression and checksum parity with generated clients, auth parity, and pagination;
  - `getOperation` parity with `<Svc>Operations` (Property 40);
  - auxiliary operations.
  `ProtocolMarshalling` already fills an unset `@idempotencyToken` member for any `ApiOperation`, so dynamic calls probably get tokens already; 13.10 verifies it. Errors stay with 13.9: `DynamicOperation.errorRegistry()` is `TypeRegistry.empty()`, so every error response becomes a plain `AwsServiceException`.
- `http-client-protocol` stays in `ignoredUnusedDeclaredDependencies`: nothing in the module references it yet. New compile dependency: `metrics-spi` (`MetricCollector`, `MetricPublisher`). New test dependency: `reactive-streams`.

**SpotBugs.** The pipeline's `EndpointResolver` is synchronous and `DynamicEndpointProvider` returns a future, so `DynamicCallResolvers` waits with `CompletableFutureUtils.joinLikeSync`, as generated clients `join()` in `resolveEndpoint`. SpotBugs' `ASYNC_BLOCKING_CALL` rejects that (generated services skip SpotBugs), so `build-tools/.../spotbugs-suppressions.xml` gets one entry for `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient.internal.DynamicCallResolvers`, with a comment. Builds of `dynamic-client` need `build-tools` installed first.

**Coercion.** `StructDocument.coerce` runs `StrictDocumentCoercion`, which walks the input once, like the lenient `convertDocument`, and checks every value before calling an `as<Type>` method. No exception is caught and rethrown. It runs in `call`/`callAsync` before the handler is called, so it runs before any interceptor. Errors name the member by its ShapeId (`ns#Struct$member`; `ns#List$member` or `ns#Map$value` inside collections) and never include the value.

| Input | smithy-java (and the lenient `StructDocument.of`) | `StructDocument.coerce` | Requirement |
|---|---|---|---|
| A name that is not a member | Dropped | `SerializationException("Structure ns#S has no member named \"x\". Its members are [a, b]. Members are keyed by Smithy member name, not by a protocol name such as jsonName.")`, at every depth | 28.10 |
| A number for a timestamp | `EPOCH_SECONDS.readFromNumber` (a `BigDecimal` loses its fraction; a `double` is multiplied by `1000f`) | Exact decimal value (`BigDecimal.valueOf(double)`, `new BigDecimal(Float.toString(f))`), times 1000, truncated toward zero to milliseconds: 1.001 is 1001 ms, 1700000000.1239 is ...123 ms. NaN, infinities and values beyond `long` milliseconds fail with `"...: the value is out of range for a timestamp"` | 28.4 |
| A string for a timestamp | `DATE_TIME.readFromString` | Same parser; a `DateTimeParseException` becomes `"Cannot coerce member ns#S$t: the string is not a valid date-time (RFC 3339) timestamp, such as 1985-04-12T23:20:50.52Z"` | 28.5, 28.11 |
| Another type for a timestamp | `asNumber()` fails without the member | `"...: expected a timestamp, number or string document, but found a boolean document"` | 28.11 |
| A string for a blob | `asBlob()` (a plain string fails without the member; a JSON Protocol_Document base64-decodes) | Rejected for every document, Protocol_Documents included: `"...: a blob member needs a blob document, but found a string document. Pass the bytes with Document.of(byte[]) or Document.of(ByteBuffer)."` | 28.6 (see the open questions) |
| byte, short, integer, intEnum, long | JLS narrowing (task 13.1 user decision): 300 becomes 44 | An integral value in range. Integral-valued doubles and `BigDecimal`s (5.0, `1E+2`) are accepted. Otherwise `"...: the value is out of range for a byte member"`, or for fractional values `"...: an integer member needs an integral number, but the value is not one"` | 28.11 |
| bigInteger | `asBigInteger()` truncates | Same integral rule | 28.11 (extension) |
| float, double, bigDecimal | JLS conversion | Unchanged, with a type check first. A NaN or infinite value for bigDecimal fails naming the member | 28.11 |
| Container and scalar type mismatches (a non-map for a structure, union or map, a non-list for a list, a string for a number, ...) | The `as<Type>` message, without the member | `"Cannot coerce member ns#S$m: expected a list document, but found a map document"` | 28.11 |
| A union with two members set | The first in schema order wins | `"Cannot coerce member ns#S$u: union ns#U can have one member set, but a and b are both set"` | Not in the requirements; strict mode should not drop data |
| A non-map at the top | `IllegalArgumentException` | `SerializationException("Cannot coerce a document to structure ns#S: expected a map or structure document, but found a string document")` | 28.11 |
| A `StructDocument` that already has the schema | Converted again | Returned as is | Lets `createStruct` results go straight into `call` |

`StructDocument.of` is unchanged and still lenient (the task 13.1 user decision and tests). Streaming members still throw the task 13.1 `UnsupportedOperationException`; dynamic streaming is task 13.13.

| Source or design | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Design C.2 `getOperation` returns `ApiOperation<DynamicRequest, DynamicResponse>` | Returns `DynamicOperation`, which is that type | The design's port table makes `DynamicOperation` public anyway |
| smithy-java `getOperation` builds a new operation per call | Created on first use and kept in a `ConcurrentHashMap`. A miss does `get`, then creates the operation outside any lock, then `putIfAbsent`, so racing threads all get the published instance and the schema converter's lock is never taken inside a map bin lock. A failed creation caches nothing | Requirement 28.9 |
| Lookup by operation name | `TopDownIndex.getContainedOperations(service)` keyed by `getId().getName(service)`, so resource operations and service `rename`s are found. Case-sensitive. An unknown name throws `IllegalArgumentException("Operation X not found in service ns#Svc")`, the design C.3 message | Requirements 28.2 and 30.6 |
| `DynamicOperation.errorSchemas()` | The operation's errors, then the service's, without duplicates, in model order | The generated `<Svc>Operations` field lists both (`errorIds(operation errors + service errors)`). The registry itself is task 13.9 |
| `effectiveAuthSchemes()` | The model's effective schemes from `DynamicAuthSchemes` (task 13.5), including `smithy.api#noAuth` for an operation without auth | Generated operations get them from codegen's `OperationModel.effectiveAuthSchemes`. 13.10 checks parity (Property 40) |
| Design C.2 `createStruct(ShapeId, Document)` returns `Document` | `createStruct(software.amazon.smithy.model.shapes.ShapeId, Document)` returns a `StructDocument` typed as `Document`. A missing shape: `IllegalArgumentException("Shape ns#X is not in the model")`. A shape of another type: `"Shape ns#X is a list, not a structure"` (unions are rejected the same way) | The builder's `serviceId` also takes the smithy-model `ShapeId`. The task text mentions a by-name form and a `Map` form; neither is added (YAGNI, and design C.2 removed `call(op, Map)`) |
| `callAsync` | Never throws: an unknown operation, a null input and every coercion failure return a failed future. The returned future is `thenApply(DynamicResponse::document)` of the handler's future, with `forwardExceptionTo` so a cancellation or failure of the returned future is forwarded to the handler's future, as generated clients do | Requirement 28.12 and the async guidelines |
| `call(op)` | `call(op, Document.EMPTY_MAP, null)` | Requirement 28.1 |
| `DynamicResponse` output builder | `DynamicOperation.outputBuilder()` returns a private `DynamicResponse.BuilderImpl` that is both a `ShapeBuilder<DynamicResponse>` (delegating to the converter's document builder) and an `AwsResponse.Builder`. `HttpClientProtocol.buildOutput` therefore sets the response metadata and the HTTP response before `build()`, so the output is not copied | Requirement 28.7 and the task 3.21 rule. `DynamicResponse.builder()` (no document builder) is for callers such as the Mock_Plugin (task 13.14) |
| Task text: `MockSyncHttpClient`/`MockAsyncHttpClient` from service-test-utils | Recording HTTP clients in the test sources (`RecordingHttpClients`) | The sync mock does not record request bodies. service-test-utils also brings `blockhound-junit-platform`, JUnit 4 and the vintage engine at compile scope |

**Tests** (63 new; `dynamic-client` now has 240, JUnit 5 and AssertJ). New fixtures: `json10.smithy` (awsJson1_0 and sigv4: `PutItem` with a member for every coercion rule, `GetThing` through a resource, `Ping` without input, operation and service errors) and `rest-json.smithy` (restJson1: label, query, header, `jsonName` and timestamp members).
- `DynamicInputCoercionTest` (39): a map becomes a `StructDocument` with the ShapeId as discriminator and the cached input schema; an already typed document is returned as is; nested lists, maps, structures (discriminator), timestamps and unions; unknown top-level and nested members (exact messages); eight timestamp inputs (int, negative long, double with sub-millisecond digits, 1.001, `BigDecimal`, `BigInteger`, `date-time`, `Instant`); an invalid `date-time`, a boolean, and NaN for a timestamp; a string for a blob rejected and bytes kept; seven out-of-range integers for byte, short, integer and long (int, long, double, `BigInteger`, `BigDecimal` inputs) and the bounds accepted (with `1E+30` for bigInteger); fractions for integer and bigInteger; floating-point members; eight type mismatches, including a list element and a map value; a union with two members; a non-map input; an unknown shape, a list and a union for `createStruct`. All messages exact.
- `DynamicOperationTest` (8): cache identity, the same instance from 8 threads released together; schemas, error schemas (operation then service), auth, service, empty error registry and `toString`; a resource operation; `smithy.api#Unit` input and output; the unknown-operation message from `getOperation` and `call`; the input builder and the output builder (which is a `DynamicResponse.Builder`).
- `DynamicClientCallTest` (10), end to end through the SDK handlers and pipeline:
  - awsJson1_0, sync: `POST /`, `X-Amz-Target: JsonTen.PutItem`, `Content-Type: application/x-amz-json-1.0`, a sigv4 `Authorization` header with the `jsonten` scope, and the exact body, with an epoch-seconds number and a `date-time` string coerced to timestamps. The output is a `StructDocument` with discriminator `test.json10#PutItemOutput`, members in order, a fractional timestamp, a nested discriminator and an absent member. The final `DynamicResponse` carries the same document, the request ID and the HTTP status.
  - restJson1, async: `PUT /widgets/w%201?dryRun=true`, the `X-Trace` header, the exact body (`display_name`), the output with a header-bound integer, a `jsonName` member and a timestamp. The sync client sends nothing.
  - restJson1, sync.
  - `call(op)` with an empty input.
  - A per-call header from the override configuration, absent on the next call.
  - Interceptors: hook order `beforeExecution`, `modifyRequest`, `beforeMarshalling`; `beforeExecution` sees the coerced `DynamicRequest` (a timestamp document for a number input, with the input schema); `API_OPERATION` is the `getOperation` instance on both calls.
  - Invalid inputs (out-of-range byte, unknown member) throw `SerializationException` before any interceptor hook, and nothing is sent.
  - `callAsync` with a string blob returns a future that has already failed, with no hook and nothing sent. An unknown operation and a null input also return failed futures.
  - A 400 error completes the future with an `AwsServiceException` (status 400) until task 13.9.
- `DynamicRequestResponseTest` (6): builders, `SerializableStruct` delegation, `toBuilder` round trips, `toString`, the missing-document failures, the public response builder that cannot deserialize, and `EqualsVerifier` for both classes (with the superclass fields; `NONFINAL_FIELDS` suppressed for `AwsResponse.responseMetadata`).
- The optional property test 13.8 (Property 45) is not written.

**Runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, from a new `/tmp/aws-sdk-137-wt` worktree of `8b36f67dfca` with the changes copied in):
- `install -pl :build-tools` (the SpotBugs suppression), then `clean install -pl :dynamic-client --am -P quick` once.
- `clean install -pl :dynamic-client`: 240 tests, 0 failures. Checkstyle, SpotBugs (0 bugs), Javadoc and `dependency:analyze` are clean; japicmp skips the module.
- `clean test -pl :architecture-tests`: 79 tests, 0 failures, 11 skipped (the disabled `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest` methods). `PortComplianceTest`, `InternalApiBoundaryTest`, `PackageContainmentTest` and `DependencyTreeTest` pass.
- `aws-core`, `sdk-core` and `codegen` did not change.

**Open questions.**
- **Blob strings from Protocol_Documents.** Design C.3 says Protocol_Documents "may" base64-decode a string for a blob. The port rejects every string, because nothing in `core/schema` tells a Protocol_Document from a plain one without trying `asBlob()`, and catching its `SerializationException` to rethrow one that names the member is the pattern the exception guidelines forbid. Supporting it needs a marker, for example a `Document` method saying whether its strings are base64 blobs. Is rejection acceptable for now?
- **Fractions for integer members.** Requirement 28.11 lists out-of-range numbers; the port also rejects fractional numbers (1.5 for an integer) instead of truncating, and accepts integral-valued doubles such as 5.0. Is that the intended reading of "no silent truncation"?
- **Sub-millisecond digits** are truncated toward zero, so -1.0005 seconds is -1000 ms, the same rounding as the codecs' `(long)` cast. Should negative values round down instead?
- **Lenient and strict conversions side by side.** `StructDocument.of` stays lenient and `StructDocument.coerce` is strict and `@SdkInternalApi`. Design A's port table describes `StructDocument` itself as strict. Should `of` become strict, which would reverse part of the task 13.1 user decision, or should `coerce` become public?
- **Unions with several members** are rejected, which no requirement asks for. Keep it?
- **The `ASYNC_BLOCKING_CALL` suppression** for `DynamicCallResolvers` mirrors generated clients. The alternative is an asynchronous endpoint resolver in sdk-core's pipeline, which changes sdk-core. Keep the suppression?

## Dynamic client errors (task 13.9)

**Sources.** smithy-java `client/dynamic-client` (commit `142eecf4d`): `DocumentException` with its nested `SchemaGuidedExceptionBuilder`, `DynamicClient.registerError` (the service registry) and `DynamicOperation.create` (the per-operation `compose`).

**Target.**
- `core/dynamic-client`, package `software.amazon.awssdk.dynamicclient`: `DocumentException` (`@SdkPublicApi`, final) `extends AwsServiceException implements SerializableStruct`, with `getContents()`, `sdkSchema()`, `serializeMembers`, `getMemberValue`, `toBuilder()`, `isRetryableException()`, `isThrottlingException()`, the static `builder(Schema errorSchema)` and the nested `DocumentException.Builder` interface (`AwsServiceException.Builder` and `ShapeBuilder<DocumentException>`). The builder implementation is the private nested `SchemaGuidedExceptionBuilder`.
- `DynamicOperation.errorRegistry()` returns the operation's `ErrorRegistry` instead of `TypeRegistry.empty()`.
- `core/schema`: `ErrorRegistry.Builder.putType(ShapeId, Class<T>, Supplier<ShapeBuilder<T>>)`, the eager entry `TypeRegistry.Builder` already has. `ErrorRegistry` is `@SdkProtectedApi`, and the module is not in the japicmp include list.

**Registry.** `DynamicOperation.create` already lists `errorSchemas()` as the operation's errors, then the service's, without duplicates, in model order (task 13.7). The registry registers each of them with `putType(id, DocumentException.class, () -> DocumentException.schemaGuidedBuilder(schema, serviceId))`, and `errorCode(code, id)` for each `aws.protocols#awsQueryError` trait. `ErrorRegistry.Builder.errorCode` keeps the first error with a code, so the operation's error wins a code that both levels use. It is built once per `DynamicOperation`, which the client caches (Requirement 28.9).

**Resolution.** No protocol code changed. Every protocol hands the registry to `HttpErrorDeserializer.createError` through `SdkExecutionAttribute.ERROR_REGISTRY`, which the pipelines seed from `ApiOperation.errorRegistry()`:
- The discriminator is read per protocol (task 3.x and design A.10): awsJson1_0, awsJson1_1 and restJson1 read `X-Amzn-Errortype`, then body `__type`, then body `code`. rpcv2Cbor reads `__type`. restXml reads `<Error><Code>`. awsQuery and ec2Query read the `<Code>` of their envelopes. With `awsQueryCompatible`, the code in `x-amzn-query-error` comes first.
- `ErrorTypeUtils.resolve` normalizes it. For the query protocols and `awsQueryCompatible` services, it first looks up `ErrorRegistry.resolveCode` (the `awsQueryError` index, which needs the registry to be an `ErrorRegistry`). It then drops the text from the first `:`, matches an absolute `ns#Name` exactly, and resolves a relative name in the service namespace (Requirement 29.5). Only IDs the registry `contains` are returned.
- A resolved ID gets a `SchemaGuidedExceptionBuilder` from the registry. The protocol's `KnownErrorFactory` fills it: the payload codec for RPC protocols, and `HttpBindingErrorFactory` (headers, `@httpResponseCode`, payload) for restJson1 and restXml. Then `errorCorrection()` runs, and `populate` sets the message, status code, request ID, extended request ID, `AwsErrorDetails` (code, message, service name, raw body, HTTP response) and the clock skew. `build()` returns the `DocumentException`.
- No discriminator, an unknown one (including an error of another operation that the service does not list), an unparseable body, or a builder that fails, gives the base exception from `ProtocolSettings`. For the dynamic client that is `AwsServiceException::builder` (task 13.5), so the result is a plain `AwsServiceException` with the same values (Requirement 29.6).

| Source, task or design | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `DocumentException extends ModeledException` | `extends AwsServiceException` and `implements SerializableStruct` | Requirement 29.2 and design C.3: callers that catch the generic service exception also catch it. `SerializableStruct` keeps smithy-java's `serialize`/`serializeMembers`/`getMemberValue`, so `Document.of(e)` gives the error's document |
| `getContents()` returns `Document` | Same. It returns a `StructDocument` whose discriminator is the error ShapeId and whose members are keyed by Smithy member name, read by the converter's `SchemaGuidedDocumentBuilder` | Requirement 29.1 |
| `DocumentException.SchemaGuidedExceptionBuilder` (public nested class, `ShapeBuilder<ModeledException>`) | A private nested class behind the public `DocumentException.Builder` interface, which extends `AwsServiceException.Builder` and `ShapeBuilder<DocumentException>`. It extends `AwsServiceException.BuilderImpl`. The registry creates it through the package-private `DocumentException.schemaGuidedBuilder(Schema, ShapeId)` | `HttpErrorDeserializer` only accepts a registry builder that is an `SdkServiceException.Builder`. A public interface with a private implementation is the SDK exception builder pattern, as in generated exceptions |
| Task text: "`compose(operationErrors, serviceErrors)` registry" | One `ErrorRegistry` per operation, filled operation errors first. Each ShapeId has one entry, and the first `awsQueryError` code wins | Same lookups as first-wins `compose` (Requirements 29.3, 29.4). `TypeRegistry.compose` returns a `ComposedTypeRegistry`, which is not an `ErrorRegistry`, so `ErrorTypeUtils.resolve` would skip `resolveCode`, and the query protocols could not resolve custom codes. An `ErrorRegistry.compose` would add API for the same result |
| smithy-java registers service errors once in the client and composes per operation | Each operation's registry holds the service errors as well | One builder pass per operation, at first use; no shared state |
| `TypeRegistry.Builder.putType(e, ModeledException.class, ...)` | New `ErrorRegistry.Builder.putType(id, DocumentException.class, ...)` | The dynamic errors have no generated class or `Schema.shapeClass()`, so `putLazyType` cannot describe them. `getSchema(id)` returns null for such an entry (documented) |
| smithy-java's message: `"<Name> error"`, with the `message`/`Message` member appended (`createMessage`) | `rawMessage()` is the message the protocol reads (header, then body `message`/`Message`/`errorMessage`). When none is read, the builder's `message()` falls back to the first string member named `message` in any case. `getMessage()` is `AwsServiceException`'s format: `<message> (Service: ..., Status Code: ..., Request ID: ...)` | Requirement 29.2 and the generated exceptions: their `message` member is the exception message (`SchemaSerdeModel` treats `message` case-insensitively), and `HttpErrorDeserializer.populate` uses `builder.message()` when the response has none |
| No retry metadata | `isRetryableException()` is true with `smithy.api#retryable`; `isThrottlingException()` is true with `@retryable(throttling: true)`. Otherwise both defer to `AwsServiceException` (429, throttling error codes) | Generated exceptions override both from the same trait (`AwsServiceModel.retryableOverrides`), and the retry strategy reads them. The trait is in the default inclusion list, so converted schemas have it |
| `ModeledException` has no builder copy | `toBuilder()` returns a `DocumentException.Builder` with the exception's values and the same contents document. Setting a member on it copies the contents into a fresh document builder first | `RetryableStageHelper` rebuilds the last exception with `toBuilder().numAttempts(n).build()`. Without the override, a retried modeled error would become a plain `AwsServiceException` |
| — | `serializableBuilderClass()` is not declared | It is a static method that only names the implementation class for bean-style deserialization. `AwsServiceException.serializableBuilderClass()` is inherited and still returns its own builder; `DocumentException`'s builder needs a schema, so it has no no-argument constructor |
| — | The schema and contents are `transient`; the service ShapeId (`Serializable`) is kept. After Java deserialization, `getContents()` and `sdkSchema()` are null, `isRetryableException()` falls back to the parent's, and `toBuilder()` throws `IllegalStateException` | `Schema` and `StructDocument` are not `Serializable`. `SdkException` is, and SpotBugs checks the fields |
