| — | `DocumentException.builder(Schema)` is public; it rejects a schema that is not a structure with `smithy.api#error` (`IllegalArgumentException` naming the ShapeId and type) | Lets tests and stubs create the exception a call would throw, with any of `DynamicOperation.errorSchemas()` |
| `deserializeMember` asserts the member targets the error schema | Delegates to `SchemaGuidedDocumentBuilder.deserializeMember`, which asserts the same | Same behavior |

**Observed behavior** (not changed here).
- awsQuery and restXml read error members by exact XML name. A query error shape with a lowercase `message` member does not get `<Message>` into its contents; the exception message still has it. A member named `Message` does. Whether generated query exceptions read `<Message>` into a lowercase `message` member was not checked; their exception message comes from `populate` either way.
- Each retry attempt is a new error response, so a retried modeled error is deserialized once per attempt, as for generated clients.

**Tests** (24 new; `dynamic-client` has 263, JUnit 5 and AssertJ). New fixtures: `errors-json10.smithy` (awsJson1_0: an operation error with nested structure, list, integer and timestamp members, a service error with `Message`, an error both levels list, a `@retryable` error, an unbound `@retryable(throttling: true)` error), `errors-rest-json.smithy` (restJson1: a 404 operation error with an `@httpHeader` member and a `jsonName` member, a 403 service error) and `errors-query.smithy` (awsQuery: an operation error and a service error with the same `awsQueryError` code; the fixture suppresses Smithy's `QueryErrorCode` DANGER, which rejects that model, and the `awsQuery` deprecation warning).
- `DynamicClientErrorTest` (15), end to end through the SDK handlers and pipeline to `RecordingHttpClients`:
  - awsJson1_0: an operation error with an absolute `__type`. The contents are a `StructDocument` with discriminator `test.errors.json#RecordInvalid`, members in order, an integer, a nested structure with its discriminator and a timestamp, and a list. `sdkSchema()` is the operation's cached error schema. Status code, request ID, extended request ID, error code, message, service name, HTTP response, raw body and the exact `getMessage()`.
  - A service error from a relative `X-Amzn-Errortype` with a `:` URI suffix, and one from a relative `__type` on another operation.
  - An error both levels list: one entry, the same schema instance from both operations, in `errorSchemas()` once.
  - Plain `AwsServiceException` (exact class) for an error of another operation, an unknown `__type` and a body with no discriminator, each with status, request ID, code and message.
  - A `@retryable` error: retried, still a `DocumentException` after `toBuilder`, with its contents and attempt count.
  - Async: the future fails with an `ExecutionException` whose cause is the `DocumentException`, or the plain `AwsServiceException` for an unknown code.
  - restJson1, sync and async: header-bound and `jsonName` members, a 404 status, a service error from an absolute `__type`, and a plain exception for an unknown body `code`.
  - awsQuery: the shared custom code resolves to the operation's error on `Publish` and to the service error on `Describe`; an unknown code gives a plain exception.
- `DocumentExceptionTest` (8): builder values and `getMessage()`; the message fallback to `message` and `Message` members; `toBuilder` keeps the class, contents and values, and setting a member keeps the others; retryable and throttling from the trait, and 429; `Document.of(e)` and `getMemberValue`; non-error schemas rejected (exact messages); Java serialization.
- Changed: `DynamicOperationTest` checks the registry (an `ErrorRegistry`, both levels, `DocumentException` class, a new builder per call, `Ping` without the errors); `DynamicClientCallTest`'s async 400 test now expects a `DocumentException` cause.
- `schema` `ErrorRegistryTest` (10, was 9): `putType` with a code, no builder created by `contains`/`getShapeClass`, a new builder per call, `getSchema` null, null arguments.

**Runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, from a new `/tmp/aws-sdk-139-wt` worktree of `49c52633602` with the changes copied in):
- `install -pl :build-tools`, then `clean install -pl :dynamic-client --am -P quick` once.
- `clean install -pl :schema`: 675 tests, 0 failures; Checkstyle and SpotBugs clean; japicmp skips the module.
- `clean install -pl :dynamic-client`: 263 tests, 0 failures; 0 Checkstyle violations, 0 SpotBugs bugs (the first run flagged `SE_TRANSIENT_FIELD_NOT_RESTORED` on a transient service ShapeId, now a normal field), Javadoc and `dependency:analyze` clean; japicmp skips the module.
- `clean test -pl :architecture-tests`: 79 tests, 0 failures, 11 skipped (the disabled `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest` methods). After the last main-code change, `PortComplianceTest`, `InternalApiBoundaryTest`, `PackageContainmentTest` and `DependencyTreeTest` (23 tests) were run again, with 0 failures.
- `aws-core`, `sdk-core`, the protocol modules and `codegen` did not change.

**Open questions.**
- **`compose` versus one registry.** The task names `compose(operationErrors, serviceErrors)`. The port builds one `ErrorRegistry` with the same first-wins lookups, because a composed registry loses the `awsQueryError` index. Is that acceptable, or should `schema` get an `ErrorRegistry.compose` that merges the indexes?
- **Message member in XML contents.** Should the dynamic client copy the protocol's message into the contents' `message`/`Message` member when the body read leaves it unset, so `getContents()` shows it for query and restXml errors? Generated exceptions do not need it (their message is the exception message).
- **`serializableBuilderClass`.** `DocumentException` inherits `AwsServiceException.serializableBuilderClass()`. Its builder needs a schema, so it cannot be created by reflection. Hide the static method with one that throws, or leave it as is?
- **Error schemas for unlisted errors.** An error that the service returns but that neither the operation nor the service lists is a plain `AwsServiceException` (Requirement 29.6). Generated clients resolve every error in the service closure (Requirement 23.7). Should the dynamic client match that by registering every `@error` shape in the service closure after the listed ones?

## Dynamic client shared pipeline features (task 13.10)

**Sources.** Not a port. The reference is what a generated client on the schema route passes to the client handler: codegen's `SchemaOperationsProtocolSpec.executionParams`, `ClientClassUtils.addEndpointTraitCode`, `HttpChecksumTrait`, `HttpChecksumRequiredTrait`, `RequestCompressionTrait`, `ProtocolSpec.credentialType`, the generated constructor (`SDK_CLIENT`, `API_METADATA`), `EndpointResolverUtilsSpec` (`hostPrefix`, `authSchemeWithEndpointSignerProperties`, `setMetricValues`), the builder's checksum options (`BaseClientBuilderClass`), and the generated sync and async paginators. The generated `DefaultCloudWatchClient` under `services/cloudwatch/target/generated-sources` was read for the result.

**Target.** `core/dynamic-client`:
- `internal.DynamicOperationMetadata` (`@SdkInternalApi`): the per-operation call settings, derived once per `DynamicOperation` from its schemas and runtime traits only (Requirement 30.4), and `apply(ClientExecutionParams, SerializableStruct)`.
- `internal.DynamicPagination` (`@SdkInternalApi`): the page-to-page step of the paginators.
- `internal.DynamicCallResolvers`: the endpoint resolver now does what a generated `resolveEndpoint` does after the provider returns.
- `DynamicClient`: the call configurations with `SDK_CLIENT` and `API_METADATA`, the new `ClientExecutionParams` settings, `paginate`/`paginateAsync` (public), and the builder's checksum options.
- `DynamicOperation.paginated()` (public): the operation's `@paginated` merged with the service's.

**Pipeline features now wired.** Every dynamic call still goes through `AwsSyncClientHandler`/`AwsAsyncClientHandler` with `withOperation(dynamicOperation)` (task 13.7), so it runs on `SyncApiCallPipeline`/`AsyncApiCallPipeline`. 13.10 adds the rest of the generated operation method's parameters, so that only the `ApiOperation` differs:

| Feature | Generated client | Dynamic client |
|---|---|---|
| Protocol metadata | `withProtocolMetadata(AwsProtocolMetadata(<model protocol>))` | The same `AwsProtocolMetadata`, for the protocol of the call's configuration (`CLIENT_PROTOCOL.id()`: rpcv2Cbor → `SMITHY_RPC_V2_CBOR`, both awsJson → `AWS_JSON`, restJson1 → `REST_JSON`, restXml → `REST_XML`, awsQuery → `QUERY`, ec2Query → `EC2`); null for a third-party protocol |
| `SDK_CLIENT`, `API_METADATA` | Constructor: `clientConfiguration.toBuilder().option(SDK_CLIENT, this).option(API_METADATA, "<SDK ID>#<major>.<minor>.x")`; the handler gets the configuration without them | The same, once per configuration (sync and async), with `VersionInfo.SDK_VERSION` converted as codegen's `VersionUtils.convertToMajorMinorX`. The handlers own and close the configurations without them, so `close()` does not reach the client (the task 13.4 recursion). Setting `API_METADATA` re-derives `CLIENT_USER_AGENT`, so the user agent gets `api/<SDK_ID>#2.49.x-SNAPSHOT`. `serviceClientConfiguration()` reads the call configuration, as generated clients do |
| Host prefix | `hostPrefixExpression(resolvedHostExpression)` computed in the method (labels validated with `HostnameValidator`), and `resolveEndpoint` adds `<Svc>EndpointResolverUtils.hostPrefix` with `AwsEndpointProviderUtils.addHostPrefix` unless host prefix injection is disabled | Both, from the operation schema's `EndpointTrait` and the input members its labels name; the same validator and messages (the "object" is the input shape name with a lower-case first letter, `putLabeledInput`, where codegen uses the request variable name). The metadata reaches the endpoint resolver through a private execution attribute (`DynamicOperationMetadata.METADATA`) |
| Endpoint auth schemes | `authSchemeWithEndpointSignerProperties`: for the entry with the selected scheme ID, copies signing name, signing region (sigv4) or region set (sigv4a) and double URL encoding to the selected auth scheme | Same, for `SigV4AuthScheme` and `SigV4aAuthScheme`; any other matching entry throws the generated `IllegalArgumentException` message. See the sigv4a row below |
| Endpoint metric values | `setMetricValues` adds `METRIC_VALUES` to `BUSINESS_METRICS` | Same |
| Endpoint override metric | `ruleParams` binds `SDK::Endpoint` through `AwsEndpointProviderUtils.endpointBuiltIn`, which records `ENDPOINT_OVERRIDE` when the endpoint is overridden | The resolver records it whenever the client endpoint is overridden. The dynamic default provider never binds `SDK::Endpoint` (task 13.5), so the metric would otherwise be missing |
| Credential type | `credentialType(TOKEN)` when bearer is the operation's preferred auth (`AuthUtils.isOpBearerAuthPreferred`) | `TOKEN` when the first effective auth scheme is `smithy.api#httpBearerAuth` |
| `HTTP_CHECKSUM_REQUIRED` | `HttpChecksumRequired.create()` for `@httpChecksumRequired` | Same, from `HttpChecksumRequiredTrait` |
| `HTTP_CHECKSUM` | `requestChecksumRequired`, `isRequestStreaming` (input has a streaming member), `requestAlgorithm(<input member>)` and `requestAlgorithmHeader(<its httpHeader>)`, `requestValidationMode(<input member>)` and `responseAlgorithmsV2` sorted fastest first | Same, from `HttpChecksumTrait` and the input schema (the header is the member's `HttpHeaderTrait`); the per-call members are read with `getMemberValue`. Codegen rejects a response algorithm the SDK does not implement at generation time; the dynamic client skips it with a DEBUG log, because a runtime model may name a newer one. Codegen also rejects a request algorithm member that is not a header; the dynamic client leaves the header null |
| `REQUEST_COMPRESSION` | `RequestCompression.builder().encodings(...).isStreaming(<streaming input>)` | Same, from `RequestCompressionTrait` |
| `REQUEST_CHECKSUM_CALCULATION`, `RESPONSE_CHECKSUM_VALIDATION` | Builder: `lazyOption` with `RequestChecksumCalculationResolver`/`ResponseChecksumValidationResolver` (profile file and system setting, default `WHEN_SUPPORTED`), only when an operation has a request algorithm member or requires a request checksum, or lists response algorithms | Same condition, read from the converted operation schemas at build, with `lazyOptionIfAbsent`. The generated builder methods `requestChecksumCalculation`/`responseChecksumValidation` are not added (open question) |
| Idempotency tokens | The schema pipeline's `IdempotencyTokens` (an `InterceptingSerializer` view of the input) fills an unset `@idempotencyToken` member for any `ApiOperation` | Unchanged: dynamic calls already got it (task 13.7 note); now tested. Interceptors see the input without the token, as for generated clients on the schema route |
| Auth | `effectiveAuthSchemes` and the model resolver (task 13.5) | Unchanged |

`DynamicOperationMetadata` is created in the `DynamicOperation` constructor, so it is created once per operation and cached with it (Requirement 28.9). Its per-call work is reading the label and checksum members from the input.

| Source or design | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Design A.18: `withOperation` derives `HTTP_CHECKSUM`, `HTTP_CHECKSUM_REQUIRED`, `REQUEST_COMPRESSION` and the host prefix from schemas once per `ApiOperation` through extensions, so the generated `putExecutionAttribute` calls are no longer needed | Not done in `sdk-core`. Generated clients keep their generated calls, and the dynamic client derives the same values in `dynamic-client` and puts them on the parameters as a generated method does | `HTTP_CHECKSUM` and the host prefix need input values, which `withOperation` does not have (generated clients call `withInput` after it). Moving the derivation into `sdk-core` would change the generated clients' call path and public `sdk-core` API, outside this task. `RequestCompression`, `AwsProtocolMetadata` and `AwsServiceProtocol` are internal-package classes, but `@SdkProtectedApi` and in `InternalApiBoundaryTest`'s generated-code allowlist, so `dynamic-client` may use them. No `sdk-core`, `aws-core` or `schema` change |
| Design C.4: auth "from `effectiveAuthSchemes`" | Unchanged from task 13.5 | Covered there |
| Generated sigv4a endpoint scheme (multi-auth sigv4a services): the endpoint's region set is not applied when the selected sigv4a option already has `REGION_SET` | The endpoint's region set replaces the option's, unless `AWS_SIGV4A_SIGNING_REGION_SET` is configured | The dynamic resolver (task 13.5) always puts a `REGION_SET`, falling back to the region, so the generated condition would never apply the endpoint's set. A configured set still wins |
| `IS_NONE_AUTH_TYPE_REQUEST`, `withLongPolling`, `discoveredEndpoint` | Not set | The schema-route spec does not set `IS_NONE_AUTH_TYPE_REQUEST` either; long polling and endpoint discovery are C2J customizations with no Smithy trait |

**Pagination (user decision: implement it).** Design C.4 lists "pagination helpers, from `PaginatedTrait`" without an API, so the API follows the generated paginator methods:
- `DynamicOperation.paginated()` returns `Optional<PaginatedTrait>`: the operation's trait merged with the service's, property by property, as `PaginatedIndex` and codegen's `PaginatorTraitTranslator` merge them. An operation without the trait is not paginated.
- `DynamicClient.paginate(op, input[, override])` returns an `SdkIterable<Document>` of output documents (pages). It uses sdk-core's `PaginatedResponsesIterator` and `SyncPageFetcher`, as generated paginators do: lazy calls, a new iterator restarts from the first page, and there is a next page when `PaginatorUtils.isOutputTokenAvailable` says so. The next input is the first input with the output token put into the input token member, coerced again.
- `paginateAsync(op, input[, override])` returns an `SdkPublisher<Document>` over `ResponsesSubscription` and an `AsyncPageFetcher`, as the generated publishers do. A failed call ends the subscription with `onError`.
- The output token may be a dot-separated member path (`meta.next`), as Smithy allows.
- The first request gets the paginator's `sdk-metrics`/`C` API name (`BusinessMetricFeatureId.PAGINATOR`), as `UserAgentUtils.applyPaginatorUserAgent` adds it.

| Generated paginators | Dynamic paginators | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Generated only when the paginator has an input and an output token (`hasAllRequiredFields`) | `paginate` and `paginateAsync` throw `IllegalArgumentException("Operation ns#Op is not paginated: it needs a smithy.api#paginated trait with an inputToken and an outputToken, ...")` for any other operation | Same set of operations, as far as `@paginated` can express it (below) |
| The paginator method validates nothing; errors come from the calls | Both forms resolve the operation, check the trait and coerce the input when called, and throw before any request | Programmer errors fail fast; failures of the calls themselves are thrown when the iteration reaches them (sync) or sent to `onError` (async) |
| `items()` flattens the result key | No items helper: each page is the output document, and `paginated()` names the `items` path | YAGNI; the caller reads `page.getMember(items)` |
| Several input or output tokens, several result keys (C2J `paginators-1.json`) | Not supported: `@paginated` has one token of each kind and one `items` path | Route 53's `ListResourceRecordSets` (three tokens) has a generated paginator but no `@paginated` trait in the converted model, so `paginate` refuses it. CloudWatch's `DescribeAlarms` has two result keys and therefore no `items` |

**Auxiliary operations (user decision: document only).** Design C.4 says the S3 Express `createSession` pattern (a required constructor argument such as `Function<SerializableStruct, CompletableFuture<SerializableStruct>> createSession`, failing with `NullPointerException("createSession")`) "is documented but not prototyped, because S3 is not in the prototype set". No feature in the prototype set needs an auxiliary operation, so no class takes one and Requirements 30.5 and 30.8 have no code or test here. A feature that needs one would take the function in its constructor, check it with `Validate.paramNotNull(createSession, "createSession")` before any client is built with it, and be given either a generated client's method or `op -> dynamicClient.callAsync("CreateSession", ...)`, so it needs neither a generated client nor a dependency on one.

**Metadata parity (Requirement 30.1, without the optional Property 40).** A `dynamic-client` test dependency on a service module is cyclic (task 13.5), so the comparison is a `codegen` test, `DynamicOperationParityTest`, which compiles the generated model package in process as `SchemaConverterEquivalenceTest` does. For CloudWatch, Secrets Manager, SQS, SNS and Route 53 (209 operations), every `<Svc>Operations` field is compared with `DynamicClient.getOperation` on the same Smithy model:
- equal: operation name and ShapeId, ordered effective auth scheme IDs, the operation schema's full trait set (so `endpoint`, `requestCompression`, `httpChecksum`, `httpChecksumRequired` and `paginated` match), the idempotency token member, the input and output stream members, and `DynamicOperationMetadata` derived from both operations;
- equal against the intermediate model the generated method is generated from: request compression encodings and streaming, `httpChecksumRequired`, the host prefix, the bearer credential type, and the paginator tokens, items and page size.

Expected differences, which the test allows and pins down:

| Difference | Where | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| Input or output ShapeId `smithy.api#Unit` (dynamic) against a structure of the operation's own with the `unitType` trait and no members (generated) | Every operation without input or output, for example CloudWatch `DeleteAlarmMuteRule` output | Codegen synthesizes a request or response class for a unit and keeps its ShapeId. The dynamic client reads the model, which targets `smithy.api#Unit`; output documents keep the `smithy.api#Unit` discriminator (Requirement 28.7). See the open questions |
| Error ShapeIds: the dynamic operation lists every error shape; the generated one lists the shape that owns each generated exception class (`SchemaCodegen.errorShapeOwners`) and omits shapes without a class | CloudWatch (`ResourceNotFound` → `ResourceNotFoundException`, `LimitExceededException` → `LimitExceededFault`); Route 53 `HealthCheckInUse` (no class) | Shared exception classes on the generated side (see `PrototypeErrorRegistryTest`). Compared after mapping through the owners |
| No `items` | CloudWatch `DescribeAlarms` (two result keys) | C2J paginator not expressible as `@paginated` |
| No `pageSize` | Route 53 operations whose limit key is a string, such as `ListCidrBlocks` | `@paginated` `pageSize` must target an integer |
| No paginator | Route 53 `ListResourceRecordSets` | Three tokens; see pagination |

None of the prototype services has an `httpChecksum` trait or an `endpoint` host prefix, so those are compared only as absent there and covered by the unit tests below.

**`pom.xml`.**
- New compile dependencies: `checksums` and `checksums-spi` (the response algorithms), and `reactive-streams` (moved from test scope; `paginateAsync` returns a `Publisher`).
- `http-client-protocol` is removed with its `ignoredUnusedDeclaredDependencies` entry. No dynamic-client code uses it (the protocols come through the six protocol modules, which depend on it). A later task that uses it, such as streaming (13.13), can declare it again.

**Tests** (36 new; `dynamic-client` now has 299, JUnit 5 and AssertJ). New fixture `pipeline-features.smithy` (restJson1 and sigv4, `sdkId: "Pipeline Features"`): a labeled and a static `@endpoint` host prefix, an `@idempotencyToken` member, `@requestCompression(gzip)`, `@httpChecksum` with request algorithm and validation mode members and three response algorithms, `@httpChecksumRequired`, a service `@paginated` that two operations inherit (one with a nested output token path), and an operation without traits. `RecordingHttpClients` now answers with a queue of stubs (the last one repeats) and records body bytes.
- `DynamicClientPipelineFeaturesTest` (20), end to end through the SDK handlers and pipelines:
  - Host prefix: labeled (sync and async) and static; an invalid label, a missing label and an empty label (async) fail with the `HostnameValidator` messages before any interceptor hook or request; `DISABLE_HOST_PREFIX_INJECTION` keeps the host.
  - Endpoint auth schemes: a configured `DynamicEndpointProvider` returns an endpoint with a sigv4 auth scheme (`othername`, `eu-central-1`) and a metric value; the request is signed with `/eu-central-1/othername/aws4_request`, gets the host prefix on the endpoint host, and the user agent carries the metric. Without endpoint auth schemes, the scope is the model's `/us-west-2/features/`.
  - Idempotency token: filled from `IdempotentUtils`' generator when absent (sync and async), kept when set.
  - Compression: a 20,000-character body is gzipped with `Content-Encoding: gzip` (sync and async, decompressed and compared); a small body and an operation without the trait are not compressed.
  - Checksums: `SHA256` as request algorithm gives `x-amz-checksum-sha256` equal to the SHA-256 of the sent body; no algorithm on a `requestChecksumRequired` operation gives `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC32` and the body's CRC32 (sync and async); `@httpChecksumRequired` gives the body's CRC32 (the SRA path's default algorithm, not `Content-MD5`); no checksum headers without the traits; response validation with `checksumMode: ENABLED` passes on a matching `x-amz-checksum-crc32` (sync and async, `HTTP_RESPONSE_CHECKSUM_VALIDATION` is `VALIDATED`), fails with "Data read has a different checksum than expected" on a mismatch, and is skipped without the mode.
  - Interceptors see `SDK_CLIENT` (the dynamic client) and `PROTOCOL_METADATA` (`rest-json`) on sync and async calls; the user agent has `api/Pipeline_Features#<major>.<minor>.x`; `serviceClientConfiguration()` still works; `close()` twice after calls returns.
- `DynamicClientPaginationTest` (9): merged traits; three pages with the token, the other input members and the paginator metric in the user agent, and no request before iterating; a new iterator restarts; an empty token ends paging; a nested output token path; a failed second call throws during iteration; `paginateAsync` publishes three pages and ends with `onError` on a failed call; non-paginated and unknown operations and invalid inputs throw before sending (exact messages).
- `DynamicOperationMetadataTest` (7): compression, `HTTP_CHECKSUM` with and without the input members (response algorithms sorted CRC32C, CRC32, SHA256; set whenever the trait names a validation mode member, as generated), `httpChecksumRequired`, host prefixes, `apply` on fresh parameters, the bearer credential type on an inline model whose first scheme is `httpBearerAuth`, and one instance per operation.
- `codegen`: `DynamicOperationParityTest` (5 services, above).
- The optional property tests 13.11 and 13.12 are not written.

**Runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, from a new `/tmp/aws-sdk-1310-wt` worktree of `e14a4bf6c8a` with the changes copied in):
- `clean install -pl :dynamic-client`: 299 tests, 0 failures. Checkstyle (one `PluralEnumNames` violation on the first class name, fixed by the rename to `DynamicOperationMetadata`), SpotBugs (0 bugs), Javadoc and `dependency:analyze` are clean; japicmp skips the module.
- `clean test -pl :architecture-tests`: 79 tests, 0 failures, 11 skipped (the disabled `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest` methods). `InternalApiBoundaryTest` passes: the internal-package classes used are allowlisted.
- `clean test -pl :codegen -Dtest=DynamicOperationParityTest,SchemaConverterEquivalenceTest,DefaultTraitInclusionSpecTest,ProtocolUtilsTest,PrototypeErrorRegistryTest`: 44 tests, 0 failures. No codegen main code changed.
- `sdk-core`, `aws-core`, `schema` and `build-tools` did not change, so their tests and japicmp were not rerun.

**Open questions.**
- **Unit inputs and outputs.** Requirement 30.1 asks for equal input and output ShapeIds. For operations without input or output, codegen's operation has its synthesized `<Op>Request`/`<Op>Response` ShapeId and the dynamic operation has `smithy.api#Unit`. Keep `smithy.api#Unit` (the model's ShapeId), or have the dynamic client synthesize the same structures?
- **Error lists.** Should the dynamic operation list error owners as the generated one does, or is the model's error list (which the error registry also uses) the right one, with Requirement 30.1 read as "after the class mapping"?
- **Where the per-operation attributes live.** Design A.18 puts the derivation in `withOperation`. Moving `DynamicOperationMetadata` into `sdk-core` (fed by the input at execution time) would let generated clients drop their generated `putExecutionAttribute` calls. Do it in a later pipeline task?
- **Checksum builder options.** The generated builders of services with flexible checksums have `requestChecksumCalculation` and `responseChecksumValidation`. The dynamic builder resolves both from the profile and system settings only. Add the two builder methods?
- **C2J-only paginators.** Multi-token paginators and several result keys exist only in `paginators-1.json`. Should the C2J_Converter record them (for example as a `com.amazonaws.c2j` trait the dynamic client could read), or is `@paginated` the limit?
- **Paginator items.** Add `items` helpers (`SdkIterable<Document> items()` on a returned paginator type), as generated paginators have? That needs a public paginator type instead of plain `SdkIterable`/`SdkPublisher`.

## Dynamic client streaming (task 13.13)

**Status.** Streaming blobs are done (Requirements 31.1, 31.4 and the blob half of 31.6). Event streams have the API shape and the fail-before-sending checks only (31.6); Requirements 31.2, 31.3 (event frames) and 31.5 are **not met**, because the schema-based pipeline cannot run an event-stream call (task 7.20 stage (c), deferred by the user). See "Event streams" below.

**Sources.** smithy-java (commit `142eecf4d`) `io` `DataStream` (the contract), `core` `DataStreamDocument`, `EventStreamDocument` and `Document.of(Schema, DataStream)`/`of(Schema, EventStream)`/`asDataStream()`/`asEventStream()`. smithy-java's dynamic client holds the streams in the input document and has the transport read them; the SDK binds them through the client handler instead, as its generated clients do (task 7.19), so the call side is SDK code.

**Target.**
- `core/schema`: `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.io.DataStream` (`@SdkPublicApi`, new package); `Document.asDataStream()`, `Document.asEventStream()`, `Document.of(Schema, DataStream)`, `Document.ofEventStream(Schema, Publisher<Document>)`; the package-private `DataStreamDocument` and `EventStreamDocument` (extending `Documents.AbstractDocument`). `Documents.documentEquals`/`hash` compare and hash stream documents by stream identity, and `typeMismatch` picks "an" for any vowel ("Expected an event stream document"; existing messages are unchanged).
- `core/sdk-core`: `SchemaSerdeSupport.dataStream(RequestBody)`, `dataStream(AsyncRequestBody)`, `requestBody(DataStream)` and `asyncRequestBody(DataStream)` (the last two return `Optional`s); `core.schema.internal.RequestBodyDataStream` and `AsyncRequestBodyDataStream` (`@SdkInternalApi`).
- `core/dynamic-client`: `DynamicStreams` (`@SdkPublicApi`: `dataStream(RequestBody)`, `dataStream(AsyncRequestBody)`, `eventStream(Publisher<Document>)`); `DynamicClient.call(op, input, ResponseTransformer<Document, R>)`, `call(op, input, override, ResponseTransformer)`, `callAsync(op, input, AsyncResponseTransformer<Document, R>)` and `callAsync(op, input, override, AsyncResponseTransformer)`; `internal.DynamicStreaming` (the checks, the body extraction and the transformer adapters). `StructDocument`, `StrictDocumentCoercion` and `SchemaGuidedDocumentBuilder.setMemberValue` accept stream documents at streaming members (the task 13.1 rejection is lifted there).

**Blob streaming.**
- **Input (31.1).** A streaming blob member holds a data stream document. `DynamicStreams.dataStream(body)` is `Document.of(PreludeSchemas.BLOB, SchemaSerdeSupport.dataStream(body))`, and coercion rewraps it with the member's schema without reading it. Before coercion, the call checks the member; after it, `DynamicStreaming` unwraps the original `RequestBody` (sync) or `AsyncRequestBody` (async) and passes it with `withRequestBody`/`withAsyncRequestBody`, as `SchemaOperationsProtocolSpec` does for generated clients. `StreamingRequestBodies` (task 7.19) then binds it: the caller's `ContentStreamProvider` becomes the request content (sync), or the transport sends the async body; `Content-Type` (header member, `@mediaType`, then the body's), `Content-Length`, `@requiresLength` and the chunked encoding of `@unsignedPayload` come from there. `StructDocument.serializeMembers` skips streaming members and `getMemberValue` returns null for them, as a generated class does, so the protocol writes no body for the member. Nothing in the dynamic client buffers or reads the body; with sigv4 and a signed payload the signer hashes it before sending, as for generated clients.
- **Output (31.4).** `call(op, input, ResponseTransformer<Document, R>)` adapts the caller's transformer to `ResponseTransformer<DynamicResponse, R>` (`transform(response.document(), body)`, `needsConnectionLeftOpen` and `name` forwarded) and calls `syncClientHandler.execute(params.withResponseTransformer(t), t)`, so `ProtocolResponseHandling.handleStreaming` (task 3.26) hands it the output document (header, prefix-header and status members) and the unread body. The async form wraps the adapter with `AsyncResponseTransformerUtils.wrapWithEndOfStreamFuture`, calls `asyncClientHandler.execute(params.withAsyncResponseTransformer(t), t)`, calls `exceptionOccurred` on failure (a failure of the callback is logged at ERROR and ignored, as `runAndLogError` does) and publishes metrics at the end of the stream, as the generated async streaming method does. Error responses never reach the transformer (task 7.19).
- **Checks (31.6), before coercion, so no interceptor runs and nothing is sent.** All throw `IllegalArgumentException` naming the operation and the member and ending with "No request was sent." (`callAsync` returns a failed future):
  - a required streaming member that is absent;
  - a value that is not a data stream document (for example `Document.of(byte[])` or a string), named by document type only, never by value;
  - a data stream of the other call form: `call` needs one from a `RequestBody`, `callAsync` one from an `AsyncRequestBody`;
  - an operation with a streaming blob output called without a transformer, or a transformer passed for an operation without one.
  An optional (`@default`) streaming member that is absent sends no body.
- **RPC protocols.** A streaming blob on awsJson, rpcv2Cbor or the query protocols is rejected by the protocol, as in task 7.19: `SdkClientException("Unable to marshall ...: Streaming blob input is not supported by aws.protocols#awsJson1_0")` during marshalling, before sending.

**Event streams: what is done and what is missing.**
- Done: `Document.ofEventStream`/`asEventStream` and `DynamicStreams.eventStream(Publisher<Document>)`; coercion keeps an event stream document at an event stream member (and rejects anything else, naming the member); a sync `call` of an operation with an event stream input or output member throws `IllegalArgumentException` (31.6); an async call checks the input event stream member (required and present, an event stream document) and then fails with `UnsupportedOperationException("The dynamic client does not support event streams yet: operation <op> has the event stream member <member>. No request was sent.")`. Every case is checked before any interceptor runs.
- Missing (task 7.20 stage (c), deferred by the user): the schema-based pipeline (`ProtocolMarshalling`, `StreamingRequestBodies`, `AsyncApiCallPipeline`, `AwsAsyncClientHandler`) has no event-stream path. Nothing turns a `Publisher` of events into a request body encoded with `HttpClientProtocol.inputEventEncoderFactory(operation)` (after the RPC `initial-request` frame) and signed per event by the existing event stream signing, and nothing decodes the response body with `outputEventDecoderFactory(operation)`, reads `initial-response`, maps exception and error frames, and publishes the events (legacy does this with generated marshallers, `EventStreamAsyncResponseTransformer` and generated response handlers, none of which exist for a `DynamicOperation`). The stage (b) encoders, decoders and protocol requests exist and are unit tested, but no client calls them. Requirements 31.2, 31.3 (event frames, signing, backpressure) and 31.5 therefore cannot be met by this task without building stage (c).
- Options: (A) implement 7.20 stage (c) in `sdk-core`/`aws-core` for generated and dynamic clients alike; the dynamic client then needs only the conversions (events as `StructDocument`s of the union, built with the converter's builders) and the async wiring. (B) A dynamic-client-only transport that drives the stage (b) factories itself: it duplicates stage (c), bypasses the shared pipeline that Requirement 31.3 asks for, and was not built. (C) Keep the current behavior and defer 31.2, 31.3 (event part) and 31.5 with 7.20 (c)–(e). This task leaves (C) in place.

| Source or design | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Design A.13: `DataStream` "ported with `Publisher<ByteBuffer>`", factories `ofEmpty`, `ofBytes`, `ofByteBuffer`, `ofString`, `ofInputStream`, `ofFile`, `ofPublisher`; smithy-java's `asInputStream`, `asByteBuffer`, `writeTo`, `asChannel`, `discard`, `isAvailable`, `hasByteBuffer`, `close` | The contract only: `contentLength`, `hasKnownLength`, `contentType`, `isReplayable` and the `Publisher`. No factories, no blocking views | Requirement 31.1 takes a `RequestBody`/`AsyncRequestBody`, which already have every factory, and task 7.19 decided that SDK model classes do not hold streams, so nothing would consume the extra API. The interface can gain them later as default or static methods |
| Design A.13 "the default `subscribe` uses an SDK `InputStream`-to-publisher adapter" | `RequestBodyDataStream.subscribe` opens a new stream of the body's provider per subscription and publishes 16 KiB chunks through the SDK's `IterablePublisher` (blocking reads on the requesting thread), closing the stream at the end, on a read failure and on cancel. `AsyncRequestBodyDataStream` delegates to the body | No new publisher from scratch (reactive streams guidelines); `RequestBodyDataStreamTckTest` runs the TCK. The dynamic client never subscribes: the pipeline sends the original body |
| `isReplayable()` | True for both adapters | The SDK's retries and signers already re-read request bodies (`newStream()` per attempt, resubscribing an async body) |
| `Document.of(Schema, EventStream<?>)`, `asEventStream()` returns `EventStream<? extends SerializableStruct>` | `Document.ofEventStream(Schema, Publisher<Document>)`, `asEventStream()` returns `Publisher<Document>` | `EventStream` is not ported (task 7.20); design C.4 gives the dynamic client `Publisher<Document>`. The distinct name avoids two `of` overloads whose second parameters are both publishers |
| `DataStreamDocument`/`EventStreamDocument` are records (equality by schema and stream) | Final classes; equal when they hold the same stream instance, whatever the schema; hash from the stream's identity | The task 11.1 rule (schemas are not compared) and no readable value to compare. A blob document never equals a data stream document |
| `serialize`/`serializeContents` call `writeDataStream`/`writeEventStream` (no-op defaults) | `serializeContents` throws `SerializationException`; `ShapeSerializer` gets no `writeDataStream`/`writeEventStream` | No SDK serializer writes streams: the pipeline binds them. A silent no-op would let a codec write a member name with no value |
| `asBlob()` / `asMap()` of a stream document: the `Document` defaults | Throw `SerializationException` saying to use `asDataStream()`/`asEventStream()` | The default message would read "Expected a blob document, but found blob" |
| Design C.4 "streaming blobs are `DataStream` Documents, created with `Document.of(schema, DataStream)` from a `RequestBody`/`AsyncRequestBody`" (through `SchemaSerdeSupport.dataStream`) | Also `DynamicStreams.dataStream(RequestBody)`/`dataStream(AsyncRequestBody)`, public, in `dynamic-client` | `SchemaSerdeSupport` is `@SdkProtectedApi`, so callers of the public dynamic client need a public factory |
| Design C.4 "`call(op, input, transformer)` overloads" | `call(String, Document, ResponseTransformer<Document, R>)` and a four-argument form with the override configuration before the transformer; the async counterparts with `AsyncResponseTransformer<Document, R>` | The transformer's response type is the output document, as `call` returns documents (Requirement 31.4: "along with the non-streaming output members as a schema-backed Document"). `call(op, input, null)` is now ambiguous at compile time; the client's own calls cast the null |
| Requirement 31.6: "an exception" | `IllegalArgumentException` (design error table: "Stream misuse, `IllegalArgumentException` before sending, operation and member") for misuse; `UnsupportedOperationException` for a valid async event-stream call | The second is not misuse: the feature is missing |
| — (not in the requirements) | A streaming blob output called without a transformer, or a transformer for a non-streaming output, throws before sending | Without the transformer, the body would be discarded unread; generated clients have no such method either |
| Task 13.1: setting or converting a streaming member throws `UnsupportedOperationException` | Members take stream documents (any other value: `SerializationException("Cannot coerce member <id>: a streaming blob member needs a data stream document, but found a blob document. Create one with DynamicStreams...")`, the same in `StructDocument.of` and the strict coercion). Reading a streaming member with a deserializer still throws `UnsupportedOperationException("Streaming member <id> (<type>) cannot be read by a deserializer")` | Codecs never deliver streams; the transformer gets the body. The event stream union itself (not a member) converts as an ordinary union, so one event can be built with `StructDocument.of(unionSchema, ...)` |

**Tests.**
- `core/schema` `StreamDocumentTest` (5): types, accessors, `asObject`, `toString`, the throwing `asBlob`/`asMap`/`serializeContents`, identity equality and hash codes (also inside map documents and against blob documents in both directions), the `asDataStream`/`asEventStream` defaults on other documents, null arguments.
- `core/sdk-core` `SchemaSerdeSupportDataStreamTest` (8): both adapters keep length (negative when unknown) and content type, read nothing, and unwrap to the same body; each subscription of a sync body publishes a fresh stream (40 KiB, three chunks); cancel and a read failure close the stream; null arguments. `RequestBodyDataStreamTckTest` (TCK `PublisherVerification`, 38 tests, 9 skipped by the TCK as optional or not applicable).
- `core/dynamic-client` `DynamicClientStreamingTest` (16), new fixtures `streaming.smithy` (restJson1 and sigv4: `@httpPayload` streaming uploads with a label and a header, an `@unsignedPayload` upload, an optional upload, a flexible-checksum upload, a download with a header member, event stream input and output operations, a plain operation) and `streaming-json10.smithy` (awsJson1_0 with a streaming blob input), through `RecordingHttpClients`:
  - sync and async uploads: exact bytes on the wire, method, path, `Content-Length`, `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`, the header member, a sigv4 `Authorization` header; interceptors see a blob-typed stream document;
  - bodies without a length on the unsigned operation: `Transfer-Encoding: chunked`, no `Content-Length`, the body's `text/plain`, sync and async;
  - flexible checksum with `SHA256`: `x-amz-checksum-sha256` is the base64 SHA-256 of the streamed bytes and `x-amz-content-sha256` its hex form (the signed payload), both signed, the body sent unchanged with its length, sync and async;
  - an absent optional streaming member sends an empty body;
  - downloads through `ResponseTransformer.toBytes()` and `AsyncResponseTransformer.toBytes()`: the bytes, and an output document with the header member and no stream member;
  - a 404 on a streaming download throws (sync) or fails the future (async) without the transformer seeing a response or body; the async transformer gets `exceptionOccurred`;
  - fail-before-sending, with no request and no `beforeExecution` hook: a missing required stream (sync and async), a blob and a string for a stream, a data stream of the other call form (both ways), a streaming output without a transformer (sync and async), a transformer on a non-streaming operation (sync, and async with `exceptionOccurred`), sync event-stream calls (input and output), async event-stream calls (`UnsupportedOperationException`), a missing and a mistyped async event stream input;
  - `createStruct` keeps both stream documents and rejects a map for an event stream member;
  - awsJson1_0 rejects the streaming blob while marshalling, before sending.
- `StructDocumentTest`: the task 13.1 rejection test now checks the new message, plus a data stream member kept with the member schema, not written by `serializeMembers` and null from `getMemberValue`.
- Not covered: Requirement 31.1's "before the stream has been fully read" and 31.4's "before the whole body is read" are properties of the shared pipeline that task 7.19 tested; the recording clients read whole bodies, so these tests do not show them.

**Runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, from a new `/tmp/aws-sdk-1313-wt` worktree of `ae13ae5dd8c` with the changes copied in; `build-tools` installed first, then `install -P quick -pl :dynamic-client --am`):
- `clean install -pl :schema`: 680 tests, 0 failures; Checkstyle, SpotBugs and japicmp clean.
- `install -pl :sdk-core -Dtest=SchemaSerdeSupportDataStreamTest,SchemaSerdeSupportTest,RequestBodyDataStreamTckTest`: 15 JUnit tests, 0 failures; TCK 38 tests, 0 failures, 9 skipped; Checkstyle, SpotBugs and japicmp clean. The rest of the `sdk-core` suite was not run: no existing `sdk-core` code changed, only `SchemaSerdeSupport` gained methods.
- `clean install -pl :dynamic-client`: 316 tests (was 299), 0 failures; Checkstyle, SpotBugs, Javadoc and `dependency:analyze` clean.
- `clean test -pl :architecture-tests`: 79 tests, 0 failures, 11 skipped (the disabled `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest` methods).
- `clean install -pl :smithy-protocol-tests` (after `install -P quick -pl :codegen,:codegen-maven-plugin,:smithy-protocol-tests-core --am`): 923 tests, 16 skipped: awsJson1_0 68/0/6, awsJson1_1 132/0/0, restJson1 264/0/7, restXml 200/0/1, awsQuery 81/0/0, ec2Query 67/0/0, rpcv2Cbor 74/0/2 (passed/failed/skipped), unchanged.
- `aws-core`, `codegen` and the protocol modules did not change.

**Open questions.**
- **Event streams.** Which option: build task 7.20 stage (c) (A), a dynamic-client-only transport (B), or keep failing before sending until 7.20 (c)–(e) resume (C, the current state)? Requirements 31.2, 31.3 (event frames) and 31.5 stay open until then.
- **`DataStream` surface.** Add the design's factories and smithy-java's blocking views now, or when a consumer needs them?
- **Call form.** `call` accepts only a data stream over a `RequestBody`, and `callAsync` only one over an `AsyncRequestBody` (the strict reading of Requirement 31.6). Should either form also adapt the other kind, or a `DataStream` from elsewhere?
- **Transformer response type.** The transformers receive the output `Document`, not the `DynamicResponse`, so the HTTP response and response metadata are not visible to them, as they are not from `call`. Keep, or offer `DynamicResponse` transformers?
- **Overload ambiguity.** `call(op, input, null)` and `callAsync(op, input, null)` no longer compile. Rename the transformer forms (for example `callStreaming`) instead?
- **Streaming outputs without a transformer** fail before sending. Keep that, or discard the body as a non-streaming call would?

## Mock_Plugin for the dynamic client (task 13.14)

**Target.** `test-utils/client-mock-plugin`: `MockQueue.enqueueDocument(Document)` (new), `internal.Responses.validateOutput` (now returns the value to serialize) and its private `StructView`, the five generators that call it, and Javadoc and message updates in `MockPlugin`, `ResponseGenerator` and `internal.ClientConfigurationAccess`. Test scope: `dynamic-client` and `smithy-model`. No `dynamic-client` or `sdk-core` code changed. Requirement 35.9; design D.4 ("works with sync, async and dynamic clients, because all three use the transport seam").

**Transport seam.** Unchanged. The dynamic client runs plugins on a `DynamicServiceClientConfigurationBuilder`, which wraps an `SdkClientConfiguration.Builder` field, so `ClientConfigurationAccess` finds it by type as it does for generated builders (the task 9.14 reflection, still not public API). The plugin runs twice per client, once for the sync and once for the async configuration (task 13.4). Each run installs its own `MockClientTransport` and `MockAsyncClientTransport` and adds one `InputRecorder` to that configuration. All transports call the same plugin, so sync and async calls consume the same matchers and queues in call order, and each call records one `MockedRequest`, whose input is the coerced `DynamicRequest`.

**`StructDocument` outputs (the task 13.1 open question, the 13.14 call sites).** `StructDocument.serialize` writes one `writeDocument` (the Document rule), so the generators must not call it for an output:
- `AwsJson`, `RpcV2Cbor`, `RestJson` and `RestXml` serialized the output with `output.serialize(...)` (the codec's `serialize`, or `ResponseSerializer.shapeValue(output)`). Without a fix, restJson1 and restXml fail with `IllegalStateException("Expected a structure for HTTP bindings, but found Schema{id='smithy.api#Document', type=document}")`. awsJson1_0 and rpcv2Cbor write the contents with a `__type` member first (`{"__type":"test.mock.things#PutThingOutput","id":"a"}`), which the clients ignore as an unknown member.
- awsQuery (`EnvelopeStruct` calls `writeStruct(member, output)`) and ec2Query (`AugmentedStruct` calls `serializeMembers`) were not affected. Neither were error bodies, because `AugmentedStruct.serializeMembers` calls the error's `serializeMembers`.
- Fix: `Responses.validateOutput` returns the output itself, or, when the output is a `Document` (the dynamic client's `StructDocument`, and the task 11.1 `Document.of(SerializableShape)` structures, which are also `SerializableStruct`s), a `StructView` that delegates `sdkSchema`, `serializeMembers` and `getMemberValue` and keeps `SerializableStruct`'s `serialize` (`writeStruct(sdkSchema(), this)`). All five output paths use the returned value. The check is on `Document`, not on `StructDocument`, so `client-mock-plugin` needs no main dependency on `dynamic-client`. This answers the 13.14 part of the task 13.1 question: `StructDocument` keeps the Document `serialize`, and the call site writes it as a structure.
- Negative evidence (not committed): with `validateOutput` returning the output unchanged, 8 of the 22 new tests fail: the restJson1 and restXml cases (the exception above) and the awsJson1_0 body check (`__type`).

**`DocumentException`.** No change was needed. It is a `SerializableStruct` whose schema has the `error` trait, so `MockQueue.enqueueError` accepts it, `MockPlugin` checks its ShapeId against `DynamicOperation.errorSchemas()`, and the generators write the protocol's error response: the `httpError` status (or 400/500), the error type where the protocol puts it, the members from `getContents()`, the message (`rawMessage()`, which falls back to the `message` member) and the request ID. The dynamic client's error registry (task 13.9) then resolves it to a `DocumentException` with equal contents and the same schema instance. A test builds one with `client.createStruct(errorId, members).asShape(DocumentException.builder(errorSchema).requestId(...))`.

| Task, design or source | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Design D.4 `MockQueue.enqueue(SerializableStruct)` ("POJO output or schema-backed Document"); smithy-java's `DynamicClient.createStruct` returns `SerializableStruct`, so `queue.enqueue(client.createStruct(...))` compiles there | New `MockQueue.enqueueDocument(Document)`: it enqueues a document that is a `SerializableStruct` and throws `IllegalArgumentException` ("Only a schema-backed document can be enqueued, but the map document has no schema. ...") for any other document. Usage: `queue.enqueueDocument(client.createStruct(outputShapeId, Document.of(members)))` | The SDK's `createStruct` returns `Document` (design C.2 and its porting map row, task 13.7), so `enqueue` would need a cast to an implementation detail. An `enqueue(Document)` overload would be ambiguous at compile time for an argument typed `StructDocument`, which is both. Changing `createStruct`'s return type would undo a recorded design decision. Not added: a `MockQueue` helper taking an operation name, which would need `dynamic-client` types or a late-bound item |
| Task context: put the dynamic-client mock tests in `client-mock-plugin` (test dependency on `dynamic-client`) or in `dynamic-client` (test dependency on `client-mock-plugin`) | In `client-mock-plugin`, with `dynamic-client` and `smithy-model` at test scope | The other direction is a reactor cycle. With a scratch `client-mock-plugin` test dependency in `core/dynamic-client/pom.xml`, `mvn -o validate -pl :dynamic-client` fails: `cloudwatch --> codegen-maven-plugin --> codegen --> dynamic-client --> client-mock-plugin --> cloudwatch` (`client-mock-plugin`'s tests use the generated CloudWatch, Route 53 and Account clients, and `codegen`'s tests use `dynamic-client`). The chosen direction validates. It also lets one test compare the generated and dynamic Account clients (Requirement 35.9) |
| `MockPlugin` Javadoc: "Mocks a generated client" | Generated or dynamic client; how the two configurations share the queue; the dynamic usage example. `ClientConfigurationAccess`'s failure message says "of a generated or dynamic client" | Documentation only |

**Tests.** `DynamicClientMockPluginTest` (22, JUnit 5 and AssertJ; `MockPluginTest` and `ResponseGeneratorRoundTripTest` unchanged). New fixture `dynamic-things.smithy`: four sigv4 services, `JsonThings` (awsJson1_0), `RestJsonThings` (restJson1), `CborThings` (rpcv2Cbor) and `XmlThings` (restXml), which share `GetThing` (label input; output with a string, an integer, a header-bound string, a timestamp, a list and a nested structure; error `ThingNotFound`, `httpError` 404) and `PutThing`, and the service error `AccessDenied`. Every client gets HTTP clients that count and fail every call; all counts stay 0.
- For each of the four protocols:
  - two queued `StructDocument`s from `createStruct`, returned in order by sync calls, each equal to the queued document, with discriminator `GetThingOutput` and the header-bound member;
  - an async call;
  - two queued `DocumentException`s, thrown by a sync call and as the cause of an async call's `ExecutionException`: exact class, equal contents, discriminator `ThingNotFound`, the same schema instance, status 404, request ID and error message.
- Sync and async calls on one queue consume it in call order.
- A raw response is deserialized unchanged (the header member and body members).
- Another operation's output fails with `"Expected one of [test.mock.things#GetThingOutput, test.mock.things#ThingNotFound, test.mock.things#AccessDenied] but queued test.mock.things#PutThingOutput"` and is consumed.
- An empty queue fails sync and async calls with `"Mock queue exhausted: no queued result or matcher for the call to test.mock.things#GetThing"`, with both requests recorded.
- `generateOutput` of a `StructDocument`: the exact awsJson1_0 body `{"id":"a"}`, and no `__type` in the CBOR body.
- `enqueueDocument` rejects a map document.
- A counting plugin that delegates to `MockPlugin` runs twice per client build. Each call records one request with its `DynamicRequest` input, `X-Amz-Target` and `Authorization`.
- A client interceptor sees the signed request (method, encoded label path, sigv4 scope) in `beforeTransmission`, and the mocked status and header in `afterTransmission`.
- Account (Requirement 35.9, the same queued item on all three client kinds), with the dynamic client built from `services/account/model/model.json`, which the generated client is generated from. The test fails if the file is missing.
  - The generated `GetAlternateContactResponse`, and the `StructDocument` created from it, each queued on the generated sync, generated async and dynamic clients: all six outputs equal `Document.of(generated)`.
  - The generated `ResourceNotFoundException`, and a `DocumentException` of the same shape, each queued on the three clients: the generated clients throw `ResourceNotFoundException` (404) and the dynamic client throws `DocumentException` (404). The documents of their members are equal.

**Observed behavior** (not changed). The Account errors' `errorType` member is bound to `x-amzn-ErrorType`, the header in which the restJson1 generator writes the error's ShapeId (`HeaderErrorSerializer.AMZN_ERROR_HEADER`). So every client kind reads `errorType` back as `com.amazonaws.account#ResourceNotFoundException`, even when the queued item had none. The kinds still agree. A queued item with an `errorType` value was not tested: the member and the error type then compete for one header.

**Not covered.** awsJson1_1, awsQuery and ec2Query on the dynamic client (the generators are the ones task 9.12 tested; only the `StructView` path is new for them). `MockPlugin` passed as a per-call plugin in the override configuration. The optional property test 13.15 (Property 49) is not written.

**Runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, from a new `/tmp/aws-sdk-1314-wt` worktree of `109065db9f3` with the changes copied in; `build-tools` installed first, then `install -P quick -pl :dynamic-client,:schema,:sdk-core`):
- `clean install -pl :client-mock-plugin`: 60 tests, 0 failures (`ResponseGeneratorRoundTripTest` 29, `MockPluginTest` 9, `DynamicClientMockPluginTest` 22). Checkstyle, SpotBugs, Javadoc, `dependency:analyze` and japicmp clean.
- `clean test -pl :architecture-tests`: 79 tests, 0 failures, 11 skipped (the disabled `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest` methods). `DependencyScopeTest` (3) and `DependencyTreeTest` (5) pass: the new dependency is test scope.
- `clean test -pl :schema-serde-source-compat` (uses `MockPlugin` through `MockedCalls`): 44 tests, 0 failures.
- `mvn -o validate -pl :client-mock-plugin,:dynamic-client` passes; the reverse dependency direction fails, as shown above.

**Open questions.**
- `MockQueue.enqueueDocument` is a new public name for "a schema-backed Document" (the second queued kind of Requirement 35.4). Should `createStruct` return `StructDocument` (or `SerializableStruct`, as in smithy-java) instead, so that `enqueue` works and this method is not needed?
- The transport is still installed by reflection (task 9.14). The dynamic client makes a public transport setter on `SdkServiceClientConfiguration.Builder` more useful, because both of its configuration builders would get it.

## Dynamic Client SEP behavioral cases DC-1…DC-30 (task 13.16)

**Target.** `core/dynamic-client` tests only; no main code changed and no bug was found. `DynamicClientBehaviorTest` has one or more methods per case, each named `dcNN_...`. Design D lists the class as `DynamicClientSepTest`; the task text names it `DynamicClientBehaviorTest`, which is used.

**Fixtures.**
- `sprockets.smithy`: the SEP's reference model, unchanged. `SprocketsModels` applies each case's "Given" traits with IDL `apply` statements, and swaps the `awsJson1_0` trait for the protocol under test. It caches one model per protocol.
- Protocol variants:
  - restJson1 and restXml get `@http` on every operation and `@httpPayload` on every streaming member, which Smithy's `StreamingTrait` validation requires. Smithy's `HttpPayload` validation then rejects `UploadSprocket`'s unbound `checksum: Blob`. No HTTP binding trait can target a blob, so in these variants the member becomes `@httpHeader("x-amz-sprocket-checksum") checksum: String`, a text edit.
  - awsQuery and ec2Query get `@xmlNamespace` on the service.
- `SprocketsProtocol`: the seven Supported_Protocols in the SDK's priority order. For each one it writes output and error responses by hand (JSON through smithy-model `Node`, CBOR through the test's own `TestCbor`, XML and query as text) and reads the top-level members of request bodies. It never uses the SDK's serializers (Requirement 41.3).
- `ProtocolTestModels`: the Smithy protocol test models, used cheaply. `maven-dependency-plugin` copies `smithy-aws-protocol-tests`, `smithy-protocol-tests`, `smithy-protocol-test-traits` and `smithy-validation-model` to `target/protocol-test-models` in `generate-test-resources`, and the test imports the JARs by path (about 1 s to assemble 17 services). They are not test dependencies: their `META-INF/smithy/manifest` would put their services into every model the other tests assemble with `discoverModels`, which breaks single-service resolution. No reactor cycle (they are external artifacts), and `dependency:analyze` is unaffected.
- Prototype model: Account (`services/account/model/model.json`, restJson1) for DC-2 and DC-5. `DynamicTestModels.accountPrototype()` now assembles it once, and `DynamicClientResolutionTest` uses it.
- Shared helpers: `CallTestClients.builder(...)` (the client builder without the endpoint override) and `CallTestClients.clientOptions(...)` (the plugin options reflection, moved from `DynamicClientResolutionTest`). `RecordingHttpClients.Stub` gains a `byte[]` body factory and `fullResponse()`.

**Cases.** "7" means all seven protocols, as a `@ParameterizedTest` over `SprocketsProtocol`.

| ID | Test method(s) | Protocols | Status | Deviation or note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DC-1 | `dc01_buildWithoutAModel_failsBecauseTheModelIsRequired` | — | Pass | `NullPointerException("A Smithy model is required")` |
| DC-2 | `dc02_modelWithOneServiceAndNoServiceId_resolvesThatService`, `dc02_accountPrototypeModel_resolvesItsOnlyService` | 7, Account | Pass | — |
| DC-3 | `dc03_modelWithTwoServicesAndNoServiceId_failsNamingBoth` | — | Pass | Given: a second service `OtherSprockets`. The message lists both IDs, sorted |
| DC-4 | `dc04_modelWithoutServices_fails` | — | Pass | The reference model with the service removed (`ModelTransformer.removeShapes`) |
| DC-5 | `dc05_noExplicitProtocol_resolvesTheImplementationOfTheServiceProtocol`, `dc05_accountPrototypeModel_resolvesRestJson1` | 7, Account | Pass | The SEP names awsJson1_0; every protocol is checked against its `ClientProtocol` class |
| DC-6 | `dc06_protocolTraitWithoutAnImplementation_failsNamingTheProtocolTraits` | — | Pass | Given: a custom `@protocolDefinition` trait in place of awsJson1_0 |
| DC-7 | `dc07_explicitTransportOfTheProtocolsMessageExchange_selectsTheMatchingProtocol` | 7 | Pass | A plugin sets `CLIENT_TRANSPORT` and `ASYNC_CLIENT_TRANSPORT` to a recording transport of `HttpMessageExchange`. Sync and async calls go through it, and the HTTP clients get nothing. All SDK protocols exchange HTTP messages, so the test cannot show a choice among protocols of different exchanges |
| DC-8 | `dc08_noEndpointProviderAndAStaticEndpointRuleSet_resolvesTheRuleUrlAtCallTime` | 7 | Pass | No endpoint override. The request goes to `https://example.com` with the protocol's path |
| DC-9 | `dc09_callWithoutInput_returnsAStructureDocument` | 7 | Pass | — |
| DC-10 | `dc10_callWithAMapDocument_serializesTheModeledMemberName` | 7 | Pass | The body has exactly one member, `id` (`Id` on ec2Query, which capitalizes member names). The SEP's native-map overload is not offered: design C.2 removed `call(String, Map)` |
| DC-11 | `dc11_interceptorInspectingTheInput_seesTheInputDocument` | 7 | Pass | Sync and async |
| DC-12 | `dc12_callOfAnOperationNotInTheService_failsNamingTheOperationAndTheService` | — | Pass | Sync throws; async returns a failed future; nothing is sent |
| DC-13 | `dc13_errorWithAnAbsoluteErrorType_throwsAModeledDocumentException`, `dc13_protocolTestInvalidGreetingResponse_throwsADocumentExceptionWithTheTestParams` | 7, 7 | Pass | The SEP's JSON body becomes each protocol's error format: `__type` in JSON and CBOR, `<Code>` in XML. The second method runs each protocol's `InvalidGreeting` response test case. Its expected contents come from the case's `params` through `createStruct`, not from the SDK's deserializer |
| DC-14 | `dc14_errorWithARelativeErrorType_resolvesItAgainstTheServiceNamespace` | 7 | Pass | — |
| DC-15 | `dc15_serviceLevelErrorWithARelativeErrorType_throwsADocumentException` | 7 | Pass | — |
| DC-16 | `dc16_serviceWithoutModeledAuth_resolvesToNoAuth` | 7 | Pass | Every operation's effective schemes are `[smithy.api#noAuth]`. The selected scheme is no-auth, and there is no `Authorization` header |
| DC-17 | `dc17_binaryValueForANonStreamingBlobMember_isWrittenInTheProtocolsBlobEncoding` | 7 | Pass | Given: `GetSprocket` gets a `checksum: Blob` input member (a text edit). The reference model's only non-streaming blob sits next to a required stream, which the RPC protocols reject (DC-21), and the REST variants cannot bind it. On the wire: base64 text in JSON, XML and query; a byte string in CBOR |
| DC-18 | `dc18_stringForABlobMember_failsInputCoercion` | 7 | Pass | Same Given as DC-17. `SerializationException` (exact message) before any interceptor, sync and async |
| DC-19 | `dc19_operationAuthDifferentFromTheServiceDefault_isExposedAndUsedInOrder` | 7 | Pass | Given: the service has sigv4 and bearer with `@auth([sigv4, httpBearerAuth])`, and `GetSprocket` has `@auth([httpBearerAuth, sigv4])`. A bearer scheme with a fixed token is registered with `putAuthScheme`, because the builder has no token provider option (task 13.5 open question). `GetSprocket` is sent with `Bearer`, and `CreateSprocket` is signed with sigv4 |
| DC-20 | `dc20_optionalAuthOperation_hasNoAuthLastInItsEffectiveAuthSchemes` | 7 | Pass | Given: sigv4 on the service and `@optionalAuth` on `GetSprocket`, whose schemes are `[sigv4, noAuth]`. With a sigv4 scheme that has no identity provider, `GetSprocket` falls back to no-auth, and `CreateSprocket` fails with `SdkException` |
| DC-21 | `dc21_streamingBlobInput_isSentWithoutBufferingIntoADocument` | 7 | Pass, with deviation on 5 | restJson1 and restXml: sync and async uploads send the exact bytes. Interceptors see a blob-typed stream document, and the body has been opened 0 times when the request reaches the transport. rpcv2Cbor, both awsJson and both query protocols: `SdkClientException("Unable to marshall UploadSprocket: Streaming [blob ]input is not supported by <protocol>")` before sending. That is task 7.19's decision, because these protocols have no streaming blob binding. smithy-java's `AwsJsonProtocol` writes the input with its codec, and `ShapeSerializer.writeDataStream` does nothing by default. So smithy-java appears to drop the stream rather than reject it. This was read in the source, not run. The SEP's reference model is awsJson1_0, so the case as written cannot pass in the SDK |
| DC-22 | `dc22_outputEventStream_failsBeforeSendingWhileEventStreamsAreDeferred` | 7 | Pass, Expect not met | Event streams are deferred (task 7.20 stage (c), user decision, 13.13 option C). Sync: `IllegalArgumentException`. Async: `UnsupportedOperationException` (exact messages). No hook runs and nothing is sent |
| DC-23 | `dc23_serviceWithSeveralAvailableProtocols_selectsTheFirstInPriorityOrder` | 13 trait lists | Pass | For each protocol P, the service carries P and every protocol after it in priority order, in priority order and reversed (13 lists; the last has one trait). P is selected every time |
| DC-24 | `dc24_walkFromTheOperationToTheIdMemberTarget_reachesStringAndTerminates` | — | Pass | The walk goes from `DynamicOperation.inputSchema()` through member targets, with a visited set. It reaches only `GetSprocketInput` and `smithy.api#String` |
| DC-25 | `dc25_jsonNameMemberTargetingAPatternString_reportsTheTargetTypeAndItsDirectJsonName` (pass); `dc25_..._exposesTheTargetPatternInItsEffectiveTraits` (`@Disabled`) | — | Partly disabled | The type, target, and effective and direct `@jsonName` pass. `@pattern` is a constraint trait, and the Trait_Inclusion_Policy excludes those from runtime schemas (Requirement 25.3, task 13.1). The SEP expects it in the effective set, so the disabled method needs a design decision |
| DC-26 | `dc26_twoUntypedCustomTraits_convertAndAreEachRetrievableByShapeId` | — | Pass | Both conversions succeed. Under the default policy, the two `com.example` traits are omitted, as policy exclusions (which the SEP allows), not because they are untyped. With a `@protocolDefinition(traits: [...])` on the service that selects them, each is retrievable by its own ShapeId through `Schema.getGenericTrait(ShapeId)` with its value. Design D says `TraitKey.generic`; the port has `getGenericTrait`, because generic traits have no `TraitKey` |
| DC-27 | `dc27_streamingBlobOutput_isDeliveredWithoutBufferingIntoADocument` | 7 | Pass, with deviation on 5 | restJson1 and restXml: `ResponseTransformer.toBytes()` and `AsyncResponseTransformer.toBytes()` get the bytes, and the output document has no `data` member. RPC protocols: `SdkClientException("Unable to unmarshall DownloadSprocket: Streaming [blob ]output is not supported by <protocol>")`, but only after the request was sent (see the open questions) |
| DC-28 | `dc28_inputEventStream_failsBeforeSendingWhileEventStreamsAreDeferred` | 7 | Pass, Expect not met | Deferred, as DC-22. The event publisher is never subscribed |
| DC-29 | `dc29_bidirectionalEventStream_failsBeforeSendingWhileEventStreamsAreDeferred` | 7 | Pass, Expect not met | Deferred, as DC-22. The message names the input member `SyncSprocketsInput$commands` |
| DC-30 | `dc30_pluginWithAnAuxiliaryOperationCallback_callsItOverTheDynamicClient` | 7 | Pass | A test `SdkPlugin` takes a required `Function<Document, Document> createSprocket` and checks it with `Validate.paramNotNull` in its constructor, so a null callback fails at wire-up. Its interceptor calls the callback, which calls `CreateSprocket` on the same dynamic client, before each `GetSprocket` and sends the new ID in a header. `GetSprocket` succeeds, and two requests are sent in order. No feature in the prototype set needs an auxiliary operation (task 13.10), so the plugin is test code |

**Summary.** All 30 cases have tests. 34 methods give 178 invocations: 177 pass, and 1 is disabled (the `@pattern` half of DC-25). The SEP's "Expect" is not met for DC-22, DC-28 and DC-29 (event streams deferred). It is not met for DC-21 and DC-27 on the five RPC protocols (no streaming blob binding in the SDK) or for the `@pattern` part of DC-25 (constraint traits are excluded). Each of those tests asserts the SDK's current behavior instead. The optional property tests (13.2, 13.3, 13.6, 13.8, 13.11, 13.12, 13.15) are not written.

**Runs** (`mvn -o`, JDK 21.0.2, from a new `/tmp/aws-sdk-1316-wt` worktree of `5c501e5eae2` with the changes copied in; `build-tools` installed first, then `install -P quick -pl :dynamic-client --am`):
- `test -pl :dynamic-client -Dtest=DynamicClientBehaviorTest`: 178 tests, 0 failures, 1 skipped (about 3 s).
- `clean install -pl :dynamic-client`: 494 tests (was 316), 0 failures, 1 skipped. Checkstyle, SpotBugs, Javadoc, `dependency:analyze` and japicmp are clean.
- `clean test -pl :architecture-tests`: 79 tests, 0 failures, 11 skipped (the disabled `NamingConventionTest` and `CodingConventionTest` methods).

**Open questions.**
- **DC-25 `@pattern`.** Keep excluding constraint traits from runtime schemas (Requirement 25.3) and record DC-25 as partly unmet, or include `@pattern` (or all constraint traits) in dynamic-client schemas? The disabled test holds the SEP's expectation.
- **Streaming blobs on RPC protocols (DC-21, DC-27).** The SEP's reference model is awsJson1_0 with a streaming blob input and output, but the SDK protocols have no binding for them. Input fails before sending, while output fails only after the request was sent. Should the protocols, or `DynamicStreaming`'s checks, reject an RPC operation with a streaming blob output before sending, as the input case does? The failure messages also differ: "Streaming blob input/output" for awsJson and rpcv2Cbor, "Streaming input/output" for the query protocols.
- **Design D's class name** is `DynamicClientSepTest`, and the task's is `DynamicClientBehaviorTest`. Update design D to match?
